# Code review 3: the requirements and documents against the code, read whole

**Scope.** Review 3 reads commit `6916954` (the tip of PR #15, branch `review-process`, whose
squash onto `main` has the same tree), measured from review 2's `294792b`. It follows
`docs/REVIEW.md`: scope **full**, every unit's code read whole against its sections (§1);
checking **quick, checked later** (§11.4): no claim was checked before it entered; then, on
Oct 25, a checker per block and unit (Sonnet 5.5 at high effort, REVIEW.md §14) checked every
claim as §8 asks, and a sweep checked what the checkers found. The verdicts sit beside each
claim in the work pad; the tables' confirmations, statuses and severities follow them; **serial**,
each session a subagent with a fresh context, run one at a time by a coordinating session, as
the user chose for this review (no parallel agents, no workflow). It ran on Oct 24 and 25, 2026
(Chicago time); its work pad, [`code-review-0003-2026-10-24/`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/),
holds every session's notes, and its `log.md` the sessions.

**Paths.** Code paths are relative to `web/server/src/moolakata/`. `mkc/` is
`connector/moolakata_connector/`. Other paths start at the repository's root. A claim's
**Code** line gives the full path; its body, copied from the work pad, may use the work pad's
short forms (`map.md`: `src/`, `ui/` for `web/ui/`, `tests/`, `mig/`).

**Ranks** follow `gaps.md`: **High** blocks the next release, **Med** is needed before it,
**Low** can follow it.

**Ids** are `CR3-<n>`, given at assembly in the order of the sections (§10), then renumbered at
the user's request, before the walk-through, in order of severity (High, Med, Low) and then of
confirmations, most first; claims alike in both keep their assembly order. Each severity has a
part of its own: its table, then its claims. The last part gives each claim's work-pad id, and
the id it had at assembly, which the branch's commits and the work pad's `log.md` cite. The
earlier reviews' ids (review 2's K, P, B, C, N, A; review 1's F) are cited as they give them.

**Claims confirmed after assembly** took the next free ids (§10), 340 to 374 at assembly: what
the sweep confirmed of what the checkers found, the defects only the model experiment's runs of
unit 05 found (checked by its comparison and by unit 05's checker; the work pad's
`model-experiment.md`), and two parts the checks split off (CR3-5 from CR3-225, CR3-374 from
CR3-249). Each sits among the claims of its severity.

**Withheld claims.** 19 security and privacy claims (High or Med when withheld; the checks later
rated two of them Low) were first published alone, as *Withheld until fixed* (§2, option 2), their
details kept in the work pad's git-ignored `withheld.md`. On Oct 28, as no household uses a server
yet, the user had them published: CR3-3 with its fix, the other 18 as they stand, and `withheld.md`
is now kept in the work pad like the units' files.

## Summary

*Ready to release: no. No High open; the Med claims are needed before it (`gaps.md`'s ranks).*

**What matters most.** Correcting a statement line's description teaches the importer to skip every
later line that starts like it, so the next statements from that bank lose those lines, silently or
all of them (CR3-1). Saving the budget's plan dialog unchanged rewrites the plan: it bakes in the
month's moves, refuses reserves and cuts an envelope budget's yearly lines (CR3-2). A file that
takes the reader's worker can reach the host commands' socket and signal the server (CR3-3). One
receipt the other server can't fetch (one over 20 MB, say) stops a link in both directions for good,
with nothing recorded and no alert (CR3-4). Across the units the same faults recur: imports that
bring back deleted rows, drop a file's positions or learn the wrong skips (CR3-13, CR3-15, CR3-16);
money moving between budgets, envelopes and reserves (CR3-39, CR3-41, CR3-46); filed tax years
changing unasked (CR3-27, CR3-33, CR3-35); links stalling or unable to start again (CR3-4, CR3-50,
CR3-51, CR3-53); a re-encryption failing part-way (CR3-58, CR3-59); and the connector's Schwab
driver and file posts (CR3-66 to CR3-70). Of the 374 claims, 4 are High, 71 Med and 299 Low; none is
withheld any more. **Checked later:** of the 347 claims the checkers judged, 273 were confirmed, 55
corrected (6 with a new severity), 18 found plausible and 1 rejected (CR3-360); the sweep confirmed
19 of the 21 things the checkers found. 19 claims are questions for the walk-through.

## Summary - High Severity

4 claims, all at 2 confirmations.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| [CR3-4](#cr3-4) | S11.4 | High | 2 | fixed `f27156a` | One receipt the pulling side can't fetch (past 20 MB, say) stops the link both ways for good, unrecorded and unalerted |

<a id="cr3-1"></a>

### CR3-1: correcting a statement line's description teaches MoolaKata to skip every line that starts like it

*Work pad: U01-4 in [`unit-01-accounts-imports.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-01-accounts-imports.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S1.3.3.3 ("each line can be corrected"), S1.3.3.4 ("the first words of
  lines they left out, which are skipped next time").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/importers/statements.py:384-394`:
  ```text
  kept_keys = {(r.date, r.description) for r in kept}
  ...
  for r in st.rows:
      if (r.date, r.amount) in present:
          continue
      if (r.date, r.description) not in kept_keys and r.description:
          words = " ".join(r.description.lower().split()[:3])
          if words and words not in out["skip"]:
              out["skip"].append(words)
  ```
  `kept` is what the dialog sent back (`ui/imports.py:1153-1163`, from `StatementRowIn`, with
  the description as the person edited it, `web/ui/screens/import.js:487-489, 391-392`);
  `st.rows` is the parse as read. `statements.parse:250-251` drops a skipped line before it is
  shown.
- **What goes wrong:** a kept line whose description was corrected no longer matches its
  parsed self by (date, description), so it is taken for a line left out, and its first three
  words become a skip. Case (run): a UMB-like statement with `09/03 DEBIT CARD PURCHASE AMAZN
  MKTP 60.00` and `09/04 DEBIT CARD PURCHASE KROGER 123 40.00`; the person keeps both and renames
  the first to *Amazon*; learned: `{'skip': ['debit card purchase']}`; the next statement from
  that institution parses to no rows at all, so it is refused as "No transactions were found"
  and offered to be kept unread. When only some lines start that way they vanish silently and
  the statement no longer adds up. Nothing in the app shows or resets a learned layout
  (`StatementLayout` is written only here and in the copy mapping), so it lasts for good, for
  every account of that owner at that institution.
- **Run:** yes, `u01/learn.py` (statements.parse, learn, parse again).
- **Remedy:** learn from what the person decided, not from a re-keyed match: the dialog sends
  each row's index (or its parsed date and description) with the edited one, and only rows sent
  with `include: false` teach a skip; a skip is learned only from a description shared by no
  kept line.
- **Alternatives:** show skipped lines left out (*skipped, as learned*) instead of dropping
  them, so a wrong skip can be ticked back and unlearned; and a way to forget an institution's
  layout (Settings › Imports or the dialog).

<a id="cr3-2"></a>

### CR3-2: Edit plan saved unchanged rewrites the plan: moves baked in, reserves refused, an envelope's yearly lines cut to a twelfth

*Work pad: U05-17 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S7.1.2 ("The plan holds from a month on"), S7.11 (a move is "for one
  month"), S7.10.1, S7.13.1 ("a yearly line a twelfth of its amount" each month).
- **Code:** `web/ui/screens/budgets.js:1625-1636`, the plan dialog starts each line from the
  month's row, taking *planned less carried* as the line's amount:
  ```js
  rows.map((r) => ({
    categoryId: r.categoryId,
    amount: String(dec(r.planned).sub(dec(r.carried))),
  ```
  but `Row.planned` is not the line's amount: for a tracking line it is amount + carried + the
  month's moves (`budgets/__init__.py:490-491`); for a reserve it is the month's put-in and
  `carried` its starting balance (`:473-474`); for an envelope row it is start + assigned +
  moved, a yearly line assigning a twelfth (`:435-444`, `envelope.py:116-118`). The estimate
  dialog keeps the rows it doesn't replace the same way, and sends no `target` or `heldInId`
  for them (`budgets.js:1539-1546`).
- **What goes wrong:** Cases (run, the dialog's lines sent as it builds them):
  1. Tracking: *Dining* $300 and *Entertainment* $200, with a $40 move from Entertainment to
     Dining this month. Opening **Edit plan** and saving with nothing changed sends Dining
     $340 and Entertainment $160: from this month on, every month, the move is in the plan,
     and this month counts it twice (Dining $380 available).
  2. With a reserve (*Car maintenance* $100 a month, target $1,000, $300 held at the start of
     December) the same save sends **−$200** for it, and the server refuses the whole plan
     (*422 amount-negative, Plan zero or more*). Any reserve past its second month blocks
     saving the plan until its amount is retyped.
  3. Envelope: *Entertainment* $1,200 a year. Saved unchanged it becomes **$100 a year**
     ($8.33 assigned a month), and To Budget jumps from $4,900 to $4,991.67.
  4. **Estimate from history** keeps the other rows the same way and drops their targets: a
     reserve with a balance answers 409 *reserve-balance*, which that dialog has no *Save
     anyway* for; one at nothing loses its target silently.
- **Run:** yes, `test_u05.py::test_plan_dialog_saved_unchanged` (`SENT [('160.00', None),
  ('340.00', None), ('-200.00', '1000.00')]`, `PUT 422 … amount-negative`) and
  `::test_plan_dialog_envelope_yearly` (`ENVSENT [('100.00', 'year')]`, then `planned 8.33`,
  To Budget `4900.00 → 4991.67`), `tmp/u05/run3.txt`. The browser itself was not driven.
- **Remedy:** the month view sends each row's line as stored (its `amount`, `target`,
  `heldInId`, alongside the figures), and both dialogs start from that; a test that saves the
  dialog's lines unchanged and finds the plan unchanged.
- **Alternatives:** the dialogs read `GET /budgets/{id}/plan?month=` (a route returning the
  lines in force) rather than the month's figures.

<a id="cr3-3"></a>

### CR3-3: the reader's worker may still open Unix sockets, and the host commands' socket trusts whoever reaches it: a file that takes the worker can rotate the master key and delete the copies

*Work pad: U06-3 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md); withheld until its fix, `05f523a`.*

- **Kind:** security. **Severity:** High. **Req:** S9.8.4.2 ("**The worker is a sandbox**, for a
  file that exploits the libraries that read it … a worker an exploit has taken can't run code
  in the server"; "a seccomp filter denies it network sockets; on a kernel with Landlock … it
  can't open the data directory"), S15.4.7 and S13.6 (host commands over the server's socket),
  S15.7.1 (rotation over the socket needs no old phrase), R1.17.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/sandbox.py:85-90`:
  ```text
              ins(jeq, 1, 0, socket_nr),  # 6: socket: on to 8
              ins(ret, 0, 0, SECCOMP_RET_ALLOW),  # 7: anything else
              ins(ld, 0, 0, 16),  # 8: its first argument, the address family
              ins(jeq, 0, 1, AF_UNIX),  # 9: a local socket: allowed at 10
              ins(ret, 0, 0, SECCOMP_RET_ALLOW),  # 10
  ```
  `host.py:1-6`: the socket `/run/moolakata/sock` "is no network listener: only the server's own
  account (or root) inside the container can open it, so reaching it is the host access that
  authenticates these commands". The worker runs under that same account
  (`receipts/reading.py:75-82`: `Popen([sys.executable, "-I", "-m",
  "moolakata.receipts.worker", …], env={}, close_fds=True)`, no change of user). `host.py:67-90`:
  `POST /host/keys/rotate` takes `phrase` (the new one), no old phrase, and `copies` with
  `delete` for each copy, deleted at once. Landlock (`sandbox.read_only`, first-version rights
  only) governs opening files, not `connect()` to a Unix socket by path, and on a kernel
  without Landlock (this build host's, and the alert's case, S9.8.4.2.5) nothing does.
- **What goes wrong:** an emailed invoice PDF (or a statement the connector fetches, a HEIC
  photo, an OFX file) carries an exploit for pdfium, libheif or Pillow, and runs code in the
  worker as it is read. The worker opens `socket(AF_UNIX)`, connects to
  `/run/moolakata/sock` and posts `/host/keys/rotate` with a phrase only the attacker knows and
  every copy marked `delete`: the keyring is re-wrapped at once, the backups and `pre-upgrade/`
  copies on the host are deleted, and Activity shows a rotation "on the host". At the next
  restart the household's phrase no longer unlocks anything. The same reach gives `/host/backup`
  and `/host/unlock` (lockout counting), and the worker can also signal the server (same
  account) to stop it, which locks it until a person comes.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u06/s/unix_after_seccomp.py`: a process under `sandbox.no_network()` is
  refused `AF_INET` (EACCES) and still connects to a 0700-directory Unix listener of its
  account and sends `POST /host/keys/rotate`. Landlock is absent on this host (kernel 4.18), so
  that path rests on reading and Landlock's documented rights.
- **Remedy:** the filter refuses `socket()` for every family (the worker talks only over the
  pipes it inherited), and `socketpair`, `connect` and `bind` too for good measure; and the
  worker runs under an account of its own (a second uid in the image, or a user namespace),
  so neither the socket nor signals reach the server. Host routes that change keys also check
  `SO_PEERCRED` against the server's pid (refusing the worker's).
- **Alternatives:** keep AF_UNIX open only for what a library needs (none of the four do) and
  move the host socket into a directory the worker's Landlock rules leave out (not enough on
  hosts without Landlock); or have rotation over the socket ask the old phrase as well, which
  S15.7.1 says it needn't (that rule would then need changing).
- **Fixed:** the worker's filter refuses a socket of every family, socket pairs, `connect` and
  `bind`, and every signal but to itself. Not done: a separate account for the worker, and a
  peer check on the host socket; with no socket to reach it, they are a second wall, left for
  a later change.

<a id="cr3-4"></a>

### CR3-4: one receipt the pulling side can't fetch stops the link both ways for good, and nothing shows it

*Work pad: U08-1 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S11.4 ("Pulls run whether or not anyone has unlocked the copy. When
  the source is unreachable, the copy stays, marked stale"), S11.2 ("each exchange carries both
  directions"), S11.5.1, S13.5 (20 MB answers), S12.11.1 (a failing link alerts); DC-1 (D2-1,
  D3-1) with D3-5; `link-protocol.md` §4.3, §4.4.
- **Code:**
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/links/receive.py:33-35` (`take`), before anything of the change is
    kept:
    ```text
        names = dict(delivered or {})
        missing = [f for f in change.get("fetch") or [] if f not in names]
        names |= fetch_files(srv, link_id, missing)
    ```
    and `fetch_files` (`:156-162`) re-raises whatever `client.iter_file` raises.
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/net.py:24` `MAX_RESPONSE = 20 * 1024 * 1024`, applied to every
    answer by `_Capped` (`:180-192`), so a `file` answer whose sealed bytes pass 20 MB raises
    `ResponseTooLarge`, which `client.iter_file` (`links/client.py:178-179`) turns into
    `LinkError("unreachable", …)`.
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/links/manage.py:482-498`: the exchange records success
    (`link.lastOkAt, link.lastError, link.failingSince = clock.now(), None, None`) before it
    takes the change, and `receive.take` (`:498`) runs outside the `try` that records
    failures; this side's own change (`:509-515`) is worked out only after it. `exchange_all`
    (`:666-667`) then swallows the `LinkError`: `with contextlib.suppress(LinkError):  #
    recorded on the link; alerts follow it`.
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/links/serve.py:354` `full = full or have != link.sentSeq`, with
    `sentSeq` and `LinkSent` committed when the change is worked out (`:400-405`), so the next
    exchange gets a full change, which names the same receipt again.
- **What goes wrong:** Alice shares *Checking* with Carol on her parents' server with
  **Include receipts**, and one of its receipts seals to more than 20 MB (a 21 MB scan: allowed,
  the limit is 25 MB by default and up to 100). Carol's server pulls hourly; the first part of
  the change names the receipt in `fetch`, its `file` answer is cut off, and `take` raises
  before the registry, the inbox or `gotSeq` is touched. Every later exchange asks again from
  the same `have`, gets a full change naming the same receipt, and fails at the same point.
  Nothing Alice shares ever arrives (not even the account's row), nothing Carol's household
  shares with Alice's ever leaves (this side's change is never sent), the link shows *active*
  with a fresh *last exchange* and no error, and no alert is raised. The same holds for any
  failure in fetching one receipt: its file missing at the source, a `not-shared` race, or the
  60-second answer check of U08-6. On the delivering side (`manage.py:510-515`), a receipt the
  peer refuses (sealed past the peer's own receipt limit, which the middleware applies to
  `deliver` bodies, `middleware.py:243-247`) stops that direction the same way, unrecorded.
  DC-1 asked whether one oversize receipt stalls a link's exchange for good: it does, both
  ways, silently, so this is High.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u08/t/test_u08.py::test_oversize_receipt_stalls_both_directions`: a
  `file` answer cut off as `net.Client` cuts one past 20 MB; four pulls later B's `gotSeq` is
  still 0, Carol is told nothing is shared with her, A has taken nothing of Bob's new share
  (`A.gotSeq 0`, `A.sentSeq 4`), `lastError` and `failingSince` stay empty, and no alert is
  open (`run2.txt`).
- **Remedy:** a receipt that can't be fetched or delivered never holds back its change: the
  change is kept with that receipt marked *not arrived* (shown as such in the linked window),
  and the receipt is tried again at the next exchange; any failure after the exchange's first
  call is recorded on the link (`lastError`, `failingSince`) so S12.11.1's alert follows; and
  the decision DC-1 asks for sets the ceiling for sealed receipt bodies (`file` answers read
  through a ceiling tied to S16.10's maximum plus the format's overhead, as `deliver` bodies
  already are). A test with a receipt over the ceiling, through the real `net.Client`.
- **Alternatives:** the source leaves out of `fetch` any receipt sealed past the ceiling and
  sends its record marked *too large to share* (DC-1's first alternative).
- **Needs the user:** the ceiling is DC-1's decision (stream to S16.10's maximum, cap at 20 MB
  and mark *too large to share*, or lower S16.10); the stall and its silence need fixing
  whichever is chosen.

<a id="cr3-5"></a>

### CR3-5: a budget's receiver sees, in *Where the money is*, the balance and deposits of an account a reserve is held in that was shared with the owner but not with them

*Work pad: U05-21b in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md); part of CR3-225, split from it by its check.*

- **Kind:** privacy. **Req:** S7.1.1 ("an account shared with the owner only if that
  account is shared with them too"), R3.4.
- **What was read:** `reserves.held_in` (`budgets/reserves.py:129-226`) takes the held-in account
  from the plan line's `heldInId` (`:143-148`) and reads it by id (`:152`), its transactions
  (`:157-164`) and balance (`:209`), with no check against the viewer's accounts (`accts` is the
  viewer-filtered list, but `by_account` is keyed by `ln.heldInId`); `where` (`ui/budgets.py:1090-1157`)
  labels it `an account not shared` and still sends `balance`, `withdrawals`, `unassigned` (each
  deposit's date, payee, amount) and `transfers`. `review` (`:1481-1489`) uses `held_in` the same way
  for *to move*.
- **Ran:** calibration run B's `test_e12.py` (reran): Carol shares her savings with Alice at read,
  Alice's budget counts it and holds a reserve there, and shares the budget with Bob; Bob's
  `GET /budgets/{id}/where` answers `accountName: "an account not shared"`, `balance: 12422.00`,
  `unassigned: [("Carol private payee", 77.00)]`; a scenario Bob copies shows the same.
- **Rating:** the arrangement is narrow (a third person's account, shared only with the owner, held
  in by a reserve of a budget shared on), as A and C said, but what leaks is the balance and the
  deposits' payees and amounts of an account whose owner never shared it with the viewer, across the
  line R3.4 and S7.1.1 draw, and the code's own `account_ids(viewer)` filter shows the line was meant
  to hold. So Med, and by §2 withheld. Parts 1 and 3 of U05-21 (the review's two figures, a card's
  name) stay Low and need no withholding.
- **Remedy:** as B's: `held_in` takes the viewer and leaves out, or reports only totals for, a
  held-in account the viewer can't see (`access.account_level`); U05-30 (the copy keeps `heldInId`)
  is the same fix's other end.

<a id="cr3-6"></a>

### CR3-6: Capital at risk is defined twice for one short option, and the two disagree

*Work pad: D1-1 in [`docs-1-money.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-1-money.md).*

- **Kind:** the same thing specified twice, differently.
- **Req:** S3.5.5, S3.5.6, S3.9.2.
- **Where:**
  - `S3-positions-and-options.md:68`: "| cash-secured put | strike × multiplier × contracts,
    less the premium |"; `:70`: "| naked call, short straddle and strangle | *unlimited*, or the
    broker's margin requirement the owner types |"; `:78–79`: "An unlimited risk counts as
    such: a total with one in it is *unlimited*, never the sum of the rest."
  - `:81–83`: "an investing account and a portfolio show **Capital at risk** beside their
    options' market value, counting each option outside a strategy as a single leg (S3.9.2)."
  - `:131–133`: "**Capital at risk**: for a long option, the premium paid; for a short one, the
    strike on every share the most contracts open at once cover (the cash to buy them for a
    put, the shares to deliver for a call)."
- **What goes wrong:** one short call, 1 contract at a $250 strike, never grouped (S3.5.1
  proposes single legs only as covered calls), counts in the account's *Capital at risk* as
  $25,000 by S3.9.2. Grouped by hand as a *short call (naked)*, the same leg counts as
  *unlimited* by S3.5.5, and makes the account's total *unlimited*. A short put likewise counts
  strike × 100 without the premium ungrouped, and less the premium grouped. The account's total
  then depends on whether the owner pressed **Group**, and mixes a capital-required measure
  (S3.9.2, meant for the per-trade return) with a max-loss measure (S3.5.5) in one sum.
- **Remedy:** S3.5.6 counts an ungrouped leg by the S3.5.5 row of its single-leg shape (long
  option: premium; short put: strike less premium; naked call: *unlimited* or the margin
  typed), and S3.9.2 says its figure is the per-trade return's base only.
- **Alternatives:** keep S3.9.2 for both, and drop the *unlimited* row for single legs (a naked
  call's capital is the shares to deliver); or show the two figures under different names
  (*capital at risk* and *capital required*).

<a id="cr3-7"></a>

### CR3-7: Who owns a category, and who may add or change one, is never said

*Work pad: D1-13 in [`docs-1-money.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-1-money.md).*

- **Kind:** behaviour named and never specified (candidate gap).
- **Req:** S7.2, S7.12.6, S10.3.1 (the Budget row), S14.10.1.1.
- **Where:** `S7-budgets-gifts-and-giving.md:36–40`: "**Categories** form a two-level tree
  (*Housing › Rent*). … A default set ships, and each category may carry a **tax tag** … and
  **Keep receipts for** a number of years"; `:273–275`: "a transaction's category, splits, tags
  and reimbursable mark are the transaction's"; `S10-users-and-sharing.md:89`: "| **Budget** |
  its categories, plan, moves, …"; `S14-storage-and-secrets.md:103–104`: "An admin sets it, in
  Settings › Storage".
- **What goes wrong:** the requirements never say whether categories are the server's, a
  user's, or a budget's, nor who adds, renames, re-parents or deletes one. Each reading breaks
  something else: per budget (S10.3's table) contradicts S7.12.6 (the category is the
  transaction's, the same in every budget); per user leaves Bob's transaction in Alice's
  shared account showing a category Bob's tree lacks, and Bob's rules (S2.7) setting one
  Alice's budget doesn't have; per server lets any user rename *Groceries* for everyone, while
  only an admin may set its retention (S14.10.1.1). The mockup's budget plan has **Add a
  category**, with no word on whose it becomes. Phase 2 has no rule to check the category
  routes against.
- **Remedy:** a G item (G25), or S7.2 specifies it: categories are the server's (one tree for
  the household, so shared accounts and budgets agree); any user adds a child category; renaming,
  moving and deleting a shipped or another user's category is an admin's; retention stays an
  admin's (S14.10.1.1). Activity records each change.
- **Alternatives:** per user with a shared household tree under a group; or per server with
  every user free to edit, recorded in Activity. **Needs the user.**

<a id="cr3-8"></a>

### CR3-8: A quarterly statement's value raises a stale alert every quarter before the next can arrive

*Work pad: D1-14 in [`docs-1-money.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-1-money.md).*

- **Kind:** the same thing specified twice, differently.
- **Req:** S4.3 (the "No free ticker" row), S1.11.7.1, S1.11.7.3.
- **Where:** `S4-prices.md:35`: "| No free ticker: 529 portfolios, 401(k) collective trusts,
  … | **manual** | — | a value from a statement or an import, or a typed price, with its date. A
  statement value older than 90 days is marked stale and raises an alert (S12.11) |";
  `S1-accounts.md:388–391`: "quarterly for 401(k), 403(b), 457(b) and 529 plans";
  `:398–400`: "a statement is overdue once the interval has passed since the last one ended,
  plus a grace period (15 days for monthly, 30 for quarterly, 60 for yearly)".
- **What goes wrong:** a 401(k)'s collective trust priced from the Mar 31 statement is 90 days
  old on Jun 29 and raises S4.3's alert; the Jun 30 statement can't exist yet, and S1.11.7
  holds the account's own overdue alert until Jul 30. Every quarterly 401(k) and 529 alerts each
  quarter for about a month, about the same missing statement S1.11.7 times with a grace period
  on purpose; a yearly-statement plan alerts for most of each year.
- **Remedy:** S4.3's statement values are stale when the account's statement is overdue
  (S1.11.7.3), and raise no alert of their own: the overdue statement's alert covers them.
- **Alternatives:** 90 days becomes the interval plus its grace period, per account.

<a id="cr3-9"></a>

### CR3-9: S9.6 shows a tax document's file to the filers only; S8.14.3 and S10.3 share documents with helpers, and S9.6 omits several kinds S9.3 links to

*Work pad: D1-25 in [`docs-1-money.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-1-money.md).*

- **Kind:** contradiction across sections (who sees what). (A disagreement between documents, naming no weakness of a running server,
  so not withheld under REVIEW.md §2.)
- **Req:** S9.6, S9.3, S8.14.3, S8.16, S10.3.1 (the Tax book row), L6.
- **Where:** `S9-receipts.md:31–34`: "whoever can read a transaction can see its receipts, a
  gift's through the gift plans that show the gift, a donation's by its owner and those who see
  the giving plan it counts in, and a tax document's file by the tax book's filers (S8.16). An
  unattached receipt is its owner's alone."; `S8-taxes.md:80–82`: "A user who helps prepare the
  return … gets **write**: they can edit filings, attach documents and resolve issues";
  `S10-users-and-sharing.md:92`: "| **Tax book** | its panels, filings and documents (S8.14.3)
  |"; `later.md:56–57`: "a tax book's document files crossing a link with Include receipts"
  (later, so local shares already carry them); `S9-receipts.md:17–20` (S9.3): receipts also link
  to "physical-asset items …, loans …, 529 qualified expenses", and statements are kept on
  accounts (S1.3.3).
- **What goes wrong:** a preparer given **write** on a tax book attaches the W-2 PDF and then,
  by S9.6, can't open it, nor the filed return; by S10.3 and S8.14.3 they can. Read literally,
  S9.6 also gives no rule for a receipt kept only on a physical-asset item, a loan or a 529
  expense, or a statement kept on an account, so the code's choice there (owner only, or every
  reader of the account) can't be checked.
- **Remedy:** S9.6: a tax document's file is seen by whoever can read the tax book (filers and
  its shares); a receipt on an item, a loan, a 529 expense or an account (a statement) by
  whoever can read the account.
- **Alternatives:** keep tax-document files to filers and `write` helpers, not `read` shares,
  and say so in S10.3's row. **Needs the user** if the narrower one is meant.

### CR3-10: Whether a tax book's document files cross a link: S11.4 and milestone 5 say yes, S11.5.1 and L6 say later

*Work pad: D2-2 in [`docs-2-platform.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-2-platform.md).*

- **Kind:** the same thing specified twice, differently (and mockup).
- **Req:** S11.4, S11.5.1, S17.9.5.8, L6.
- **Where:**
  - `S11-linked-servers.md:69–70`: "The snapshot holds each shared item as S11.3.1 says (for an
    account: balances, holdings, positions, transactions and series), and receipts and
    documents when the share includes them."
  - `S11-linked-servers.md:91–92`: "A tax book's document files come later ([later
    work](later.md), L6)."; `later.md:56–57` (L6): "a tax book's document files crossing a link
    with Include receipts (S11.5.1)".
  - `S17-implementation-09-milestones.md:125–127` (milestone 5, part 8): "receipts and tax
    documents shown from the copy through decryption".
  - Mockup, the Share dialog (`MoolaKata-mock.html:1793`): "Include receipts · Sends its
    receipts (a tax book's documents too) and the text read from them to ${srv}."
- **What goes wrong:** a filer shares the 2026 tax book with their CPA on a linked server and
  ticks *Include receipts*. By the Share dialog and S11.4 the W-2 and 1099 PDFs cross; by
  S11.5.1 they stay home until L6. The unit for links (and its privacy check) can't tell
  which behaviour is right, and a tax document is among the most sensitive files a household
  holds.
- **Remedy:** decide one. If L6 stands: S11.4 says "receipts when the share includes them",
  S17.9.5.8 says "receipts", and the mockup's line drops "(a tax book's documents too)". If
  they cross: L6 loses that clause and S11.5.1 says so. **Needs the user** (which).
- **Alternatives:** none beyond the two.

<a id="cr3-11"></a>

### CR3-11: joins D2-1 and D3-1, a receipt over about 20 MB can't cross a link

*Work pad: DC-1 in [`docs-compare.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-compare.md); it joins D2-1, D3-1, whose texts are in their blocks' files.*

- **Kind:** contradiction across blocks (join).
- **Ids:** D2-1 (S13.5, S13.7.6, S16.10, S11.9.6) and D3-1 (`link-protocol.md` §1.4, §4, §4.3).
  D3-1 says itself that it is "the protocol's side of `D2-1`".
- **Where:** both claims quote the lines; the two that carry the conflict:
  - `S16-settings.md:46`: "**Receipts**: the size limit (25 MB by default, 1 to 100)".
  - `link-protocol.md:22–23`: "Bodies are JSON, UTF-8, at most 20 MB. A caller waits 10 seconds
    to connect and 20 seconds for each read, and cuts off an answer past 20 MB."
- **What goes wrong:** as D2-1 and D3-1 say: a receipt of 20–100 MB with **Include receipts**
  on can travel neither in `deliver` nor in a `file` answer. Read with D3-5 (receipts held
  until the part that names them arrives), the part that names such a receipt may never apply,
  so the unit should look for an exchange that stalls for good rather than one receipt left
  out; if it does stall, the claim becomes High there.
- **Remedy:** one decision, written into S13.5, S13.7.6 and `link-protocol.md` §1.4: sealed
  receipt bodies (`deliver` with `MK-Link-File`, `file` answers) are streamed and bounded by
  the receipt limit's maximum (S16.10's 100 MB plus the format's overhead), JSON bodies stay
  at 20 MB.
- **Alternatives:** cap a receipt that may cross a link at 20 MB and show it as *too large to
  share* on the receiving side; or lower S16.10's maximum to 20 MB.
- **Units:** 08 linked-servers (S11), 09 web-ui-architecture (S13.5, S13.7.6), 10
  encryption-settings (S16.10).
- **Needs the user:** which of the three.

<a id="cr3-12"></a>

### CR3-12: joins D2-3 with `DEPLOY.md` and `operations.md`, which disagree on whether a relied-on TrueNAS server runs `tls` mode

*Work pad: DC-2 in [`docs-compare.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-compare.md); it joins D2-3, whose texts are in their blocks' files.*

- **Kind:** contradiction across blocks (join, extending D2-3).
- **Ids:** D2-3 (S13.3, S15.1.7, S16.15, S17.8.3.4); `DEPLOY.md` intro; `operations.md` §1 and
  §2.2. No block-3 claim covers it.
- **Where:**
  - `DEPLOY.md:5–7`: "both serve HTTPS themselves on port 37373 with a self-signed certificate
    (`tls` mode, requirements S13.3), which is what a test on a local network needs. A server
    the household relies on goes behind its reverse proxy instead ([`operations.md`](operations.md) §2)".
  - `operations.md:25–26`: "`install/compose.tls.yaml` runs its own HTTPS with a self-signed
    certificate, for trying it on a local network."
  - `operations.md:121–125` (§2.2, the runbook's TrueNAS install, the one DEPLOY sends a
    relied-on server to): "a **custom app** from **Install via YAML**
    (`install/compose.truenas.yaml`) … and the configuration in `MOOLAKATA_CONFIG`"; and
    `web/install/compose.truenas.yaml:34`: `mode = "tls"`.
- **What goes wrong:** DEPLOY sends a relied-on server to `operations.md` §2 to get it behind
  a proxy, and `operations.md` §2.2 installs it on TrueNAS in `tls` mode, the mode S13.3,
  S15.1.7 and S16.15 call testing-only. The decision D2-3 asks for has to reach these two
  documents and the TrueNAS compose file too, or they keep saying the opposite of it.
- **Remedy:** D2-3's decision, carried into `DEPLOY.md`'s intro, `operations.md` §1 and §2.2,
  and the comment in `compose.truenas.yaml`, in the same change.
- **Alternatives:** as D2-3.
- **Units:** 09 web-ui-architecture (S13.3), 10 encryption-settings (S15.1.7, S16.15), 11
  implementation (S17.8.3, the install files).
- **Needs the user:** with D2-3.

<a id="cr3-13"></a>

### CR3-13: a transaction deleted from an imported account comes back with the next import

*Work pad: U01-1 in [`unit-01-accounts-imports.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-01-accounts-imports.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S1.3.2 ("a re-imported file adds only what is new"), S1.11.4 ("remove
  a duplicate"), S1.11.5, S18 (the Connector's daily downloads).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/store/repos/ledger.py:299-307`:
  ```text
  existing = s.scalars(
      select(Transaction).where(
          Transaction.accountId == account.id, Transaction.deleted == false()
      )
  ).all()
  ...
  by_fit = {x.fitId: x for x in existing if x.fitId}
  ```
  and `:327-349`: a row whose FITID (or date, amount and payee) matches nothing live is added.
- **What goes wrong:** the match is made against live rows only, so a row the owner deleted is
  "new" again in every later file that carries it. Case: `bank.ofx` imported (5 added); the owner
  deletes one of them (say a true duplicate, `20260903-1#2`, as S1.11.4 tells them to); the same
  file, or the next download that overlaps it, adds it back (`added: 1, matched: 4`). With the
  Connector's OFX driver fetching 45 days every day (S18.9), a deleted row returns daily for six
  weeks, and the mismatch it caused returns with it; a FITID a bank repeats in every file can't
  be got rid of at all.
- **Run:** yes, a scratch test against the test server (`u01/t/test_u01_scratch.py::
  test_deleted_row_returns_on_reimport`): upload `fixtures/bank.ofx`, `DELETE` one
  transaction, upload again: `added == 1`.
- **Remedy:** match a file's rows against deleted rows too (by FITID, and by date, amount and
  payee for rows without one), counting a match with a deleted row as matched, not added; keep
  the deleted row's id for that.
- **Alternatives:** keep a per-account list of FITIDs the owner deleted (a "skip" list), shown
  in Reconcile with an undo; or ask on delete of an imported row whether the file should bring it
  back.

<a id="cr3-14"></a>

### CR3-14: a broker's trade-history CSV makes every security class `other`, which no price source fetches

*Work pad: U01-2 in [`unit-01-accounts-imports.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-01-accounts-imports.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S1.3.2 ("brokers' and exchanges' trade-history CSVs"), S2.1
  (the instrument classes), S4.3 (the sources by class).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/importers/csvfile.py:280-285`:
  ```text
  out.securities[symbol] = Security(
      key=symbol,
      symbol=symbol,
      name=None,
      instrumentClass="option" if option else "other",
  )
  ```
  `store/repos/ledger.py:296-297` keeps `other` (only a crypto account turns it to `crypto`);
  `prices/tiingo.py:17` `classes = frozenset({"equity", "etf", "mutual-fund"})`,
  `prices/cboe.py:64` `{"option", "equity", "etf"}`; only `manual` (and dev mode's `stub`)
  serve `other`. Entering the same trade by hand makes it `equity` (`ui/transactions.py:590`).
- **What goes wrong:** a brokerage kept from its broker's CSV history (Schwab, Fidelity and
  Vanguard all offer one) gets every stock, ETF and fund it trades as class `other`, so the
  daily refresh never prices them: the holdings show no value (or only a typed price), and the
  account's value, net worth, portfolios and projections leave them out. Instruments are kept
  once per server by symbol, so the first CSV to name a symbol also fixes it as `other` for every
  later account that imports it by symbol. Dev mode's stub source prices `other`, so the dev
  server hides it.
- **Run:** yes, scratch test `test_csv_trade_history_instrument_class`: a one-row trade CSV into
  a brokerage makes the instrument class `other`, served by `['manual', 'stub']` only.
- **Remedy:** a CSV security in an investing account is `equity` (as a typed trade is), or
  `mutual-fund` where its name says fund; let a later file or the owner correct the class; and
  the price refresh treats `other` with a ticker-shaped symbol as `equity` for the sources that
  take it.
- **Alternatives:** ask the class once per new symbol in the mapping step.

<a id="cr3-15"></a>

### CR3-15: one cash dividend stops an account being kept by snapshots, and its holdings vanish

*Work pad: U01-3 in [`unit-01-accounts-imports.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-01-accounts-imports.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S1.3.2 ("For an account kept by snapshots (R3.2), with no trades
  of its own, they are its holdings"), S1.3.3.8.1 ("An account kept by snapshots is one kept from
  statements only, with no trades of its own"), R3.2.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ledger/lots.py:144-146`:
  ```text
  def kept_by_snapshots(transactions: list, lots: list) -> bool:
      """No trades of its own: no transaction in an instrument, and no lot typed on its own."""
      return not any(t.instrumentId for t in transactions) and all(x.transactionId for x in lots)
  ```
  used by `store/repos/ledger.py:399-406` (positions become a check instead) and by
  `lots.replay` (`:163-166`, snapshots dropped).
- **What goes wrong:** any transaction naming an instrument counts as a trade: a cash dividend,
  interest on a bond, a capital-gain distribution. A buy-and-hold brokerage or a 401(k) whose
  OFX carries positions and a dividend (most quarterly files do) is then not kept by snapshots:
  its positions are only a holdings check, and its holdings are the lots its trades made, none.
  Case: positions VTI 100 on Sep 30 and a cash dividend on VTI Sep 15: `replay(...).quantity`
  is 100 without the dividend and 0 with it; the account's value drops to its cash, and Reconcile
  shows a difference of 100. Lots can't come from a dividend, so nothing in the files ever fixes
  it; the owner must type every lot.
- **Run:** yes, `u01/snap.py` (lots.replay with and without the dividend: 100, then 0).
- **Remedy:** a trade is a transaction that makes or takes away a lot (buy, sell, reinvest,
  transfer in or out, an option trade, a corporate action); income and fees on an instrument
  don't end keeping by snapshots.
- **Alternatives:** S1.3.2 says income counts too, and the account's first import offers to
  turn its first positions into opening lots (basis missing) so the check has something to hold.

<a id="cr3-16"></a>

### CR3-16: a brokerage statement read beside the account's files teaches its cash lines as skips

*Work pad: U01-5 in [`unit-01-accounts-imports.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-01-accounts-imports.md).*

- **Kind:** bug (C44 of review 2, still open for brokerage statements). **Req:** S1.3.3.4 ("A
  line left out because it was already in the account teaches nothing"), S1.3.3.8 ("learned per
  institution as for banks"), S1.3.3.8.3 ("every line does [start left out] when the account is
  kept from files").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/imports.py:793-802`:
  ```text
  have = {(a.date, a.amount) for a in b.activity}
  left = [
      x.description
      for x in body.rows
      if not x.include and x.kind in CASH_KINDS and (x.date, x.amount) in have
  ]
  ...
  learned = brokerage.learn(json.loads(row.layout) if row else None, b, left)
  ```
  `have` is the statement's own lines, not what the account held. `web/ui/screens/import.js:72`
  starts every line unticked when `byFile`, and every present line otherwise.
- **What goes wrong:** every cash line left out, those already in the account and all of a
  file-kept account's, has its first three words learned as a skip for the owner's statements
  from that institution (`brokerage.parse:310` drops them unseen). Case: Schwab brokerage kept
  from OFX; its September statement is read to keep it, every line unticked as S1.3.3.8.3 says;
  *Qualified Dividend VTI*, *Bank Interest …* and *Funds Received …* become skips; the owner's
  Schwab IRA kept from statements only then loses those lines from every later statement, and
  its dividends, interest and deposits are never entered (flagged against the summary, at best).
- **Run:** no; reading alone (the call chain is the four lines above; `brokerage.learn` adds
  every description it is given).
- **Remedy:** as `_apply` does for bank statements: take `_present(s, account.id)` before
  `apply_import` and leave out of `left` every row present there, and teach nothing when the
  account is kept from files.

### CR3-19: a trade history's columns can't be named in the mapping dialog, so an exchange's CSV imports as cash

*Work pad: U01-11 in [`unit-01-accounts-imports.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-01-accounts-imports.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S1.5 ("kept current from their CSV exports … one import mapping
  per exchange"), S1.3.2 ("brokers' and exchanges' trade-history CSVs", "a saved column mapping
  per institution").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/importers/csvfile.py:151-153` drops every trade column
  unless both a symbol and a quantity column were guessed:
  ```text
  if not (mapping["symbol"] and mapping["quantity"]):
      for f in ("action", "symbol", "quantity", "price", "fees"):
          mapping[f] = None
  ```
  with the quantity guesses `["quantity", "shares", "units", "qty"]` (`:65`); and the dialog
  offers only the bank columns, `web/ui/screens/import.js:29-38`:
  ```js
  const COLUMNS = /** @type {[string, string, string][]} */ ([
    ['date', 'Date column', ...], ['payee', ...], ['amount', ...], ['debit', ...],
    ['credit', ...], ['balance', ...], ['memo', ...], ['id', ...],
  ]);
  ```
  so `action`, `symbol`, `quantity`, `price` and `fees` can't be chosen by the person.
- **What goes wrong:** a file whose quantity column is named otherwise (Coinbase's history
  names it *Quantity Transacted*, as its own `GUESSES` entries for *Spot Price at Transaction*
  and *Fees and/or Spread* show the reader was written for) is proposed as a bank file, and the
  person has no way to say which column is the asset or the quantity: every buy, sell and send
  becomes a `deposit` or `withdrawal` by the amount's sign, no instrument, no lot. And where a
  trade history writes its totals unsigned (a buy as a positive *Subtotal*), even a mapped file
  makes a buy's lot with a negative basis (`store/repos/ledger.py:514-524`, `costBasis=
  -row.amount`), since nothing ties a trade's sign to its action. (The Coinbase column names
  are from its export as commonly documented, not from a file in the repository.)
- **Run:** no; reading (the drop and the dialog's list).
- **Remedy:** the mapping dialog offers the trade columns (action, symbol or asset, quantity,
  price, fees) whenever the account is an investing or crypto one; add *quantity transacted*
  (and *asset*) to the guesses; give a buy's or a sell's cash the sign its action says.
- **Alternatives:** ship mappings for the exchanges S1.5 names, as the statement layouts ship.

<a id="cr3-20"></a>

### CR3-20: taking a key out of a wallet marks every transaction it brought *no longer on the chain*

*Work pad: U02-1 in [`unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S1.6.1.3 ("Taking a key or address out of the list stops watching its
  addresses"), S1.6.2.7 ("a recorded transaction that the explorer or node no longer has …
  marked *no longer on the chain* and the owner alerted").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/bitcoin/wallet.py:39-42`:
  ```text
  for value, k in current.items():
      if value not in wanted:
          k.mark_deleted(user_id, now)
          s.execute(delete(WalletAddress).where(WalletAddress.keyId == k.id))
  ```
  and `bitcoin/sync.py:246-264` (`mark_gone`), which counts as missed every recorded `btc:`
  transaction of the account not in the read:
  ```text
  seen = {f"btc:{t.txid}" for t in txs}
  ...
      elif r.offChainMissedAt is None:
          r.offChainMissedAt = now  # one miss: a node catching up, or a reorganisation
      else:
          r.offChainAt = now
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** once a key's addresses are no longer read, its transactions are no
  longer in the history, and `mark_gone` takes that for the chain having dropped them. Case: a
  wallet holds a zpub; a sync records 0.5 in, 0.2 out, 0.1 in; the owner replaces the zpub
  with another key (or an xpub they entered by mistake, or a typed address they no longer
  want watched); two hourly reads later all three are marked *no longer on the chain*, and the
  owner gets the S12.11.1.2 alert "a bitcoin transaction is no longer on the chain" for coins
  that are still there. The alert invites deleting them, which takes the coins and their basis
  out of holdings and taxes.
- **Run:** yes, a scratch test (`tmp/u02/t/test_u02_wallets.py::
  test_removing_a_key_marks_its_transactions_off_chain`, using `test_wallets.Fake`): after the
  key is replaced and two syncs, all three transactions carry `offChainAt`.
- **Remedy:** `mark_gone` judges only transactions that touched an address still watched (keep
  the addresses a recorded transaction touched, or its key, and skip those whose addresses are
  all gone); or replacing a key asks what becomes of its recorded transactions (kept as they
  are, the default, or deleted).
- **Alternatives:** keep a removed key's addresses watched read-only until its transactions are
  kept or deleted; or mark the removed key's transactions kept (`offChainKeptAt`) when it is
  taken out.

<a id="cr3-21"></a>

### CR3-21: a HELOC in its draw period shows a payment of $0, and nothing is offered to link

*Work pad: U02-4 in [`unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S1.9.1 ("A HELOC has a draw period with interest-only
  payments"), S1.9.3 (bank transactions "within 2% of the payment … offered"), S1.9.5 (the
  screen shows the payment).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/loans/__init__.py:284-287` (`_pi_payment`) gives the
  repayment period's level payment on the terms' principal while drawing:
  ```text
  if t.drawMonths and not _in_draw(t, day):
      left = max(1, _months_between(day, t.end))
      return level_payment(owed, t.rate_on(day), left)
  return scheduled_payment(t)
  ```
  and `scheduled_payment` (`:160-166`) is `level_payment(t.principal, …)`, where a HELOC's
  `principal` is what was owed at the start (usually 0; draws come later). `summary` (`:412`)
  takes it as the payment, and `ui/loans.py:546-549` looks for bank transactions near it:
  ```text
  sm, _ = loans.summary(loans.terms_of(row), payments, draws, clock.today())
  want = sm.total
  if not want:
      return []
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** case: a HELOC opened Jan 1 with principal 0, 240 months, 8%, a
  120-month draw period, and $30,000 drawn Feb 1. The schedule's next row is right (interest
  $200.00, principal 0), but the loan's screen shows **Payment $0.00**, the payment dialog
  opens at $0.00 (`loan.js:438`, `amount: v.total`), and *Looks like a payment* never lists the
  $200 bank debits, for the whole ten-year draw period. A HELOC whose terms carry an opening
  balance shows the repayment period's P&I instead of the interest due.
- **Run:** yes, a scratch script against `loans.summary` (`tmp/u02/loans_check.py`, case A):
  `payment 0.00 total 0.00`, first row's payment `200.00`.
- **Remedy:** while `_in_draw(t, day)`, `_pi_payment` returns that month's interest on the
  balance owed (as the schedule's row does), so the summary, the dialog and the candidates use
  the interest-only payment; after the draw period the level payment over what is left.
- **Alternatives:** take the summary's payment from the schedule's next row (its principal plus
  interest), which already follows the draw period, resets and recasts.

<a id="cr3-22"></a>

### CR3-22: a payment recorded on the loan alone, then linked as the guide says, is counted twice

*Work pad: U02-15 in [`unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S1.9.3 (a payment "matched as a transfer"; bank transactions
  "offered on the loan's screen to link as its payments").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/loans.py:478-507`: linking a bank transaction always
  makes a new loan-side row, whether or not the loan already holds that payment:
  ```text
  if body.transactionId:
      other = s.get(Transaction, body.transactionId)
      ...
  txn = Transaction(
      id=ids.new_id("Txn"),
      accountId=a.id,
      ...
  ```
  and `candidates` (`:556-568`) offers any bank debit within 2% that isn't a transfer, with no
  regard to loan payments already recorded on that day. The guide for the payment dialog
  (`web/ui/guides/dialog-loan-payment.md`) says: "**None** records it on the loan only, for a
  payment already in the bank account that you'll link later."
- **What goes wrong:** case: the October payment of $1,798.65 is in Checking (imported). The
  owner records it on the loan with *From account: None*, as the guide says, and the loan owes
  $299,701.35. *Looks like a payment* then offers the Checking debit; **Link** records a second
  payment, and the loan owes $299,402.70: two payments for one, in the balance, net worth, the
  schedule, the interest on Taxes and the budget's split.
- **Run:** yes, a scratch test (`tmp/u02/t/test_u02_loans.py::
  test_payment_recorded_on_loan_only_then_linked_counts_twice`): two loan rows, balance
  $299,402.70.
- **Remedy:** linking first looks for an unlinked payment on the loan of the same amount within
  a few days and pairs the two (`transferId` both ways) rather than making a new row;
  `candidates` leaves out a bank debit that such a loan payment already answers, or offers it
  as "pair with the payment of Oct 1".
- **Alternatives:** drop *None* from the dialog and the guide (a payment is always recorded with
  its bank half or linked to it), and let the S1.7 transfer matching pair a loan row typed or
  imported on its own.

<a id="cr3-23"></a>

### CR3-23: the underlying of an option held on its own is never priced

*Work pad: U03-1 in [`unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S4.4 ("loads the day's close for every **held or watched**
  instrument"), S3.6 (expiry "by the underlying's closing price"), S3.7 and S4.5 (intrinsic
  value "the underlying's latest close"), S3.5.4–5 (shares and *now* at today's prices).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/prices/refresh.py:183-199`, `priced()` starts from
  `held()`, the instruments an open lot holds, and nothing adds an open option's underlying:
  ```text
  def priced(s: Session, benchmarks: bool = True) -> list[tuple[Instrument, dt.date]]:
      """Every instrument held in a live account, or used as a benchmark, ...
      holding = held(s)
      ...
          if iid in holding:
              firsts[iid] = day
  ```
  and `refresh.py:329-337` fetches an option by its own OCC ticker only. Nothing in the unit
  "watches" an instrument (no watchlist table or route).
- **What goes wrong:** a cash-secured put or a long call on a stock the account doesn't hold
  (the usual case for both) leaves the stock unpriced for ever. Case: Alice sells to open 1 XYZ
  50 P; the refresh fetches `XYZ 20NOV26 50 P` and never `XYZ`. Then (1) `expire_due`
  (`options/__init__.py:227`) finds no close on the expiry day, so an option that expired out
  of the money is never applied automatically, and S3.6's "applied automatically the day
  after" becomes a question every time; (2) an option with no source mark has no intrinsic
  value (`valuation.intrinsic` needs `per_share`), so its position is unvalued; (3) the Open
  tab's *in the money* flag and the expiring-soon list read `per_share`, which falls back to
  the owner's own last trade in the stock (`valuation.prices`, `source "trade"`), however old.
  With U03-2, the fallback can even decide an expiry wrongly.
- **Run:** yes, a scratch test (`tmp/u03/t/test_u03.py::
  test_underlying_of_an_option_alone_is_not_priced`): after a sell-to-open on XYZ,
  `priced(s, benchmarks=False)` is `['XYZ 20NOV26 50 P']`. `test_options.py`'s expiry test
  inserts the underlying's close by hand, so no test sees this.
- **Remedy:** `priced()` adds the underlying (and an adjusted contract's deliverable parts) of
  every option an open lot holds, from the option's first transaction, and keeps it until the
  day after the option's expiry.
- **Alternatives:** have CBOE's chain answer, which carries the underlying's quote, store the
  underlying's close with the option marks (only when CBOE is the option source).

<a id="cr3-24"></a>

### CR3-24: an automatic expiry is decided by any trade, any user's, on the expiry day, not by a close

*Work pad: U03-2 in [`unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S3.6 ("The close used is the expiry day's; without it, the user
  is asked too"), S4.10 ("never from another user's").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/options/__init__.py:227-232`:
  ```text
  close = per_share(s, c, c.expiry)
  if close is None or close[1] != c.expiry:
      continue  # only the expiry day's close decides (S3.6)
  if in_the_money(c, close[0]):
      continue
  settle(s, a.ownerId, a, s.get(Instrument, iid), "expired", c.expiry)
  ```
  `per_share` is called without an owner, so `valuation.prices(..., owner=None)`
  (`valuation.py:96-123`) falls back, with no `_owned` filter, to any account's statement value
  and then to **any user's last trade** of the stock on or before the day, as `source "trade"`.
  The check only asks that its date be the expiry day.
- **What goes wrong:** where no close is stored for the underlying (always so under U03-1, and
  for any day a source failed), a trade on the expiry day stands in for the close. Case: Alice
  is short 1 XYZ 55 C, not holding XYZ; XYZ closes at 56 on the expiry day, so the call was
  assigned. That morning Bob, another user, bought XYZ at 54 (or recorded an assignment of a
  52 put, whose stock leg is dated that day at 52). The next day `expire_due` takes 54 as the
  close, finds the call out of the money, and records it as **expired worthless** in Alice's
  account: her realized gain counts the whole credit, and the assignment's shares and cash are
  missing until she notices. Bob's trade also reaches Alice's account, against S4.10.
- **Run:** no; reading alone (the path is the one U03-1's run shows has no close).
- **Remedy:** `expire_due` reads only a stored close (`source == "close"`) for the expiry day,
  as `valuation.prices` would with the account owner; a typed price of the owner's may count
  if S3.6 is read to allow it.
- **Alternatives:** pass the account's owner to `per_share`, and refuse `source` `trade` and
  `statement` for this decision.

<a id="cr3-25"></a>

### CR3-25: a nested repeat inside a group passes the rule check, and one payee freezes the server

*Work pad: U03-3 in [`unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S2.7.1 ("a nested repeat such as `(a+)+` is
  refused, as it can take a payee forever"), R3.1/S13 (one server for the household).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ledger/rules.py:143-145`:
  ```text
  # A repeat inside a repeated group, such as (a+)+ or (\w*)*: on a payee that nearly matches it
  # backtracks for ever.
  NESTED = re.compile(r"\((?:[^()\\]|\\.)*[+*}](?:[^()\\]|\\.)*\)\s*[+*{]")
  ```
  The group's body may hold no parenthesis, so a repeat one group deeper isn't seen:
  `re:((a+))+$`, `re:(?:(a+))+$`, `re:((ab)+)+$` all compile (`compile_pattern` returns a
  pattern). Alternation such as `re:(a|aa)+$` isn't looked for either.
- **What goes wrong:** any signed-in user saves the rule `re:((a+))+$` (Transactions › Rules).
  Python's `re` holds the GIL while it backtracks, and the time doubles with each character:
  22 a's and a `!` take 0.27 s, so 40 take about a day and the 500 the matcher reads
  (`rules.py:202`) never end. The rule runs on GET `/ui/api/rules` (`_list` counts what it
  could set), on *Apply to the uncategorised*, on every import (`store/repos/ledger.py:460`,
  a connector's fetch included) and on a receipt's merchant (`receipts/reading.py:303`). One
  payee such as "AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA!" (a bank's text, or a transaction
  the user types) stops every request of the household's server until it is restarted, and it
  stops again at the next import or listing. Only the user who saved it can delete it, and
  only while the server answers.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u03/t/test_u03.py::test_nested_repeat_inside_a_group_passes`: the three
  patterns compile; 22 + 1 characters take 0.27 s (exponential growth).
- **Remedy:** refuse any repeat applied to a group that contains a repeat at any depth (parse
  the pattern with `re._parser` / `sre_parse` and walk the tree for MAX_REPEAT inside
  MAX_REPEAT, and for alternation whose branches overlap under a repeat), and run matching
  with a time limit: the `regex` module's `timeout=`, or a match on a bounded payee in a
  worker with a deadline.
- **Alternatives:** drop `re:` for a small glob syntax (`*`, `?`, `^`, `$`) that can't
  backtrack; or compile with Google's RE2 (`google-re2`), which has no backtracking.

<a id="cr3-26"></a>

### CR3-26: a specific-identification pick of a lot a wash sale split takes the rest from another lot

*Work pad: U03-4 in [`unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S2.3 (specific identification, "chosen per sale"), S3.8.3
  ("Where only some of a lot replaces, that part is split into its own lot").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ledger/lots.py:252-261`, the replacement part keeps the
  lot's id:
  ```text
  out.open.append(
      OpenLot(
          lot.lotId,
          lot.instrumentId,
  ```
  and `lots.py:636-643`, the picks find a lot by its id through a dict, which keeps one of the
  two parts:
  ```text
  if method == "specific" and picks:
      by_id = {lot.lotId: lot for lot in held}
      for p in picks:
          lot = by_id.get(p.lotId)
          take = min(p.quantity, lot.quantity, left) if lot else ZERO
  ```
  What the pick can't take from that part falls through to FIFO (`lots.py:644-652`).
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): buy 10 AAPL at $100 on 2025-01-02 (lot K); buy 4 at $200
  on 2026-03-02 (M); sell M's 4 at $150 on 2026-03-20 (a $200 loss); buy 10 at $160 on
  2026-04-01 (L), 4 of which replace, so L shows as 6 (basis $960) and 4 (basis $840, held
  from 2026-03-14). Selling 10 on 2026-05-01 with the pick "lot L, 10" takes L's 4-share part
  and then **6 shares of K**, FIFO: L's other 6 stay open, K drops to 4. The sale's gain is
  worked out on K's $100 basis, long-term, instead of L's, short-term; the Open tab and Form
  8949 follow.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u03/t/test_u03.py::test_partial_wash_then_pick`: after the pick, the
  open lots are K 4 ($400) and L 6 ($960).
- **Remedy:** give the replacement part its own key (the lot's id with a suffix, or keep a
  list of parts per id) and let a pick of the lot take every part of it, the replacement part
  first or by the method's order within the lot.
- **Alternatives:** key `by_id` to a list of parts and spend the pick across them.

<a id="cr3-27"></a>

### CR3-27: a buy in another account that washes a closed year's loss changes that year unasked

*Work pad: U03-5 in [`unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S2.8, S2.8.2 ("When it moves the realized gains of a closed year,
  it is refused with the years named"), S2.8.3, S3.8.4.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/taxes/closed.py:60-63` (unit 04's file, used here), the
  guard looks at the edited account only:
  ```text
  def __init__(self, s: Session, account: Account, confirm: bool, by: str | None = None):
      self.s, self.account, self.confirm, self.by = s, account, confirm, by
      self.books = closing_books(s, account)
      self.before = gains(s, account, set(self.books)) if self.books else {}
  ```
  while `ui/transactions.py:683-686` reruns the whole household's wash sales before it checks:
  ```text
  if inst_id:
      washes.recompute(s, a.ownerId)
  if guard is not None:
      guard.check()
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** a wash sale across the year end is the classic case. Case (run): a
  brokerage sells 10 AAPL on 2025-12-20 at a $500 loss; the 2025 federal return is filed. On
  2026-01-05 the user records a buy of 10 AAPL in their **IRA**. The request answers 201 with
  no question, and the 2025 sale's realized loss goes from −500.00 to 0.00. Nothing is
  recorded on the 2025 tax book (S2.8.3), so the filed return no longer matches and nobody is
  told. The same happens with a buy in another taxable account, a spouse's account, an import,
  or a deleted buy that no longer replaces.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u03/t/test_u03.py::test_a_buy_in_another_account_moves_a_closed_year_unasked`:
  status 201, the 2025 sale −500.00 → 0.00.
- **Remedy:** the guard takes a before and after of every account whose gains the wash-sale
  rerun can move (the household's taxable accounts), or compares the household's `WashSale`
  rows before and after the rerun and checks the closed years of each sale whose row changed.
- **Alternatives:** the rerun keeps a closed year's wash sales as they were unless confirmed,
  and lists the change on the tax book.
