### CR3-35: saving the amended return again settles edits made after it was filed

*Work pad: U04-12 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S8.15 (*Changed after filing*: an amended federal return
  "settles every change made before it"; "A later edit made anyway, once the year is closed
  again (S2.8.1), is listed afresh").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/taxes.py:842-845`, called from both `add_filing`
  (`:837`) and `change_filing` (`:873`):
  ```text
  def _amending(s, c: Caller, b: TaxBook, f: Filing) -> None:
      """An amended federal return settles the edits made since the return was filed (S8.15)."""
      if f.jurisdiction == "federal" and f.amendsId:
          closed.settle(s, b, c.id, "amended")
  ```
  and `closed.settle()` (`taxes/closed.py:113-119`) settles every unsettled change, whenever
  made.
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): the 1040-X is filed Jun 20; the year is closed again. Alice
  makes an edit anyway (+$200 to 2025's gains), listed in Issues. In August the IRS accepts
  the 1040-X and she sets its progress to *Accepted*: the save settles the +$200 as
  *amended*, the issue and the Dashboard's *Needs attention* entry go, though the 1040-X
  never had it in. Typing a confirmation number, a result or a carried figure into the 1040-X
  does the same.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u04/t/test_u04t.py::test_patching_the_amended_return_settles_later_edits`:
  the issue before, `[]` after the PATCH (`tmp/u04/run3.txt`).
- **Remedy:** settle on adding an amended federal return, and on changing a filing into one
  (its `amendsId` newly set); otherwise settle only the changes made before the amended
  return was filed (`TaxBookChange.createdAt` ≤ its `filedOn`, or before it was added while
  it is being prepared).
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

<a id="cr3-36"></a>

### CR3-36: a tax document can't be changed, removed or given its file later in the browser; through the API a co-filer can't change one the other filer attached a file to

*Work pad: U04-13 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S8.8 ("each marked received (attach the PDF, S9) or missing"),
  S8.16, S8.14.3 (a helper with write can "attach documents"), S9.3 ("whoever can change one
  of them can detach it from that").
- **Code:** `web/ui/screens/taxes.js:907-920`, the document dialog only adds
  (`api.post(`/tax-books/${b.id}/documents`, …)`); the Documents tab (`:481-580`) offers
  *Attach* for a missing one and nothing on a received or filed one: no edit, no delete, no
  file for a document kept without one, though `PATCH /tax-documents/{id}` and `DELETE` exist
  (`ui/taxes.py:949-976`). And `_doc_values` (`ui/taxes.py:919-920`) re-checks the receipt on
  every change:
  ```text
  if body.receiptId:
      receipts.to_attach(s, c.id, body.receiptId)
  ```
  which needs write on the receipt; a co-filer has read on the other's (`receipts/__init__.py:
  248-249`, `best = "read"`).
- **What goes wrong:** a user who keeps the wrong form (a 1099-INT marked as the 1099-DIV) or
  the right one under the wrong account can't fix or remove it: the checklist shows it
  received for good. A W-2 kept from the checklist before its PDF arrived never gets the PDF.
  Through the API (run), Bob, an accepted co-filer, changing the note on the W-2 Alice
  attached gets **403 receipt-read-only**; a write helper likewise.
- **Run:** the co-filer's 403, yes, `tmp/u04/t/test_u04t.py::test_documents_files_and_helpers`
  (`tmp/u04/run4.txt`); the browser, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** each document row opens the dialog to change its form, issuer, account, notes
  and file, with *Remove*; `_doc_values` checks `to_attach` only when the receipt changes
  (a new `receiptId` differing from the stored one).
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

<a id="cr3-37"></a>

### CR3-37: a Roth conversion or an IRA-to-IRA transfer counts as a contribution, over the limit; an HSA's employer money and catch-up are left out

*Work pad: U04-15 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S8.6 ("contributions per account against the year's limits
  (with catch-up by age) ... backdoor-Roth steps (non-deductible contribution, conversion
  ...)"), S1.4.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/taxes/panels.py:491-508`, a contribution is any positive
  `deposit` or `transfer` without an instrument whose source isn't set otherwise:
  ```text
  Transaction.kind.in_(("deposit", "transfer")),
  ...
  func.coalesce(Transaction.contributionSource, "employee") == "employee",
  ```
  A matched transfer from another retirement account is never told apart (its other half's
  account isn't read); `:527-529` gives an HSA the family limit with no catch-up at 55, and
  the `employee` filter drops an employer's HSA money, which counts against the HSA limit.
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): Alice converts $50,000 from her traditional IRA to her Roth
  IRA in 2026, the two halves matched as a transfer. The Retirement panel shows the Roth IRA
  *contributed $50,000.00 of $7,500*, **over the limit**, and the traditional IRA flagged with
  it (the person's IRA total); the conversion appears only as a $50,000 distribution. A
  trustee-to-trustee move between two IRAs, or a 401(k) rolled into an IRA without the
  *rollover* source set by hand, does the same. An HSA with $3,000 from the employer and
  $5,000 from the owner shows $5,000 against $8,750 where $8,000 counts.
- **Run:** yes for the conversion, `tmp/u04/t/test_u04t.py::test_roth_conversion_counts_as_contribution`
  (`tmp/u04/run5.txt`); the HSA, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** a transfer whose other half is in a retirement account (or a 529, S1.8) is a
  conversion or rollover, not a contribution, and is listed as such (S8.6's conversion step);
  an HSA counts employee and employer money against the limit, with $1,000 more at 55 (a
  tax-table figure, S16.11), and asks self-only or family on the account.
- **Alternatives:** the transfer dialog sets `contributionSource = rollover` on the receiving
  half whenever both accounts are retirement accounts.

<a id="cr3-38"></a>

### CR3-38: foreign tax paid and crypto income are not on the Income panel

*Work pad: U04-17 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md).*

- **Kind:** not built. **Req:** S8.5 ("foreign tax paid, and crypto income (staking, rewards)
  at fair value when received").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/taxes/panels.py:395-428`, `income()` reads only
  `interest`, `dividend`, `reinvest` and `capital-gain`:
  ```text
  Transaction.kind.in_(("interest", "dividend", "reinvest", "capital-gain")),
  ```
  and `store/tables/accounts.py:51-81` (`TXN_KINDS`) has no kind for foreign tax withheld or
  for a staking or reward receipt; `grep -i "foreign\|staking\|reward"` finds none in the
  server. No milestone, gap or later item schedules them.
- **What goes wrong:** a household holding an international index fund (foreign tax on the
  1099-DIV, box 7, for the foreign tax credit) sees no foreign tax; a wallet's or exchange's
  staking rewards, taxable as ordinary income at their value when received and often reported
  on no form, appear nowhere on the book, and their lots' basis is whatever the import gave.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** a dividend's foreign tax as a field (or a `foreign-tax` kind) imported from OFX's
  `<INCOME>`/1099-DIV data, summed by payer; a `reward` kind for crypto income, valued at the
  day's price, counted on the Income panel and giving the lot that basis.
- **Alternatives:** S8.5 drops both until L-numbered later work, and the Income guide says the
  1099-DIV's foreign tax and any crypto income are the preparer's to add.

<a id="cr3-39"></a>

### CR3-39: spending paid straight from the account reserves are held in asks to move the same amount out of it again

*Work pad: U05-1 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S7.10.4 ("when they are behind, after a repair paid from
  checking or a card, it goes back to it … when they agree, the account is **backed**").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/budgets/reserves.py:124-126`, *to move* is the reserves'
  balance less what was transferred in from the budget's accounts and the landed money given:
  ```text
  @property
  def toMove(self) -> Decimal:
      return self.total - self.movedIn - self.given
  ```
  and `moved_in` (`:180-187`) counts only transfers whose other half is in the budget's other
  accounts. Spending in a reserve's category lowers `total` wherever it is paid from, so a
  payment made from the held-in account itself lowers `total` while nothing lowers `movedIn`.
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): a *Roof* reserve of $500 a month, target $5,000, held in Ally
  Savings; November's and December's $500 moved in from Checking: total $1,000, moved in
  $1,000, *backed*. The roofer is paid $700 straight from Ally. Ally now holds $300, the
  reserve $300: the account is backed, yet *Where the money is* says **$700 to move from Ally
  Savings to Checking** (`toMove` −700.00). Doing it leaves Ally $700 short of the reserve, and
  counts a withdrawal against the limit. The guide and S7.10.4 name only repairs paid from
  checking or a card, where the code is right.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u05/t/test_u05.py::test_spending_from_the_held_in_account_asks_to_move_it_back`:
  before `1000.00 1000.00 0.00`, after `300.00 1000.00 -700.00` with balance `300.00`
  (`tmp/u05/run1.txt`).
- **Remedy:** count money leaving the held-in account other than by a transfer to the budget's
  accounts (spending paid from it) as moved out: `moved_in` less every outflow of the account
  in a reserve's category (or simply every non-transfer debit since the reserves began), so
  *to move* stays what the reserves gained less what the account received for them, net.
- **Alternatives:** work *to move* from the account's balance: reserves' total less (balance
  less the account's own money), with the account's own money set when reserves begin and
  changed by *kept as the account's own*.

<a id="cr3-40"></a>

### CR3-40: Compare over a range of months counts a rolled-over remainder once in every month it carries through

*Work pad: U05-4 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S7.12.3 ("for a month or a range of months: per category,
  what is available, spent and remaining").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/budgets.py:1377-1388` sums each month's *available*,
  which already holds what rolled into it:
  ```text
  avail = r.balance + r.actual if r.target is not None else r.planned
  got[0] += avail if r.cadence == "month" or m == last else 0
  got[1] += r.actual if r.cadence == "month" or m == last else 0
  ```
  (`Row.planned` is "this period's plan, with what rolled over into it",
  `budgets/__init__.py:337`; a reserve's `balance + actual` and an envelope row's `planned`
  hold the month's starting balance the same way.)
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): *Entertainment* $100 a month with rollover from January,
  nothing spent; Compare with a copy for the year to March shows **available $600, spent $0**:
  100 + 200 + 300, where $300 was ever made available. Every rolling line, reserve and envelope
  category in a range overstates what was available, and so what remained, by its carried
  balances; the two budgets part by those carries, not by their plans.
- **Run:** yes, `test_u05.py::test_compare_a_range_counts_carries_again`:
  `('Entertainment', 600.00, 0.00)`.
- **Remedy:** for a range, available is the first month's carried balance plus each month's
  own plan and moves (`planned − carried` summed, plus the first month's `carried`); a
  reserve's the first month's start plus what was put in and moved.
- **Alternatives:** show a range as its last month's available and the range's spending.

<a id="cr3-41"></a>

### CR3-41: an envelope budget that plans a category and its parent takes spending from both, and To Budget rises

*Work pad: U05-6 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S7.13.1 ("To Budget is the cash in the budget's accounts,
  less what its categories hold"), S7.1.2 ("A plan line on a parent category counts its
  children with it").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/budgets/envelope.py:113-133` works each planned
  category's envelope from `_actual`, which counts a category with its children
  (`budgets/__init__.py:372-379`), so a child's spending comes off the child's envelope and the
  parent's:
  ```text
  available = start + assigned + mv[cat_id] - _actual(tr, cash_spend, cat_id)
  ...
  spent = _actual(tr, cash_spend, cat_id) + charged
  ```
  `set_plan` (`ui/budgets.py:738-747`) refuses a category listed twice, not a parent and its
  child both listed.
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): envelope budget, $1,000 of salary in, *Food* $300 and
  *Food › Groceries* $200 planned: To Budget $500. A $150 Kroger charge in Groceries leaves
  Food $150 and Groceries $50 (each lost $150) and **To Budget $650**: spending raised the money
  left to hand out by $150. In a tracking budget the same plan shows Groceries' spending in both
  rows, which reads as intended roll-up; in an envelope budget it makes money.
- **Run:** yes, `test_u05.py::test_envelope_parent_and_child_both_planned`: `ENV before 500.00`,
  `ENV after 650.00 150.00 50.00`.
- **Remedy:** in an envelope budget a parent's envelope counts only spending not in a child
  that has a line of its own (or `set_plan` refuses a parent and its child together in an
  envelope budget, saying why).
- **Alternatives:** S7.1.2 says a parent line counts only children with no line of their own,
  in both methods, and the tracking figures follow.

<a id="cr3-42"></a>

### CR3-42: the month cache can keep figures worked out before a write under the generation after it

*Work pad: U05-8 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S7.3.10 ("a month's figures are kept, and worked out again when
  anything they come from changes").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/budgets/__init__.py:396-401` reads the cache, works the
  month out, and stores it under whatever generation holds when it is done:
  ```text
  key = (budget.id, month, today, viewer)
  hit = cache.get(key)
  if hit is None:
      hit = _month_view(s, budget, month, today, viewer)
      cache.put(key, hit)
  ```
  `cache.put` (`budgets/cache.py:77-79`) files it under the current `_generation`, which the
  mapper events move on at **flush** (`:48-51`, `after_insert`/`after_update`/`after_delete`),
  before the writing transaction commits. Routes run on a thread pool in one process, so a
  read can run while a write is between its flush and its commit.
- **What goes wrong:** an import (or any write) flushes its transactions, moving the
  generation from G to G+1, and keeps working before it commits. Meanwhile the Dashboard's card
  or the budget screen reads the month: its read transaction sees the data before the import,
  misses the cache at G+1, works the month out without the import and stores it at G+1. The
  import commits; nothing else is written; every later read hits that entry and shows the month
  without the import, with nothing to say so, until another write anywhere or **Refresh**. The
  same holds for a read that began before a write and finishes after it. Two inputs are also
  not watched: group membership (`UserGroup`, which decides what a receiver's figures count
  through a share to a group, S7.1.1), and an account's `Holding` and `Import` rows, which
  `ledger.load` reads for the balances an envelope budget's To Budget and *Where the money is*
  stand on.
- **Run:** no, reading alone (a race; not reproduced).
- **Remedy:** take the generation when the computation starts and store under it (a write
  during the computation then makes the entry unreachable); move the generation on commit
  (`after_commit` on the session, or both flush and commit) rather than at flush alone; add
  `UserGroup`, `Holding` and `Import` to the watched tables.
- **Alternatives:** key a month by the latest `updatedAt` of the budget's inputs, read in the
  same transaction.

<a id="cr3-43"></a>

### CR3-43: saving a gift plan cuts every gift from its anniversaries and custom occasions

*Work pad: U05-11 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S7.7.1 (anniversaries "each with its own date"), S7.7.3 ("each with
  its budget, the gifts planned so far, and what is still missing"), S7.8.1 (a gift is for "an
  occasion (and its year)").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/gifts.py:314-331`, every save deletes the plan's
  occasions and adds new ones with new ids:
  ```text
  def _set_occasions(s, gp: GiftPlan, wanted: list[OccasionIn], user_id: str) -> None:
      for o in gifts.occasions_of(s, [gp.id])[gp.id]:
          o.mark_deleted(user_id)
      for o in wanted:
          ...
          s.add(
              Occasion(
                  id=ids.new_id("Occsn"),
  ```
  called from `change_person` whenever `occasions` is sent (`:405-406`), and the plan dialog
  always sends it (`web/ui/screens/gifts.js:382-395`). A gift keeps its occasion as
  `occasion:<id>` (`ui/gifts.py:457`), matched by key in `gifts.upcoming`
  (`gifts/__init__.py:286`, `g.occasion == d.key`).
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): Emma's plan has *Graduation* (Jan 10); a $200 watch is
  planned for it. Upcoming shows *Graduation: 1 planned, nothing bought*. Alice edits the plan's
  notes and saves: Upcoming now says **Graduation: no gift yet** with no gifts, and the watch's
  occasion reads **An occasion**. Each save of any plan does this to every gift for a custom
  occasion or an anniversary other than the wedding (holidays and birthdays are keyed by name);
  the occasion then raises its lead-time alert as if nothing were planned.
- **Run:** yes, `test_u05.py::test_saving_a_gift_plan_loses_its_gifts_occasions`:
  `UP BEFORE [('Graduation', '1 planned, nothing bought', 1)]`, `UP AFTER [('Graduation', 'no
  gift yet', 0)]`, `GIFT LABEL [('Watch', 'An occasion')]` (`tmp/u05/run2.txt`).
- **Remedy:** the dialog sends each dated occasion's id, and `_set_occasions` updates those in
  place, adds the new and deletes only those left out (moving their gifts to *An idea* or
  asking first).
- **Alternatives:** leave occasions alone when the list sent equals the one stored.

<a id="cr3-44"></a>

### CR3-44: *Cash before payday* counts each recurring payment once before payday, and none that is due today or late

*Work pad: U05-22 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S7.6.1 ("when its balance, less the recurring payments
  expected from it before its next expected money in … would fall below zero").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/alerts.py:559-565` takes one date per series, its next:
  ```text
  ins = sorted(x.next for x in series if x.usual > 0 and x.next > today)
  horizon = ins[0] if ins else today + dt.timedelta(days=31)
  outs = sorted(
      (x.next, -x.usual) for x in series if x.usual < 0 and today < x.next < horizon
  )
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** case: Checking holds $700 on the 2nd; payday is the 15th (a monthly
  salary series); daycare takes $400 every week (the 4th and the 11th). The alert subtracts one
  $400 and finds $300 left: no alert, though the account dips to −$100 on the 11th. With no
  income series the horizon is 31 days and a weekly payment still counts once. A payment whose
  next date is today (`today < x.next`), or one a few days late and still to come, is left out
  too. The same holds for a twice-monthly payment before a monthly payday.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** for each outgoing series, step its dates by its interval from `next` (and from a
  late one's due date) up to the horizon, counting each; include payments due today that
  haven't posted.
- **Alternatives:** none simpler.

<a id="cr3-45"></a>

### CR3-45: deleting an account outside a budget erases the past transfers to it from the budget's months

*Work pad: U05-24 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** R3.10 ("A record that ends or is deleted … changes what comes
  after its end, never what came before: past budget months … keep it"), S7.3.2, S7.3.3.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/budgets/__init__.py:245-259` finds a transfer's other half
  only in live accounts, and `:202-204` drops a transfer whose other half isn't found:
  ```text
  .where(
      Transaction.id.in_(transfer_ids[chunk : chunk + 500]),
      Transaction.deleted == false(),
      Account.deleted == false(),
  )
  ...
  if t.transferId:
      if other is None or other[1].id in inside:
          continue  # between two of the budget's accounts: no money moved in or out
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): October's $300 from Checking to Ally Savings (outside the
  budget), filed under *Savings › Emergency fund*: October shows saved $300. Ally is closed and
  deleted in December: October now shows **saved $0.00**, the line's actual $0.00, and a
  reviewed October is flagged as changed. The same happens to cash taken out to a deleted
  `cash` account (*Cash* spending gone) and to every past payment to a paid-off loan that is
  deleted (its interest, escrow and principal gone from past months).
- **Run:** yes, `test_u05.py::test_deleting_the_other_account_changes_past_months`:
  `OCT BEFORE saved 300.00 300.00`, `OCT AFTER saved 0.00 0.00` (`tmp/u05/run4.txt`).
- **Remedy:** read the other half and its account whether deleted or not (a deleted account's
  transfers keep counting by their category up to its deletion, as R3.10 says), and treat a
  missing half (a deleted transaction) as money leaving or entering the budget.
- **Alternatives:** none.

<a id="cr3-46"></a>

### CR3-46: a reserve set to put in $0 a month disappears with its balance, unasked

*Work pad: U05-26 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S7.10.1 ("At the target it is **Funded** and puts in nothing"),
  S7.10.3 ("Taking its category out of the plan, or its target off, asks first and names the
  balance that stops being kept").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/budgets/__init__.py:321-327` treats any line of 0 as no
  plan, a target or not:
  ```text
  def in_force(all_lines: list[BudgetLine], month: dt.date) -> dict[str, BudgetLine]:
      """Per category, the line that holds in a month (a line of 0 is no plan)."""
      ...
      return {k: v for k, v in out.items() if v.amount != 0}
  ```
  so `reserves.reserve` (`reserves.py:80-86`) resets the balance to nothing that month, while
  `set_plan`'s question (`ui/budgets.py:806-818`) is asked only when the new line has no target.
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): *Emergency fund* $500 a month toward $10,000 since August,
  $2,000 held. In December the user sets the monthly amount to $0 to pause it, target kept: the
  save is accepted without a question (200), and December's month has **no Emergency fund row**:
  the $2,000 balance is gone from the budget, from Compare and from *Where the money is* (where,
  if other reserves are held in the same account, *to move* then asks for $2,000 back to
  checking; held there alone, the account leaves the page).
- **Run:** yes, `test_u05.py::test_a_reserve_set_to_put_in_nothing`: `NOV0 2000.00`, `PUT0 200`,
  `DEC0 []` (`tmp/u05/run5.txt`).
- **Remedy:** a line with a target is in force at any amount (0 puts in nothing and keeps the
  balance); only a line of 0 without a target takes the category out of the plan.
- **Alternatives:** refuse an amount of 0 with a target, saying to leave the amount and let
  *Funded* stop it, or ask as for taking the target off.

<a id="cr3-47"></a>

### CR3-47: a receipt's dialog names every thing it is kept with, in accounts, gift plans and tax books the viewer can't see

*Work pad: U06-1 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** privacy. **Req:** S9.6 ("Sharing follows what the receipt is
  attached to"), S9.3 ("its dialog names each thing it is kept with"), R3.4 (a share is never
  passed on), S10 (one user never sees what isn't theirs), S7.7 (gift plans kept from the
  person they're for).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/receipts.py:111-117, 181-197` (and the gift, donation,
  item, expense, statement and tax-document branches at `:118-179, 198-214`):
  ```text
  def _links(s, r: Receipt, user_id: str | None = None) -> list[LinkOut]:
      ...
      out = []
      for ln in receipts.links(s, r.id):
      ...
          t = s.get(Transaction, ln.itemId)
          if t is None or t.deleted:
              continue
          a = s.get(Account, t.accountId)
          out.append(
              LinkOut(
                  kind="transaction",
                  itemId=t.id,
                  transactionId=t.id,
                  accountId=a.id,
                  accountName=a.name,
                  date=t.date,
                  amount=t.amount,
                  payee=t.payee,
  ```
  `user_id` is used only for a gift's or donation's `canDetach`; nothing filters a link by
  whether the viewer can read what it points to. `row()` (`:234`) and so `GET /receipts`,
  `GET /receipts/{id}`, `GET /transactions/{id}/receipts` and every answer that carries a
  receipt's detail return every link.
- **What goes wrong:** Alice keeps a receipt on a transaction in her private account
  ("Alice private", *Secret Jeweller*, −42.18) and on one in the joint account she shares with
  Bob for reading. Bob opens the receipt from the joint account and its links list the private
  account's id and name and the transaction's id, date, amount and payee. The same holds for a
  gift (*The gift {description}* with its cost: a receipt for Bob's birthday present kept on
  the joint account's transaction and on the gift tells Bob what he is getting), a donation
  (organisation and amount), an item or 529 expense in an unshared account, a statement's
  account, and a tax document in a book Bob doesn't file (form, year, issuer).
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u06/t/test_u06.py::test_links_of_an_unshared_account_reach_a_reader`:
  Bob's `GET /receipts/{id}` and `GET /transactions/{joint}/receipts` both list
  `('Alice private', 'Secret Jeweller', -42.18)` beside `('Joint', 'Blue Bottle', -42.18)`.
- **Remedy:** `_links` takes the viewer and keeps a link only when the viewer can read what it
  points to (the account's level, `_kept_level` for gifts and donations, the book's filers for
  a tax document); the owner sees all of them. Keep the count of the rest if the dialog wants to
  say "and 2 more things you can't see".
- **Alternatives:** show a hidden link as *something not shared with you*, with no id, name,
  date or amount (as S11.3.1 names *an account not shared*).

<a id="cr3-48"></a>

### CR3-48: an upload holds the database's write lock while the worker draws its thumbnail, so one slow file fails every other write for up to a minute

*Work pad: U06-2 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S9.5 (thumbnails made on upload, in the reader's worker), S9.8.4.2
  ("a hostile or broken file cannot take down the server"), S14.1.4 (`Database.tx()` is
  `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, 10-second busy timeout).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/receipts.py:326-327`:
  ```text
      with srv.db.tx() as s:
          stored = receipts.store(s, srv, c.id, file.filename or "receipt", data)
  ```
  and `receipts/__init__.py:177-188`, inside that transaction:
  ```text
      r = Receipt(
          ...
          # Drawn in the reader's worker, never here (S9.5, S9.8.4.2).
          thumbnail=_parsing().thumbnail(data, media),
  ```
  `parsing._call` (`parsing.py:20-27`) waits on the one quick worker's lock, starts the worker
  if it isn't running (`reading.py:84`, up to 120 s for its hello), and gives the job
  `LIMIT = 60` seconds. `store/db.py:95` opens every `tx()` with `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, and
  `BUSY_MS = 10_000` (`store/db.py:16`). The same holds for a statement kept by an import
  (`ui/imports.py:925`) and by the connector (`connector/files.py:142`).
- **What goes wrong:** Alice uploads a PDF whose first page takes the worker 50 seconds to draw
  (a page of millions of vector paths; or any file while the quick worker is busy decoding a
  large HEIC photo for someone's viewer, `ui/receipts.py:499`, or reading an OFX import). For
  those 50 seconds her upload holds the write lock; every other write on the server (Bob's
  transaction edit, a price refresh, a session's touch, the reading queue's own
  `read_one`) waits 10 seconds and fails with *database is locked*, a 500. A first upload after
  a start also pays the worker's start-up inside the lock. The reading queue was kept out of
  requests for this reason (S9.8.4); the thumbnail brings it back.
- **Run:** no, reading alone (the timings are the code's limits, not measured).
- **Remedy:** draw the thumbnail before the transaction (the bytes and type are known), or after
  it, in the reading queue's job, storing it with what was read; keep `files.write` before the
  transaction too, with the orphan check (S14.6) cleaning up a file whose row never commits.
- **Alternatives:** keep it in the request but outside the transaction, with a short limit
  (5 s) for thumbnails, so a slow one is skipped and drawn later by the queue.

<a id="cr3-49"></a>

### CR3-49: correcting an invited user's email leaves the invitation sent to the wrong address working

*Work pad: U07-1 in [`unit-07-users-sharing.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-07-users-sharing.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S10.1.3 (the invitation link; "sending a new
  one ends the one before"), S10.1.5 ("An admin corrects a user's email address only until the
  user has joined").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/auth/users.py:676-686` (`update_user`):
  ```text
      if email is not None and email.strip().lower() != (user.email or ""):
          if user.passwordHash is not None:
              raise refuse(
                  ...
          _raise(_email_problems(s, email, user.id))
          user.email = email.strip().lower()
          fields.append("email")
  ```
  Nothing ends the `OneTimeToken` of purpose `invitation` (only `_issue` deletes one, when a new
  invitation is sent), and `ui/users.py:175` (`change_user`) sends nothing. The dialog promises
  the opposite: `web/ui/screens/users.js:354-355`:
  ```js
          hint=${user.invited
            ? 'Fix it before they join; the invitation then goes to the new address.'
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** an admin invites Eve as `eve@hme.lan` (a typo; or a typo-squatted
  domain), in the Admins group. The mistyped address's owner receives the link. The admin
  notices and corrects the address to `eve@home.lan` in Edit user; per the hint they believe
  the invitation now goes there. Nothing is sent to the new address, and the first link keeps
  working for its 7 days: whoever holds it opens it, chooses a password and is signed in as
  `eve`, with her groups (an admin's if she was invited into Admins). Eve's later Resend makes
  a new link and ends the first, but by then the account is taken.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u07/t/test_u07.py::test_corrected_invitation_old_link_still_works`: after
  the PATCH no email is written, and the old token's `invitations/accept` answers 200 as `eve`.
- **Remedy:** when an invited user's email changes, delete their pending invitation token and
  send a new invitation to the new address (`_send_invitation`), recorded as
  `user.invitation-resent`; a test that the old link answers 404 afterwards.
- **Alternatives:** end the old token and leave sending to **Resend invitation**, with the hint
  saying so.

<a id="cr3-50"></a>

### CR3-50: two servers whose link ended can never link again: the join answers 500 on both sides

*Work pad: U08-2 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S11.6 ("marks the link broken and alerts admins, who end it or link
  again"), S11.1.2; DC-3 (D3-6: "linking again with the same key is unspecified").
- **Code:**
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/store/tables/links.py:82` `unique_live(Link, "peerKey")`: unique
    among rows not deleted, and nothing ever soft-deletes a `Link` (an ended link "stays
    listed", `:18-20`).
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/links/manage.py:258-265` (join) and `:300-307` (`answer_join`)
    look for an existing link with `Link.state != "ended"`, so an ended one passes the
    `already-linked` check, and then `:271-284` adds a new row with the same `peerKey`, and
    `:314` sets it on the invitation.
- **What goes wrong:** A was restored from an older backup; B's link shows *broken*, and the
  alert says "end the link, or link again". The admins end it on both sides, A invites, B
  enters the code: A's `/link/v1/join` fails with `IntegrityError: UNIQUE constraint failed:
  Link.peerKey` (an unsigned 500), and B's `POST /ui/api/settings/links/join` answers 500 too
  (B's own ended row has the same key). The same happens to any two households that end a link
  and want it back. The only way out is a new link key on one server, which nothing offers.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u08/t/test_u08.py::test_relink_after_end` (`run1.txt`: 500 from
  `/link/v1/join` and from Settings' join, both `IntegrityError` on `Link.peerKey`).
- **Remedy:** a join from a key whose link here has ended (or is broken) is allowed: the ended
  row is soft-deleted (or its `peerKey` moved aside) in the same transaction, and a broken one
  ends first, as D3-6's remedy says; the unique index then holds for live links only. A test
  that ends a link and links the same two servers again.
- **Alternatives:** the index covers only links not ended (`NOT deleted AND state <>
  'ended'`).

<a id="cr3-51"></a>

### CR3-51: a peer's exchange or delivery is answered on the server's event loop, so the whole server waits for it

*Work pad: U08-3 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S11.4 (pulls hourly and on demand), S13 (one server answers the
  household's browsers, the API and `/link`); no requirement names the event loop.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/links/routes.py:183-196`:
  ```text
  @router.post("/exchange")
  async def exchange(request: Request):
      ...
      out = answer_exchange(
          srv, link, exactjson.loads(body or b"{}"), request.headers.get("mk-link-version")
      )
  ```
  `answer_exchange` (`manage.py:561-592`) is synchronous and runs `serve.change_for`, which
  works out every shared item for every viewer through the API's own routes (`serve.py:229-263`:
  every account's whole transaction history, series, holdings, every budget's month, trends and
  cash flow), hashes and seals it, all before it returns. The same holds for `deliver`
  (`routes.py:225-243` → `answer_deliver` → `receive.take`, which may call the peer back over
  the network through `fetch_files` with its 10 s and 20 s timeouts), `_deliver_file`
  (`:250-302`, writing and reading the body's file, `keep_delivered` with `fsync`), `join`,
  `ping` and `certificate`, all `async def` calling blocking database, file and network work.
  The server is one Hypercorn process on one asyncio loop (`listener.py:19, 122-125`).
- **What goes wrong:** while A answers B's hourly exchange, nothing else on A is answered: the
  household's browsers, `/healthz`, the connector's reports and other links' calls all wait
  until A has worked out B's change (seconds for a modest household, longer for a first full
  snapshot with years of transactions) and, in `deliver`, until any receipt it fetches back
  from B arrives or times out. Two links exchanging at the same time double it. A slow answer
  also blocks the 20 MB `deliver` body read and sealed-receipt streams of other peers, whose
  own 20-second read timeouts then fail (U08-4).
- **Run:** no; rests on reading (`async def` routes calling synchronous work, one loop).
- **Remedy:** the `/link` routes are plain `def` (FastAPI runs them in its thread pool), or
  each hands its synchronous work to `run_in_threadpool`; `_deliver_file` reads the stream on
  the loop and writes through a thread. A test that a slow `answer_exchange` doesn't hold up
  `/healthz`.
- **Alternatives:** none simpler.

<a id="cr3-52"></a>

### CR3-52: anyone who knows a linked server's public key can use up that link's call limit and upload budget, and stall the link

*Work pad: U08-5 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** security.
- **Req:** S13.7.5.4 (`/link`'s 60 calls a minute, per link), S13.7.6 (100 MB of uploads in
  flight per caller, "a linked server's key"), S11.1.3 (only a signed call from a linked key
  counts as that link's); `link-protocol.md` §7 (`limit`).
- **Code:**
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/links/routes.py:15-30`, the router's dependency, run before any
    signature check:
    ```text
        key = request.headers.get("mk-link-key", "")
        link_id = None
        if key:
            with srv.db.read() as s:
                link_id = s.scalar(select(Link.id).where(Link.peerKey == key, Link.deleted == false()))
        if link_id:
            srv.link_limit.check(link_id, "calls from this linked server")
    ```
    The header is whatever the caller writes; `link_limit` is a `Window(60, 60)` (`app.py:68`).
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/middleware.py:229-231` (`BodyLimit._caller`): an upload is
    counted against `"key:" + headers[b"mk-link-key"]`, unchecked, toward `IN_FLIGHT` (100 MB).
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/links/client.py:129-133`: the genuine peer's call, answered 429
    unsigned, becomes `LinkError("limit")`, which `manage.exchange` records as a failure
    (`manage.py:477-480`).
  - The key is public: `GET /link/v1/hello` answers it to anyone (`routes.py:131-142`), and
    `/link/` is the part a household exposes (operations.md §1.1).
- **What goes wrong:** Mallory reads B's key from B's `hello`, guesses (or tries) that B links
  to A, and sends A 61 unsigned POSTs a minute to `/link/v1/ping` with `MK-Link-Key: <B's
  key>`. Each is refused (`unsigned` or `bad-signature`), but each counts against B's link
  first, so B's own exchanges get `429 limit` for as long as Mallory keeps it up: nothing of
  either household crosses, and after the threshold A's admins are alerted that B "isn't
  reached", though B is fine. With `Content-Type: application/octet-stream` and a declared
  length near 100 MB, a few held-open uploads with B's key also fill B's in-flight budget at A
  (`429 uploads-in-flight` for B's receipts). The probe also tells Mallory whether A links to
  B (the 429 comes only for a linked key). And a `deliver` with that header and an
  octet-stream body is written whole to `links-in/` before its signature is checked
  (`routes.py:259-278`: `known(request)` reads only the header), up to the receipt limit per
  call, so each held call also costs A that much disk until it ends.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** count calls against a link only once their signature verifies (the check is
  cheap and reads only headers and the body's hash); before that, count every call against
  its address, whatever key it names, as unknown keys are (`link_unknown`), with a limit
  generous enough for a peer's own calls behind a shared NAT; key the in-flight budget for
  `/link` by address until verified. A test that unsigned calls naming a linked key don't
  touch that link's limit.
- **Alternatives:** a separate, smaller per-address limit for calls that fail their
  signature, so a flood from one address is cut off without touching any link.

<a id="cr3-53"></a>

### CR3-53: a receipt that takes over a minute to cross is refused as a clock problem

*Work pad: U08-6 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S11.1.3 (a timestamp more than 60 seconds off is refused, "named as
  such on the link, since the fix is NTP"), S11.2 (receipts' bytes cross in the exchange),
  S16.10 (receipts up to 100 MB); `link-protocol.md` §3.2, §4.3.4.
- **Code:**
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/links/routes.py:269-278`: the delivered receipt's body is read to
    the end, then `signing.verify_request(request.headers, "POST", request.url.path, b"", fp,
    srv.link_nonces, digest=digest)` checks the call's `MK-Link-Time` against the clock *now*
    (`signing.py:239-240`, `abs(now - t) > SKEW`).
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/links/client.py:174-185`: the `file` answer's bytes are read to
    the end, then `signing.verify_answer(peer_key, 200, b"", asked, answer_headers,
    digest=digest)` checks the answer's `MK-Link-Time`, stamped when its headers were sent,
    against the clock now (`signing.py:181-182`).
- **What goes wrong:** a 25 MB receipt (the default limit) over a 3 Mbit/s home uplink takes
  about 70 seconds. Delivered, it is refused `clock-skew` by the receiver once it has arrived
  whole; fetched, the fetching side refuses the answer as `clock-skew` once it has read it
  whole. Either way the receipt never crosses, and by U08-1 the link stops both ways, with an
  error that, where it is shown, blames the clocks. At 100 MB (S16.10's maximum) a 15 Mbit/s
  uplink is enough to fail it.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading (both checks take the clock after the stream).
- **Remedy:** check the time (and the nonce) from the headers when the call or answer
  arrives, before its body is read, and check only the signature over the digest at the end;
  the signature still covers the time it was made.
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

<a id="cr3-54"></a>

### CR3-54: the minute's sweep deletes receipts that arrived while the copy was locked, if it runs while an unlock is opening the inbox

*Work pad: U08-7 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S11.9.5.2 (receipts written straight into `receipts/`), S11.9.6.6
  ("A sweeper removes files that no row names, left behind after a crash"), S11.9.5.3.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/links/copies.py:117-131` (`orphans`, run every minute
  for each open copy by `sweep`, `:135-143`):
  ```text
      with o.engine.connect() as conn:
          kept = set(conn.execute(select(copydb.File.c.fileName)).scalars())
      waiting = set(DELIVERED.get(link_id, {}).values())
      old = time.time() - ORPHAN_MINUTES * 60
      ...
          if f.stat().st_mtime > old or f.name in kept or f.name in waiting:
              continue
          if f.suffix in (".mkr", ".tmp"):
              f.unlink(missing_ok=True)
  ```
  A receipt fetched while the copy was locked is named only in its inbox entry's `local`
  (`receive.py:94`, `changes.py:121-124`) until `apply` commits its `File` row. `unlock` puts
  the copy in `open` (`copies.py:61`) and then `apply` (`:72-73`) opens and checks every
  inbox change (`changes.py:168-185`) before its transaction begins (`:210`).
- **What goes wrong:** the parents' copy stays locked for a week while pulls bring in Alice's
  receipts. Carol unlocks; while `apply` is still opening the week's changes, the minute's
  sweep runs: the copy is open, no `File` row names the receipts yet, and they are older than
  ten minutes, so it deletes them. `apply` then writes `File` rows that name missing files.
  The receipts are listed, and opening one answers 500 (`FileNotFoundError`); nothing
  repairs it but Reset copy, and nobody is told. The larger the inbox (a first full snapshot),
  the wider the window. The same happens to receipts a pull fetches while the copy is open,
  when fetching a part's receipts takes more than ten minutes.
- **Run:** yes,
  `tmp/u08/t/test_u08.py::test_sweep_during_apply_deletes_receipts_that_arrived_while_locked`:
  a receipt pulled while locked, its file's time set an hour back, `orphans` called from
  inside `apply`'s opening loop as the minute's tick would; the receipt is listed, its file is
  gone, and fetching it raises `FileNotFoundError` (`run4.txt`).
- **Remedy:** `orphans` keeps every file named by an inbox entry's `fileNames` and every file
  of a pull in progress, or runs under a per-link lock that `take` and `apply` also hold.
- **Alternatives:** receipts are written under `receipts/` only by `apply`, from a staging
  folder the sweeper leaves alone.

<a id="cr3-55"></a>

### CR3-55: every change waiting in a locked copy's inbox keeps its registry beside it in plain JSON: the other household's owners' names, what kinds they share, with whom and when

*Work pad: U08-8 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** privacy.
- **Req:** S11.9.1 ("The main database holds only what is needed while the copy is locked …
  File and folder names are random, so the folder shows no names, payees or dates"), S11.9.10
  ("A stolen disk, a backup that leaves the house, and root reading files while the copy is
  locked reveal nothing of a linked copy beyond its size, its number of receipts and when
  changes arrived"), S11.9.9 (the host's backup carries the copies "encrypted under their
  access keys"), S11.9.5.1.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/links/changes.py:121-124` (`enqueue`):
  ```text
      registry = {k: v for k, v in change.items() if k != "sealed"}
      entry = exactjson.dumps({"change": registry, "local": local or {}}, separators=(",", ":"))
      sealed.write(d / f"{name}.json", [entry.encode()])
      sealed.write(d / f"{name}.mks", [unb64(change["sealed"])])
  ```
  `sealed.write` (`sealed.py:112-124`) writes the bytes as given: the `.json` beside each
  sealed `.mks` in `links/<random>/inbox/` is plain. The registry (`changes.py:39`, protocol
  §4.4) holds each item's `id`, `kind`, `ownerName`, `sharedAt`, `viewers` (this server's user
  ids) and `includeReceipts`, the change's `asOf`, `progress.through` (the newest record's date
  reached) and the receipts' ids. The same facts in the main database are under SQLCipher
  (S15); here they are not. The files stay until the copy is unlocked and applies them, which
  by design may be days (S11.9.5).
- **What goes wrong:** the parents' server keeps the copy of their daughter's shares locked, as
  intended. A disk pulled from it, a host backup of the data directory taken off site, or root
  reading files while it is locked shows, in `inbox/0000000001.json` …, "Alice Griffin" sharing
  a `tax-book` and an `account` with receipts, with which local user ids, since when, and how
  far back her records reach: the who-shares-what-with-whom that S11.9.1 keeps out of the
  folder and S11.9.10 promises a locked copy doesn't reveal.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** the registry the copy needs to apply a change stays inside the sealed part (the
  source seals the whole change, and the receiver keeps its registry in the main database as
  it already does in `LinkedItem`), or the `.json` is sealed to the copy's public key like the
  change beside it; the `local` file names go into the main database (they are random) or into
  the same sealed entry.
- **Alternatives:** the `.json` holds only `seq` and the receipts' random names, and `apply`
  reads the rest from the opened change, which carries its registry under its signature.

<a id="cr3-56"></a>

### CR3-56: an upload is read whole into memory before anyone is signed in, and the in-flight cap is keyed by whatever cookie the request carries

*Work pad: U09-1 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Severity:** Med (High if the release is meant to face the internet
  directly).
- **Req:** S13.7.6 ("at most 100 MB of uploads in flight per user … an upload is held in
  memory"), S13.7.5.3 (60 uploads an hour per user), S16.14 (no ranges by default), DEPLOY.md
  §1 (2 GB for the server).
- **Code:**
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/middleware.py:216-233` (`BodyLimit._caller`): the caller an
    upload is counted against is the SHA-256 of the `mk_session` cookie's value, unchecked:
    ```text
            if COOKIE in cookie:
                return "session:" + hashlib.sha256(cookie[COOKIE].value.encode()).hexdigest()
    ```
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/app.py:154-158`: `MultiPartParser.spool_max_size = 128 * 1024 *
    1024`, so each part up to 128 MiB is held in memory; `upload_cap` lets a body be the larger
    size limit plus 1 MB (26 MiB by default, up to 201 MiB).
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/receipts.py:313-318` (and `ui/imports.py:235-237, 1047-1049`):
    `async def upload(request: Request, file: UploadFile = File(...), c: Caller =
    Depends(caller))`. FastAPI 0.143 reads and parses a form body before it solves any
    dependency (`fastapi/routing.py`, `get_request_handler`: `body = await request.form()`, then
    `solve_dependencies`), so the session check, `upload_rate` and `api_limit` all come after
    the whole file is in memory.
  - `OriginCheck` only compares `Origin` with `Host`, which a non-browser client sets at will;
    `networkAppRanges` defaults to `[]`, every address (`settings.py:251`).
- **What goes wrong:** an anonymous client that reaches `/ui` sends, at once, 100 POSTs of a
  25 MB multipart file to `/ui/api/receipts`, each with `Origin` equal to its `Host` and a
  different random `Cookie: mk_session=<random>`. Each is a different "caller" to `BodyLimit`,
  so none hits the 100 MB cap; each body is parsed into memory (under `spool_max_size`); only
  then does `caller` answer 401. While they arrive the server holds about 2.5 GB, past the
  2 GB DEPLOY gives it: the process is killed, comes back locked (S15.4), and stays down until
  an admin types the phrase. Without a cookie the cap is per address (100 MB), which a few
  addresses defeat too.
- **Run:** yes, `/home/dev/.claude/jobs/b8ac83ff/tmp/u09/upload.py` (output `upload.txt`): an
  anonymous multipart POST of 5 MB to `/ui/api/receipts`, with a random `mk_session` and a
  matching `Origin`, through `create_app`'s whole middleware stack: all 5,242,988 bytes were
  read before the session check ran, then 401. The concurrency and memory figures rest on
  reading.
- **Remedy:** decide who the caller is before reading a body: `BodyLimit` (or a middleware
  before it) looks the session up (a read of `Session` by the token's hash) and counts an
  upload against the user only for a live session; any other upload to `/ui/api` is refused
  401 on its headers, before its body. Also a process-wide ceiling on uploads in flight from
  all callers (a few hundred MB), past which a 503 `Retry-After` answers.
- **Alternatives:** the upload routes take the body as a stream (`request.stream()`) after
  `Depends(caller)` has run, and parse the multipart themselves; or the in-flight cap for an
  unverified session counts by address, with one small shared budget for all of them.

<a id="cr3-57"></a>

### CR3-57: the 20 MB ceiling counts compressed bytes, so a peer, an address in a link code or the explorer can answer with a gzip bomb that decompresses to gigabytes

*Work pad: U09-4 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** security.
- **Req:** S13.5 ("every answer is cut off past 20 MB, whether or not it announces its
  length"), S11.1 (a link code names an address anyone can type), S15.4 (a restart waits for
  the phrase).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/net.py:220-230` (`_Transport.handle_request`):
  ```text
          length = response.headers.get("content-length")
          if length and length.isdigit() and int(length) > self._max:
              ...
          response.stream = _Capped(response.stream, self._max, url.host)
  ```
  `response.stream` is the raw, still-encoded stream; httpx decodes `Content-Encoding` (gzip,
  deflate, and br/zstd when installed) after it, in `iter_bytes()`, with no limit, and its
  default headers ask for gzip. Only `Client.get_bytes` (`:258-267`) counts decoded bytes, and
  only the price sources use it. Everything else reads `.content`, `.json()` or `.text`, which
  decode the whole answer in memory: `links/client.py:102` (`hello`, unsigned, to whatever
  address a link code or a typed address names), `:136, :141, :215, :219` (`call`, decoded
  before the signature is checked), `bitcoin/sources.py:91-99, 323-331` (the explorer, the
  node).
- **What goes wrong:** someone hands a household a link code naming their own server, or a
  linked server turns hostile, or the explorer is compromised. It answers `hello` (or any call)
  with `Content-Encoding: gzip` and 20 MB of compressed zeros: under the ceiling on the wire,
  about 20 GB decoded. `r.json()` decodes it whole; the process is killed for memory and comes
  back locked (S15.4). For the explorer, the wallet read repeats it every few minutes.
- **Run:** yes, `/home/dev/.claude/jobs/b8ac83ff/tmp/u09/bomb.py` (output `bomb.txt`): a local
  server answering a POST with 203,860 bytes of gzip; `net.Client(...).post(...).content` was
  209,715,200 bytes (ceiling 20,971,520), peak RSS 456 MiB. 20 MB on the wire scales that by
  100.
- **Remedy:** `net.Client` sends `Accept-Encoding: identity` and `_Transport` refuses an
  answer with any `Content-Encoding` other than identity (none of the sources needs one); or
  `_Capped` wraps the decoded stream (count after decoding, e.g. by giving `httpx.Response` a
  decoded-size-checking `iter_bytes`), so `.content` raises at 20 MB of decoded bytes. A test
  with a gzip answer over the ceiling, through `.json()`.
- **Alternatives:** read every answer through `get_bytes` (and a POST equivalent), which
  already counts decoded bytes, and forbid `.content`/`.json()` on `net.Client` responses.

<a id="cr3-58"></a>

### CR3-58: a re-encryption interrupted just after `keyring.next` replaces the keyring makes a third data key on resume, and the database and receipts are lost

*Work pad: U10-1 in [`unit-10-encryption-settings.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-10-encryption-settings.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S15.7.2 ("**A journal** in the data directory
  records each step before it is taken … **Interrupted** by a stop or a crash, the
  re-encryption carries on from the journal at the next unlock, with the phrase given then,
  redoing nothing").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/store/reencrypt.py:233-239`:
  ```text
      old_ring_path = config.data_dir / OLD
      if steps.get("keyring"):
          old_ring = master.read_keyring(old_ring_path)
          new_ring = ring
      else:
          old_ring = ring
          new_ring = master.read_keyring(nxt) if nxt.exists() else None
  ```
  `:271-278` (`if new_ring is None: new = master.new_data_key() … master.write_keyring(nxt,
  new_ring) … _step(config, j, "next", new_ring.data_key_id)`), and `:303-308`:
  ```text
      if not steps.get("keyring"):
          if not old_ring_path.exists():
              shutil.copy2(config.keyring_path, old_ring_path)
              os.chmod(old_ring_path, 0o600)
          os.replace(nxt, config.keyring_path)
          _step(config, j, "keyring", True)
  ```
  The journal's `next` step records the new data key's id, but nothing reads it back.
- **What goes wrong:** the step is taken (the rename) before it is recorded. A stop, crash or
  power cut between `os.replace` and the journal's write leaves: the keyring holding the new
  data key D2, `keyring.old` holding D1, no `keyring.next`, and a journal whose steps say
  rekeyed, secrets and receipts done but not `keyring`. At the next unlock the run takes the
  keyring (D2) for the *old* one, finds no `keyring.next`, makes a third data key D3, skips the
  database, secrets and receipts as done, and puts D3 in the keyring; then it deletes
  `keyring.old`. The database and every receipt file are under D2, which is now nowhere: the
  server refuses every unlock with "does not open with the data key", and only the backup made
  at the run's start (and receipts from the host's own backups from before the run) remain.
  The window is short (a rename and one synced write), but the loss is total and silent until
  the unlock.
- **Run:** yes, `/home/dev/.claude/jobs/b8ac83ff/tmp/u10/crash_keyring_step.py` (output
  `crash_keyring_step.txt`): `_step` made to stop at `"keyring"`; the resumed run put a new
  data key id (`6cb2…`) in the keyring where the journal's `next` was `c8a5…`, and
  `open_store` then refused; `keyring.old` was gone.
- **Remedy:** on resume, when `steps` has `next` and the keyring's `dataKeyId` equals it, the
  keyring step is done (record it and go on); never make a new data key once `next` is in the
  journal (refuse instead, naming the files). Keep `keyring.old` until the report is written
  and the store has opened.
- **Alternatives:** record `keyring` as "about to be done" before the rename, and on resume
  decide by comparing the keyring's `dataKeyId` with `next`.

<a id="cr3-59"></a>

### CR3-59: a receipt file damaged after its first 64 KiB stops a re-encryption at every unlock, the server locked for good (K5's first case, half done)

*Work pad: U10-2 in [`unit-10-encryption-settings.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-10-encryption-settings.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S15.7.2 ("A receipt or a secret that opens under
  neither key is set aside and reported in Settings › Key storage, never fatal"); review 2's K5.1.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/store/reencrypt.py:130-136` (whether a file opens is
  decided by its first chunk: `next(files.open_stream(path, key, name))`) and `:151-155`:
  ```text
          if opens(path, new_key, name):
              continue
          if opens(path, old_key, name):
              files.write(path, files.open_stream(path, old_key, name), new_key.public_key(), name)
              continue
  ```
  `keys/files.py:91-94` raises `FileError` for a later chunk that fails; nothing in
  `rewrap_files` or `run` catches it. `runtime.py:362-364` keeps only the exception's type:
  `server.unlock_error = str(e) if isinstance(e, RefuseStart) else type(e).__name__` and logs
  "the re-encryption at the unlock failed: FileError".
- **What goes wrong:** a receipt file cut short or with a flipped bit past its first 64 KiB
  (a disk error, a file restored from the host's backup half written) opens under the old key
  by its first chunk, so it is rewritten, and the rewrite raises at the damaged chunk. The run
  stops with the journal still pending, leaving a `<id>.tmp` beside the file. Every later
  unlock resumes, meets the same file and stops again: the server stays locked, the locked
  screen and the log say only "FileError", with no file named, and the only way on is to find
  and delete the file by hand. The same holds for `keys reencrypt`, an offline rotation, and a
  restore's re-wrap (`maintenance._rewrap_restored`).
- **Run:** yes, `/home/dev/.claude/jobs/b8ac83ff/tmp/u10/rewrap_damaged.py` (output
  `rewrap_damaged.txt`): a 200 KB file with one byte changed in its second chunk; `opens` said
  True, `rewrap_files` raised `FileError`, and `bbbb.tmp` was left in the folder.
- **Remedy:** in `rewrap_files`, catch `FileError` from the rewrite, remove the `.tmp`, and set
  the file aside as one that opens under neither key (reported as damaged); and log the file's
  name with any failure of the run.
- **Alternatives:** decide `opens` by reading the whole file (doubles the read time of a
  re-encryption).

<a id="cr3-60"></a>

### CR3-60: copies made after a re-encryption's choices, the run's own first backup among them, are left under the old data key unasked; after a leak they open with the leaked phrase

*Work pad: U10-3 in [`unit-10-encryption-settings.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-10-encryption-settings.md).*

- **Kind:** security.
- **Req:** S15.7.2 ("for each the admin chooses **re-encrypt** (the default …), **delete**, or
  **leave**"; "A verified backup is made first, under the old key"), S15.7.1 ("**Then the
  copies on the host** … are each re-encrypted (the default), deleted or left"; "Re-wrapping
  alone would change only which phrase opens the keyring: an old phrase and any copy made
  before would still give the data key that opens every later copy, so a rotation always
  re-encrypts"), S15.8.2.2 ("so the leaked phrase opens nothing made from now on").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/store/reencrypt.py:309-316`:
  ```text
      outcomes = dict(steps.get("copies") or {})
      found = copies.copies(config)
      progress.set("copies", 0, len(found))
      for i, c in enumerate(found):
          if c.name in outcomes:
              continue
          choice = j["copies"].get(c.name, "leave")
  ```
  The choices are the copies listed when the run was scheduled (`schedule`, `:89-105`; the
  rotation through the running server, `maintenance.rotate_running:521-531`; Settings,
  `ui/settings.py:841`). The run's own first backup (`:255-268`, `backup(config, st)` under
  the old data key, with the keyring as it then is) and every daily backup made between the
  scheduling and the next unlock (`runtime.py:67-74`, the server still serving under the old
  data key) are not among them, so they take `"leave"`. And `rotate_running:528-530` tells the
  admin `"re-encrypted at the next unlock"` for every copy chosen `reencrypt`, though a copy
  under the old phrase with an earlier data key ends `"left: needs the phrase of …"`
  (`reencrypt._copy:350-353`; `keys rotate` over the socket never sends `oldPhrase`,
  `keys/cli.py:382-385`).
- **What goes wrong:** the household's phrase A leaks (a lost sheet beside the bucket the
  backup directory syncs to, the case S15.8.1 opens with). The admin follows S15.8.2: rotates
  through the running server (`keys rotate`, choosing delete or re-encrypt for each copy,
  `--delete-old` for a script), and plans a restart for the weekend. Until then the daily task
  keeps making backups whose database is under the old data key D1 (keyring wrapped by the new
  phrase B, database keyed from D1); the restart's run then makes one more, its "verified
  backup first", also under D1. None of these was listed, so all are left, and the report says
  "left". The bucket now holds full copies, made *after* the rotation, that the leaked phrase
  A plus any older copy opens (A unwraps D1 from the older copy's keyring; D1 keys these
  databases directly). S15.8.2.3 tells the admin to delete the copies made *before* the
  rotation, so these are kept, and the host's snapshots carry them on.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading (`run` defaults any unlisted copy to `leave`, and the backup
  step runs before the copies step).
- **Remedy:** a copy not in the choices takes the default S15.7.1 and S15.7.2 give
  (re-encrypt) when it is under the data key being replaced, which covers the run's own backup
  and the daily ones; once the run has finished, the first backup is re-encrypted too (it was
  only the safety net). Report "re-encrypted at the next unlock" only for copies the run will
  be able to rewrite, and say "left unless you give the old phrase" for the rest.
- **Alternatives:** stop the daily backup while a rotation's re-encryption is pending (the
  host's own snapshots still capture the live database under D1, so the requirement should
  also say a leak rotation is followed by a restart at once); or delete the first backup once
  the run succeeded and say so.

<a id="cr3-61"></a>

### CR3-61: the re-encryption a rotation brought can be cancelled in Settings, with no password again, leaving a rotation that only re-wrapped

*Work pad: U10-4 in [`unit-10-encryption-settings.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-10-encryption-settings.md).*

- **Kind:** security.
- **Req:** S15.7.1 ("so a rotation always re-encrypts"), S16.13 ("**Re-encrypt at the next
  restart** … schedules the re-encryption for the next unlock …; **Cancel** withdraws one
  scheduled"), S13.7.2 (the password again for key actions), S15.8.2.2.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/settings.py:857-863`:
  ```text
  @router.delete("/settings/keys/reencrypt", response_model=KeyStorage)
  def key_reencrypt_cancel(request: Request, c: Caller = Depends(admin)):
      """A scheduled re-encryption not yet begun, cancelled."""
      from ..store import reencrypt

      srv = server(request)
      if not reencrypt.cancel(srv.config):
  ```
  `store/reencrypt.py:108-114` (`cancel` removes any journal whose `steps` are empty, whatever
  its `why`); `web/ui/screens/settings.js:1335-1350` shows **Cancel it** under "A
  re-encryption is scheduled for the next restart, brought by a rotation on the host" whenever
  it hasn't started. Also `ui/settings.py:841` (`reencrypt.schedule(... "settings")`) replaces
  a pending rotation's journal, choices and `why` included, since `schedule` refuses only a
  journal with steps (`reencrypt.py:94-96`).
- **What goes wrong:** after `keys rotate` through the running server, the keyring is wrapped
  under the new phrase B but still holds the old data key D1, and only the journal makes the
  next unlock re-encrypt. Any admin session (no password again: the route has no
  `recent_auth`) presses **Cancel it**, perhaps to avoid the re-encryption's downtime: the
  journal goes, the next unlock re-encrypts nothing, and the rotation has only re-wrapped. The
  old phrase A plus any copy made before (every host snapshot) then opens the live database
  and every copy made afterwards for good, the case S15.7.1 rules out. Activity records a
  `keys.reencrypt-cancelled` with no sign it undid a rotation's re-encryption. Scheduling a
  Settings re-encryption over a pending rotation's likewise drops the rotation's copy choices.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** `cancel` refuses a journal whose `why` is `rotation` (409, "a rotation's
  re-encryption can't be cancelled"), and the screen shows no Cancel for it; `schedule`
  refuses over any pending journal rather than replacing it; Cancel asks for the password
  again.
- **Alternatives:** keep Cancel for a rotation's journal but turn it into "rotate back"
  (re-wrap under the old phrase, which then needs it), so a cancelled rotation is a rotation
  undone, not half done.

*Work pad: U10-7 in [`unit-10-encryption-settings.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-10-encryption-settings.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S16.7 ("No wallet is read until an admin saves
  this section (R3.5)"), S1.6.2; review 2's C36.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/bitcoin/sync.py:43-47`
  (`chosen` is `settings.saved(s, "bitcoinSource")`, a row existing); `ui/settings.py:118-120`:

  ```text
          changed = [k for k, v in clean.items() if settings.get(s, k) != v]
          for k in changed:
              settings.put(s, k, clean[k], c.id)
  ```
  `settings.py:223-224`: the defaults are `bitcoinSource` `"esplora"` and `bitcoinEsploraUrl`
  `"https://mempool.space/api"`, exactly what `web/ui/screens/settings.js:887-893` sends for **Public:
  mempool.space**.
- **What goes wrong:** an admin opens Settings › Bitcoin, leaves the suggested mempool.space
  (or picks it) and presses Save: every value equals its default, so nothing is written, no
  `bitcoinSource` row exists, and `chosen` stays false. The toast says "Saved", the section
  looks saved, and the hourly task never reads a wallet; Blockstream, a node, or any other

### CR3-63: an admin's session alone deletes every backup but one: *Copies kept* set to 1, then *Back up now*

*Work pad: U10-21 in [`unit-10-encryption-settings.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-10-encryption-settings.md).*

- **Kind:** security.
- **Req:** S16.13 ("**Remove**, which asks for the password and the phrase"), S13.7.2 (the
  password again), S16.12 ("the copies kept (14 by default, 1 to 365)"), S14.6 (backup
  pruning); review 2's K23 ("a stolen admin password deletes every backup one call at a time.
  **Remedy:** Remove asks for the phrase").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/settings.py:928-930` (Remove's own reason):
  ```text
      """Remove one copy (S15.8 step 4): the password again and the recovery phrase, so an
      admin's session alone deletes no backup (S13.7.2, S16.13). A wrong phrase counts toward
      the sign-in's lockout."""
  ```
  but `settings.py:266` (`_d("backupCopies", "backups", 14, _int(1, 365))`) is an ordinary
  setting: `ui/settings.py:94-103` asks for the password again only for `REAUTH` and `network*`
  keys; `POST /settings/backups/run` (`:1037-1046`) needs nothing more than an admin; and
  `store/maintenance.py:231-236` (`prune`) deletes every backup past `keep` at once, from the
  daily task and from Back up now (`runtime.py:67-74`, `host.py:118-122`).
- **What goes wrong:** whoever holds an admin's session (a browser left signed in, a stolen
  cookie) PATCHes `{"backupCopies": 1}` and posts `/settings/backups/run`: the new backup is
  made and the other thirteen are deleted, with no password and no phrase, the very thing
  Remove's phrase was added to stop. They can first change data (soft deletes are reversible,
  but edits to amounts, payees and tax books are not, without a backup) and then leave only a
  backup made after the change. Only the host's own snapshots, if any, still hold the earlier
  state.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** lowering `backupCopies` asks for the password again (add it to `REAUTH`) and
  takes effect only for backups made after the change (prune never deletes a backup older
  than the change's time by more than the old count's days), or pruning never deletes more
  than one backup per run.
- **Alternatives:** lowering *Copies kept* below the backups present asks for the phrase, as
  Remove does.

<a id="cr3-64"></a>

### CR3-64: a report's sign-in address skips the check the sign-in call makes, so a stolen or compromised connector shows owners any page as "Schwab's sign-in"

*Work pad: U12-1 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** security.
- **Req:** S18.7.1 ("the connector reports the sign-in address, which MoolaKata takes only on
  the driver's own sign-in host and shows with that host"), `connector-protocol.md` §4.3.4
  (refusing another with `address-invalid`), S18.2.4.2 and S18.2.4.4 (what a compromised
  connector, or a stolen volume, can do: post files and read the answers; nothing about the
  owners' passwords). Review 2's K24 point 2, marked done.
- **Code:**
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/connector/manage.py:446-466`: the `sign-in` call checks the host
    and path (`sign_in_allowed`, `SIGN_IN_PAGES = {"schwab": ("api.schwabapi.com",
    "/v1/oauth/authorize")}`).
  - `manage.py:349-350`, in `apply_report`, for every Connection a report names:
    ```text
            address = rep.get("signInAddress")
            c.signInAddress = address if isinstance(address, str) and len(address) < 2000 else None
    ```
    no scheme, host or path check.
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/connector.py:112-113` hands it out as `signInAddress` and
    `signInHost`; `web/ui/components/connections.js:303-305` renders it for a Schwab
    Connection as `<a href=${existing.signInAddress} target="_blank">Open Schwab's sign-in at
    ${existing.signInHost}</a>`, and Profile › Connections offers **Sign in again** whenever
    one is set (`connections.js:87`).
- **What goes wrong:** whoever holds the connector's identity key (a copied volume or backup,
  S18.2.4.4, or a compromised connector) sends a signed report naming every Connection with
  `"state": "needs-sign-in", "signInAddress": "https://api.schwabapi.com.example/v1/oauth/authorize?..."`.
  MoolaKata keeps it; each Schwab owner gets *needs a sign-in* (an alert to them and the
  admins) and a dialog whose link reads "Open Schwab's sign-in at api.schwabapi.com.example";
  the page there collects the owner's Schwab username and password. The `sign-in` call with
  the same address is refused `address-invalid`, so the check K24 added is bypassed by the
  report.
- **Run:** yes, `/home/dev/.claude/jobs/b8ac83ff/tmp/u12/test_u12_unreadable.py` (output
  `test_u12_unreadable.txt`), with the server's fixtures: a paired, confirmed connector's
  report naming `https://evil.example/schwab-login` was kept as the Connection's
  `signInAddress`; the `sign-in` call with the same address got 403 `address-invalid`.
- **Remedy:** `apply_report` runs the same `sign_in_allowed(c.driver, address)` and keeps
  nothing that fails it (or ignores `signInAddress` in reports altogether and takes it only
  from the `sign-in` call); a test that a report's address off the driver's page is dropped.
- **Alternatives:** the browser shows the sign-in link only when its host and path are the
  driver's own (a list in `connections.js`), as a second check.

### CR3-65: the "Its page" link follows whatever any report says, even one from an address no admin accepted, so a stolen volume's holder can collect the vault's words

*Work pad: U12-2 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** security.
- **Req:** S18.2.4.4 ("A stolen volume … yields the identity key and the pairing … The vault
  stays closed without the words"), S18.5.1 and S18.5.4 (the locked alert sends admins to
  its page; a report from a new address alerts until accepted), S18.11.1 ("Its address …
  goes in every report, and Settings › Connector links to it"), `connector-protocol.md` §4.2.3.
- **Code:**
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/connector/manage.py:301-304`, `apply_report`, before and
    regardless of the address check that follows at `:305-319`:
    ```text
        row.locked = bool(body.get("locked", True))
        ...
        row.pageAddress = _page(body.get("page"))
    ```
    `_page` (`:384-398`) accepts any `https://host:port`.
  - `web/ui/components/connector.js:77-81`: Settings › Connector shows it as "Its page …
    unlock, pair and lock it there", a link; the locked alert says "unlock it on its page"
    (`alerts.py:276-279`).
- **What goes wrong:** a thief has a copy of the connector's volume (`vault.json` and
  `pairing.json`, S18.2.4.4) and reports with its identity key from their own machine:
  `{"locked": true, "page": "https://connector.griffin-home.example:37374"}`. MoolaKata
  alerts *the connector is locked* and *reports from a new address*, and points Settings ›
  Connector's "Its page" at the thief's host. An admin who goes to unlock it, as the alert
  says, lands on a page that looks like the connector's and types the 12 words: the thief
  now opens the copied vault, every credential and the sealing key. The genuine connector's
  next report puts its own address back, so the link flips between the two and looks normal
  most of the time. S18.2.4.4's "stays closed without the words" holds only until the words
  are typed into a page MoolaKata linked to.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** take `page` only from a report that comes from the known (accepted) address,
  and keep the last good one otherwise; while a new address waits (`newAddress`), show the
  page link with that warning or not at all.
- **Alternatives:** the page's address is set by an admin in Settings › Connector (pre-filled
  from the first report) rather than taken from every report.

### CR3-66: one option trade in the Schwab window makes the driver's whole OFX file unreadable

*Work pad: U12-3 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S18.9.2 ("positions, transactions and balances,
  posted as OFX"), S18.8, S3 (options).
- **Code:** `connector/moolakata_connector/drivers/schwab.py:93-110`, `_trade`, writes an
  option trade as `BUYOPT`/`SELLOPT` with no `SHPERCTRCT`:
  ```text
      tag = f"{side}OPT" if opt else f"{side}STOCK"
      ...
          f"<UNITS>{qty}<UNITPRICE>{_num(item.get('price'))}<COMMISSION>{fees}"
          f"<TOTAL>{_num(t.get('netAmount'))}<SUBACCTSEC>CASH<SUBACCTFUND>CASH</INV{side}>"
          f"{typ}</{tag}>"
  ```
  OFX 1.0.2 requires `SHPERCTRCT` in both aggregates, and the server's reader
  (`web/server/src/moolakata/importers/ofx.py:78-87`, ofxtools) refuses the file.
- **What goes wrong:** an owner who bought or wrote one option contract in the last 45 days:
  the driver's file for that account (all its trades, dividends, positions and cash) is
  refused as a whole, every day, for 45 days after each option trade. With U12-5 and U12-6 it
  also stops every other Connection's posts.
- **Run:** yes, `/home/dev/.claude/jobs/b8ac83ff/tmp/u12/schwab_ofx.py` and `schwab_opt.py`
  (outputs `.txt` beside them): `to_ofx` with a stock buy reads (rows `buy`), the same with an
  option bought or sold gives `OFXSpecError: Can't set BUYOPT.shperctrct to None` (and
  `SELLOPT`), `ReadError` from `importers.ofx.read`. A name with `&` (*AT&T INC*) reads.
- **Remedy:** write `<SHPERCTRCT>` (Schwab's `instrument.optionDeliverables` or 100) in
  `BUYOPT`/`SELLOPT`; a driver test that runs `to_ofx` with an option trade through the
  server's OFX reader, as `test_end_to_end` does for stocks.
- **Alternatives:** leave option trades out of the file, saying so in the Connection's
  message, until they are written whole.
