### CR3-67: the Schwab driver takes opening or closing from the sign, and writes every position as stock

*Work pad: U12-4 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S18.9.2, S3.2 (the four option trades),
  R3.2 and S1.3.3.8.2 (positions in a file are a check).
- **Code:** `connector/moolakata_connector/drivers/schwab.py:100-104`:
  ```text
      typ = (
          f"<OPT{side}TYPE>{'BUYTOOPEN' if qty > 0 else 'SELLTOCLOSE'}"
          if opt
          else f"<{side}TYPE>{side}"
      )
  ```
  `:128-136`, `_position`, writes every position as `<POSSTOCK>`, options, funds and bonds
  too; `:174-177` drops every other transaction type (`else: continue`), Schwab's
  `RECEIVE_AND_DELIVER` (expiry, assignment, exercise, securities moved in) among them.
- **What goes wrong:** once U12-3 is fixed, a covered call written (sold to open, Schwab's
  `positionEffect: OPENING`) is imported as `SELLTOCLOSE`, closing a long call the owner never
  held; buying it back is `BUYTOOPEN`, a new long. The lots, the strategy and the gains are
  wrong, and an expiry or assignment never arrives, so the position check shows a difference
  that no transaction explains. The reader maps these exactly as written
  (`importers/ofx.py:233-241`).
- **Run:** no; rests on reading (the OFX reader's mapping read at `importers/ofx.py`).
- **Remedy:** take the trade type from `transferItems[].positionEffect` (OPENING, CLOSING):
  BUYTOOPEN, BUYTOCLOSE, SELLTOOPEN, SELLTOCLOSE, and SELLSHORT/BUYTOCOVER for stock; write
  `POSOPT`, `POSMF`, `POSDEBT` by asset type; map `RECEIVE_AND_DELIVER` expiries and
  assignments to `CLOSUREOPT`, or name what was left out in the Connection's message.
- **Alternatives:** none reasonable: the import has the four option trades (review 2's B5).

<a id="cr3-68"></a>

### CR3-68: a file MoolaKata can't read is answered with an unsigned 500, not a signed refusal

*Work pad: U12-5 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S18.8 ("A file refused for any other reason is
  dropped, and its Connection shows the refusal"), `connector-protocol.md` §3.3 (every answer
  to a signed call is signed), §7 (`file-refused`).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/connector/files.py:77-97` reads the file outside the
  `try` that turns a `ReadError` into a refusal (`:100-108`):
  ```text
      if ofx.looks_like_ofx(data):
          parsed = parsing.read_ofx(data)
      elif csvfile.looks_like_csv(data):
          ...
          parsed = parsing.read_csv(data, json.loads(saved.columns))
  ```
  `ReadError` is a `ValueError` the route doesn't catch (`connector/routes.py:232-237` catch
  `files.Limit` and `files.Refused` only), so the app's last handler answers 500
  `server-error` (`errors.py:124-130`), without the connector's signature.
- **What goes wrong:** an OFX server answers with a statement the reader can't parse (or the
  Schwab driver's file of U12-3): MoolaKata logs an unhandled error each time and answers 500
  unsigned; the connector can't tell it from a forged answer (U12-6), and the owner is never
  told why nothing arrives.
- **Run:** yes, `/home/dev/.claude/jobs/b8ac83ff/tmp/u12/test_u12_unreadable.py` (output
  `test_u12_unreadable.txt`): the Schwab driver's file with one option trade, posted by a
  paired, confirmed connector, got `500 application/problem+json`, no `MK-Conn-Signature`.
- **Remedy:** move both reads inside the `try` (or catch `ReadError` around them) and refuse
  `file-refused` with the reader's message, signed; a server test posting an unreadable OFX.
- **Alternatives:** the connector routes catch every exception and answer a signed
  `server-error`, so a fault there is never unsigned.

<a id="cr3-69"></a>

### CR3-69: an unsigned answer, or a dropped connection, while posting files stops every later post and the after-run report, for good

*Work pad: U12-6 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S18.8 (a file refused for the limit is kept;
  any other refusal drops it), S18.5 (a report after each run), `connector-protocol.md` §7.
  Bears on D3-11.
- **Code:** `connector/moolakata_connector/core.py:449-463`:
  ```text
          for f in c.backlog:
              try:
                  out = self._post(c, f)
              except ServerError as e:
                  ...
                  continue
              ...
          c.backlog = kept
  ```
  An answer without `MK-Conn-Time` raises `signing.Refused("answer-unsigned")`
  (`signing.py:58-63`, via `client.py:171-174`), which isn't a `ServerError`; an `OSError`
  or `EgressRefused` from the transport isn't either. Either leaves `_post_backlogs` at once:
  `c.backlog = kept` never runs, the Connections after it are never posted, and the report
  after the runs (`core.py:320-322`) is skipped; `run_forever` waits and tries again
  (`:265-269`).
- **What goes wrong:** unsigned answers to a post come from MoolaKata itself: the 500 of U12-5,
  the body limit's 413 `too-large` and 429 `uploads-in-flight` (`middleware.py:251-270`, `:300-310`), and
  the 503 `locked` (S13.6). For a file that always gets one (U12-3 with U12-5), every round
  posts that file first, fails, and stops: the other Connections' files wait in memory until
  the connector restarts, and the restart's run fetches the same file again. Nothing reaches
  MoolaKata to say so (no report follows the run).
- **Run:** no; the connector side rests on reading; the unsigned 500 was run (U12-5).
- **Remedy:** catch every exception per file in `_post_backlogs`: keep the file for a later
  round on a transport error, `locked`, `limit` or `uploads-in-flight` (signed or not), drop it
  and set the Connection `failing` on anything else, and go on to the next file and
  Connection; always send the after-run report. A core test with an unsigned 500 on one file.
- **Alternatives:** the server never answers the connector unsigned except `locked` (the body
  limit signs too), as D3-11's remedy reads, and the connector drops any other unsigned
  answer per file.

<a id="cr3-70"></a>

### CR3-70: new credentials for an existing OFX Connection are sealed with no host, so the connector refuses every one

*Work pad: U12-7 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S18.6.4 (**Sign in again**, **Change
  credentials**), S18.7.1 ("seals, with the credentials, the host the dialog shows the owner
  they go to"), S18.7.3 (after a changed address "the owner enters the credentials again,
  knowingly, for the new host"). Review 2's K12 (the browser seals the host the dialog
  showed), marked built in milestone 9.
- **Code:** `web/ui/components/connections.js:163`:
  ```js
      const host = f.driver === 'ofx-direct' ? hostOf(c.settings?.url || f.url) : null;
  ```
  For an existing Connection `c` is the row from `/profile/connections`, whose `ConnectionOut`
  has no `settings` (`web/server/src/moolakata/ui/connector.py:35-51`), and the dialog shows
  no address field for one (`connections.js:224`, `${existing ? null : html`…), so `f.url`
  stays `''` (`:113`) and `hostOf('')` is `null`. The connector then compares the box's host
  with the settings' (`connector/moolakata_connector/core.py:381-384`):
  ```text
          if host != self._binds(c):
              c.state, c.message = "failing", "the credentials were sealed for another server"
              return
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** Alice's credit union password changes; the run reports *needs a
  sign-in*; she clicks **Sign in again**, types the new password, **Seal and send**. The box
  says `host: null`, the connector refuses it as *sealed for another server*, the box is never
  taken (it stays in MoolaKata, offered at every report), and the Connection never works
  again. The same after S18.7.3's changed address. Only removing the Connection and making a
  new one (and setting each account's sync again) gets past it. Nor does the dialog show the
  owner the host the sign-in will go to, which S18.7.1 asks of it.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading (the chain above, end to end).
- **Remedy:** `ConnectionOut` carries the host a credential would be bound to (the settings'
  `url` host, for a driver with one), the dialog shows it ("sealed for ofx.example.com only")
  and seals that host; a browser test of **Change credentials** on an OFX Connection, and an
  end-to-end test that the connector takes it.
- **Alternatives:** the dialog for an existing OFX Connection shows the address field filled
  from the settings, so the owner sees and may change it (with the password again, as the
  PATCH already asks).

<a id="cr3-71"></a>

### CR3-71: any caller that reaches `/connector` has a whole upload read into memory before its signature is checked, and picks its own in-flight budget with the key header

*Work pad: U12-14 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** security.
- **Req:** S13.7.6 (at most 100 MB of uploads in flight per caller, held in memory),
  S13.7.5.4 (`/connector` 60 calls a minute), S16.14 (`/connector` reachable from the private
  ranges by default), S15.4 (a restart waits for the phrase). The `/ui/api` case is U09-1
  (withheld); this is the same pattern on another path, keyed by another header, which
  U09-1's remedy (a session looked up before the body) doesn't reach.
- **Code:**
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/middleware.py:226-228` (`BodyLimit._caller`): a request with an
    `MK-Conn-Key` header is counted against `"key:" + headers[h]`, unverified.
  - `middleware.py:242-249`: any `multipart/` body, `/connector` included, may be the upload
    size (the larger of Settings' two, plus 1 MB).
  - `web/server/src/moolakata/connector/routes.py:61-73`: `_signed` reads the whole body
    (`body = await request.body()`) before `verify_request`, and the call limit
    (`srv.conn_limit.hit(...)`) applies only after a signature verified, to that key; an
    unknown key is answered `not-paired` after its body was read (`:77-85`).
- **What goes wrong:** a device on the LAN (or anywhere, where an admin widened Settings ›
  Network's connector ranges for a laptop away from home, `operations.md` §8) sends, at once,
  100 POSTs to `/connector/v1/report`, each `Content-Type: multipart/form-data` with a 25 MB
  body and its own random `MK-Conn-Key`. Each is a different caller under the 100 MB cap,
  each body is held whole, and only then refused: about 2.5 GB in flight against the 2 GB
  DEPLOY gives the server, which is killed and comes back locked until an admin types the
  phrase. Separately, any signed-in user can read the paired connector's identity key
  (`GET /ui/api/connector`, `ui/connections.py:87-95`) and send 100 MB under its header, so
  the genuine connector's posts get the unsigned 429 `uploads-in-flight`, which U12-6 turns
  into a stall of every post.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading (U09-1's run showed the body read before any check through
  the same middleware stack).
- **Remedy:** for `/connector`, refuse before the body: a call whose `MK-Conn-Key` isn't the
  paired connector's (one indexed read) is answered `not-paired` on its headers, unsigned, as
  the link protocol does for a large body from an unknown key; count the in-flight budget
  only for the paired key, and keep a process-wide ceiling on uploads in flight.
- **Alternatives:** `/connector` takes multipart only on `…/files` and refuses a body over
  1 MB on any other path on its headers.

<a id="cr3-72"></a>

### CR3-72: a join call from any address is read whole, up to 20 MB, before its signature is looked at

*Work pad: U08-F1 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md); found while checking, confirmed by the sweep.*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S13.7.5.4 (calls not yet signed by a known key,
  "checked before a body is read"; a body of an unknown key over 1 MB refused on its headers),
  S13.7.6; review 2's K22.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/links/routes.py:162-165`:
  ```text
  async def join(request: Request):
      ...
      srv = request.app.state.server
      body = await request.body()
  ```
  every other `/link` route reads its body through `body_of` (`routes.py:55-62`), which refuses
  an unknown key's body over `UNKNOWN_BODY` (1 MB) on its headers; `join` is the one route a
  stranger's key may call, and it skips that. `middleware.py:38` `LINK_LIMIT` is 20 MB for
  every `/link` path, and `join`'s body is a few hundred bytes (`code`, `address`, `t`).
- **What goes wrong:** anyone who can reach `/link/` posts a 20 MB body to `/link/v1/join`; it
  is held in memory and hashed before `verify_request` refuses it, as often as the per-address
  limit allows (60 a minute), and from many addresses as often as they like.
- **Remedy:** `join` refuses a body over a few KB on its headers (a limit of its own, below
  `UNKNOWN_BODY`), before it is read.

<a id="cr3-73"></a>

### CR3-73: shares transferred in start their holding period on the day they arrive

*Work pad: U03-16 in [`unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S2.2 (lots created "by transfers in with a cost basis"),
  S2.4.1 ("Each lot keeps the day it was acquired"), S3.1 (days held, short- or long-term per
  lot); IRC §1223 (a carried-over basis carries its holding period).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/transactions.py:645-658`, a transfer-in's lot is
  acquired on the transaction's date, and `TxnIn` (`:126-148`) has no field for when the shares
  were bought:
  ```text
  if inst_id and body.kind in ("buy", "reinvest", "transfer-in"):
      basis = body.costBasis if body.kind == "transfer-in" else -body.amount
      s.add(
          Lot(
              ...
              acquiredOn=body.date,
  ```
  and an edit moves it to the new date again (`:777-782`). `Replay.moved`
  (`ledger/lots.py:99-101, 676-677`), which keeps the lots a transfer out took, is read only by
  bitcoin's pairs (`bitcoin/transfers.py:121`); a matched transfer of shares opens no carried
  lots (run: a transfer out and in linked by hand leave the shares *basis missing*).
- **What goes wrong:** Case: 100 VTI bought in 2019 at Schwab, moved to Fidelity on 2026-01-10
  (ACATS) and recorded as a transfer-in with its $15,000 basis, sold on 2026-03-02 at a $9,000
  gain. MoolaKata counts the lot as acquired 2026-01-10: the gain is short-term on the Closed
  and Gains tabs and on Form 8949 (box A/B instead of D/E), taxed at the ordinary rate, and the
  Open tab showed it short-term the whole time.
- **Run:** partly: `tmp/u03/t/test_u03.py::test_shares_moved_between_brokers_are_their_own_replacement`
  shows a linked transfer of shares carries no lots; the acquired date rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** a transfer-in of shares takes the day (or days, per lot) they were acquired, as it
  takes their basis; a transfer linked to the owner's transfer out opens the lots it left with,
  as bitcoin's pairs do (`Replay.moved`).
- **Alternatives:** S2.2 says a transfer-in asks for each lot's basis and acquired date, and the
  dialog offers several lots.

<a id="cr3-74"></a>

### CR3-74: a write helper attaches a tax document but can't open its file, nor any the filers attached (D1-25)

*Work pad: U04-14 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S8.14.3 (a helper with write "can edit filings, attach documents
  and resolve issues"), S10.3.1 (a tax book share includes "its panels, filings and
  documents"), against S9.6 ("a tax document's file by the tax book's filers (S8.16)").
  Phase 1's D1-25, open: **needs the user**.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/receipts/__init__.py:248-249, 289-301`, a tax document
  gives its file to accepted filers only:
  ```text
  if best is None and _on_tax_document(s, user_id, r.id):
      best = "read"  # a tax document's file, for the book's filers (S8.16)
  ...
      if any(f.userId == user_id and f.acceptedAt for f in books.filers(s, book)):
          return True
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): Alice and Bob's joint 2026 book is shared with Dave, their
  preparer, at write. Alice attaches the W-2's PDF. Bob opens it (200); Dave gets **404**, and
  so does a grown child helping at read. Dave sees the checklist mark it received, but can't
  read the figures he is preparing from. The code matches S9.6's reading (filers only) and not
  S8.14.3 and S10.3's (the share includes documents). A file Dave uploads himself he owns, so
  he opens his own but not theirs.

  write it. If filers only: S8.14.3 and S10.3.1 say a helper attaches files but opens only
  their own, and the Documents tab says so beside a file a helper can't open.

### CR3-75: categories are the server's; any user adds one through the API, the browser can't, and nobody renames or removes one (D1-13)

*Work pad: U05-20 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** not built. **Req:** S7.2 ("**Categories** form a two-level tree … A default set
  ships"), S7.12.6, S14.10.1.1; Phase 1's D1-13 (who owns a category and who may change one,
  open, **needs the user**).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/transactions.py:951-1056`: one tree for the server
  (`list_categories` reads every live `Category`, no owner); `POST /categories` lets **any**
  user add a top-level or child category of any kind, with any tax tag, and records it nowhere
  but `createdById`; `PATCH /categories/{id}` is an admin's and changes only `keepReceiptsYears`.
  There is no route to rename, re-parent or delete a category, and no screen calls
  `POST /categories` (the browser only lists them: `components/txtable.js:17`,
  `screens/budgets.js:1623`, …).
- **Against the readings:** with categories per user or per budget, the code differs throughout.
  With D1-13's proposed reading (the server's, any user adds a child, renaming, moving and
  deleting a shipped or another user's category an admin's, each change in Activity), the code
  holds the first part only: a user adds top-level categories too, and with a tax tag that
  feeds every user's Taxes (S8.5); no one can rename or delete; nothing goes to Activity.
- **What goes wrong:** in the browser a household can't add *Pets* or *Giving › Church*, so the
  shipped tree is all it has, and S7.2's two-level tree beyond the default set is reachable only
  through the API; there, Bob can add *Medical › Botox* tagged *medical*, which then appears in
  Alice's category lists and her deductions' tag choices, and no one can remove it.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** decide D1-13 first (needs the user); then a category screen (or a field in the
  plan and transaction dialogs) to add one, and the rename, move and delete the decision
  gives, recorded in Activity.
- **Alternatives:** S7.2 says the shipped tree is fixed until later work, and `POST /categories`
  is removed or kept admin-only.

<a id="cr3-76"></a>

### CR3-76: in an envelope budget that plans a category and its parent, a card's payment line sets a charge aside twice

*Work pad: U05-27 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md); added from calibration runs B and C, checked by the comparison and by unit 05's checker.*

*From calibration run B's U05-3 (the card line, the part U05-6 does not give), checked by the
comparison.*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S7.13.2 ("A charge to the card from a
  category with money in it moves that much from the category to the payment line"), S7.1.2.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/budgets/envelope.py:124-131`, run for every planned
  category, parent and child alike:
  ```text
  charged = ZERO
  for cid in card_ids:
      charge = _actual(tr, per_card[cid], cat_id)
      covered = min(max(charge, ZERO), max(available - charged, ZERO))
      if charge < 0:  # a refund to the card comes back to the category
          covered = charge
      line[cid] += covered
      charged += covered
  ```
  `_actual` counts a category with its children (`budgets/__init__.py:372-379`), so the child's
  charge goes onto the line once under the child and once under the parent. This is U05-6's
  cause, here in the card's line.
- **What goes wrong:** case (run in calibration run B's scratch): *Food* and *Food › Groceries*
  both planned, a $100 grocery charge on the Visa: the card owes $100 and its payment line sets
  aside $200 (*Food* 400.00, *Groceries* 200.00). The payment of $100 then takes $100 from the
  line (`:146`), leaving $100 set aside for a card that owes nothing.
- **Run:** yes, calibration run B's `test_e20.py` (reran here): `setAside 200.00, owed 100.00`.
- **Remedy:** as U05-6: a parent's envelope counts only spending not in a child with a line of
  its own (the charge then goes on the line once), or `set_plan` refuses a parent and its child
  together in an envelope budget.
- **Alternatives:** none beside U05-6's.

<a id="cr3-77"></a>

### CR3-77: when two users follow the same budget, *Cash before payday* alerts only one of them

*Work pad: U05-28 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md); added from calibration runs B and C, checked by the comparison and by unit 05's checker.*

*From calibration run B's U05-19 (and C's U05-2), checked by the comparison.*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S7.6 ("raised as alerts to its owner"), S7.6.1,
  S7.12.5 ("a household budget shared at `write` can be active for both"); D1-2 is judged in
  this unit's Phase 1 section.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/alerts.py:572`, the cause's key leaves out the user, and
  `:843`, `evaluate` keeps causes by key:
  ```text
  key=f"cash:{b.id}:{a.id}",
  ...
  holding[cause.key] = cause
  ```
  `cash_short` loops over every user whose active budget it is (`:539-542`) and yields a cause
  for each, so the second user's cause replaces the first's.
- **What goes wrong:** Alice owns the household budget and shares it at write with Bob; both
  follow it. Checking dips below zero before payday. One alert opens, for whichever of the two
  the users' rows list last; the other never hears of it. Calibration run B's test: one
  `cash-short` alert, for Bob; the owner of the account and the budget gets none.
- **Run:** yes, calibration run B's `test_e17.py` (reran here), and calibration run C's
  `test_two_followers_one_alert` (reran): one alert, for Bob.
- **Remedy:** `key=f"cash:{b.id}:{a.id}:{u.id}"`, as the occasion alert includes the user
  (`alerts.py:749`).
- **Alternatives:** one alert per budget and account, shown to every follower.

<a id="cr3-78"></a>

### CR3-78: the month's *uncategorised* count includes every split transaction and every transaction left out of the budget, and sorting never clears it

*Work pad: U05-29 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md); added from calibration runs B and C, checked by the comparison and by unit 05's checker.*

*From calibration run B's U05-1 (and C's U05-5), checked by the comparison.*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S7.3.1 ("A split transaction counts through
  its splits"), S7.3.5 (a left-out transaction "counts nowhere in it"), S7.4, S7.14.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/budgets/__init__.py:293-308`:
  ```text
  Transaction.transferId.is_(None),
  Transaction.categoryId.is_(None),
  Transaction.instrumentId.is_(None),
  ```
  A transaction with splits has `categoryId` None (`ui/transactions.py:549-551`), and the count
  looks neither at splits nor at the budget's leave-outs (`Tree.skip`, which `amounts` honours).
  The Transactions screen's own *Uncategorised* filter leaves splits out
  (`ui/transactions.py:366-371`).
- **What goes wrong:** case (run): one $100 purchase split into two categorised parts: the month
  says `uncategorised: 1`, the review lists it as a loose end, and the link *1 uncategorised
  transaction to sort* opens a list with nothing in it. It cannot be cleared: it is fully
  categorised. After a car bought outright is left out of the budget, the count is 2 though
  `spent` is unchanged. Every split transaction in a budget's accounts is counted for ever.
- **Run:** yes, calibration run B's `test_e1.py` (reran here): `UNCAT ... 1`, then `2` after
  leaving out the car, `spent 100.00`; calibration run C's `test_split_counts_as_uncategorised`
  gives `UNCAT 2`.
- **Remedy:** count a transaction as uncategorised when it has no splits and no category, or a
  split with no category, and skip `tr.skip`.
- **Alternatives:** share one *needs a category* predicate with the Transactions screen's filter.

<a id="cr3-79"></a>

### CR3-79: copy as scenario keeps a line's held-in account when the copy does not count that account

*Work pad: U05-30 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md); added from calibration runs B and C, checked by the comparison and by unit 05's checker.*

*From calibration run B's U05-12 (the copy part) and C's U05-14 (its last sentence), checked by
the comparison.*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S7.10.4 ("one of the budget's accounts"), S7.12.4
  ("the same accounts and plan").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/budgets.py:1303`, the copy's accounts are the source's
  that the copier can see, and `:1304-1318` copies each line whole:
  ```text
  _set_accounts(s, b, [a for a in budgets.account_ids(s, src, c.id) if a in mine], c.id)
  ...
  heldInId=ln.heldInId,
  ```
  `set_plan` refuses a `heldInId` that is not one of the budget's accounts (`:782`); the copy
  does not.
- **What goes wrong:** Bob, who sees only some of Alice's accounts, copies her budget as a
  scenario: a reserve held in an account the copy does not count still names it. The copy's
  reserves are held in an account that is not one of its accounts.
- **Run:** yes, calibration run B's `test_e12.py` (reran here), the copy case: the copy keeps
  the held-in account.
- **Remedy:** clear `heldInId` (keeping the target) for an account not among the copy's.
- **Alternatives:** refuse the copy while a reserve is held in an account the copier cannot hold.

<a id="cr3-80"></a>

### CR3-80: landed money is listed as unassigned only in the month it landed

*Work pad: U05-31 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md); added from calibration runs B and C, checked by the comparison and by unit 05's checker.*

*From calibration run B's U05-21 (and C's U05-16), checked by the comparison.*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S7.10.5 ("is listed under **Where the money
  is** as **unassigned** until the user gives it").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/budgets/reserves.py:195-201`:
  ```text
  for t in txns:
      landed = t.amount > 0 and not (t.transferId and others.get(t.transferId) in inside)
      if not landed or month_of(t.date) != month:
          continue
  ```
  Only deposits dated in the month shown are listed, and `give_landed` looks the deposit up in
  the month it is dated in (`ui/budgets.py:1207-1214`).
- **What goes wrong:** $40 of interest lands in Ally on November 3 and is not given. On
  December 1 the *Where the money is* of December lists nothing for it, though it is still
  unassigned; it shows again only if the user opens November. The month's review of November
  lists it only for November.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** list every deposit since the reserves began with something left to give, whatever
  its month, and let it be given from the month shown.
- **Alternatives:** S7.10.5 says unassigned money is listed for its own month.

<a id="cr3-81"></a>

### CR3-81: in an envelope budget with reserves, landed money given to a reserve changes *Where the money is* but not the reserve's row nor To Budget

*Work pad: U05-32 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md); added from calibration runs B and C, checked by the comparison and by unit 05's checker.*

*From calibration run B's U05-11 (and C's U05-10), checked by the comparison.*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S7.10.5 ("Money given to a reserve is put
  into it as a move (S7.11) and counts as moved in"), S7.13.1, S7.1.4.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/budgets/envelope.py:100-102` skips the moves that give
  landed money:
  ```text
  for x in moves:
      if x.month != m or x.transactionId:
          continue
  ```
  while `reserves.net_moves` (`reserves.py:30-38`), which `reserve` and `held_in` use, counts
  them.
- **What goes wrong:** case (run): an envelope budget with reserves holds *Car maintenance* in
  Ally; $100 is transferred in and $40 of interest lands there. Before giving it: To Budget
  $2,940 and the reserve's row balance $100. After *Give to a reserve*: *Where the money is*
  says the reserves hold $140, but the row still says $100 and To Budget is still $2,940: the
  $40 is both in the reserve and still cash to hand out.
- **Run:** yes, calibration run B's `test_e10.py` (reran here): `BEFORE 2940.00 / 100.00`, `AFTER
  2940.00 / 100.00 | where total 140.00`.
- **Remedy:** `envelope.month` treats a given-landed move as money moved from To Budget into its
  category, as other moves are (a *kept as the account's own* one has no category and moves
  nothing).
- **Alternatives:** say in S7.1.4 that an envelope budget has no reserves.

<a id="cr3-82"></a>

### CR3-82: in a tracking budget a move to or from a yearly line changes only the other side

*Work pad: U05-33 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md); added from calibration runs B and C, checked by the comparison and by unit 05's checker.*

*From calibration run B's U05-14 (and C's U05-8), checked by the comparison.*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S7.11 ("each one's available amount that month
  changes by it"), S7.3.7 (a yearly line compares with the year to date).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/budgets/__init__.py:486-491`:
  ```text
  if ln.cadence == "year":
      planned, carried = ln.amount, ZERO
      actual = _actual(tr, ytd, cat_id)
  else:
      carried = _carry(s, tr, accts, every, cat_id, month, moved_by) if ln.rollover else ZERO
      planned = ln.amount + carried + mv
  ```
  `mv` is read for the row (`:432`, shown as `moved`) but a yearly row's `planned` leaves it
  out. `make_move` accepts any category and the Move dialog lists yearly rows on both sides.
- **What goes wrong:** case (run): *Entertainment* $100 a month, *Housing › Insurance* $1,200 a
  year; a $40 move from Entertainment to Insurance in December: Entertainment reads planned
  60.00, *−$40 moved out*; Insurance reads planned 1,200.00, *+$40 moved in*, remaining
  1,200.00. The $40 is in neither line; moved the other way, it appears from nowhere.
  *Cover from…* on an overspent yearly row never covers it.
- **Run:** yes, calibration run B's `test_e14.py::test_move_to_yearly_line` (reran here) and C's
  `test_move_to_yearly_line`: planned 60.00 and 1200.00, moved 40.00.
- **Remedy:** a yearly line's planned adds the year's moves to the month, or a move with a
  yearly line on either side is refused.
- **Alternatives:** say in S7.11 that moves are between monthly lines.

<a id="cr3-83"></a>

### CR3-83: a birthday or wedding anniversary cannot carry a budget of its own, so a plan budgeted per occasion leaves them out

*Work pad: U05-34 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md); added from calibration runs B and C, checked by the comparison and by unit 05's checker.*

*From calibration run C's U05-24, checked by the comparison.*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S7.7.1 ("a yearly **gift budget** or one per
  occasion"; the birthday and wedding anniversary "come from the person"), S7.7.3 (each date
  "with its budget"), S7.3.6.
- **Code:** a budget is kept on the plan (`GiftPlan.giftBudget`) or on an `Occasion` row; the
  birthday and the wedding anniversary come from the person and carry none
  (`gifts/__init__.py:196-211`, `dates_in`):
  ```text
  Date(
      "birthday",
      "Birthday",
      _safe(year, p.birthMonth, p.birthDay),
      None,
      "birthday",
  ```
  The budgets' *Gifts* line sums `o.budget` over the `Occasion` rows only (`budgets/plan.py:117-120`).
- **What goes wrong:** Emma's plan budgets $40 for Christmas and $60 for her birthday, per
  occasion. There is nowhere to put the $60: Upcoming shows her birthday with no budget, and the
  household budget's *Gifts* line plans $40 for her year.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** a birthday and a wedding-anniversary budget on the gift plan, used by `dates_in`,
  Upcoming and `planned_elsewhere`.
- **Alternatives:** S7.7.1 says per-occasion budgets are for the plan's own dates, and a birthday

<a id="cr3-84"></a>

### CR3-84: the Giving screen's income counts no split transaction, so a split paycheck leaves the target at nothing

*Work pad: U05-35 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md); added from calibration runs B and C, checked by the comparison and by unit 05's checker.*

*From calibration run B's U05-16 (and C's U05-18), checked by the comparison; the donations half is
U05-14.*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S7.9.2 ("the year's transactions in
  *Income* categories"), S7.3.1 ("A split transaction counts through its splits").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/giving/__init__.py:172-191`, `income()`:
  ```text
  cats = [c.id for c in s.scalars(select(Category).where(Category.kind == "income"))]
  ...
  Transaction.categoryId.in_(cats),
  ```
  A transaction with splits has `categoryId` None (`ui/transactions.py:549-551`), so none of its
  parts is read, whatever their categories.
- **What goes wrong:** case (run): a $5,000 paycheck entered as Salary $4,000 and Other income
  $1,000: the Giving screen's income is $0.00 and a 10% target is $0 (`target`,
  `giving/__init__.py:195-200`); the percentages in *history* are blank for the same reason. A
  household that enters its paychecks with splits (salary, withholding, retirement) gets a
  target and a pace from only its unsplit income. The budget's own figures count the same
  paycheck through its splits.
- **Run:** yes, calibration run B's `test_e15.py` (reran here): `GIVING income 0.00 given 0.00
  target 0.00`.
- **Remedy:** read amounts through the splits as `budgets.amounts` does (a split's category and
  amount, else the transaction's), for income and for donations alike.
- **Alternatives:** none.

<a id="cr3-85"></a>

### CR3-85: a giving plan's receiver gets a 500 when adding an organisation (write) or recording a donation (read), saves nothing, and cannot keep a plan of their own that year

*Work pad: U05-36 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md); added from calibration runs B and C, checked by the comparison and by unit 05's checker.*

*From calibration run B's U05-6 (items 1, 2 and 4; the rest is U05-16), checked by the comparison.*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S7.9.1 ("per user, or per group"), S10.3.2 (a write
  share edits entries), S10.3.1 (read cannot change).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/giving.py:300-306`, `add_organisation`, and `:604-605`,
  `_out`:
  ```text
  o = Organisation(id=ids.new_id("Orgzn"), ownerId=c.id, **values)
  ...
  return next(x for x in _orgs_view(s, c) if x.id == o.id)
  ```
  `_orgs_view` and `donations_view` list the rows of `giving.givers(...)`, the plan owner's
  (`giving/__init__.py:60-65`), so what a receiver has just made, owned by them, is not in the
  list the route looks it up in: `StopIteration`, a 500, and `db.tx` rolls back. `put_plan`
  (`:204-207`) answers 403 to a receiver at read, and at write edits the owner's plan.
- **What goes wrong:** Alice shares her 2026 giving plan with Bob. (1) At write, Bob adds an
  organisation: 500, nothing saved (U05-16 says it "never shows"). (2) At read, Bob records a
  donor-advised-fund contribution from his own account: 500 (any donation of his is filed
  under himself and read back the same way). (3) At read or write, Bob's `PUT /giving/plan` for
  2026 answers 403 *shared with you to read* (or changes Alice's): he cannot keep a plan of
  his own for a year in which one is shared with him, though a giving plan is per user.
- **Run:** yes, calibration run B's `test_e5.py` (reran here): the DAF contribution at read
  raises `StopIteration` (500), the organisation add at write raises `StopIteration`, and the
  own-plan PUT at read answers 403.
- **Remedy:** read the new row back by id instead of through the view, let a receiver's own
  plan win (`put_plan` makes one when no *own* plan exists), and accept the plan's writers in
  `_own`/`_own_org` (U05-16).
- **Alternatives:** say in S7.9.1 that a shared plan is the receiver's only plan that year, and
  refuse a receiver's organisation or donation with a 403.

### CR3-86: three help guides say other than S7 or the code

*Work pad: U05-37 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md); added from calibration runs B and C, checked by the comparison and by unit 05's checker.*

*From calibration run B's U05-26 (items 1, 3 and 5) and C's U05-23.4, checked by the comparison
(D43 to D45); the other guide mismatches are in U05-18, U05-19 and U05-23.*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S7.5.1, S7.9.1, S7.7.3.

     yearly" and leaves out *twice a month*, which S7.5.1 and `recurring.INTERVALS`
     (`budgets/recurring.py:19-26`) have.
  2. `web/ui/guides/giving-overview.md:15`: "**Share** shares the plan, so a household gives
     toward one target." S7.9.1: a plan counts its owner's accounts only, and sharing "shows
     them, and never reads the accounts of those it is shared with"; the code counts the
     owner's (`giving.givers`).

### CR3-87: in an envelope budget the *Gifts* and *Giving* lines show a plan, but nothing is assigned to them

*Work pad: U05-38 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md); added from calibration runs B and C, checked by the comparison and by unit 05's checker.*

*From calibration run B's U05-28, checked by the comparison.*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S7.3.6, S7.13.1 ("the plan's lines assign their
  amounts (a yearly line a twelfth of its amount)").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/budgets/__init__.py:513-533` adds the rows of
  `plan.planned_elsewhere` to every budget's month (`planned=amount`, `cadence="year"`), but
  `envelope.month` (`budgets/envelope.py:94, 113`) assigns only the lines of `in_force(every,
  m)`, and these are not lines (`set_plan` refuses to save them, `planned-elsewhere`).
- **What goes wrong:** case (run): an envelope budget; Emma's gift plan has a yearly gift budget
  of $1,200; $3,000 of income and a $500 *Food* line: To Budget $2,500 (a twelfth, $100, set
  aside for gifts would make it $2,400), and the *Gifts* row reads planned 1,200.00, remaining
  1,200.00. A $100 toy purchase takes To Budget from $2,500 to $2,400: the envelope's "$1,100

  after, *Gifts* remaining 1100.00.

<a id="cr3-88"></a>

### CR3-88: no test holds the month cache's inputs, Compare over a range, copying with moves, the review's flags beyond planned rows, a second follower's alert or a shared giving plan's receivers

*Work pad: U05-39 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md); added from calibration runs B and C, checked by the comparison and by unit 05's checker.*

*From calibration run B's U05-27, checked by the comparison.*

- **Kind:** test gap. **Req:** S7.3.10, S7.12.3, S7.12.4, S7.14, S7.6.1, S7.9.1.
- **What the tests hold, and what they don't:** `test_review.py::test_month_figures_are_kept_and_refreshed`
  writes with a bulk `update()` and checks Refresh; the cache's seventeen inputs
  (`budgets/cache.py:19-45`) are exercised only through transactions and plan lines; nothing
  tests a share, a category, a group membership or the in-flight read. `test_scenarios.py`
  compares one month (`"month": "2026-10"`) and copies without moves; `test_review.py`'s flag
  test changes only a planned row; `test_cash_before_payday` has one follower and a monthly
  payment; `test_giving.py::test_a_shared_plan_counts_its_owners_accounts_only` reads as the
  owner and as a reader and records nothing as a receiver. No test has a parent and a child
  planned in an envelope budget, a move to a yearly line, a split paycheck or a category under
  *Giving*, or a deleted account's transfers.
- **Remedy:** a test with each case in U05-4, U05-5, U05-6, U05-8, U05-3, U05-24, U05-28,
  U05-33, U05-35 and U05-36, written with its fix.
- **Alternatives:** none.

<a id="cr3-89"></a>

### CR3-89: a budget keeps counting, and naming, an account whose share was removed

*Work pad: U05-40 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md); added from calibration runs B and C, checked by the comparison and by unit 05's checker.*

- **Kind:** privacy. **Req:** R3.4 ("Revoking it ends access on the next
  request"), S7.1.1, S10.3.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/budgets/__init__.py:55-65`, the owner's view takes every
  account the budget was given, with no check that the owner can still see it:
  ```text
  rows = s.scalars(
      select(Account)
      .join(BudgetAccount, BudgetAccount.accountId == Account.id)
      .where(
          BudgetAccount.budgetId == budget.id,
          BudgetAccount.deleted == false(),
          Account.type.not_in(TYPES),
      )
  ).all()
  if viewer is None or viewer == budget.ownerId:
      return [a.id for a in rows]
  ```
  Access is checked only when accounts are chosen (`ui/budgets.py:_check`, `:429-442`:
  `mine = {a.id for a, _ in access.visible_accounts(s, user_id)}`); a grep of `BudgetAccount`
  finds no writer that removes a row when an account's share ends (`ui/budgets.py:454-475` are the
  choose-accounts route, `seed_budgets.py` the dev seed). `budget_recurring` names every account for
  the owner (`ui/budgets.py:1644`: `reach = access.reach(s, c.id) if c.id != b.ownerId else None`),
  and `alerts.cash_short` (`alerts.py:546`) reads `account_ids(s, b, u.id)` the same way.
- **What goes wrong:** Bob shares *Bob Checking* with Alice; she adds it to her budget; Bob
  removes the share (Alice's `GET /accounts/{id}` is now 404). Bob's later spending still counts in
  Alice's month (Food actual $42), her recurring list shows *Secret Clinic, Bob Checking, −$42.00*,
  the budget lists the account's id, its balance feeds an envelope budget's To Budget and *Where the
  money is*, and the cash-before-payday alert names it and its dips to her. Her receivers see the
  same figures from the owner's own accounts. R3.4 says revoking ends access on the next request.
- **Run:** yes, calibration run C's `test_unshared_account_still_counted` (reran by the checker):
  `ACTUAL 42.00`, `RECURRING [('Secret Clinic', 'Bob Checking', -42.00)]`, `BUDGET ACCOUNTS`
  holding Bob's account id.
- **Remedy:** `account_ids` keeps, for every viewer the owner included, only accounts the owner
  can still see (`access.account_level(s, budget.ownerId, a)`), and the receiver rule on top; a
  test that removes a share.
- **Alternatives:** when a share ends, mark the account's `BudgetAccount` rows deleted in every
  budget of the user who lost it (and say so in Activity).

### CR3-90: Budget alerts go to "its owner", but the active budget can be another user's

*Work pad: D1-2 in [`docs-1-money.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-1-money.md).*

- **Kind:** contradiction within the block.
- **Req:** S7.6, S7.12, S7.12.5.
- **Where:** `S7-budgets-gifts-and-giving.md:106–107`: "**Budget alerts** come from a user's
  active budget only (S7.12), raised as alerts to its owner (S12.11)"; `:253–254`: "Each user
  follows at most one **active** budget, among those they own or that are shared with them";
  `:269–271`: "a household budget shared at `write` can be active for both, while one of them
  tries a scenario of their own".
- **What goes wrong:** Alice owns the household budget and shares it at `write` with Bob. Bob
  makes it active; Alice makes a scenario of her own active. Read as written, *Cash before
  payday* comes from Bob's active budget and is raised to its owner, Alice, who doesn't follow
  it, and Bob, who does, hears nothing. S7.12.2 ("Only the active budget … raises budget
  alerts") leaves the same doubt.
- **Remedy:** S7.6 says the alerts go to each user whose active budget it is.
- **Alternatives:** alerts go to the owner and to every `write` receiver who has it active.

- **What goes wrong:** the OCC symbol pads the root to six characters (`AAPL  2612…`); the
  `O:` prefix and unpadded root of S4.5 are Massive's own form. S3.10.3 ("an option named by
  its OCC ticker is made a contract") and S3.2.1 read a file's OCC ticker, so a reader can't
  tell which form a file or a source is matched in, or whether `O:` is ever stored.
- **Remedy:** S4.5 shows the OCC form and says Massive is asked with `O:` and the root
  unpadded.

<a id="cr3-98"></a>

### CR3-98: S4.5's typed mark, intrinsic value and greeks sit inside its item 3, on Massive

*Work pad: D1-11 in [`docs-1-money.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-1-money.md).*

- **Kind:** structure (an item's text under the wrong number).
- **Req:** S4.5, S4.5.3.
- **Where:** `S4-prices.md:57–61`: "3. **Massive** is asked for one contract at a time, with its
  history. A typed mark is a price on the / option's instrument, entered from the open position
  (**Type a mark**). Intrinsic value is / the underlying's latest close past the strike … Greeks
  / are out of scope". Lines 58–61 are indented three spaces, so Markdown continues item 3.
- **What goes wrong:** the typed mark and intrinsic value, which hold for every source (S3.7),
  read as part of S4.5.3 (Massive), and code citing them cites S4.5.3.
- **Remedy:** move lines 57 (from "A typed mark") to 61 to a paragraph after the list, or to
  an item 4.

<a id="cr3-99"></a>

### CR3-99: R3.5 says price sources are off until configured; S4 never says which are on, and loads benchmarks "always"

*Work pad: D1-12 in [`docs-1-money.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-1-money.md).*

- **Kind:** contradiction / behaviour named and never specified.
- **Req:** R3.5, S4.2.3, S4.3, S4.4.
- **Where:** `README.md:303–304`: "Price sources and the bitcoin explorer are off until
  configured."; `S4-prices.md:15–18`: "**Settings › Prices** lists the enabled sources … **Add
  a source** picks from every source MoolaKata ships. A source that needs a key can't be enabled
  without one."; `:40–41`: "SPY, VT and BND are always loaded, as the benchmarks (S5.2.1)."
- **What goes wrong:** S1.6.2.1 spells out the explorer's "off until saved" (a wallet says it
  waits); S4 has no such rule. On a new server it isn't said whether CBOE and Coinbase (no key)
  are enabled, or whether an admin must add them; if they are off, "always loaded" loads
  nothing and the benchmark charts are empty without saying why.
- **Remedy:** S4.2.3 says a new server enables no source (R3.5), what the screens show until an
  admin adds one (*no price source: Settings › Prices*), and S4.4 says the benchmarks load once
  a source that serves them is enabled.
- **Alternatives:** the keyless sources (CBOE, Coinbase) are enabled at the welcome, which R3.5
  then names as its exception. **Needs the user** (which).

*Work pad: D1-15 in [`docs-1-money.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-1-money.md).*

- **Req:** S5.2.1, S4.4.
- **Where:** `S5-portfolios-and-charts.md:16`: "against a **benchmark** (SPY, VT, a 60/40
  mix)"; `S4-prices.md:40–41`: "SPY, VT and BND are always loaded, as the benchmarks".
- **What goes wrong:** which funds the 60 and 40 are (VT or SPY, with BND, as S4.4's loading
  suggests), and whether the mix is rebalanced (daily, monthly, yearly, never) are not said;
  each choice gives a different line over 5Y.

<a id="cr3-101"></a>

### CR3-101: S3.9 makes an option trade an entity, which R2.20 says a trade is not

*Work pad: D1-16 in [`docs-1-money.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-1-money.md).*

- **Kind:** term used other than R2 defines it.
- **Req:** R2.20, S3.9, S3.9.1, S3.10.4.
- **Where:** `README.md:267–268`: "*Trade* as an entity: a trade is a pair of transactions on a
  position."; `S3-positions-and-options.md:128–130`: "An option trade runs from the first
  contract opened in an account to the moment none is open. Premium in and out are the cash
  received and paid, fees included"; `:157–159`: "On Option trades, the old contract's trade
  ends *adjusted*".
- **What goes wrong:** S3.9's option trade is any number of transactions (scale-ins, partial
  closes), listed on its own tab with its own dates, P/L and end state: an entity in all but
  name, and not "a pair". A reader holding R2.20 looks for pairs.
- **Remedy:** R2.20 adds: "an **option trade** (S3.9.1) is a position's run from open to none
  open, a view, not a stored record", or S3.9 uses another word (*a run*).

<a id="cr3-103"></a>

### CR3-103: "One active projection per savings target": what makes two targets the same is not said

*Work pad: D1-18 in [`docs-1-money.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-1-money.md).*

- **Kind:** behaviour named and never specified.
- **Req:** S6.7.1, S6.2.3.
- **Where:** `S6-projections.md:92–94`: "**At most one per subject**: one retirement projection
  per person or couple, one education projection per beneficiary, one per savings target.
  Making one active asks before turning off the one it replaces."
- **What goes wrong:** a savings target is an amount by a date (S6.2.3) with no identity of its
  own: two savings projections for a house deposit at different amounts are either one subject
  (and making the second active turns off the first) or two (and any number can be active).
  The code must pick one, and the rule can't be checked.
- **Remedy:** savings projections have no limit on active ones (each is its own target), or a
  savings projection names its target (*House deposit*) and one per name is active.

<a id="cr3-104"></a>

### CR3-104: "The 4% rule" is never defined, and the mockup shows it as 4% of the balance

*Work pad: D1-19 in [`docs-1-money.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-1-money.md).*

- **Kind:** term not defined / mockup vs section.
- **Req:** S6.2.1.
- **Where:** `S6-projections.md:16–17`: "**withdrawals** (the 4% rule, a fixed amount rising
  with inflation, or a percentage of the balance)"; `MoolaKata-mock.html:1404`: "`field('Withdrawal',
  sel(['4% of balance', 'Fixed $80,000/yr, rising with inflation', 'Fixed % …'], '4% of
  balance'))`"; the projections guide: "Withdrawal: A percentage of the balance each year, or a
  fixed amount that rises with inflation."
- **What goes wrong:** the 4% rule is usually 4% of the balance at retirement, then that dollar
  amount rising with inflation; S6.2.1 lists it beside "a percentage of the balance", so it must
  differ from it, but the mockup's default *4% of balance* is the percentage-of-balance method,
  and its guide drops the rule. The two readings give different chances the money lasts.
- **Remedy:** S6.2.1 defines it ("4% of the balance at retirement, then that amount rising with
  inflation") and the mockup's choice and guide follow.

*Work pad: D2-7 in [`docs-2-platform.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-2-platform.md).*

- **Where:** `S11-linked-servers.md:115–118`: "each pull is recorded on both sides (who, when,
  which shares, how much changed) … The receiving side records who viewed linked data, and
  every unlock (who), failed unlock, release …"; `S12-web-ui.md:202–203`: "**Never mixed**:
  nothing from a linked household appears in this household's lists, totals, charts, search,
  portfolios, budgets, taxes or Activity."; `S11-linked-servers.md:48–50`: the receiving admin
  sees each share "only as its kind, its owner's display name, who it names and the date …:

  (`:68`) and **Remove** (`:69`). Mockup `MoolaKata-mock.html:1665`: **Test** answers "Key

  things about a code shown and not yet used. Read one way, an admin who unpairs a lost

### CR3-128: Status and history words in S14, S15 and S17

*Work pad: D2-24 in [`docs-2-platform.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-2-platform.md).*

- **Kind:** CLAUDE.md rule (present tense, no status, no changelog, no approval words).
- **Req:** S14.1.3, S15.6.7, S17.8.3.1, S17.9, S17.10.2.
- **Where:**

<a id="cr3-130"></a>

### CR3-130: When a received receipt's type is found: "when shown" in the link protocol, "when it arrived" in S11.9.6.5, and the change carries one anyway

*Work pad: D3-2 in [`docs-3-protocols.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-3-protocols.md).*

- **Kind:** the same thing specified twice, differently.
- **Req:** S11.9.6.5, S9.4; `link-protocol.md` §4.4.
- **Where:**
  - `link-protocol.md:180–181`: "The receiver keeps them as they came; its copy opens them when
    they are shown, and its type is found from the bytes then, never from the change."
  - `link-protocol.md:175`: `files`, "receipts' files added … (their id, item, type, length,
    hash, thumbnail and what was read from them)".
  - `S11-linked-servers.md:220–222`: "**Showing a receipt** streams it through decryption into
    the response, with the type checked from its bytes when it arrived (S9.4)".
- **What goes wrong:** receipts arrive sealed and usually while the copy is locked (S11.9.5), so
  the receiver can't read their bytes on arrival; S11.9.6.5's "when it arrived" can't be done
  as written, and the code will be checked against one of two moments. The `type` in `files`
  is a third source that the protocol says is never used.
- **Remedy:** S11.9.6.5 says the type is found from the bytes when the copy first opens the
  receipt (and kept in `shared.db`), as the protocol says; `files` drops `type`, or the
  protocol says it is shown only until the bytes are read.

<a id="cr3-131"></a>

### CR3-131: The caller is to check "the hash the change carried" before keeping a fetched receipt, but that hash is sealed where a locked copy can't read it

*Work pad: D3-3 in [`docs-3-protocols.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-3-protocols.md).*

- **Kind:** contradiction within the block, and with S11.9.6.4. Not withheld: the bytes are still signed by the peer's link key (the
  answer's signature over their SHA-256), so this names no forged or replayed message; it is a
  check that can't be done where the document puts it.
- **Req:** S11.9.5, S11.9.6.4; `link-protocol.md` §4, §4.4.
- **Where:**
  - `link-protocol.md:91` (`file`): "the answer's signature takes the sealed bytes' SHA-256 in
    place of the body's hash, and the caller checks it, and the hash the change carried, before
    keeping the file".
  - `link-protocol.md:174`: `fetch`, registry: "the ids of the receipts the part sends";
    `:175`: the `hash` is in `files`, inside `sealed`, "which only the copy opens" (`:163–164`).
  - `S11-linked-servers.md:217–218`: "`shared.db` records each receipt's file name, length and
    plaintext SHA-256 … The hash is checked as the file is shown".
- **What goes wrong:** pulls run while the copy is locked (S11.9.5), when the receiving server
  can read only the registry; the registry names the receipts but carries no hash, and the
  hash in `files` is of the plaintext anyway. "Before keeping the file" can only check the
  answer's signature; a code reviewer can't tell which check the protocol requires.
- **Remedy:** the protocol says the caller checks the answer's signature over the sealed
  bytes before keeping them, and the plaintext hash when the copy opens them (S11.9.6.4).
- **Alternatives:** the registry's `fetch` carries each sealed file's SHA-256, so the check
  before keeping is possible while locked.

<a id="cr3-132"></a>

### CR3-132: A large change's parts still to send are kept "in memory", with a 2 GB change as the example

*Work pad: D3-4 in [`docs-3-protocols.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-3-protocols.md).*

- **Kind:** behaviour specified in a way that can't hold (a bug candidate).
- **Req:** S11.4, S11.7.1; `link-protocol.md` §4.4.3; DEPLOY.md §1.
- **Where:**
  - `link-protocol.md:198–201`: "the receiver shows how far it has come (*Up to July 2022, 47 MB
    of 2 GB*). … The source keeps the parts still to send in memory, so after it restarts
    `next` answers nothing and the caller's next exchange asks for everything."
  - `DEPLOY.md:18–19`: "with **2 GB of memory** for MoolaKata (1 GB with receipt reading turned
    off".
- **What goes wrong:** read literally, the source holds the rest of a first full snapshot in
  memory while the peer pulls it part by part; for the protocol's own 2 GB example that is more
  than DEPLOY gives the whole server. Two links pulling at once double it. A server that runs
  out of memory restarts locked (S13.6), and the peer then asks for everything again.
- **Remedy:** the protocol says what is kept between `next` calls: a cursor (the item and date
  reached) from which each part is worked out when asked, so memory holds at most one part
  (about 4 MB); a restart still answers nothing and the caller asks for everything.
- **Alternatives:** the parts are written sealed to a spool under the data directory and
  deleted as they are fetched or when the exchange is abandoned.

<a id="cr3-133"></a>

### CR3-133: Receipts delivered ahead of their part are kept "until the part that names it arrives", with nothing for a part that never comes

*Work pad: D3-5 in [`docs-3-protocols.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-3-protocols.md).*

- **Kind:** behaviour named and never specified. Not withheld: only an approved, signing peer can deliver, and the effect is
  disk used on the receiving side, which the admin can end by ending the link.
- **Req:** S11.9.5.2, S11.9.6.6; `link-protocol.md` §4.3.4.
- **Where:**
  - `link-protocol.md:150–155`: "The caller's receipts for a part travel in `deliver` calls of
    their own before it … the answering side keeps each as it came until the part that names it
    arrives, so it never has to call back to fetch them."
  - `S11-linked-servers.md:224–226`: "each file is written under a temporary name, synced to disk
    and renamed before its row is committed. A sweeper removes files that no row names, left
    behind after a crash."
- **What goes wrong:** the caller delivers three receipts, then its part is refused
  (`change-refused`) or the caller restarts and its next exchange asks for everything: the three
  files have no row. Either the sweeper removes them at once (they have no row even while
  waiting, so it could remove them before their part arrives) or nothing ever does.
- **Remedy:** the protocol says how long a delivered receipt waits for its part (until the end
  of that exchange, or an hour), and the sweeper's rule leaves files that wait and removes them
  after that.

<a id="cr3-134"></a>

### CR3-134: S11.1.2's approval that reaches the joining side "at once" has no call in the protocol, and the test plan expects *active* within a minute

*Work pad: D3-7 in [`docs-3-protocols.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-3-protocols.md).*

- **Kind:** behaviour named and never specified.
- **Req:** S11.1.2; `link-protocol.md` §4.1.3; `test-plan.md` T5–T6.
- **Where:**
  - `S11-linked-servers.md:16–17`: "B's link waits for the approval, which reaches it at once or at
    its next exchange."
  - `link-protocol.md:107–111`: "The joining side's link waits as **pending** until the first
    `exchange` from the inviting side, which only an approved link makes, or until its own
    `exchange` is answered as active."
  - `test-plan.md:57–59`: "A: compare the fingerprints by phone, Approve. Passing: both show
    *active* within a minute".
- **What goes wrong:** exchanges are hourly (§4.3.1), and nothing says the inviting side makes
  one when its admin approves. In T6's one-way setup (only A serves), A can't reach B, so B
  learns at its own next exchange, up to an hour later; T5.2 fails though the code does what
  the protocol says.
- **Remedy:** the protocol says the approving side calls `exchange` at once when it can reach
  the peer, and the joining side, while pending, calls `ping` (or `exchange`) every minute for
  the first ten minutes, as the connector reports every 15 s while waiting (S18.5).
- **Alternatives:** T5.2 passes "within an hour, or at once after Pull now on B".

  - `link-protocol.md:171`: `items`: "`id`, `kind`, `ownerName`, …"; `:129–130`: "a kind of up to

    Items of a kind the receiver doesn't know are kept in its copy and not shown (section 5.3)."

  points to for it, names none. Two releases that spell a kind differently (`taxBook`,
  `tax-book`, `TaxBook`) each hide the other's tax books as "a kind this server doesn't know".
- **Remedy:** §6 lists the eight kinds' wire names (R2.9, S10.3), each with its documents
  (§4.4.1 already lists them, by kind, without the names).

<a id="cr3-136"></a>

### CR3-136: The unsigned `hello` of both protocols and `/healthz` tell anyone the exact MoolaKata version, though nothing they serve needs it

*Work pad: D3-9 in [`docs-3-protocols.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-3-protocols.md).*

- **Kind:** security. Not withheld: Low, and a
  version number is no weakness of itself; it only spares an attacker a guess when a release
  has one.
- **Req:** S13.6, S16.14, S11.1.2, S18.4.2; `link-protocol.md` §4, `connector-protocol.md` §4,
  `operations.md` §1.1, §5.
- **Where:**
  - `link-protocol.md:85`: "`GET /link/v1/hello` | no | any | `{protocol, version, fingerprint,
    key}` … lets a joining server check the fingerprint in a link code before it sends
    anything"; `connector-protocol.md:127` likewise for `/connector/v1/hello`.
  - `operations.md:196–197`: "point an uptime monitor … at `https://<your address>/healthz`: it
    answers `{"status": "ok", "version": …}`"; `S16-settings.md:87–88`: "`/healthz` and `/demo`
    answer any address".
  - `operations.md:30–46` (§1.1): the household exposes only `/link/` to the internet.
- **What goes wrong:** a household that follows §1.1 exposes `/link/` alone, and
  `/link/v1/hello` then names its exact release to every scanner, unsigned; a public uptime
  monitor does the same through `/healthz`. The joining server needs only `protocol`,
  `fingerprint` and `key`; the peer's version reaches Settings from `MK-Link-Version` on signed
  calls (`link-protocol.md:45`, §5.4). (S13.6 has `GET /ui/api/unlock` name the version to
  anyone who reaches `/ui`, which §1.1 keeps on the household's network.)
- **Remedy:** `hello` answers `{protocol, fingerprint, key}`, and `/healthz` its status alone;
  the version stays on signed answers and signed-in screens.
- **Alternatives:** keep it, and say in S13.6 that the version is public by choice.

<a id="cr3-137"></a>

### CR3-137: The connector protocol disagrees with itself on the pairing call's fields and the report's answer

*Work pad: D3-10 in [`docs-3-protocols.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-3-protocols.md).*

- **Kind:** contradiction within a document.
- **Req:** S18.4.2, S18.5; `connector-protocol.md` §1.2, §4.1, §4.2.
- **Where:**
  - `connector-protocol.md:23`: "`POST /connector/v1/pair {code, sealingKey, sealingSig}`" against
    `:147`: "with `{code, sealingKey, sealingSignature, version, protocol}`".
  - `connector-protocol.md:48–50`: "every report's answer, signed, carries its key's fingerprint
    as `certificate`", while the answer it shows (`:197–208`) has `connections`, `removed` and
    `confirmed` and no `certificate`; the glance (`:27–28`) shows neither `confirmed` nor
    `certificate`, and gives `sealed` as "only if unlocked", leaving out the confirmation
    `:213–214` also requires.
  - `connector-protocol.md:159–160`: "The connector then keeps, per server, the address, … One
    connector is paired with one server".
- **What goes wrong:** a test or a second implementation written from the glance sends
  `sealingSig` and is refused; one written from §4.2's example never reads `certificate`, so a
  renewed own certificate is refused at the next report instead of being accepted beside the
  pinned one (§1.2).
- **Remedy:** one field name (`sealingSignature`, as S18.4.2's "the sealing key signed with the
  identity key" and §4.1.2 give); `certificate` and `confirmed` in the example and the glance;
  "keeps with the pairing" for "per server".

<a id="cr3-138"></a>

### CR3-138: Which refusals are unsigned differs between the two protocols, though the connector's "follows the link protocol wherever the two do the same thing"

*Work pad: D3-11 in [`docs-3-protocols.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-3-protocols.md).*

- **Kind:** the same thing specified twice, differently. Not withheld: about answers the caller can't verify and therefore drops,
  not about one it would wrongly accept.
- **Req:** S13.7.5.4, S13.7.6, S18.8; `link-protocol.md` §7, `connector-protocol.md` §3.3, §7.
- **Where:**
  - `connector-protocol.md:6–7`: "It follows the link protocol (`link-protocol.md`) wherever the
    two do the same thing".
  - `link-protocol.md:253`, `:255`: `not-linked` "unsigned when the call's body is over 1 MB,
    refused on its headers before the body is read"; `limit` "(429, unsigned, with
    `Retry-After`)".
  - `connector-protocol.md:317–319`: "signed as section 3 says when the call carried a nonce";
    `:338`: only `locked` is "(503, unsigned)"; `:334`: `limit`; `:337`: "`too-large` | (413) a
    body over 1 MB of JSON, or a file over the upload size"; `:113`: an answer's signature
    covers "SHA-256 of the call's body"; `:342`: the connector refuses "`answer-unsigned` (an
    answer without its signature)".
- **What goes wrong:** to sign a `too-large` answer MoolaKata must read and hash the whole
  oversized body it is refusing; to sign a `limit` it must verify before rate-limiting. If the
  code refuses on the headers, as the link protocol does, the answer is unsigned, and the
  connector drops it as `answer-unsigned`: it doesn't keep the file for a later run as §4.4.3
  says for 429, and learns nothing of a file over the limit.
- **Remedy:** the connector protocol marks `limit` and `too-large` unsigned, as the link
  protocol marks `limit`, and says the connector acts on an unsigned `locked`, `limit` or
  `too-large` and on nothing else unsigned.

<a id="cr3-139"></a>

### CR3-139: A MoolaKata restored from a backup older than the pairing makes the connector delete every credential, where a link in the same case is only marked broken

*Work pad: D3-12 in [`docs-3-protocols.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-3-protocols.md).*

- **Kind:** the same case specified twice, differently.
- **Req:** S18.4.4, S11.6; `connector-protocol.md` §4.1.4, §7; `link-protocol.md` §7;
  `operations.md` §6.
- **Where:**
  - `connector-protocol.md:160–162`: "`moolakata-connector unpair` forgets it and deletes every
    credential it held for it, as a signed `not-paired` answer from MoolaKata makes it do";
    `:327`: "`not-paired` | the key isn't the paired connector's, or the pairing has ended".
  - `link-protocol.md:264–265`: "A `not-linked` answer (a peer restored from an older backup, say)
    only marks the link broken and alerts admins (S11.6)."
- **What goes wrong:** an admin pairs a connector on Monday evening; Tuesday's disk failure is
  restored from Monday's 02:30 backup (operations §6.2), which holds no pairing. The
  connector's next report gets a signed `not-paired`, and it deletes every Connection's
  credentials and its sealing key: every owner enters every sign-in again, and Schwab's
  sign-in too. One code serves both "this server ended our pairing" and "this server doesn't
  know you", and only the first should destroy anything; the link protocol makes exactly that
  distinction.
- **Remedy:** two codes: `unpaired` (the pairing this server ended: delete, as now) and
  `not-paired` (an unknown key: stop, alert on the connector's page, keep the vault until
  `unpair` or a new pairing).
- **Alternatives:** keep one code, and have operations §6 say that restoring a backup older
  than the pairing costs every credential.

<a id="cr3-140"></a>

### CR3-140: OFX Direct Connect's `days` setting, which S18.9.1 names, is missing from the driver's settings

*Work pad: D3-13 in [`docs-3-protocols.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-3-protocols.md).*

- **Kind:** contradiction with a requirement.
- **Req:** S18.9.1; `connector-protocol.md` §6.
- **Where:**
  - `S18-connector.md:351–352`: "it asks for the last 45 days (the Connection's `days`), or 90
    while no account matches the Connection's account number".
  - `connector-protocol.md:301`: `ofx-direct` settings: "`url`, `org`, `fid`, `accountType` (…),
    `accountId`, and `bankId` (the routing number) for a bank account".
- **What goes wrong:** the settings that cross in the report's answer have no `days`; either
  the driver can't honour a Connection's own period, or `days` travels unlisted.
- **Remedy:** §6 adds `days` (45 by default) to `ofx-direct`'s settings, or S18.9.1 drops "(the
  Connection's `days`)".

<a id="cr3-141"></a>

### CR3-141: Which address the pairing code carries: "MoolaKata's address", the public URL, or "the public URL, or the link address"

*Work pad: D3-14 in [`docs-3-protocols.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-3-protocols.md).*

- **Kind:** behaviour named and never specified.
- **Req:** S18.4.1, S11.2, S16.9; `connector-protocol.md` §1.1, §4.1; `operations.md` §8;
  `DEPLOY.md` §8.3.
- **Where:**
  - `connector-protocol.md:42–43`: "to `/connector/v1/` on MoolaKata's address, the one in the
    pairing code"; `:137–138`: `{"a": address, …}`; `S18-connector.md:167`: "**pairing code**: MoolaKata's address".
  - `operations.md:350–351`: "The connector reaches MoolaKata at the address in the code (the
    public URL, or the link address)".
  - `DEPLOY.md:475–477`: "**Settings › General › Public URL** must be the address the connector
    reaches it at …: the pairing code carries it."
- **What goes wrong:** the link address may differ from the public URL (S11.2, S16.9), and
  operations §1.1 shows a proxy that passes only `/link/*` at the link address. A code that
  carries the link address sends the connector's calls to a proxy that answers `/connector/v1/`
  with 404, and the pairing fails with `not-moolakata`.
- **Remedy:** S18.4.1 and the protocol say the code carries the public URL, and operations §8
  step 5 drops "or the link address".
- **Alternatives:** a connector address of its own in Settings › Connector, defaulting to the
  public URL.

  - `S16-settings.md:81–84`: "**Network**: the address ranges allowed to reach each path, one

- **What goes wrong:** S16.14 names three lists by path (the app, linked servers, the
  connector); the documents name one field, "Allowed to connect", that no section defines. A

  say "Settings › Network, the connector's ranges".

<a id="cr3-143"></a>

### CR3-143: operations.md and DEPLOY.md differ on the VM's memory, what to empty after a stolen welcome, a restore's path, and the health states

*Work pad: D3-16 in [`docs-3-protocols.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-3-protocols.md).*

- **Kind:** the same thing specified twice, differently.
- **Req:** S13.6, S15.4.4, S17.8; `operations.md` §2, §2.1, §3, §5; `DEPLOY.md` §1, §3.1, §4.3,
  §7.
- **Where:**
  1. Memory: `DEPLOY.md:18–19`: "**2 GB of memory** for MoolaKata (1 GB with receipt reading
     turned off"; `operations.md:112–113`: "Give the VM 2 GB of memory, or 1 GB with receipt
     reading turned off"; but `DEPLOY.md:89–90`: "4 GB of memory (MoolaKata needs 2 GB; the rest
     leaves the receipt reader room)", which makes 2 GB the need without the reader.
  2. A welcome someone else finished: `operations.md:98–100`: "its `data/` emptied … and started
     again"; `DEPLOY.md:373–375`: "empty its `data` and `backups` folders … Remove everything in
     them".
  3. Going back: `operations.md:162–163`: "restore the copy made before the upgrade with `db
     restore data/pre-upgrade/<copy>`", a host path, where §6.1 (`:239`) runs `db restore` in
     the container with container paths (`/var/backups/moolakata/<backup>`); the copy is at
     `/var/lib/moolakata/pre-upgrade/<copy>` there.
  4. Health: `operations.md:196–200` lists `ok`, `re-encrypting`, `locked`, `uninitialised`;
     `DEPLOY.md:297–298`: "`uninitialised`, `locked` or `ok`"; S13.6 (`S13-architecture.md:71–72`)
     also answers `starting` "between the unlock and readiness".
- **What goes wrong:** (1) a reader sizes a VM at 2 GB from one page and 4 GB from the other;
  (3) the command as written finds no file in the container; (4) an uptime monitor set up from
  either page meets a `starting` neither names.
- **Remedy:** one memory figure with its reason in both; one list of what to empty (`data` and
  `backups`, with the reason DEPLOY gives); the container path in §3; `starting` in both lists.

<a id="cr3-144"></a>

### CR3-144: S17.8.2's "no other tag … and none that moves" against the per-architecture tags the release pushes, keeps and overwrites

*Work pad: D3-17 in [`docs-3-protocols.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-3-protocols.md).*

- **Kind:** contradiction with a requirement.
- **Req:** S17.8.2; `RELEASE.md` §2, §6, §11; `GENESIS.md` §4.
- **Where:**
  - `S17-implementation-08-packaging.md:33–34`: "tagged with the version, one tag for both
    architectures. There is no other tag, no `latest` and none that moves".
  - `RELEASE.md:70–71`: "Each image gets exactly its version's tag on both registries (`0.2.0`),
    plus the build's per-architecture tags on ghcr.io"; `:296–297`: "pushes its images as
    `0.2.0-amd64` or `0.2.0-arm64`"; `:463–464`: "The next run overwrites the per-architecture
    tags".
  - `GENESIS.md:151–152`: "Each package keeps `0.1.0`, `0.1.0-amd64` and `0.1.0-arm64`."
- **What goes wrong:** the requirement says one tag; the published packages carry three, and
  after a failed build the `-amd64`/`-arm64` tags move, which S17.8.2 says no tag does. A check
  written from S17.8.2 (one tag per version) fails on every release.
- **Remedy:** S17.8.2 names the per-architecture tags as the build's own, on ghcr.io only, not
  for installs, and moved only before a release is published.
- **Alternatives:** the joining job deletes them once the joined tag is attested.

<a id="cr3-145"></a>

### CR3-145: dependencies.md puts the attestation in "the release's last job", and RELEASE.md §12's list of what `setup` checks leaves out the `main` ruleset

*Work pad: D3-18 in [`docs-3-protocols.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-3-protocols.md).*

- **Kind:** contradiction within the block.
- **Req:** S17.10.3; `dependencies.md` (GitHub Actions), `RELEASE.md` §6, §12, `GENESIS.md` §7.
- **Where:**
  - `dependencies.md:119`: "**egress** to Sigstore and the registry, by design, in the release's
    last job only, which alone holds `id-token: write`".
  - `RELEASE.md:318–319`: "From 0.2.0 on, the Docker Hub copy and the draft follow the joining
    job" (the job that attests, `:298–299`).
  - `RELEASE.md:482–485`: "`web/release.sh setup` checks everything `GENESIS.md` set up:
    immutable releases, the tag ruleset and its bypass list, the `docker-hub` environment …, and
    both registries' repositories being public (GENESIS.md §7)"; `GENESIS.md:296–297` (§7
    item 3): "the `main` branch's ruleset: active, on `main`, taking changes only through pull
    requests whose `check` passed".
- **What goes wrong:** a reader checking the workflow's permissions against the register looks
  for `id-token: write` on the last job (the draft), which must not have it. RELEASE's list
  reads as complete and omits a check `setup` makes.
- **Remedy:** "in the joining and attesting job only"; RELEASE §12 adds the `main` ruleset, or
  says "everything GENESIS.md §7 lists".

    `LotPicks.saleId`"; `:264` (keeps ref): "`Lot.transactionId`, `Lot.instrumentId`;
    `LotPicks.saleId`".

  say a key may be both *deleted with* and *keeps ref* (deleted with its parent, and ending no
  earlier than it).
