  (`refusing to start:`)"); `operations.md` §5.1 agrees with S13.6.
- **Where:**
  - `operations.md:163–165`: "An older release refuses a database that a newer one has
    migrated: it says so in a `refusing to start:` line (§5.1)."
  - `operations.md:225–227`: "A server that started but stays locked after the phrase was
    entered says why in its log (`refusing to unlock:`): a database from a newer MoolaKata".
  - `S13-architecture.md:69–71`: "What the phrase then cannot open (a database from a newer
    release) is refused at the unlock, with the reason in the log, and the server stays
    locked."
- **What goes wrong:** the database is SQLCipher, so its revision can only be read after the
  unlock; S13.6 is the one the code can follow. An operator going back a release per §3 looks
  for a `refusing to start:` line and a container in `Restarting`, and finds a healthy locked
  server instead.
- **Remedy:** `operations.md` §3 says "it refuses to unlock, with a `refusing to unlock:` line
  (§5.1)".
- **Units:** 09 web-ui-architecture (S13.6), 06 receipts-storage (S14.1.3's migrations).

<a id="cr3-155"></a>

### CR3-155: `schema.md` keeps sign-in and access-key attempt counters as rows; S13.7.5.5 holds every counter in memory

*Work pad: DC-9 in [`docs-compare.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-compare.md).*

- **Kind:** the same thing specified twice, differently (storage).
- **Ids:** S13.7.5.5 (block 2 digest: "rate counters in memory; a restart clears them") and
  `schema.md` §4.4.4 (block 3 digest: "work queues and counters (… sign-in and access-key
  attempt counters …)").
- **Where:**
  - `S13-architecture.md:108–110`: "sign-in, two-step codes, re-authentication, … and the
    unlock (S15.4.3): 10 failures in 15 minutes lock **the address they came from**";
    `:115`: "linked copies' access keys: S11.9.3.3"; `:122`: "The counters are held in memory:
    a restart clears them."
  - `schema.md:191–193`: "**work queues and counters**: receipt-reading jobs, emails waiting
    to go out …, sign-in and access-key attempt counters, the background tasks' last runs, and
    the live stream's state".
- **What goes wrong:** one says the lockouts live in a table (they survive a restart, and are
  in every backup), the other that a restart clears them. The unlock's own counter can't be in
  the database at all, since the database is closed while locked (D2-17's side of it).
- **Remedy:** `schema.md` §4.4.4 drops the attempt counters (S13.7.5.5 holds them in memory),
  or S13.7.5.5 names which counters are kept in the database and why.
- **Units:** 09 web-ui-architecture (S13.7.5), 08 linked-servers (S11.9.3.3), 06
  receipts-storage (the store's tables).

<a id="cr3-156"></a>

### CR3-156: the link protocol keeps remote usernames up to 64 and display names up to 120; S10.1.1's limits (32, 80) govern the same `User` rows

*Work pad: DC-10 in [`docs-compare.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-compare.md).*

- **Kind:** limit given differently.
- **Ids:** S10.1.1, S10.3.3.1 (block 2 digest: "username 2–32 chars …; display name ≤ 80";
  "a remote user is a `User` row with `linkId` set") and `link-protocol.md` §4.2 (block 3
  digest: "display name ≤ 120 chars, username ≤ 64, sex other than `female`/`male` as
  `male`").
- **Where:**
  - `S10-users-and-sharing.md:7–10`: "A username is 2 to 32 lowercase letters, digits, dots,
    dashes or underscores, beginning with a letter or digit; … A display name is at most 80
    characters".
  - `S10-users-and-sharing.md:108–111`: "**A remote user is a `User` record** with its
    `linkId` set … and its fields come only from its server (S11.3)."
  - `link-protocol.md:128–130`: "It keeps a display name of up to 120 characters and a
    username of up to 64, and a sex other than `female` or `male` as `male`".
- **What goes wrong:** whichever the code's `User` checks enforce, one document is wrong: a
  `CHECK` or validator built from S10.1.1 refuses a 33–64 character remote username the
  protocol says to keep, and a whole directory exchange may fail on one row. "Keeps … up to"
  also doesn't say whether a longer one is cut or refused.
- **Remedy:** S10.3.3.1 says a remote user's fields follow `link-protocol.md` §4.2, not
  S10.1.1, and §4.2 says a longer value is cut to the limit (or the row skipped, and which).
- **Units:** 07 users-sharing (S10), 08 linked-servers.

<a id="cr3-157"></a>

### CR3-157: S11.1.2 says the joining server sends "B's own address and key … and nothing else"; the protocol's `join` also carries its certificate's fingerprint

*Work pad: DC-11 in [`docs-compare.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-compare.md).*

- **Kind:** the same thing specified twice, differently.
- **Ids:** S11.1.2 (block 2 digest: "sends B's address and key signed with it, nothing else")
  and `link-protocol.md` §4.1.2 (block 3 digest: "`join` with `{code, address, t}` (its own
  cert fingerprint when own)").
- **Where:**
  - `S11-linked-servers.md:13–15`: "sends B's own address and key, signed with it, and
    nothing else: no user list crosses before A's admin approves."
  - `link-protocol.md:103–104`: "It then calls `join`, signed by its own key, with
    `{code, address, t}`, `t` being its own certificate key's fingerprint when its certificate
    is its own".
- **What goes wrong:** A needs `t` to verify B's own certificate when A calls B (S11.1.3), so
  the protocol is right; S11.1.2's "nothing else" reads as forbidding it, and a unit checking
  the code against S11.1.2 would flag a correct field.
- **Remedy:** S11.1.2: "sends B's own address and key, and its certificate's fingerprint when
  the certificate is its own, signed with the key; no user list crosses before A's admin
  approves."
- **Units:** 08 linked-servers.

<a id="cr3-158"></a>

### CR3-158: S9.2 encrypts local receipts in S11.9.6's format, whose key label and associated data are written only for links

*Work pad: DC-12 in [`docs-compare.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-compare.md).*

- **Kind:** behaviour named and never specified, across blocks. Not withheld: an unstated label is a gap in the description, not a
  weakness of the format.
- **Ids:** S9.2 (block 1 digest: "streaming format of S11.9.6 under the receipts key
  (S15.3.2), file id as associated data") and S11.9.6 (block 2 digest: "label
  `moolakata link receipt` or `moolakata link change`; … associated data names link, receipt
  or change, and chunk number").
- **Where:**
  - `S9-receipts.md:14–16`: "Files use the streaming format of S11.9.6 under the receipts key
    (S15.3.2), with the file's id as what the associated data names".
  - `S11-linked-servers.md:210–212`: "**Key**: HKDF-SHA256 over the X25519 exchange between
    the one-time key and the copy's key, with the salt, under the label `moolakata link
    receipt` or `moolakata link change`."
  - `S11-linked-servers.md:215–216`: "The associated data names the link, the receipt or
    change, and the chunk's number".
- **What goes wrong:** a household's own receipt has no link and no copy key; which label it
  is derived under, and what its associated data holds besides the file's id (the chunk's
  number?), is left to the code. Two readers of S9.2 could build files the other can't open,
  and a re-encryption or restore tool written later has nothing to check against.
- **Remedy:** S11.9.6 gives the local case: the receipts key in place of the copy's, its own
  label (`moolakata receipt`), and associated data of the file's id and the chunk's number.
- **Units:** 06 receipts-storage (S9.2), 10 encryption-settings (S15.3.2), 08 linked-servers
  (S11.9.6).

<a id="cr3-159"></a>

### CR3-159: a connector's file is limited to "the upload size in Settings", and Settings has two

*Work pad: DC-13 in [`docs-compare.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-compare.md).*

- **Kind:** limit given differently / unspecified. Related to D2-16 (statements up to 200 MB against 100 MB in flight), not the same.
- **Ids:** `connector-protocol.md` §1.4 (block 3 digest: "a file post multipart, the file ≤
  the upload size in Settings (S13.7.6)"), S13.7.6, S16.8 and S16.10 (block 2 digest:
  "Imports: statement file types and size (25 MB default, 1–200 MB)"; "Receipts: size limit
  25 MB (1–100)").
- **Where:**
  - `connector-protocol.md:54–55`: "a file post is multipart, the file up to the upload size
    in Settings (S13.7.6)."
  - `S13-architecture.md:124`: "uploads up to the size in Settings (25 MB by default)".
  - `S16-settings.md:38–39`: "the file types and size accepted for statement import (25 MB by
    default, 1 to 200)"; `:46`: "**Receipts**: the size limit (25 MB by default, 1 to 100)".
- **What goes wrong:** the connector posts statements (OFX, CSV, PDF), so S16.8's limit is the
  natural one, but S13.7.6 and the protocol name a single "size in Settings"; a PDF statement

- **Remedy:** S13.7.6 names both ("a receipt up to S16.10's size, a statement or other import
  file up to S16.8's"), and `connector-protocol.md` §1.4 and §7's `too-large` cite S16.8.

### CR3-164: S13.8.6 says secrets have no version; `schema.md` gives every table with an `id` one

*Work pad: DC-18 in [`docs-compare.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-compare.md).*

  (block 3 digest: "every table with an `id` has `version` …, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`"; §3.1
  has `Secr`).
- **Where:**
  - `S13-architecture.md:227–229`: "Pairs that only link two records (a receipt and a
    transaction, an account counted in someone's totals) and secrets, which are replaced
    outright, have no version."
  - `schema.md:50–52`: "**Record fields.** Each table with an `id` also has `version` (an
    integer that changes with every save, which the API's `If-Match` compares, requirements
    S13.8.6)".
- **What goes wrong:** the pairs are many-to-many tables with no `id`, so they agree; the
  secret is a `Secr` row with an `id`, so `schema.md`'s test (§5) requires a `version` the API
  section says it lacks. The likely meaning (the column exists, the API asks no `If-Match` for
  a secret) is said by neither.
- **Remedy:** S13.8.6: "… and secrets, which are replaced outright, are changed without
  `If-Match`."
- **Units:** 09 web-ui-architecture (S13.8.6), 10 encryption-settings (S15.3.3's secrets), 06
  receipts-storage (the store's tables).

  S14.9.3 (the reason `_raw_dates` exists).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/importers/ofx.py:117`, `:130`, `:177`, `:186`:
  ```text
  out.coveredFrom, out.coveredTo = _day(tl.dtstart), _day(tl.dtend)
  ...
  out.balances.append((_day(st.ledgerbal.dtasof), st.ledgerbal.balamt))
  ...
  asOf=_day(pos.dtpriceasof) or _day(st.dtasof),
  ```
  `_day` takes ofxtools' UTC datetime's date; only the rows' dates are read raw (`_raw_dates`,
  `:59-71`).
- **What goes wrong:** (1) `DTEND` and `DTASOF` of `20260930200000[-5:EST]` become Oct 1 while
  the Sep 30 row stays Sep 30 (run): the anchor is compared with the balance at the end of Oct 1,
  so Oct 1 rows from the next file make it a mismatch, and the coverage and the statement
  expected end a day late (a monthly account's next is named *the November statement*). (2) An
  `INVPOS`'s `DTPRICEASOF` is the date of its price, often the previous close, while its
  `UNITS` are as of the statement's `DTASOF`: a Sep 30 buy in the same file shows as a holdings
  difference against Sep 29 (run with `DTPRICEASOF` moved to Sep 29: the check is dated Sep 29).
- **Run:** yes, `u01/ofxdates.py` (the fixtures with an evening EST time and an earlier price
  date).
- **Remedy:** read `DTSTART`, `DTEND`, `LEDGERBAL`'s and `INVSTMTRS`'s `DTASOF` as the
  institution wrote them, as the rows are; date positions by the statement's `DTASOF`.

*Work pad: U01-8 in [`unit-01-accounts-imports.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-01-accounts-imports.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S1.11.2 ("a newest-first file, told by its dates, is read from the
  bottom up").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/importers/csvfile.py:303-304`:
  ```text
  if len(out.rows) > 1 and out.rows[0].date > out.rows[-1].date:
      out.rows.reverse()
  ```
  and `ledger/balances.py:25-27` (a day's balance is its last row's, in creation order).
- **What goes wrong:** a file whose rows all fall on one day (a day's download) can't be told by
  its dates, so a newest-first one is kept in file order and the day's anchor is its oldest
  row's running balance: a mismatch on a balance that is right, with the first parting named on
  a row that is fine.
- **Run:** no; reading alone.
- **Remedy:** when the dates tie, tell the order by the running balance (each row's balance less
  its amount is the next row's balance in a newest-first file).

*Work pad: U01-12 in [`unit-01-accounts-imports.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-01-accounts-imports.md).*

- **Kind:** bug (a resource held). **Req:** S13.7.6 ("at most 100 MB of uploads in flight per
  user … an upload is held in memory"), S13.7.5.3 ("uploads: 60 files an hour per user"), S16.8
  (imports up to 200 MB).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/imports.py:46, 120-129`:
  ```text
  HELD_MINUTES = 30
  ...
  _held[key] = Held(
      data, account_id, user_id, name, now + dt.timedelta(minutes=HELD_MINUTES), **extra
  )
  ```
  called for every CSV without a saved mapping (`:165`), every statement to check (`:408, 423,
  468, 534, 581`) and every statement to keep (`:1081`); entries go only when used or when a
  later `_hold` sweeps the expired ones.
- **What goes wrong:** the in-flight limit (`middleware.py:263`) counts a body only while it is
  received; once the job holds it, it counts against nothing. One user's 60 uploads an hour,
  never confirmed, keep about 30 files in memory at a time: 750 MB at the default 25 MB, 6 GB at
  S16.8's 200 MB, on the same process that serves every household member (with the read lines and
  parse of each statement besides).
- **Run:** no; reading.

*Work pad: U01-15 in [`unit-01-accounts-imports.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-01-accounts-imports.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S1.5, S2.1 ("Instruments are kept once per server"), S4.3
  (prices by class).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/store/repos/ledger.py:157-168` (found by symbol whatever its
  class, and given the file's CUSIP when it has none) and `:296-297`; `store/tables/accounts.py:170`
  `unique_live(Instrument, "symbol")`.
- **What goes wrong:** tickers are shared between coins and listed shares: SOL (Solana, and
  ReneSola on the NYSE), LINK, COMP, FET. Case: Alice's Coinbase CSV brings *SOL* first, made
  `crypto`; Bob's Schwab OFX then buys ReneSola as `SOL` with its CUSIP: `instrument()` finds the
  coin by symbol, gives it the CUSIP, and Bob's 100 shares are priced at Solana's close (about
  $150 against $2). In the other order Alice's coins are priced as the stock. Nothing warns.

  security with a listed security, CUSIP first); keep a coin's symbol in its own namespace (as

### CR3-175: two OFX rows that share a FITID take the later one's date

*Work pad: U01-16 in [`unit-01-accounts-imports.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-01-accounts-imports.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure (dates). **Req:** S1.3.2 ("An id a file repeats is another row, kept
  beside the first"), S14.9.3.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/importers/ofx.py:62-71`:
  ```text
  out: dict[str, dt.date] = {}
  for el in root.iter():
      ...
      if fitid and raw and len(raw.strip()) >= 8:
          r = raw.strip()
          out[fitid] = dt.date(int(r[:4]), int(r[4:6]), int(r[6:8]))
  ```
  and `:121` / `:221` / `:229`, `dates.get(t.fitid) or …`.
- **What goes wrong:** the raw dates are keyed by FITID, so when a bank repeats a FITID (the case
  B19 made two rows of), every row with it takes the last one's date. Case: FITID `1001` on a
  Sep 3 $40 debit and a Sep 9 $40 debit: both are saved on Sep 9, the account's balance from
  Sep 3 to Sep 8 is $40 too high (and any check in those days with it), and the pair looks like
  a same-day duplicate the person is invited to delete.
- **Run:** no; reading.
- **Remedy:** key the raw dates by the element's position (the n-th `STMTTRN`/`INVTRAN`), or
  read each row's date from its own element.

<a id="cr3-176"></a>

### CR3-176: an address a key derives, typed as well and then taken out, is never watched again

*Work pad: U02-2 in [`unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S1.6.1 ("addresses derived … with a gap limit"), S1.6.1.2, S1.6.1.3.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/bitcoin/wallet.py:110-116`:
  ```text
  last_used = max((r.idx for r in rows if r.used), default=-1)
  top = max((r.idx for r in rows), default=-1)
  for i in range(top + 1, last_used + k.gapLimit + 1):
      at = address(chain, i)
      if at in taken:
          continue
  ```
  An index skipped because another key (or a typed address) holds it is passed over for good:
  later calls start at `top + 1`.
- **What goes wrong:** case: the owner pastes a zpub with its first receive address (the review
  2 fix B35 for that case keeps it to one row, held by the typed address). Later they tidy the
  list down to the zpub alone. The typed address's row goes with it, and the zpub's receive 0
  is never derived again: the wallet watches 39 addresses, not 40, and coins later sent to its
  first address are never seen. (Two keys that derive all the same addresses recover, since the
  second holds no rows and starts again at 0; it is a key that holds rows on both sides of the
  skipped index that never goes back.) The used flag of the shared address counts only for the
  row's key, so the other key's gap is counted from a lower index.
- **Run:** yes, a scratch test (`tmp/u02/t/test_u02_wallets.py::
  test_a_typed_address_taken_out_leaves_the_keys_own_address_unwatched`): after the second
  save, receive 0 is not among the watched addresses (`watched == 39`).
- **Remedy:** `extend` derives every index from 0 to `last_used + gapLimit` and adds what is
  missing (a set difference against the key's rows), rather than starting at `top + 1`; and
  `last_used` reads the used flag of any row with that address in the account.
- **Alternatives:** keep one row per key and address (unique on key, address) and dedupe only
  when reading, so each key's chain is whole.

<a id="cr3-177"></a>

### CR3-177: an Electrum read that spans a new block takes the server's header notice for an answer

*Work pad: U02-3 in [`unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S1.6.2.3 (an Electrum server's history read over TCP), S1.6.2.5.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/bitcoin/sources.py:274` subscribes to headers in the
  middle of a read:
  ```text
  tip = self.call("blockchain.headers.subscribe")["height"] if txs else 0
  ```
  and `:222-242` takes the next line as the answer to whatever was asked, never checking its
  `id`:
  ```text
  line, self.buf = self.buf.split(b"\n", 1)
  out = json.loads(line, parse_float=Decimal)
  if out.get("error"):
      ...
  return out.get("result")
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** after `blockchain.headers.subscribe`, an Electrum server (Fulcrum,
  electrs) pushes a `{"method": "blockchain.headers.subscribe", "params": […]}` line whenever a
  block is found. The read then asks for every transaction and every input's previous
  transaction one call at a time, which for a long-used wallet is hundreds of round trips; a
  block found meanwhile (one every ten minutes on average) is read as the answer to the next
  `blockchain.transaction.get` or `blockchain.block.header`, whose `result` is `None`, and
  `bytes.fromhex(None)` raises `TypeError`. The wallet's read fails for that hour ("a wallet
  couldn't be read; the server's log says why" from `sync_all`, or a 500 from **Check now**,
  which catches only `SourceError`), and the larger the wallet the likelier.
- **Run:** no, reading alone (no Electrum server here); the protocol's notifications are as the
  Electrum protocol documents them.
- **Remedy:** match each answer to its request by `id`, skipping lines with a `method` and no
  `id`; or read the tip with `blockchain.headers.subscribe` on a connection of its own, or
  before the history and never again on the same one.
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

<a id="cr3-178"></a>

### CR3-178: a plan that rounds the payment up still expects PMI after it has ended (B21's remainder)

*Work pad: U02-5 in [`unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S6.10.5 (whether actual overpayments are "ahead of it or
  behind"), S1.9.5 (PMI ends at 78%); review 2's B21 ("A payoff plan's extra payments still
  add the full PMI after it has ended"), marked done.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/loans/payoff.py:158-169` calls the extras without the
  balance:
  ```text
  extras = _Extras(dataclasses.replace(plan, lumps=[x for x in plan.lumps if x[0] > since]), t)
  ...
          total += extras(day, pi)
  ```
  so `:81` takes the full PMI:
  ```text
  pmi = self.terms.pmi if owed is None else self.terms.pmi_due(owed)
  ```
  `ui/loans.py:822` uses it for the plan being followed.
- **What goes wrong:** B21's fix reached the planner's schedule (`schedule` passes the balance)
  but not *the plan I'm following*. Case: a $300,000 loan at 6%, escrow $300, PMI $90 that has
  ended (78% of the original value passed), following "round each payment up to $100" for five
  payments: the schedule rounds $2,098.65 up, $1.35 a month, $6.75 in all; the expected figure
  rounds $2,188.65 up, $11.35 a month, $56.75. A borrower who pays exactly the plan is shown
  $50 behind it, and further behind every month.
- **Run:** yes, a scratch script (`tmp/u02/loans_check.py`, case C): `expected_extra 56.75`
  against the schedule's `6.75` over the same months.
- **Remedy:** `expected_extra` works out the balance on each payment day (from the history, or
  the schedule's rows) and passes it, so `_Extras` charges PMI only while `pmi_due`; or take the
  expected figure from the planned schedule's own `overpayment` on the days since.
- **Alternatives:** none needed. A test like `test_rounding_up_counts_pmi_only_while_it_is_due`
  for the followed plan.

<a id="cr3-179"></a>

### CR3-179: after a rate reset or a recast, one extra payment makes every later payment count as an overpayment

*Work pad: U02-6 in [`unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S1.9.3 ("Principal beyond the schedule is recorded as an
  overpayment"), S1.9.5 (the screen), S6.10.5 (actual overpayments since a plan was chosen).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/loans/__init__.py:276-283`:
  ```text
  recast = [d for d, _ in t.recasts if d <= day]
  reset = [d for d, _ in t.resets if d <= day]
  if recast or reset:
      # Re-amortized from then on over what is left of the term: the end stays.
      return level_payment(owed, t.rate_on(day), max(1, _months_between(day, t.end) + 1))
  ```
  `_scheduled_principal` (`:270-273`) calls it for every past payment with that payment's own
  balance, so after any reset or recast the scheduled payment is re-amortized every month.
- **What goes wrong:** the servicer re-amortizes at the reset (or recast) and then keeps the
  payment level until the next one; overpayments shorten the loan (as `schedule` itself says,
  `:341-342`). Here, once a reset date has passed, an extra payment lowers the "scheduled"
  payment of each later month, so the borrower's unchanged level payment is counted as an
  overpayment again every month. Case: $300,000 at 6% from Jan 2025, an ARM reset to 7% on Jan
  1 2026 (level $1,991.99 from then), the borrower pays that, plus $10,000 once on Mar 1 2026.
  By Jan 2027 the loan's **Overpaid** is $10,694.16, not $10,000: each month after March shows
  an overpayment of $67–69 that was never made, and a followed plan shows the borrower ahead by
  that much.
- **Run:** yes, a scratch script (`tmp/u02/loans_check.py`, case D).
- **Remedy:** `_pi_payment` re-amortizes once, at the latest reset or recast on or before the
  day, from the balance owed on that day, and keeps that payment until the next change, as
  `schedule` does ahead; the past rows' scheduled principal uses the same.
- **Alternatives:** keep the level payment worked out at each change on the loan (a computed
  list beside `resets` and `recasts`).

*Work pad: U02-7 in [`unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S1.9.7.1 ("The loan names the categories; by default they are the
  shipped *Loan payments › Interest*, *Escrow*, *PMI and fees* and *Principal*").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/store/tables/loans.py:30-33` keeps one category, for
  *Whole* only:
  ```text
  Column("budgetCounting", Text, nullable=False, server_default=text("'split'")),
  Column("budgetCategoryId", Text, ForeignKey("Category.id")),
  ```
  `ui/loans.py:403` drops it unless whole (`"budgetCategoryId": body.budgetCategoryId if
  body.budgetCounting == "whole" else None`), `web/ui/components/loan.js:186-195` shows a
  category picker only for *whole*, and `budgets/__init__.py:150-157` (`LOAN_PARTS`) maps each
  part to a fixed shipped key.
- **What goes wrong:** a household that budgets its mortgage's escrow under *Housing › Property
  tax* and *Insurance*, or its principal under a savings category of its own, can't: a split
  payment always lands in *Loan payments › …*. The requirement's "the loan names the
  categories" is not built. (The shipped tree also differs a little in wording: *PMI* and
  *Fees* are two categories, where S1.9.7.1 names one, *PMI and fees*.)
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** a category per part on the loan (interest, escrow, PMI and fees, principal),
  defaulting to the shipped ones, offered in the terms form when *Split* is chosen, and used by
  `_count_loan_payment`.
- **Alternatives:** S1.9.7.1 says the split's categories are the shipped ones, which a user
  may rename but not change per loan, and names *PMI* and *Fees* apart; the mockup to match.

*Work pad: U02-8 in [`unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md).*

- **Kind:** not built. **Req:** S1.9.1 ("an ARM's adjustment dates, caps and the rate after
  each reset, typed when known"), S1.9.6 ("mortgage interest, points and PMI (Form 1098) …
  appear in Taxes").

  ```text
  class Reset(Model):

      rate: ResetRate
  ```
  and `TermsIn` (`:53-70`) has no caps and no points; the Taxes panel's loan row
  (`taxes/panels.py:790-797`) carries `interest`, `pmi` and `propertyTax`, no points. Nothing in
  `loans/`, `ui/loans.py` or `components/loan.js` names either (searched: `cap`, `points`).
- **What goes wrong:** an ARM's periodic and lifetime caps can't be typed, so an assumed future

### CR3-182: a 529's rollover to a Roth IRA is counted as a distribution not covered by expenses

*Work pad: U02-9 in [`unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S1.8.3 ("Each year shows distributions (from Form 1099-Q)
  against the qualified expenses, and the part not covered, whose earnings may be taxable
  (S8.10)"), S1.8.4.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/education/__init__.py:178-194`:
  ```text
  for t in s.scalars(
      select(Transaction).where(
          Transaction.accountId == account.id,
          Transaction.deleted == false(),
          Transaction.amount < 0,
          Transaction.kind.not_in(NOT_MONEY_IN_OR_OUT),
          Transaction.instrumentId.is_(None),
      )
  ):
      taken[t.date.year] += -t.amount
  ...
  return [WithdrawalYear(y, taken[y], spent[y], max(taken[y] - spent[y], ZERO)) for y in years]
  ```
  while `rollover()` (`:225-236`) recognises the same transfers to a `roth-ira` as rollovers.
- **What goes wrong:** case: a 529 open 16 years transfers $7,000 to the beneficiary's Roth IRA
  in 2026 (the S1.8.4 rollover the screen proposes), and pays no qualified expenses that year.
  The 529's Withdrawals and the Taxes education panel (`taxes/panels.py:713`) show 2026:
  distributions $7,000, **not covered $7,000**, "whose earnings may be taxable" with S8.10's
  10% additional tax, though a rollover within the limit is neither. A 529-to-529 rollover (to
  a sibling's plan) is miscounted the same way.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** leave transfers to a `roth-ira` (and to another `529`) out of `notCovered`; show
  them on their own line ("rolled over") beside the distributions.
- **Alternatives:** count them in `distributions` (1099-Q reports them) and in a covered column
  of their own, so the year still adds up to the 1099-Q.

<a id="cr3-183"></a>

### CR3-183: a five-year election above five times the exclusion is spread over five years, not counted in the first

*Work pad: U02-11 in [`unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S1.8.2 ("A five-year election … spreads one contribution over
  five years of exclusions and is shown that way"), S7.8.7, S8.11.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/education/__init__.py:151-156`:
  ```text
  if r.fiveYear:
      for k in range(5):
          counted[(r.contributor, r.date.year + k)] += (r.amount / 5).quantize(
              Decimal("0.01")
          )
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** the election spreads at most five times the year's exclusion; anything
  above it is a gift in the year it is made. Case: a grandparent puts $100,000 into a 529 in
  2026 and elects, with an exclusion of $19,000: $95,000 is spread ($19,000 a year, none over)
  and $5,000 is a 2026 gift beyond it, so 2026 is $24,000, over by $5,000, and 2027–2030 are at
  the exclusion. The code counts $20,000 in each of the five years and flags every one of them
  over by $1,000 (five Form 709 years where the election's one return covers it). The fifths
  are also each rounded, so five of them can miss the contribution by a few cents.
- **Run:** no, reading alone (the arithmetic is the quoted lines').
- **Remedy:** spread `min(amount, 5 × the contribution year's exclusion)` over the five years
  and count the rest in the first; put any cent left by rounding in the first year.
- **Alternatives:** refuse an election above five times the exclusion and say why.

*Work pad: U02-12 in [`unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S1.10.1 (how an item was acquired: "bought, a gift,
  inherited"; "an inherited item's value at death"), S1.10.5 (a sale realizes a gain on Form
  8949), S8.13.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/taxes/panels.py:318-336` (the Form 8949 rows for items,
  built on this unit's `Item`):
  ```text

  ...
              longTerm=long_term,
              box="F" if long_term else "C",
  ```
  `it.acquiredHow` (`store/tables/assets.py:18`, checked `bought`, `gift`, `inherited`) is
  never read for the holding period.
- **What goes wrong:** property from a decedent is held long-term whatever its holding period
  (IRC §1223(9)). Case: a coin collection inherited on 2026-03-01 at a stepped-up value of
  $40,000, sold 2026-09-01 for $46,000: MoolaKata files the $6,000 gain in box C, short-term,
  where it is long-term (box F, and for a collectable the 28% rate rather than ordinary income).
  A gift with a carried-over basis likewise takes the donor's holding period, which the form
  only gets if the owner types the donor's date as *date acquired*; the item dialog's guide
  (`web/ui/guides/dialog-item.md`, *Date acquired*) says the opposite: "The day it was bought,
  given or inherited. A sale's holding period starts here."
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** an item acquired `inherited` is long-term on any sale; the item form's *date
  acquired* hint says that for a gift with the donor's basis it is the donor's date (or a
  second date, *held since*, as lots have `heldFrom`). S1.10.1 or S8.13 states both.
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

### CR3-187: a short sale's loss is taken as a wash sale and adjusted, where S3.8.5.1 flags it

*Work pad: U03-8 in [`unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S3.8.5, S3.8.5.1 ("a loss on a **short sale** closed with shares
  bought within 30 days … flagged for review, never adjusted"), S3.4 (the short stock lot of an
  assignment with no shares).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ledger/lots.py:567-579`, shares bought while short close
  the short through `_cover`, whose disposal carries the buy as its `saleId`; and
  `web/server/src/moolakata/washes.py:129-132` takes every losing disposal of a stock as a
  loss sale, short covers included:
  ```text
  for d in led.replay().disposals:
      raw = d.raw_gain
      if d.instrumentId in classes and raw is not None and raw < 0 and d.saleId:
          losses.append((led.account, d))
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): short 1 XYZ 50 C for $100, assigned on 2026-08-21 with no
  XYZ held, so the account is short 100 XYZ at 50 (S3.4). Shares bought on 08-25 at 60 cover it,
  a $900 loss. Another 100 bought on 09-05 at 58 are taken as its replacement: the whole $900
  is disallowed (realized 0.00, **W** 900.00) and added to the 09-05 lot's basis, though S3.8.5.1
  says the rules are unsettled there and only the preparer decides. The Closed tab names the
  cover's *how* as "buy".
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u03/t/test_u03.py::test_a_short_sale_loss_is_adjusted_not_flagged`:
  one `WashSale` of 100 shares, $900 disallowed.
- **Remedy:** `washes._round` skips a disposal that closes a short (`d.written` on a stock, or a
  flag `short` on `Disposal`), leaving it to the S3.8.5.1 issue; `ui/positions._how` names a
  short cover *bought back*.
- **Alternatives:** none reasonable; the rule is the requirement's.

<a id="cr3-188"></a>

### CR3-188: a corporate action is checked and sized by today's holding, not by the holding on its date

*Work pad: U03-10 in [`unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S2.4, S2.4.1 ("the holding, the date, and what the action needs"),
  S2.8 (editing history is allowed), S3.10.1.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/corporate.py:42-44`:
  ```text
  held = repo.load(s, account).replay().quantity(inst.id)
  if held <= 0:
      e.append(FieldError("instrumentId", "nothing-held", "Nothing of it is held here."))
  ```
  `held` is today's quantity, and sizes the merger's cash (`:81`) and the legs' quantities
  (`:83`, `:95`). `options/adjust.py:115-122` (`open_contracts`) and `:78` (`carry`) likewise
  take the options open today for a split dated in the past. The dialog
  (`web/ui/components/corporate.js:25-29`) offers only holdings open now.
- **What goes wrong:** history entered late can't be completed. Case: XYZ spun off NEWCO on
  2026-03-02 (Form 8937: 13.65% of the basis moves); the household sold all its XYZ in May and
  records the spin-off in October while checking its 1099-B. It is refused ("Nothing of it is
  held here"), so the sold lots keep their whole basis and the NEWCO shares that arrived have
  none: the May sale's gain is understated by the basis that should have moved, and NEWCO
  shows *basis missing*. A split recorded late adjusts options opened after it (pairs that move
  nothing) and misses those open at the split and closed since, whose closing trades then
  stand against a contract never opened (*close-without-open*) while the old one stays open.
- **Run:** no; reading alone.
- **Remedy:** replay the account up to the action's date (`replay` over the transactions dated
  on or before it) for the check, the quantities and the cash, and for the options open on that
  day; the dialog lists what was held on the date chosen.
- **Alternatives:** none simpler that keeps late entry working.

### CR3-189: a statement value is never marked stale, and Positions shows no price date or staleness

*Work pad: U03-11 in [`unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S4.3 ("A statement value older than 90 days is marked stale"),
  S4.7 ("The UI shows *delayed* or *as of 4:00 PM* on every value derived from it. A price older
  than a threshold … is marked stale").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/valuation.py:108-109`, a statement's price is made with
  `stale` left at its default, `False`:
  ```text
  if h.quantity:
      out[h.instrumentId] = Price(h.marketValue / h.quantity, h.asOf, "statement")
  ```
  (`prices/refresh.py:46-47` knows the 90 days, but only for stored `Price` rows, and statement
  values are `Holding` rows). `ui/positions.py:38-59`, `OpenRow` carries `priceSource` and no
  `priceAsOf` or `stale`, and `web/ui/screens/positions.js:189-193` shows only the source's word.
- **What goes wrong:** Case: a 401(k) collective trust valued from a statement dated
  2026-03-31. In October the portfolio's Holdings tab shows it without the *stale* pill, as
  current, though the alert of `alerts.py:509` fires for the same value. On Positions › Open,
  no position shows the date of its price, and a stock whose close is a week old (a source
  failing) looks as fresh as today's.
- **Run:** no; reading alone.
- **Remedy:** `valuation.prices` sets `stale` on a statement value by `refresh.is_stale(cls,
  "statement", asOf, day)`; `OpenRow` gains `priceAsOf` and `stale`, shown as the portfolio's
  Holdings tab shows them.
- **Alternatives:** none.

### CR3-190: a period's return is shown over less than the period when the history is shorter

*Work pad: U03-12 in [`unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S5.2.2 (returns per period); the guide `portfolio-performance.md`
  ("a dash means the history doesn't go back that far").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/figures.py:280-290`:
  ```text
  started = next((d for d, v in values if v), values[0][0])
  for key in ("1M", "3M", "YTD", "1Y", "3Y", "5Y"):
      first = first_day(key, today, None)
      if first < started and key in ("3Y", "5Y"):
          out[key] = None
          continue
      window = [(d, v) for d, v in values if d >= max(first, started)]
  ```
  Only 3Y and 5Y are left out when the history is shorter; the docstring (`:275-276`) says
  "none when the history doesn't reach back that far" for all of them.
- **What goes wrong:** Case: an account opened on 2026-07-01 that has gained 8% since. On
  2026-10-13 its *1Y* (and *YTD*) return reads 8.00%, the same as its 3M, as if it were a year's
  return; a reader comparing accounts takes a 3½-month gain for a 12-month one.
- **Run:** no; reading alone.
- **Remedy:** a period that starts before the history does is `None` (a dash), as for 3Y and
  5Y; YTD may keep the partial year if S5.2.2 says so.
- **Alternatives:** keep the figure and mark it *since <date>*.

### CR3-191: the Gains tab counts a position with long- and short-term lots as all short-term

*Work pad: U03-13 in [`unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md).*

  lot, S3.1).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/positions.py:316-323`:
  ```text
  for r in open_rows:
      if r.unrealized is None:
          continue
      ...
      g = un.setdefault(key, [ZERO, ZERO])
      g[1 if r.term == "long" else 0] += r.unrealized
  ```
  `r.term` is `mixed` whenever its lots differ (`_term`, `:107-112`), and a mixed position's
  whole gain goes to short-term.
- **What goes wrong:** Case: 100 VTI bought in 2023 (+$9,000 now) and 1 more last month (+$5).
  The Gains tab's unrealized row shows short-term $9,005 and long-term $0, the opposite of what
  selling would realize; someone deciding what to sell before year end reads it the wrong way.
- **Run:** no; reading alone.

  the position's price) less its basis, to the lot's term (by `heldFrom` or `acquiredOn`).
- **Alternatives:** none.

<a id="cr3-194"></a>

### CR3-194: the header calls prices stale for ever when anything is valued from a statement or at intrinsic value

*Work pad: U03-17 in [`unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S4.7 (stale past a threshold), S4.3 (a statement value is stale
  after 90 days), S12.5 (the header's price status).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/prices/refresh.py:398-406`:
  ```text
  ids = [i.id for i, _ in priced(s, benchmarks=False)] if viewer is None else _held_by(s, viewer)
  today = clock.today()
  closes = stored_closes(s, ids, today)
  ...
  stale = any(p.stale for p in closes.values()) or (bool(ids) and len(closes) < len(ids))
  ```
  Only stored closes and the viewer's typed prices count; a holding valued from a statement
  (`Holding.marketValue`), at intrinsic value, or by the owner's last trade has no stored close,
  so it makes the whole status stale.
- **What goes wrong:** Case: a household holds a 401(k) collective trust valued from last
  week's statement (S4.3's "No free ticker" row) next to stocks priced this evening. The header
  dot shows *stale* ("Some prices are older than they should be") every day, for ever. So does
  any option CBOE doesn't list once Massive isn't set up (valued at intrinsic value, S3.7), and
  a stock held in a shared account whose owner typed its price (the viewer's typed prices are
  read, not the owner's). The flag stops meaning anything.
- **Run:** no; reading alone.
- **Remedy:** judge each held instrument by the price that values it (`valuation.prices` with
  the owning account's owner, then intrinsic), stale by its own rule: a statement value after
  90 days, a typed price after 90 days, a close after S4.7's trading days; an instrument with no
  price at all counts as stale.
- **Alternatives:** leave statement-valued and intrinsic-valued instruments out of the header,
  which then speaks for fetched prices only, and say so in its tip.

<a id="cr3-195"></a>

### CR3-195: no symbol is checked with a source before it is used

*Work pad: U03-18 in [`unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md).*

- **Kind:** not built. **Req:** S4.2.2 ("where it can, `lookup(symbol)` to check a symbol
  before it is used").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/prices/base.py:75-77` declares `lookup`, and
  `prices/tiingo.py:49-55` implements it, but nothing calls it (no `.lookup(` outside the
  sources). A typed symbol becomes an instrument at once: `ui/transactions.py:584-590`
  ```text
  if body.symbol:
      symbol = body.symbol.strip().upper()
      ...
      return repo.instrument(s, symbol, None, "crypto" if crypto else "equity").id
  ```
  and likewise the option trade's underlying (`options.contract`) and the corporate action's
  new symbol (`corporate.py:92`).
- **What goes wrong:** Case: a buy typed as `VTIX` instead of `VTI`. MoolaKata makes the
  instrument `VTIX` (class `equity`), the refresh asks CBOE and Tiingo for it every day and
  stores nothing, the position is valued from the buy's own price for ever, and nothing says
  the symbol is unknown. A fund typed by hand is always `equity`, so a mutual fund is asked of
  CBOE first, which doesn't list it (S4.3), and its class is wrong in the wash-sale and
  projection classes.
- **Run:** no; reading alone (search: no caller of `lookup`).
- **Remedy:** on a new symbol, ask the enabled sources that can `lookup` (in
  a job or at the next refresh, never inside the request, S4.6) and mark an instrument no source
  knows (*unknown symbol*) where it is shown; take the class from the answer.
- **Alternatives:** S4.2.2 drops the promise, and the refresh's misses are listed per
  instrument in Settings › Prices.

<a id="cr3-196"></a>

### CR3-196: a bitcoin model's growth is applied a year early

*Work pad: U04-3 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S6.3.1 ("Each model gives a median path from today's
  price"), S6.4.2.3 ("A bitcoin model sets the median growth of the bitcoin each year").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/projections/__init__.py:243-247` builds `crypto_path`
  as the growth from year *k* to *k+1* at index *k*:
  ```text
  prices = bitcoin.median_path(
      model, taxtables.figures(s, year), btc_now, list(range(year, year + years + 1))
  )
  if prices:
      crypto_path = [b / a for a, b in itertools.pairwise(prices)]
  ```
  and `engine.py:90` and `:108` read index *t* for the growth that brings the balance to year
  *t* (`_simulate`, `:125-128`: index *t* is "the balance t years on, after that year's
  growth"), so year *t* gets the growth of year *t* to *t+1*:
  ```text
  ratios = np.array(plan.crypto_path[: plan.years])
  ```
  The first year's growth (`ratios[0]`) is never used.
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): stock-to-flow from Oct 2026 steps up about ten-fold at the
  2028 halving (its model price is 101.7k in 2027, 1,019k in 2028); the projection's expected
  path makes that jump in 2027 (100 → 1,001.9) a year before the model does. Every model is
  shifted the same way; for the smooth ones (power law, log regression) the error is a year's
  difference in the growth rate, for stock-to-flow a whole halving step.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u04/t/test_u04p.py::test_s2f_step_year` (`tmp/u04/run1.txt`).
- **Remedy:** `crypto_path[t-1]` for year *t* (or prepend 1.0 to the ratios), in `_returns`,
  `_expected_return` and `scenario_paths`; a test that the projection's bitcoin follows the
  model's price for the same year.
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

  No route or screen sets two projections' answers or fans beside each other; the only

- **What goes wrong:** to weigh retiring at 60 against 65 a user opens one, notes its chance

### CR3-198: the Inputs form can't set several things S6 names: other income, its age, the horizon, a birth year, several accounts, a projection's own assumptions

*Work pad: U04-5 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S6.1.1 ("Built on chosen accounts"), S6.1.2 (contributions: "an
  amount, its frequency, growth per year, and until when"), S6.2.1 (each person's birth year
  and retirement age; other income "Social Security from an age, a pension, each with its
  amount"; the horizon "age 95 by default"), S6.3 ("Every projection uses them unless it
  overrides a figure").
- **Code:** `web/ui/screens/projections.js:388-404`, other income is only the rows already
  there, each with its monthly amount:
  ```js
  ${(f.income || []).map((/** @type {any} */ inc, /** @type {number} */ i) =>
    ...
        <${Field} label=${`${inc.label || 'Other income'} a month`} id=${'income-' + i}
          help="other-income"
          hint=${`From age ${inc.fromAge}, rising with inflation`}>
  ```
  `NewProjection` (`:608-615`) creates one *Social Security* row from 67 and no way to add a
  pension, remove a row or change `fromAge`; no field sets `horizonAge`, a person's
  `birthYear` (the hint shows it, `:337`), or `overrides` (which `prepare()` reads,
  `projections/__init__.py:181-186`); `BuiltOn` (`:522-527`) picks one account
  (`{ kind, ids: [id] }`), so several accounts only come through a portfolio; contributions
  are "per year" until retirement, with no frequency or end.
- **What goes wrong:** a couple with a pension from 60 and Social Security at 70 can't enter
  either; a user born in a year Profile doesn't hold (or a partner whose birthday is unset,
  `:603`, takes the user's) can't correct it; a user who wants 95 changed to 100 can't. The
  server takes every one of these (`_check_inputs`), so only the screen is missing them.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** the form adds and removes other-income rows (label, person, from age, monthly,
  rises with inflation), shows birth year and horizon as fields, picks several accounts, and
  offers the per-projection overrides of S6.3 under an *Assumptions* fold.
- **Alternatives:** S6.1.2 drops frequency and "until when" (contributions are yearly until
  retirement or the date, as S6.4.2.1 has them), and S6.3 drops per-projection overrides but
  fees and the bitcoin model.

### CR3-199: making an education projection active turns off the other one for the same person without asking

*Work pad: U04-6 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S6.7.1 ("Making one active asks before turning off the one it
  replaces").
- **Code:** `web/ui/screens/projections.js:568-578`, the dialog names a replaced plan only for
  retirement, and even then without knowing it is the same subject:
  ```js
  const others = (/** @type {any[]} */ (list.data) || []).filter((r) =>
    r.active && r.goal === p.goal && r.id !== p.id && r.level === 'owner'
  );
  ...
      <p style=${{ marginTop: 0 }}>${others.length && p.goal === 'retirement'
        ? html`<b>${others[0].name}</b> stops being the active plan for the same people, if
          they are the same. Its tracking ends, and it stays as a scenario.`
        : 'It becomes an active plan.'}</p>
  ```
  while the server (`ui/projections.py:361-371`) replaces by `projections.subject()`, which
  for education is the beneficiary (`projections/__init__.py:415-417`).
- **What goes wrong:** *Emma's college (public)* is active; the user makes *Emma's college
  (private)* active; the dialog says "It becomes an active plan", and the first one's
  baseline and tracking are dropped (`other.baseline = None`), which can't be undone. For
  retirement, two plans for different people both show the warning naming the first active
  one, "if they are the same".
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** the server says what would be replaced before it acts (a `GET …/activate`
  preview, or `activate` answering 409 with the name unless `confirm` is passed), and the
  dialog names exactly that plan, for every goal.
- **Alternatives:** the dialog computes the subject the same way, from the list's inputs.

### CR3-200: a projection's unchecked inputs answer 500, and a typed mix may be levered

*Work pad: U04-7 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S13.8.3 (problems as problem+json, a field's error named), S6.4.2.2
  (a typed amount "gives its own mix").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/projections.py:200-261`, `_check_inputs()` checks
  neither `withdrawal.amount` nor `withdrawal.percent`, `overrides`, `income[].person`, nor a
  typed `builtOn.allocation`; `projections/__init__.py:85-92` takes the mix as given:
  ```text
  mix = spec.get("allocation") or {"usStocks": 60, "bonds": 40}
  total = sum(mix.values()) or 1
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): `withdrawal: {method: fixed, amount: "lots"}`,
  `overrides: {inflation: "x"}` and `income: [{person: "x"}]` each raise in the engine
  (`ValueError`, `TypeError`), an unhandled 500 with an error id in place of a 422 naming the
  field; a typed allocation `{usStocks: 300, cash: -200}` is accepted (201) as weights 3.0 and
  −2.0, a levered portfolio no screen can show. The transaction rolls back, so nothing bad
  is stored; the browser's form sends none of these.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u04/t/test_u04p.py::test_unchecked_inputs` (`tmp/u04/run2.txt`).
- **Remedy:** `_check_inputs` checks every key `prepare()` reads: amounts and percents as
  numbers in range, `person` an index of `people`, overrides as `assumptions.check` checks a
  class, and an allocation's shares as numbers of 0 or more summing to more than 0.
- **Alternatives:** a pydantic model for the inputs, so the OpenAPI document states them too.

### CR3-201: a shared projection names the owner's account a viewer isn't shared, and shows its mix

*Work pad: U04-8 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md).*

- **Kind:** privacy. **Req:** S6.1 ("a viewer sees its results, and the values and tracking of
  only the accounts shared with them"), S10.3, S5.1.1. Review 2's P7 (third point), marked
  done for tracking.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/projections/__init__.py:188` builds on the owner's
  accounts for every viewer, and `:106-110` labels a single account by its name:
  ```text
  base = built_on(s, viewer if viewer == p.ownerId else p.ownerId, json.loads(p.builtOn))
  ...
  label = (
      (accounts[0].name if len(accounts) == 1 else f"{len(accounts)} accounts")
  ```
  `compute()` (`:284-289`) sends `start`, `builtOnLabel` and `weights` and
  `ui/projections.py:99` sends `builtOn` (the account ids) to any viewer; `tracking()` alone
  narrows to the viewer's accounts.
- **What goes wrong:** Alice shares *Retire at 62*, built on her *Fidelity Rollover IRA*,
  with Bob, who isn't shared the IRA. Bob's list and detail read "on Fidelity Rollover IRA",
  "Starts from $812,400 (US stocks 72%, bonds 20%, crypto 8%)". The total is arguably a
  result (the fan starts there), but the account's name and its asset mix are what S5.1.1
  hides in a portfolio. Low: a name and a mix, never a transaction.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** for a viewer who isn't shared every account, the label is the count ("2
  accounts", or "accounts not shared with you"), `builtOn` carries no ids they can't see,
  and the weights are left out (or stated as the projection's mix, not the accounts').
- **Alternatives:** S6.1 says a share of a projection shows what it is built on, by name and
  mix, and the total.

### CR3-202: an education goal for `{kind: user}` takes any user, a private or remote one's person included

*Work pad: U04-9 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md).*

- **Kind:** privacy. **Req:** S10.10.2 (a private person is seen through a share), S10.3.3.1
  (a remote user's person "is used only for gifts"), S6.2.2.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/projections.py:180-186`, a person is checked for the
  caller's level, a user only for existence:
  ```text
  if who.get("kind") == "person":
      ...
      return [] if p is not None and people.level(s, user_id, p) is not None else bad
  if who.get("kind") == "user":
      return [] if s.get(User, who.get("id") or "") is not None else bad
  ```
  and `projections/__init__.py:334-340` then reads that user's person's name and birth year
  into the answer (`name`, `firstYear` = birth year + start age).
- **What goes wrong:** Bob posts an education projection with `beneficiary: {kind: "user",
  id: <a remote user's id, as the share picker lists it>}`; the answer names that person and
  gives their birth year (as `firstYear − 18`), though the person is private or a linked
  server's. The browser never sends `kind: user` (its picker lists persons), so this is the
  API only. Low: a name and a year.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** `kind: user` resolves to the user's person and checks `people.level` as a person
  does, local users only; or the API takes persons only.

### CR3-203: Roth eligibility by MAGI and the backdoor-Roth steps are not built

*Work pad: U04-16 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md).*

- **Kind:** not built. **Req:** S8.6 ("Roth eligibility by MAGI as the user enters it,
  backdoor-Roth steps (non-deductible contribution, conversion, Form 8606 figures)").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/taxes/panels.py:517-518`, the only trace of either:
  ```text
  if a.type == "roth-ira":
      note = (note + "; " if note else "") + "Roth eligibility depends on MAGI"
  ```
  and the carried 8606 basis (`:581-584`). No field takes a MAGI, no table holds the phase-out
  ranges (S16.11 lists none), and nothing pairs a non-deductible contribution with the
  conversion that follows it. The guide (`web/ui/guides/taxes-retirement.md`, *Contributions*)
  says MAGI "is the preparer's to check", which S8.6 doesn't. No milestone, gap or later item
  schedules it.
- **What goes wrong:** a couple above the Roth phase-out who contribute directly to a Roth IRA
  are never told; a backdoor Roth (a $7,500 non-deductible contribution, converted a week
  later) shows as two unrelated rows and, by U04-15, as over the limit.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** a MAGI field on the book (as typed), the phase-out ranges per filing status in
  the tax tables, and a Roth contribution flagged above them; the backdoor steps as a short
  list: the non-deductible contribution, the conversion matched to it, the 8606 basis carried
  and the pro-rata note when other pre-tax IRA money exists.
- **Alternatives:** S8.6 drops both, the guide's sentence becomes the requirement, and the
  backdoor goes to `later.md`.

### CR3-207: a corrected federal due date is taken as any list of numbers, and a bad one fails every tax book

*Work pad: U04-21 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S16.11 (a corrected figure), S8.15.3 (the federal due date).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/projections/taxtables.py:243-252`, the length check is
  meant for the bitcoin figures and compares a list with itself for any other:
  ```text
  if len(value) != len(_BTC.get(key, value)):
      raise Invalid(f"{len(_BTC[key])} numbers")
  return value
  ```
  and `taxes/books.py:161-162` unpacks and builds the date on every book read:
  ```text
  month, day = taxtables.figures(s, year)["federalDue"]
  return next_business_day(dt.date(year + 1, int(month), int(day)))
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): `check("federalDue", v)` accepts `[4]`, `[4, 31]`, `[13, 1]`
  and `[4.5, 15]`. An admin who types *4, 31* for a postponed date saves it; every
  `GET /tax-books/{id}` of that year (it calls `federal_due`, `ui/taxes.py:521`) and every new
  federal filing then fails with an unhandled 500 until the figure is corrected.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u04/t/test_u04t.py::test_federal_due_correction_unchecked`
  (`tmp/u04/run5.txt`).
- **Remedy:** `federalDue` takes two whole numbers forming a real date (try
  `dt.date(2001, m, d)`), refused with *a month and a day* otherwise.
- **Alternatives:** store the due date as a date per year, not a month and day (DC-14).

*Work pad: U04-22 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S8.14.3 (a helper doesn't add accounts), S8.15.5 (estimated
  payments "matched to transactions"), S8.17 (the refund or amount owed carried; "puts the
  capital-loss carryover in its Summary").
- **Code:** `web/ui/screens/taxes.js:193-196` and `:214-230` show *Leave out*, *Count it* and
  *Add an account* when `b.canWrite`, which a write helper has, while `ui/taxes.py:570-571`
  refuses anyone but a filer (403 `filers-only`); `taxes.js:705-712` sends payments without
  `transactionId` and `filingForm` (`:667-672`) drops it, so a payment can't be matched in the
  browser and saving a filing unmatches any matched through the API; `CARRIED` (`:42-50`) and
  the server's `Carried` (`ui/taxes.py:263-270`) have no refund or amount owed, so *From last
  year* never shows them; `SummaryFigures` (`components/taxpanels.js:63-72`) shows short plus

### CR3-209: a projection's percentiles and answers go out as raw floats, not rounded to cents (C40 half done)

*Work pad: U04-23 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S6.9 ("Its percentiles are rounded to cents and sent as exact
  numbers (S13.8.2.1)"), R1.9's exception as review 2's C40 decided it.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/projections/engine.py:176-177` keeps NumPy's floats:
  ```text
  pct = {p: np.percentile(path, p, axis=0).tolist() for p in PERCENTILES}
  real = {p: (np.array(v) / deflate).tolist() for p, v in pct.items()}
  ```
  and `projections/__init__.py:279-292` sends them, with `expected`, the scenario paths and
  the answers' `atRetirement`, `atStart`, `needed`, `neededMonthly`, unrounded;
  `exactjson.marked` (`exactjson.py:39-48`) marks Decimals only and passes floats through.
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run, U04-1's): the median at the target date goes out as
  `5979.46611909651`; every point of every fan carries 11–15 digits, which the browser reads
  as exact decimals (`app/dec.js`) and the baseline stores as such. The start value and
  tracking are exact, as C40 asked; the other half isn't.
- **Run:** yes, seen in `tmp/u04/run1.txt` (`median at target [Exact(0.0),
  Exact(5979.46611909651)]`).
- **Remedy:** round each percentile, path and money answer to cents as a Decimal in
  `compute()` (`Decimal(repr(x)).quantize(CENTS)`), and the baseline as stored.
- **Alternatives:** S6.9 says the figures go out as floats, which the client rounds for show.
