### CR3-212: a filing with an extension filed but no new due date typed is shown past due

*Work pad: U04-26 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S8.15 ("A filing past its due date that is not filed and has no
  extension shows in the Dashboard's *Needs attention*"), S8.15.3 (an extension: "date filed,
  new due date").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/taxes/books.py:175-187`, the extension is read only
  through its new date:
  ```text
  def due_of(f: Filing) -> dt.date | None:
      return f.extendedDue or f.dueDate
  ...
      return (
          due is not None
          and due < today
          and f.progress not in ("filed", "accepted")
  ```
  `extensionFiledOn` is never read.
- **What goes wrong:** a user files Form 4868 on Apr 14 and types *Extension filed* only (the
  federal extension's date is fixed, Oct 15, so it is easy to leave out): from Apr 16 the
  filing shows *past its due date* and the Dashboard's *Needs attention* lists it, though it
  has an extension.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** a federal extension filed on time gives `extendedDue` six months on (Oct 15,
  moved as S8.15.3 says) when none is typed; for others, `overdue` is false while an extension
  is filed and its date unknown, the filing saying *extended, new due date not typed*.
- **Alternatives:** the filing dialog requires *Extended to* with *Extension filed*.

<a id="cr3-213"></a>

### CR3-213: a reserve's balance is carried into its line after its target is taken off

*Work pad: U05-2 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S7.10.3 ("Taking its category out of the plan, or its target off,
  asks first and names the balance that stops being kept; a balance is never carried into a
  line without a target").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/budgets.py:821-826` stores every reserve line with
  rollover on:
  ```text
  new.amount,
  new.cadence,
  new.rollover or new.target is not None,  # a reserve always rolls over
  ```
  and `budgets/__init__.py:599-609` (`_carry`) walks every earlier month from the category's
  first line, carrying any rolling monthly line, a reserve's months included:
  ```text
  ln = in_force(mine, m).get(cat_id)
  if ln is None or not ln.rollover or ln.cadence != "month":
      carry = ZERO
  else:
      carry = (
          ln.amount
          + carry
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): *Car maintenance* $100 a month with a $1,000 target from
  August, nothing spent: $400 at the end of November. In December the target is taken off,
  the line kept at $100 with rollover; the server answers 409 *holds 400.00: it stops being
  kept*, and on confirm December shows **carried $400.00, planned $500.00**. The balance the
  user was told stops being kept is carried into a line without a target. (Had the target
  capped the put-in, `_carry` would also count the full $100 a month a funded reserve never
  put in.)
- **Run:** yes, `test_u05.py::test_a_reserve_balance_carried_into_a_line_without_target`:
  `NOV reserve balance 400.00`, `DEC no target None carried 400.00 planned 500.00`.
- **Remedy:** `_carry` starts afresh after a run of lines with a target (a month whose line
  has a target sets `carry` to nothing), so a line without one carries only its own months.
- **Alternatives:** store reserves with `rollover` as the user set it and treat a target as
  rolling over in `reserves.reserve` alone.

<a id="cr3-214"></a>

### CR3-214: copying an envelope budget with this month's moves fails when a move went into a card's payment line

*Work pad: U05-3 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S7.12.4 ("with this month's moves when asked"), S7.13.2.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/budgets.py:1330-1342` copies a move without its
  `cardId`:
  ```text
  s.add(
      BudgetMove(
          id=ids.new_id("BdgMv"),
          budgetId=b.id,
          month=month,
          fromCategoryId=m.fromCategoryId,
          toCategoryId=m.toCategoryId,
          amount=m.amount,
          note=m.note,
          transactionId=m.transactionId,
          createdById=c.id,
      )
  )
  ```
  while `store/tables/budgets.py:109-114` requires one of the four (`ck_BudgetMove_somewhere`).
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): an envelope budget with a $60 move from *To Budget* into
  Visa's payment line this month; **Copy as scenario…** as envelope with *this month's moves*
  fails with `IntegrityError: CHECK constraint failed: ck_BudgetMove_somewhere` (a 500, and
  no copy). A move from a category into the card's line is copied as a move from that
  category to *To Budget*, silently changing what the copy holds. A tracking copy also drops
  landed money given to a reserve (`:1328-1329` skips any move without both categories), so
  the copy's reserve lacks it and the deposit shows as unassigned again.
- **Run:** yes, `test_u05.py::test_copy_with_a_card_move`: the IntegrityError above.
- **Remedy:** copy `cardId` with the move when the copy is an envelope budget whose accounts
  hold the card; skip a card move otherwise, and keep landed-money moves (they need no
  category on the *from* side).
- **Alternatives:** copy only category-to-category moves and say so in the dialog.

<a id="cr3-215"></a>

### CR3-215: a reviewed month isn't flagged when a late transaction lands outside the plan, uncategorised, or leaving the budget

*Work pad: U05-5 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S7.14 ("A reviewed month whose figures later change (a late
  transaction, a recategorised one, a move in it) is flagged *changed since reviewed*").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/budgets.py:1413-1440` keeps `spent` but never compares
  it, nor anything of what is to sort:
  ```text
  for k, name in (("income", "Income"), ("saved", "Saved"), ("toBudget", "To Budget")):
      if then.get(k) != now.get(k) and not out:
          out.append(f"{name}: {then.get(k)} then, {now.get(k)} now")
  ```
  Only planned rows, income, saving and To Budget are compared; spending in a category with no
  line (`Month.unplanned`), the uncategorised count and the transfers that left or entered the
  budget (`Month.moved`) are not in `_figures` at all.
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): November reviewed with only *Food* planned. A late $900 vet
  bill under *Entertainment* (no line) and a late uncategorised $50 arrive; the review shows
  **no changes** (`changes []`), though November now has $950 more spending and one
  transaction to sort. The month the user marked as done stays marked so.
- **Run:** yes, `test_u05.py::test_review_misses_unplanned_and_uncategorised`.
- **Remedy:** compare `spent`, the unplanned rows, the uncategorised count and the count and
  total of transfers to sort, and name each change.
- **Alternatives:** keep a hash of the month's counted transactions (ids, amounts, categories)
  with the review, and flag any difference.

<a id="cr3-216"></a>

### CR3-216: landed money spread among reserves that hold nothing all goes to the last one

*Work pad: U05-7 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S7.10.5 ("spread among them by balance").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/budgets.py:1225-1235`:
  ```text
  weights = [(k, max(v, Decimal(0))) for k, v in h.reserves]
  total = sum((w for _, w in weights), Decimal(0)) or Decimal(len(weights))
  ...
  else (body.amount * (w if total else 1) / total).quantize(Decimal("0.01"))
  ```
  When every balance is nothing, `total` falls back to the count, but `w` is still 0 (`total`
  is never falsy there), so every share but the last is $0 and the last takes the remainder.
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run): two reserves at Ally, both at $0 (a first month, or after a
  repair); $100 of interest **spread**: *Housing › Maintenance* $0.00, *Car maintenance*
  $100.00. The equal split the fallback was written for never happens.
- **Run:** yes, `test_u05.py::test_spread_with_no_balances`.
- **Remedy:** use weight 1 for each reserve when the balances sum to nothing
  (`w if any_positive else 1`).
- **Alternatives:** spread by target, or by the month's put-in, when no reserve holds anything.

<a id="cr3-217"></a>

### CR3-217: an estimate keeps one recurring series per category: two yearly bills in one category become the last one's line

*Work pad: U05-9 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S7.1.5 ("The recurring transactions of S7.5 … are offered as
  lines of their own (*Rent $1,850 a month*, *Car insurance $1,140 a year*), a yearly one as a
  reserve when reserves are on").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/budgets/plan.py:204-220`, each yearly or quarterly series
  overwrites its category's proposal, the history average and any earlier series alike:
  ```text
  for x in recurring.detect(txns, today):
      cid = x.categoryId
      ...
      yearly = abs(x.usual) * (4 if x.every == "quarter" else 1)
      out[cid] = Proposal(
  ```
  and an income series is proposed as a reserve too (`target=_round(yearly) if b.reserves`,
  no check of the category's kind).
- **What goes wrong:** case: *Insurance* holds car insurance ($1,140 a year), home insurance
  ($2,400 a year) and a monthly health premium ($300). The history average would propose about
  $7,140 a year; the estimate proposes one line, whichever series `detect` lists last (sorted by
  next date), say *$2,400 a year* (*$200 a month toward a $2,400 target* with reserves on): the
  plan is $4,740 a year short. A yearly bonus under *Income › Bonus* is proposed, with reserves
  on, as a reserve with a target, which S7.10 makes for costs only. Monthly series are never
  offered as lines of their own (`:209-211`), so *Rent $1,850 a month* appears only as
  *Housing › Rent*'s average with "spent in N of M months".
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** sum the series of one category (and add the rest of its history average when
  something else is spent there), naming each in the detail; propose reserves for expense and
  savings categories only.
- **Alternatives:** S7.1.5 says the estimate is per category, with the recurring series named in
  its detail, since a plan holds one line per category.

<a id="cr3-218"></a>

### CR3-218: a HELOC draw into a budget's account is taken for a loan payment: it lowers spending, or vanishes

*Work pad: U05-10 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S7.3.3 ("Loan payments count at the bank account they come
  from"), S7.3.2 (a transfer entering the budget from an account it doesn't count "counts like
  any transaction, by the category of its side in the budget … One with no category … is listed
  under … **Entered the budget**").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/budgets/__init__.py:202-207` sends every matched transfer
  with a loan account to `_count_loan_payment`, whichever way the money goes:
  ```text
  if t.transferId:
      if other is None or other[1].id in inside:
          continue  # between two of the budget's accounts: no money moved in or out
      if other[1].type in _loan_types():
          _count_loan_payment(s, tr, out, t, other, loans_seen)
          continue
  ```
  There (`:274-281`) a loan counted *whole* adds the bank half's amount, positive for a draw, to
  its category; a *split* loan (the default) looks the transfer up among the loan's payments,
  where `loans.history` (`loans/__init__.py:202-205`) files a draw under `draws`, finds nothing
  and returns without counting or listing it.
- **What goes wrong:** case: a HELOC draw of $10,000 into Checking in May to pay a contractor,
  whose $10,000 is spent from Checking under *Home improvement*. With the loan counted whole in
  *Loan payments*, May's *Loan payments* shows **−$9,600 spent** (the $400 payment less the draw)
  and the month's spending falls by $10,000. With the default split the draw counts nowhere and
  is not under *Entered the budget*, so it can't be sorted. An escrow refund paid from the loan
  into Checking goes the same way.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** only money out to the loan (`t.amount < 0`) is a payment; money in from a loan
  is money entering the budget (S7.3.2): counted by its category, or listed to sort.
- **Alternatives:** S7.3.3 names draws and escrow refunds and says how they count (for example
  as income in a *Loan proceeds* category).

<a id="cr3-219"></a>

### CR3-219: spending by person leaves out a deleted person, and a plan budgeted per occasion shows no budget

*Work pad: U05-13 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S7.8.6 ("gifts bought, given or received stay, still count in past
  spending, and show the person as *Emma (deleted)*"), S7.8.4 ("the spending per year against
  their budget"), S7.7.1 ("a yearly **gift budget** or one per occasion").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/gifts/__init__.py:393-399` builds *by person* from the
  live plans only, with the plan's yearly budget:
  ```text
  byPerson=[
      (x.person, by_person.get(pid, ZERO), x.plan.giftBudget)
      for pid, x in plans.items()
      if me is None or pid != me.personId
  ],
  ```
  `plans` comes from `visible_plans` (`:339`), which drops a deleted person (`:49-51`), and
  `delete_person` deletes their plan (`:428-431`).
- **What goes wrong:** case: Emma is deleted in March after $400 of birthday and Christmas
  gifts last year. Gifts › Spending for last year lists everyone but Emma *by person*, while
  *by occasion* and *by year* still count her $400, so the two lists no longer add up and her
  row (*Emma (deleted)*) is not there. A plan with budgets per occasion (birthday $50, Christmas
  $100) shows its person with no budget to compare with.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** add a row for every person in `by_person` whose plan is gone, named with
  *(deleted)*; a plan's budget is its yearly one or the sum of its occasions' for the year.
- **Alternatives:** list a deleted person's spending under *Others (deleted)*.

<a id="cr3-220"></a>

### CR3-220: donations are read only from the *Giving* category itself: not its sub-categories, nor a split's Giving part

*Work pad: U05-14 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S7.9.3 ("**Donations** come from transactions categorised
  under *Giving* (tax tag *charitable*, S7.2)"), S7.3.1 ("A split transaction counts through
  its splits").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/giving/__init__.py:131-153`:
  ```text
  cats = [c.id for c in s.scalars(select(Category).where(Category.builtIn == "giving"))]
  ...
          select(Transaction).where(
              Transaction.accountId.in_(accounts),
              Transaction.categoryId.in_(cats),
  ```
  Only the shipped *Giving* itself (`ledger/categories.py:81` ships it with no children), and
  only the transaction's own category: splits are not read.
- **What goes wrong:** case: the household adds *Giving › Church* and *Giving › Food bank* to
  tell its giving apart; from then on its monthly $300 to the church counts nowhere on the
  Giving screen: not toward the target, not by organisation, not among acknowledgements due.
  A $120 charity-auction receipt split $80 *Giving*, $40 *Dining* doesn't count either. The
  budget's *Giving* line (which counts children, `budgets._actual`) and the Giving screen then
  disagree on the same money.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** read transactions and splits in *Giving* and its children (or any category with
  the *charitable* tax tag), each split part as its own gift.
- **Alternatives:** S7.9.3 says only *Giving* itself counts, and the category routes refuse a
  child under it.

<a id="cr3-221"></a>

### CR3-221: a pledge paid by a transaction the Giving screen counts still raises *due*, and a month's alert lapses unpaid

*Work pad: U05-15 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S7.9.5 ("A pledge is **due** from the 25th of the month for a
  monthly one, and from December 1 for a yearly one, while less than it has been given to the
  organisation in that month or year; a due pledge raises an alert to its owner (S12.11.1.3),
  resolved by the donation").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/alerts.py:701-722` counts recorded donations only, and
  only while the period is the current one:
  ```text
  if o.pledgeEvery == "month" and today.day >= 25:
      first, period = today.replace(day=1), f"{today:%B}"
  ...
  select(Donation).where(
      Donation.organisationId == o.id,
  ```
  while the Giving screen counts toward an organisation every transaction under *Giving* whose
  payee is its name (`giving/__init__.py:150-153`, `ui/giving.py:140-141`, `given_to`).
- **What goes wrong:** case: a $50 monthly pledge to St. Mark's, paid by card on the 3rd and
  imported under *Giving* with the payee *St. Mark's*. The Giving screen shows the month given;
  on the 25th the alert *Your pledge to St. Mark's is due* is raised anyway and stays until the
  month ends. And when a pledge really is unpaid, the alert disappears on the 1st of the next
  month (the check looks only at the current month), resolved without a donation.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** the alert counts what `giving.donations` counts for the organisation (recorded,
  and transactions matched by name); a month's alert holds until that month is met or the
  pledge is changed.
- **Alternatives:** S7.9.5 says the alert covers the current month only, and the Giving screen
  counts only recorded donations toward a pledge.

<a id="cr3-222"></a>

### CR3-222: a write share of a giving plan can add a donation but can't change or delete it, nor add or change an organisation

*Work pad: U05-16 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S10.3.2 ("write: also change what the item holds … for the other
  kinds, edit their entries"), S10.3 (a giving plan's share includes "its target, pace,
  donations and organisations"), S7.9.1.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/giving.py:549-551` files a write receiver's donation
  under the plan's owner:
  ```text
  plan, lvl = giving.plan_for(s, c.id, body.date.year)
  # Recorded in the plan the caller writes to: its owner's giving (S7.9.1).
  owner = plan.ownerId if plan is not None and lvl == "write" else c.id
  ```
  but every later change needs the caller to own it (`:616-620`, `_own`:
  `if d is None or d.deleted or d.ownerId != c.id: raise Problem(404, …)`), and `canWrite`
  says the same (`:413`). Organisations are changed only by their owner (`:309-313`), and one a
  receiver adds is theirs (`:303`), so it is not among the plan's (`_orgs_view` lists the
  owner's, `:242-245`) and is refused for the plan's donations (`:554-555`).
- **What goes wrong:** case: Alice shares her giving plan with Bob at write. Bob records a $500
  gift to the food bank from Checking: it lands in Alice's plan. The letter arrives; Bob can't
  tick *acknowledged* or attach it (404 *Not found*), can't fix the organisation, can't delete
  it; Alice must. Bob adds *Habitat for Humanity*: it never shows in the plan's organisations
  and can't be chosen for a donation. A read receiver with no plan of his own gets the shared
  plan as his year's (`giving.plan_for`), so he can't record a donation to his own
  organisations at all (`organisation-unknown`).
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** `_own` and `_own_org` accept the plan's owner and its write receivers; a write
  receiver's new organisation belongs to the plan's owner. `plan_for` gives a user their own
  giving when the only plan for the year is someone else's at read.
- **Alternatives:** S7.9.1 says a giving plan is shared to read only, and the Share dialog
  offers no write for it.

<a id="cr3-223"></a>

### CR3-223: turning reserves off changes nothing: the lines with targets go on as reserves

*Work pad: U05-18 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S7.1.4 ("**Options**, chosen when the budget is made and turned on
  or off later in its settings, so a budget shows only what it uses: **reserves** (S7.10)");
  the settings guide (`web/ui/guides/dialog-budget-settings.md`): "Turned off, the reserves'
  lines stay in the plan as ordinary lines."
- **Code:** `Budget.reserves` is read only when a plan is saved (`ui/budgets.py:772-781`, a
  target refused without it) and by the estimate (`budgets/plan.py:215-219`). The month's
  figures (`budgets/__init__.py:463-485`, `if ln.target is not None and ln.cadence == "month":`),
  envelope assignment (`envelope.py:120-121`) and *Where the money is* (`reserves.py:143-148`)
  test the line's `target` alone; `change_budget` (`ui/budgets.py:618-624`) only flips the flag.
- **What goes wrong:** case: a budget with a *Roof* reserve ($150 a month, target $5,000, held
  at Ally) has reserves turned off in Settings. The month still shows *Roof* under **Reserves**
  with its balance and *Funded*, puts in only up to the target, and *Where the money is* still
  asks to move money to Ally; the plan dialog no longer shows the Target column, so its next
  save drops the target and asks about the balance. What the guide promises (ordinary lines)
  happens only then, and only through a 409.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** with reserves off, lines are read as ordinary lines (the targets kept, unused, so
  turning reserves back on restores them), after asking, as S7.10.3 does, about the balances
  that stop being kept.
- **Alternatives:** refuse to turn reserves off while a line has a target, naming them.

<a id="cr3-224"></a>

### CR3-224: Compare shows no totals, no To Budget and no reserve balances; three screen texts say other than the code

*Work pad: U05-19 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S7.12.3 ("per category, what is available, spent and remaining,
  the reserves' balances, and *To Budget* for an envelope budget, with the totals and where the
  two part"), S7.10.4 ("A reserve held nowhere reads *in the budget's accounts*"), S7.13.4.
- **Code and what differs:**
  1. `web/ui/screens/budgets.js:766-799` renders the rows only: no totals row, nothing marking
     the categories where the two budgets part, and `toBudget`/`otherToBudget`, which the server
     sends (`ui/budgets.py:1408-1409`), are never shown; a reserve appears as its available and
     spent, not its balance.
  2. `budgets.js:923`, a reserve shows `in ${held}` only when it has an account; one held
     nowhere says nothing (S7.10.4's *in the budget's accounts*).
  3. `web/ui/guides/budgets-month.md` (*To Budget*): "Income adds to it; only assigning money
     takes from it." Money leaving the budget's accounts takes from it too (S7.13.4, and
     `envelope.py:159`, cash at the month's end), as do a left-out purchase and an overspent
     line set to *from next month's To Budget* (`envelope.py:136-137`, taken in the same
     month).
  4. `budgets.js:1062-1063`, every card on *Where the money is* says "paying the card moves
     nothing in a tracking budget", in an envelope budget too, where a payment takes from the
     card's line.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** a totals row and To Budget in Compare, with the rows where the two differ marked
  and reserves shown by balance; *in the budget's accounts* for a reserve held nowhere; the
  guide's sentence says income adds to To Budget and money leaving the accounts takes from it;
  the card note by method.

<a id="cr3-225"></a>

### CR3-225: a budget's receiver sees figures of an account shared with its owner but not with them, through the review

*Work pad: U05-21 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** privacy. **Req:** S7.1.1 ("Its receivers see the figures from the owner's own
  accounts, and from an account shared with the owner only if that account is shared with them
  too, so a budget never passes on an account someone else shared (R3.4)").
- **Code:**
  1. `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/budgets.py:1524` stores a review's figures as the reviewer
     sees them (`_figures(budgets.month_view(s, b, m, clock.today(), c.id))`), and `_reviewed`
     (`:1460-1466`) compares them with the viewer's own, writing both into the text:
     `f"{label}: spent {a[1]} then, {b[1]} now"` (`:1434`).
  2. Withheld (part 2 of this claim; see its check).
  3. `_moves_out` (`ui/budgets.py:888`) names a card move by any credit card on the server.
- **What goes wrong:** case: Carol shares her card with Alice (read); Alice's household budget
  counts it and is shared with Bob at write; the card is not shared with Bob. Alice marks
  October reviewed. Bob opens the review: *changed since reviewed: Dining: spent 812.40 then,
  355.10 now*, the $457.30 being Carol's card, which Bob can't see anywhere else; the flag also
  never clears for him, since his figures never match Alice's.
- **Run:** no, reading alone. Low: one household's members, a narrow arrangement (an account
  shared to the owner by a third person, inside a shared budget).
- **Remedy:** keep a review's figures per viewer's reach, or store only the owner-view and
  compare each viewer against figures worked out from the accounts they share; name cards from
  the viewer's accounts.
- **Alternatives:** S7.14 says a review compares figures per user, each marking it for
  themselves.

<a id="cr3-226"></a>

### CR3-226: the Giving screen's projected year end leaves out this month's unpaid monthly pledges; the donation guide says *basis* for short-held shares

*Work pad: U05-23 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S7.9.6 ("the projected year end"), S7.9.5 (pledges "shown as
  expected donations"), S7.9.3 ("otherwise the lesser of the market value and the basis").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/giving/__init__.py:210-211` counts a monthly pledge for the
  months after this one only, given or not:
  ```text
  if o.pledgeEvery == "month":
      out += o.pledge * (12 - today.month)
  ```
  and `web/ui/guides/dialog-donation.md` says: "For shares held a year or less, the deduction is
  their basis", where the code takes the lesser of value and basis (`ui/giving.py:522`), as
  S7.9.3 and review 2's C48 say.
- **What goes wrong:** case: a $100 monthly pledge, nothing given yet in December: *projected*
  is what was given plus $0, though $100 is still expected this month; on December 1 every
  monthly pledge vanishes from the projection. (A monthly pledge already met this month is, on
  the other hand, right to leave out.) The guide tells a user donating shares that dropped below
  basis that they deduct the basis, which is more than the code (rightly) counts.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** add this month's monthly pledge less what was given to the organisation this
  month (not below nothing); the guide says "the lesser of their market value and their basis".

  recurring rows beside them hide the same accounts (`ui/budgets.py:1642-1654`: "An entry

<a id="cr3-228"></a>

### CR3-228: one worker reads every user's files in turn, so a file that takes it sees the household's later files and can answer with them

*Work pad: U06-4 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** privacy. **Req:** S9.8.4.2 (the worker as a sandbox "for a file that exploits the
  libraries that read it"), S10 (a user sees only what is theirs).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/parsing.py:20-27`:
  ```text
  def _call(op: str, data: bytes, **params) -> dict:
      global _worker
      from .receipts.reading import Worker

      with _lock:
          if _worker is None:
              _worker = Worker(_data_dir)
          return _worker.call(op, data, LIMIT, **params)
  ```
  one process for every thumbnail, HEIC view, OFX and CSV of every user, kept until it crashes
  or times out; the reading queue's workers likewise (`reading.py:204-209`, one `Worker` per
  thread for the server's life, restarted only after a failure, `reading.py:111-141`).
- **What goes wrong:** Alice uploads a receipt crafted to exploit the HEIC decoder; the code it
  runs stays in the quick worker and keeps each later request's bytes: Bob's OFX download,
  Carol's photos. When Alice next uploads a CSV, the worker's `csv-propose` answer carries
  Bob's transactions as its sample rows (500 cells of 500 characters pass `propose_csv`'s
  checks), or a later receipt's OCR lines carry Carol's statement text, which is stored as
  Alice's receipt text and shown to her. Nothing in the server can tell. Only an exploit
  makes this possible, which is the threat S9.8.4.2 is written for.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** a fresh worker for each file brought by a different user than the last (or for
  every file in the reading queue, where a minute's job dwarfs the start), and for imports;
  the quick jobs may keep one worker per user.
- **Alternatives:** restart every worker after N files or M minutes, which narrows the window
  without closing it.

<a id="cr3-229"></a>

### CR3-229: on an x86_64 kernel with the x32 ABI, the seccomp filter lets a network socket through

*Work pad: U06-5 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S9.8.4.2 ("a seccomp filter denies it network sockets").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/sandbox.py:79-86`:
  ```text
              ins(ld, 0, 0, 4),  # 0: the call's architecture
              ins(jeq, 1, 0, arch),  # 1: ours: on to 3
              ins(ret, 0, 0, SECCOMP_RET_ERRNO | errno.EPERM),  # 2
              ins(ld, 0, 0, 0),  # 3: the call's number
              ins(jeq, 0, 1, io_uring_nr),  # 4: io_uring_setup: refused at 5
              ins(ret, 0, 0, SECCOMP_RET_ERRNO | errno.EPERM),  # 5
              ins(jeq, 1, 0, socket_nr),  # 6: socket: on to 8
              ins(ret, 0, 0, SECCOMP_RET_ALLOW),  # 7: anything else
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** x32 system calls arrive with the x86_64 architecture and the number with
  bit 30 set (`socket` is `0x40000000 + 41`). On a kernel built with `CONFIG_X86_X32_ABI` and
  x32 enabled, code a hostile file runs in the worker calls `socket(AF_INET, …)` through the
  x32 entry: the number isn't 41, so step 7 allows it, and the worker has the network S9.8.4.2
  denies it (to fetch more code, or send what it read out). Most distribution kernels ship x32
  off, so this is a hardening gap rather than an open door.
- **Run:** no, reading alone (this host's kernel has no x32 to try).
- **Remedy:** after loading the number, refuse anything at or above `0x40000000` (the usual
  `JGE 0x40000000 → ERRNO` line), so only native calls are judged.
- **Alternatives:** an allow-list filter of the calls the four libraries need, which closes
  this and more besides.

<a id="cr3-230"></a>

### CR3-230: *Read again* on a pruned receipt leaves it *Reading…* for good

*Work pad: U06-6 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S9.8.6 (**Read again** queues the job), S9.8.4.4 (a receipt that fails
  is marked *couldn't read it*), S14.10.5 (a pruned record keeps its row).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/receipts.py:392-396` queues any receipt the caller can
  write, pruned or not:
  ```text
      with srv.db.tx() as s:
          r, lvl = receipts.get(s, c.id, receipt_id, "write")
          if not settings.get(s, "readReceipts"):
              raise Problem(409, "reading-off", "Reading receipts is off in Settings › Receipts.")
          reading.read_again(s, r)
  ```
  and `receipts/reading.py:242-248` skips it without changing its state:
  ```text
              if (
                  r is None
                  or r.deleted
                  or r.filePrunedAt is not None
                  or r.readStatus not in ("waiting", "reading")
              ):
                  return
  ```
  The dialog offers the button on a pruned receipt (`web/ui/components/receipt.js:219`,
  `canEdit && !reading`). `Reader.start` (`reading.py:195-203`) queues every `waiting` receipt
  again at each start, to the same end.
- **What goes wrong:** an admin prunes an expired receipt; its owner opens it and presses
  **Read again**: it answers 200 with `readStatus: waiting`, and the receipt shows *Reading…* in
  the grid, the dialog and the Receipts toolbar's count for ever.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u06/t/test_u06.py::test_read_again_on_a_pruned_receipt`: `POST
  /receipts/{id}/read` gives `waiting`, and `Reader.read_one` leaves it `waiting`.
- **Remedy:** refuse *Read again* on a pruned receipt (409 `file-pruned`, as the file route's
  410), hide the button with the file, and have `read_one` mark a pruned one `failed` with "its
  file was pruned" rather than return.

<a id="cr3-231"></a>

### CR3-231: deleting an account makes a lot's receipt expire from the buy's date at once, not from the deletion

*Work pad: U06-7 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S14.10.2 ("at the latest end of the records it is attached
  to"), `schema.md` §6.2 ("A deleted record's own end is the earlier of its declared end and its
  `effectiveDeletedAt`").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/receipts/retention.py:117-121, 128-132`:
  ```text
  def _deleted(row, end: dt.date | None) -> dt.date | None:
      if row.deletedAt is None:
          return end
      gone = row.deletedAt.date()
      return gone if end is None else min(end, gone)
  ```
  ```text
      end: dt.date | None = t.date
      for lot in ctx.s.scalars(select(Lot).where(Lot.transactionId == t.id)):
          lot_end = ctx.lot_ends(t.accountId).get(lot.id, t.date)
          end = None if lot_end is None or end is None else max(end, lot_end)
      end = _deleted(t, end)
  ```
  Only the row's own `deletedAt` is read, never its `effectiveDeletedAt` (the account's
  deletion, through `TransactionEffective`); and `lot_ends` replays the deleted account, whose
  lots `live()` leaves out (`store/repos/ledger.py:73`), so an open lot's end falls back to the
  buy's date.
- **What goes wrong:** Alice's brokerage account holds a 2020 buy of VTI, still open, with its
  trade confirmation attached (category kept 2 years): *Kept while the lot is open*. On
  Mar 1, 2033 she deletes the account (it moved to another broker). The receipt at once shows
  *Expired Jan 2022, prunable*, and the next prune removes it. By §6.2 it ended with the
  deletion and is kept until Mar 2035.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u06/t/test_u06.py::test_a_lot_in_a_deleted_account`: before the delete
  `('open', 'Kept while the lot is open')`, after it `('expired', 'Expired Jan 2022, prunable')`.
- **Remedy:** read each record's end through its `Effective` view: the end is the earlier of the
  declared end (none for an open lot) and `effectiveDeletedAt`; an item in a deleted account
  likewise.

### CR3-232: the test of review 2's C3 (a deleted receipt doesn't keep a file) can't fail: both uploads are one receipt

*Work pad: U06-8 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** test gap. **Req:** S14.10.5 ("A file several receipts share is removed once no live
  receipt needs it (a deleted one doesn't count)"), S9.7; review 2's C3.
- **Code:** `web/server/tests/test_retention.py:180-188`:
  ```text
      first = upload(c, png((9, 8, 7))).json()["id"]
      second = upload(c, png((9, 8, 7))).json()["id"]
      with server.db.tx() as s:
          a, b = s.get(Receipt, first), s.get(Receipt, second)
          assert a.fileId == b.fileId, "one file for the same bytes"
          b.mark_deleted(None)
  ```
  The same user's second upload of the same bytes gives back the first receipt
  (`receipts/__init__.py:153-162`; `test_receipts.py:73-74` asserts it), so `a` and `b` are one
  row: the test deletes the receipt it then prunes, and never has a live receipt beside a
  deleted one.
- **What goes wrong:** if `prune_files` counted deleted rows again (C3's old fault), this test
  would still pass: nothing holds the rule.
- **Run:** no, reading alone (the identity follows from `store`'s same-owner rule).
- **Remedy:** the second upload by another user (or a second receipt row made directly), the
  other's receipt deleted, then the first pruned and the file asserted gone; and a case where
  a live other receipt keeps the file.

### CR3-233: file pruning removes files before its transaction commits, and prunes the rows it finds again, not the count the admin typed

*Work pad: U06-9 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S14.10.5 (asks the admin to type the count; each record keeps its row
  and shows *File pruned*).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/settings.py:1136-1153`:
  ```text
      with srv.db.read() as s:
          rows = retention.prunable(s, body.categories)
      if not rows:
          raise refuse("categories", "nothing-to-prune", "No expired files in what is ticked.")
      if body.count != len(rows):
          raise refuse("count", "count-mismatch", f"Type {len(rows):,} to prune them.")
      with srv.db.tx() as s:
          rows = retention.prunable(s, body.categories)
          freed = retention.prune_files(s, srv.config.receipts_dir, rows, c.id)
          activity.record(
  ```
  and `receipts/retention.py:333-336` unlinks each file inside that transaction:
  ```text
          p = file_path(receipts_dir, file_id)
          try:
              freed += p.stat().st_size
              p.unlink()
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** (1) the files are gone before `activity.record` and the commit; if
  either fails (the database busy past its 10 s, a full disk), the rows roll back to *not
  pruned* while their files no longer exist, and each such receipt's file answers 500
  *can't be read*, its retention still showing *Expired …, prunable*. (2) The count is checked
  against one read and the rows pruned are found again in the write: a category's period
  shortened by another admin, or midnight passing, between the two prunes more files than
  the admin typed.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** find the rows once inside the transaction and refuse when their count isn't the
  one typed; mark them and commit first, then remove the files whose receipts are all pruned
  (a removal that fails leaves a file the orphan count or a later prune picks up).

### CR3-234: after a prune, an identical file uploaded again by two people is stored twice

*Work pad: U06-10 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S9.2 ("Identical files are stored once, found by the hash in the
  database").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/receipts/__init__.py:163-170`:
  ```text
      any_row = s.scalars(select(Receipt).where(Receipt.sha256 == digest)).first()
      path = None
      if any_row is not None:
          file_id = any_row.fileId
          path = file_path(server.config.receipts_dir, file_id)
      if path is None or not path.exists():
          file_id = secrets.token_hex(16)
  ```
  `first()` takes any row with the hash, pruned or not (the oldest, by id); when its file was
  pruned, a new file is written even though another row's file holds the same bytes.
- **What goes wrong:** Alice's receipt X is pruned (its file F1 removed). She uploads X again:
  a new receipt over a new file F2, as S14.10.5 says. Bob then uploads X: `any_row` is Alice's
  pruned row, F1 is missing, and a third file F3 is written beside F2. Each later prune or
  re-upload can add another.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u06/t/test_u06.py::test_a_third_copy_after_a_prune`: three file ids, two
  `.mkr` files on disk for one content.
- **Remedy:** look for a row with the hash whose file is not pruned (`filePrunedAt IS NULL`,
  deleted or not) before writing a new file.

### CR3-235: a receipt's merchant is never matched against the payees its owner already has

*Work pad: U06-11 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** not built. **Req:** S9.8.2.5.3 ("**merchant**: from the top lines, normalised by
  the user's payee rules (S2.7) and matched against payees they already have").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/receipts/reading.py:299-303`:
  ```text
      if values["payee"]:
          # The merchant as the uploader's payee rules show it (S2.7.7, S9.8.2.5.3).
          from ..store.repos.rules import Rules

          values["payee"] = Rules(s, r.ownerId).rename(values["payee"])
  ```
  The rules are applied; nothing compares the result with the owner's existing payees
  (`parser.find_merchant`, `parser.py:171-185`, returns the top line as it is, title-cased when
  all capitals). The only use of payees is the match score's word overlap
  (`receipts/__init__.py:560, 565-573`).
- **What goes wrong:** the reader gives *Blue Bottle Coffee #042* → *Blue Bottle Coffee*;
  Alice's transactions say *BLUE BOTTLE COFFEE SF*, and with no rule for it the receipt's payee
  stays as read, so her receipts and transactions name one merchant two ways, and search by
  her payee name misses the receipt.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** after the rules, pick the owner's existing payee (from her transactions' payees)
  that the read name best matches above a threshold (word overlap, as `_alike`), and keep the
  read name otherwise.
- **Alternatives:** S9.8.2.5.3 drops "matched against payees they already have", leaving the
  rules to do it.

*Work pad: U06-12 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

  disclosure, a statement)").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/store/tables/receipts.py:76-79`:
  ```text
      CheckConstraint(
          "kind IN ('transaction', 'account', 'item', 'expense', 'gift', 'donation')",
          name="kind",
      ),
  ```
  A loan's documents could ride on `account` (the loan is an account), but
  `receipts.attach_account` is called only for statements, by an import
  (`ui/imports.py:935`) and the connector (`connector/files.py:146`); no route attaches an
  uploaded receipt to an account or loan, and the loan screens offer none.
- **What goes wrong:** Alice uploads her mortgage's closing disclosure: she can attach it to a
  transaction (the closing's), an item (the house, S1.10) or nothing; not to the loan, as S9.3
  promises, so it isn't found from the loan and its retention isn't the loan's.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** `POST /loans/{id}/receipts` (or `/accounts/{id}/receipts`) attaching with kind

  a non-statement document on an account follows the loan's end (paid off) rather than the

### CR3-237: *Jobs at a time* changes nothing until a restart, and receipts uploaded while reading was off are never read when it comes back on

*Work pad: U06-13 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S9.8.7 (Settings › Receipts: **Read receipts** on or off, the
  number of jobs at a time), S9.8.4.1 (an upload queues the receipt).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/receipts/reading.py:190-209`: the thread count is read
  once, when the reader starts at the unlock:
  ```text
          with self.server.db.read() as s:
              n = self.count or settings.get(s, "receiptReadJobs")
  ```
  nothing calls `Reader.start` or `stop` when the setting changes (`runtime.py:390-392` is
  the only start). And `receipts/__init__.py:187` stores an upload made while reading is off as
  `"off"`, which nothing queues again when it is turned on (only *Read again* or *Re-read all*).
- **What goes wrong:** an admin on a Raspberry Pi raises *Jobs at a time* from 1 to 2 for a
  batch of 300 receipts: the queue keeps one thread until the server restarts (which locks it
  until someone types the phrase). An admin who turned reading off for a week of heavy imports
  turns it back on: the week's receipts stay unread, with no *Reading…* or failure to say so,
  and the guide (`settings-receipts.md`) says neither.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** a change of `receiptReadJobs` stops and starts the reader's threads (the queue's
  ids kept); turning **Read receipts** on queues every `off` receipt not pruned.
- **Alternatives:** the guide says the number applies from the next start, and turning reading
  on asks whether to read what came in meanwhile.

### CR3-238: statements go through the receipt reader: *Re-read all* reads a scanned one's first 3 pages and gives it an amount, and one kept by the connector waits *Reading…* until a restart

*Work pad: U06-14 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S9.8.6 (only empty or OCR-suggested fields are replaced), S1.3.3.2
  (a statement read to 30 pages within five minutes), S9.8.4.1.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/settings.py:505-510` marks every receipt row
  `waiting`, statements kept on accounts included:
  ```text
          for r in s.query(Receipt).filter(
              Receipt.deleted.is_(False), Receipt.filePrunedAt.is_(None)
          ):
              r.readStatus, r.readError = "waiting", None
  ```
  and `receipts/reading.py:259` reads each as a receipt, `worker.read(data, media)` with the
  default 3 pages (`ocr.MAX_PAGES`) and 60 s; `save_reading` (`:289, 304-309`) replaces
  `text` and fills the empty `amount` with the parser's total (the largest amount when no
  TOTAL line). A statement the connector keeps (`connector/files.py:142-146`) is stored by
  `receipts.store` as `waiting` and never queued (only `ui/receipts.py:332, 400` and
  `ui/settings.py:517` queue), so it waits for `Reader.start` at the next unlock.
- **What goes wrong:** a scanned 12-page bank statement imported by photo has its 12 pages'
  text; after **Re-read all receipts** it has 3 pages' text (search misses the rest) and an
  amount of, say, $14,210.55 (its largest figure), so its dialog proposes attaching it to a
  transaction of that size. A statement the connector fetched shows *Reading…* in the grid for
  days, then is OCR'd as a receipt.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** the reader knows a statement (a receipt with an `account` link, or a flag set
  when it is kept) and reads it as one (`pages=ocr.STATEMENT_PAGES`, `STATEMENT_TIME_LIMIT`),
  filling no amount; a statement kept without reading is stored `read` (or `off`), not
  `waiting`.

### CR3-239: Settings › Storage leaves deleted receipts' files out of its groups, and nothing can ever remove them

*Work pad: U06-16 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S14.10.4 (the size of the receipts directory and a pie of expired,
  expiring later and kept for ever), S14.10.2 (a file attached to nothing never expires),
  S9.7 ("a deleted receipt doesn't keep it from file pruning").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/receipts/retention.py:257-260` and `:308`, both
  `select(Receipt).where(Receipt.deleted == false())`:
  ```text
      out = Storage(_dir_bytes(receipts_dir), Group(), Group(), Group(), 0)
      years: dict[int, dict[str, Group]] = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(Group))

  ```
  while `orphan_files` (`:286-291`) counts only files no row names, "deleted rows included".
- **What goes wrong:** Alice uploads 40 mistaken 25 MB scans and deletes
  them (nothing links to them). Their 1 GB stays in *Receipts and statements* (the directory's
  size) but in no part of the pie, so the pie's three parts add up to less than the size shown,
  and no button frees it: not prunable (deleted rows aren't looked at), not an orphan (a row
  names it). By S14.10.2 such a file is *kept for ever* and belongs in that part; by S9.7 it
  shouldn't hold its file at all.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** count a file named only by deleted receipts in its own group (*Deleted receipts,
  can be removed*) and let the prune remove it like an expired one (the rows stay, marked
  *File pruned*).
- **Alternatives:** count them under *Kept for ever* and say in S14.10.2 that a deleted
  receipt's file stays until the receipt is restored or for good.

### CR3-243: no test holds S14.2's modes for receipt files and their directory

*Work pad: U06-20 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** test gap. **Req:** S14.2 ("owned by the server's account, mode 0600 in a 0700
  directory").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/keys/files.py:102-104` builds it:
  ```text
      path.parent.mkdir(mode=0o700, parents=True, exist_ok=True)
      tmp = path.with_suffix(".tmp")
      fd = os.open(tmp, os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_TRUNC, 0o600)
  ```
  but no test checks a receipt file's or `receipts/`'s mode (`grep 0o600|0o700` in the tests
  finds the keyring, the queued host commands, a pre-upgrade copy and the socket only), and
  `mkdir(…, exist_ok=True)` leaves an existing directory's mode as it was.
- **What goes wrong:** a change to the writer (or a `receipts/` made by an image or restore
  with 0755) goes unnoticed; the files stay encrypted, so what leaks is sizes, times and count.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** a test that uploads a receipt and asserts 0600 on the file and 0700 on
  `receipts/`; `files.write` sets the directory's mode when it differs, as `host.prepare` does
  for the socket's.

<a id="cr3-244"></a>

### CR3-244: writing a job to the worker has no deadline, so a worker that stops reading hangs its caller and every quick job behind it

*Work pad: U06-22 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S9.8.4.2 ("restarted after a crash or a timeout, so a hostile or
  broken file cannot take down the server").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/receipts/reading.py:120-126`:
  ```text
          head = json.dumps({"op": op, **params, "length": len(data)}).encode()
          try:
              stdin = self.proc.stdin
              stdin.write(len(head).to_bytes(4, "big") + head + len(data).to_bytes(4, "big"))
              stdin.write(data)
              stdin.flush()
              out = self._recv(time.monotonic() + limit)
  ```
  The deadline and memory watch start only in `_recv`; a blocking write of up to 110 MB into
  the pipe (64 KiB of buffer) waits as long as the worker doesn't read. `parsing._call`
  (`parsing.py:24-27`) holds the quick worker's lock across it.
- **What goes wrong:** code a hostile file runs in the worker (or a worker wedged in a library
  call between jobs) stops reading its stdin. The next upload's thumbnail blocks in `write`
  with no limit, holding `parsing._lock`: every later thumbnail, HEIC view, OFX and CSV import
  waits behind it for good, and with U06-2 the first of them holds the database's write lock
  too. Nothing restarts the worker, since no deadline passes.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** write through `select` on the pipe (non-blocking) under the same deadline as the
  answer, killing the worker when it passes.

- **Kind:** untraced. **Req:** —.

  MAPPING_KEYS = {

- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** check the proposal's keys against it (with the column keys the mapping also
  carries), or remove it.

<a id="cr3-246"></a>

### CR3-246: a sign-in from a new address after a month with no sign-ins sends no notice

*Work pad: U07-2 in [`unit-07-users-sharing.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-07-users-sharing.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S10.1.9 ("a sign-in from an address the user hasn't signed in
  from in 30 days (once they have signed in from somewhere; the addresses seen are kept on the
  user for those 30 days …)").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/session.py:352-358` (`_note_address`):
  ```text
      seen = {
          k: v
          for k, v in seen.items()
          if t - dt.datetime.fromisoformat(v) < dt.timedelta(days=ADDRESS_DAYS)
      }
      new = bool(seen) and address not in seen
  ```
  "Once they have signed in from somewhere" is read as "from somewhere in the last 30 days":
  the addresses older than 30 days are dropped first, so after a quiet month `seen` is empty and
  no sign-in is new.
- **What goes wrong:** Gil signs in on Sep 1 and not again. On Oct 2 someone with his password
  signs in from 203.0.113.9: no email, and only an Activity entry of an ordinary sign-in (no
  `signin.new-address`). The users least likely to notice a stranger in their account, those
  who sign in rarely, are the ones the notice never reaches. The same holds the first time
  after an invitation is accepted, as `invitation_accept` records no address
  (`ui/session.py:504-514`), which matches the "first sign-in" reading.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u07/t/test_u07.py::test_new_address_after_a_quiet_month_is_not_emailed`
  (clock moved 31 days; the sign-in from 203.0.113.9 writes no email).
- **Remedy:** decide "has signed in before" from `lastSignInAt` (or any session ever), not from
  the pruned list: `new = (user.lastSignInAt is not None) and address not in seen`, taken before
  `lastSignInAt` is set in `_signed_in`; a test of the quiet month.
- **Alternatives:** keep the last address past the 30 days, so the list is never emptied.

<a id="cr3-247"></a>

### CR3-247: password-reset requests never lock the address; the throttle allows 30, shared with six other routes

*Work pad: U07-3 in [`unit-07-users-sharing.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-07-users-sharing.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S13.7.5.1 ("10 failures in 15 minutes lock **the address they
  came from** for 15 minutes, whoever they named, and so do 10 password-reset requests").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/app.py:59-60`:
  ```text
      # Password resets, re-authentications, reactivations: 30 in 15 minutes per address.
      password_asks: ratelimit.Window = field(default_factory=lambda: ratelimit.Window(30, 15 * 60))
  ```
  and `ui/session.py:431-437`, `password_reset` calls only `_throttle(request)` (that window);
  nothing calls `srv.sign_in.failed` for a reset request. The same window is hit by re-auth,
  sign-up, sign-up confirm, invitation lookup and accept, email-change confirm, reactivation and
  the welcome's admin step (`ui/session.py:416, 444, 463, 480, 494, 507, 520, 531`,
  `ui/welcome.py:117`).
- **What goes wrong:** from one address, 12 reset requests in a row all answer 204, and a
  sign-in from that address right after succeeds: the address is neither locked for 15 minutes
  after the 10th nor kept from signing in. Up to 30 reset emails per address per 15 minutes can
  be sent at a user (each ending the one before, S10.1.5), and the window slides rather than
  locking for a fixed 15 minutes. Review 2's C37 said S13.7.5.1 counts reset requests, not
  failures; the figure and the lock are still not built.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u07/t/test_u07.py::test_ten_reset_requests_do_not_lock_the_address`.
- **Remedy:** count each reset request in `SignInGuard` as a failure of the address (with the
  Settings figures), so the 10th locks the address for sign-in and resets alike; keep the 30
  window for the other token routes or give them their own.
- **Alternatives:** S13.7.5.1 says what the code does (30 a quarter hour per address, shared,
  no lock), if the spam bound is thought enough.

<a id="cr3-248"></a>

### CR3-248: concurrent sign-ins from one address all hash past the lockout; forty at once exhaust a 2 GB server's memory

*Work pad: U07-4 in [`unit-07-users-sharing.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-07-users-sharing.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Severity:** Med (High if the release is meant to face the internet
  directly; the server comes back locked, waiting for the recovery phrase). **Req:** S13.7.5.1
  ("10 failures in 15 minutes lock the address"), S13.7.3 (argon2id at 64 MiB), DEPLOY.md §1
  ("2 GB of memory for MoolaKata (1 GB with receipt reading turned off)"), S15.4 (a restart
  waits for the phrase).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/session.py:178-191` (`sign_in`, a sync route run on
  the thread pool, 40 threads by default):
  ```text
      locked = guard.locked(address)
      if locked:
          raise too_many(locked, "failed sign-ins from this address")
      ...
      with srv.db.tx() as s:
          user = accounts.by_login(s, login)
          ok = passwords.verify(user.passwordHash if user else None, body.password)
          if not ok:
              count_failure(s, guard, login, address, user)
  ```
  The lock is read before the hash and the failure counted only after it
  (`ratelimit.py:102-107, 123-140`), and nothing bounds how many argon2 hashes run at once in
  the process (argon2-cffi releases the GIL; `passwords.py:14`, 64 MiB each). The `_throttle`
  routes count first (`Window.hit`), so they bound at 30 per address; sign-in doesn't.
- **What goes wrong:** one anonymous client, with no account, opens 40 connections and posts
  `{"login": "admin", "password": "x"}` on each at the same moment. All 40 pass `locked()`,
  all 40 hash at once: about 2.6 GiB on top of the server's own memory. On the documented 2 GB
  host (or the 1 GB one) the kernel or the container runtime kills the process; it restarts
  locked and stays down until an admin types the 12 words (S15.4). The address is locked
  afterwards, but a second address (or the next quarter hour) does it again. Below the memory
  limit, it still lets 40 guesses through per address per lockout window, not 10.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u07/s/argon_parallel.py`: 16 threads checking the guard and verifying at
  once: all 16 passed the lock check and hashed; peak RSS 1,070 MiB from 107 MiB (so 40 is
  about 2.6 GiB). Not run against the live server (it would take the machine's memory).
- **Remedy:** (1) a process-wide semaphore around every argon2 hash and verify (sign-in, setup,
  invitation, reset, sign-up, change, re-auth, reactivation), sized to the memory (2 at a time,
  64 MiB each), with a short wait and a 429/503 `Retry-After` beyond it; (2) the guard reserves
  an attempt before the hash (failures plus attempts in flight against the threshold), so the
  11th concurrent attempt is refused before it hashes.
- **Alternatives:** lower argon2's memory cost (S13.7.3 would change) — weaker against offline
  guessing; or a per-address in-flight cap of 1 for sign-in.

<a id="cr3-249"></a>

### CR3-249: *Share these accounts too* shares to each person of the moment: a group's members keep the accounts after leaving it, and a linked server's user gets them without the password again

*Work pad: U07-5 in [`unit-07-users-sharing.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-07-users-sharing.md).*

- **Kind:** privacy. **Req:** S5.1.2 ("offers **Share these accounts too** (at read, and only
  for accounts the owner may share)"), S10.5 (a member holds a group's shares "while they are in
  the group"), S13.7.2 (sharing with a linked server needs the password again).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/sharing.py:235-243`:
  ```text
  def _share_accounts_too(s: Session, by: User, p: Portfolio) -> int:
      """Share these accounts too (S5.1.2): at read, only accounts the owner may share."""
      n = 0
      people = [u for u in access.holders(s, "portfolio", p.id, p.ownerId) if u != p.ownerId]
      for uid, missing in hidden_accounts(s, p, people).items():
  ```
  `holders` expands a group share into its current members, and each missing account is shared
  with `userId=uid` (`:244-253`), never with the group. The re-auth check
  (`ui/sharing.py:215-219`) looks only at linked users newly in `body.users` and at Include
  receipts:
  ```text
      for uid, lvl in body.users.items():
          if uid in linked and lvl != "none" and uid not in current:
              return True
  ```
  so a linked user who already held the portfolio gets the accounts with no password asked.
  The dialog names such a person from `view.users` only (`ui/components/share.js:62-63`), which
  holds no linked users, so the warning reads "can't see …" with no name.
- **What goes wrong:** (1) Alice shares her portfolio *Family* with the group Family at read and
  presses **Share these accounts too**: Carol, Dan and Eve each get a direct read share of
  Alice's brokerage account. When Carol leaves the group, she loses the portfolio but keeps
  the account, and Frank, who joins later, sees the portfolio without it. (2) Alice shared the
  portfolio with Bob on a linked server an hour ago; now she presses the button: her accounts
  cross to Bob's server at the next exchange, with no password asked, under a warning that
  names nobody.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** share each missing account with the same grantee the portfolio has (the group, or
  the user), and run `_reaches_a_link`'s check on the accounts the button would add (linked
  holders among the people); name linked people in the warning from `view.links`.
- **Alternatives:** leave group shares out of the button and say so in the dialog.

### CR3-250: the family tree drops in-laws, step-parents and stepchildren when a spouse dies

*Work pad: U07-6 in [`unit-07-users-sharing.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-07-users-sharing.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S10.10.5 (the table: in-laws "through a spouse, or a child's
  spouse"; "ex-wife, ex-husband | a marriage ended by divorce; the in-laws through it end with
  it"; "wife, husband, widow, widower | *spouse*, ended by death for the last two").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/people/tree.py:83-92` puts a marriage ended by death into
  `widowed`, not `spouses`, and every step and in-law relation reads `spouses` only
  (`:167-190`):
  ```text
          for sp in self._sp(ego):
              for p in self._p(sp):
                  put(p, 5, "parent-in-law")
  ```
  `_joined` (`:227-236`) walks `spouses` too, so the dead spouse's family isn't even a
  *relative*.
- **What goes wrong:** Ann married Bob in 2000; Bob died in 2020; Mae is Bob's mother and Kid
  Bob's son by an earlier marriage. On 2019-01-01 Ann's relations are Bob (husband), Kid
  (stepson), Mae (mother-in-law). On 2021-01-01 they are Bob (husband) alone: Mae and Kid are
  gone, and Kid's stepmother Ann is gone from his. The table ends in-laws only for a divorce;
  a widow keeps her mother-in-law. Gift plans show the relation (S10.10.6), so Mae's plan
  stops saying who she is to Ann.

- **Remedy:** treat `widowed` like `spouses` for step and in-law relations (and in `_joined`),
  keeping `exes` out, as the table says.
- **Alternatives:** if a death is meant to end them too, the table says so beside the divorce
  row.

      if display_name is not None and display_name.strip() != user.displayName:

### CR3-253: re-authentication as the first request after a change of privilege answers 500

*Work pad: U07-9 in [`unit-07-users-sharing.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-07-users-sharing.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S13.7.1 (a new token at every change of privilege), S13.7.2.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/session.py:418-423` (`reauth`) reads the session again
  by the cookie the request came with:
  ```text
      with srv.db.tx() as s:
          user = s.get(User, c.id)
          right = passwords.verify(user.passwordHash, body.password)
          if right:
              row = sessions.find(s, request.cookies.get(sessions.COOKIE)).session
              row.authAt = sessions.now()
  ```
  but `_resolve` (`ui/deps.py:62-67`) has just rotated that token when the user's admin flag or
  setup state changed, deleting the old row and giving the caller the new one (`c.session`).
- **What goes wrong:** an admin adds Bob to Admins. Bob's open tab, idle past 10 minutes, asks
  for his password before making a token (the dialog posts `/session/reauth` first when it
  already knows it must, as Edit user's two-step reset does, `screens/users.js:295`). That
  request rotates his token, then `sessions.find(old)` is `None` and `.session` raises
  `AttributeError`: a 500, his password right. The next try works.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** `row = s.get(Session, c.session.tokenHash)` (the rotated row).

### CR3-254: a sign-up request keeps the person's password hash for good

*Work pad: U07-11 in [`unit-07-users-sharing.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-07-users-sharing.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S10.1.4.2 ("The request stays as the record of it"), R1.13 /
  S14.4 (what is kept), S13.7.3.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/store/tables/users.py:168`
  (`Column("passwordHash", Text, nullable=False)` on `SignupRequest`); `auth/signup.py:209`
  sets the decision and copies the hash to the user (`:200`) but never clears the request's,
  and `reject` (`:234-236`) keeps it too.
- **What goes wrong:** a rejected person's argon2 hash stays in the database (and every backup)
  for ever; an approved user's first password stays beside their later ones after they change
  it. A stolen backup gives an offline guess at passwords people reuse elsewhere, for people
  who may never have been let in.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** make the column nullable (a revision) and clear it when the request is decided
  or its confirmation lapses.

<a id="cr3-255"></a>

### CR3-255: the sign-in guard keeps every username ever tried, at any length, until a restart

*Work pad: U07-12 in [`unit-07-users-sharing.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-07-users-sharing.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S13.7.5.1, S13.7.5.5 (counters in memory).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ratelimit.py:127-131`:
  ```text
          with self._lock:
              now = self._clock()
              u = self._by_user[username.lower()]
              self._trim(u, now)
              u.append(now)
  ```
  `_trim` empties a deque but no key is ever removed (`_failures` likewise, by address); the
  login is the body's `login` with no length limit but the 1 MB body (`ui/session.py:54-56`).
  `_codes` keeps a pending sign-in's count when it expires with fewer than five wrong codes.
- **What goes wrong:** each failed sign-in with a new login string adds an entry kept until the
  process restarts; at up to 1 MB a login, ten per address per quarter hour, a scan from many
  addresses grows the server's memory without bound. Low on its own (the lockout and argon2's
  cost slow it), but it grows on the documented 1–2 GB hosts.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** cap `login` (254 characters, an email's limit) in `SignIn`, and drop empty deques
  and lapsed entries in `_trim`'s callers (or sweep them with the hourly prune).

  `GET /signup` and the reset screen carry `passwordMinLength`.
