### CR3-260: `/mcp` answers 500, not a JSON-RPC error, when `params` or `arguments` isn't an object

*Work pad: U07-18 in [`unit-07-users-sharing.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-07-users-sharing.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S10.9.6 (JSON-RPC 2.0), S10.9.5 (each tool's input checked against
  its schema, review 2's B32), S13.7.8.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/mcp.py:404` and `:321-327`:
  ```text
      method, id_, params = msg["method"], msg.get("id"), msg.get("params") or {}
  ...
      name = params.get("name")
      args = params.get("arguments") or {}
      ...
      unknown = set(args) - set(t.params)
      if unknown or not isinstance(args, dict):
  ```
  `params` is used as a dict without a check (`initialize` at `:408` too), and `args` is put in
  a `set` before its type is checked.
- **What goes wrong:** a token holder's client sends `"params": [1]` to `initialize` or
  `tools/call` (`AttributeError`), or `"arguments": [{"a": 1}]` (`TypeError: unhashable`): each
  is a 500 with an error id, where JSON-RPC asks for -32602 *Invalid params*. Only a token's
  holder can reach it; nothing leaks.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u07/t/test_u07_mcp.py` (the three bodies raise `AttributeError`,
  `AttributeError`, `TypeError`; `run4.txt`).
- **Remedy:** refuse a non-object `params` with -32602 before the method is read, and test
  `isinstance(args, dict)` before anything else; a test of each.

<a id="cr3-261"></a>

### CR3-261: the answer to an exchange is worked out whole before a byte goes, and a caller waits 20 seconds

*Work pad: U08-4 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S11.4 (a first full snapshot fills the copy part by part), S13.5
  ("waits at most 20 seconds for each read"); D3-4 (the parts kept in memory).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/links/manage.py:582-591` works out *every* part of the
  change (`serve.change_for`, which builds every document, splits, signs and seals them all,
  `serve.py:347-414`) inside the request, before answering with the first; `PENDING[link.id] =
  parts[1:]` keeps the rest in memory. `serve.py:400-405` commits `sentSeq` and `LinkSent` as
  it does. The caller's client gives up after 20 s without a byte (`net.py:23` `TIMEOUT =
  httpx.Timeout(20.0, connect=10.0)`).
- **What goes wrong:** a household whose change takes A more than 20 s to work out (a first
  full snapshot of a few accounts with decades of transactions, worked out once per viewer,
  each document dumped to JSON three times for its hash, size and date: `serve.py:342-344,
  467, 471-472`) is never pulled by B: B's read times out (*didn't answer (ReadTimeout)*), A has
  already moved `sentSeq` on, so B's next `have` differs and A works out a full change again,
  larger than the last, every hour. The whole change, every part sealed, is held in A's memory
  at once (D3-4's case: the protocol's 2 GB example is more than DEPLOY gives the server).
- **Run:** no; rests on reading. How large a household has to be for the 20 s to bite was not
  measured.
- **Remedy:** the answering side commits `sentSeq` only when the caller confirms (its next
  `have`), works out the first part first and answers with it, and works out each later part
  when `next` asks for it, from a cursor (D3-4's remedy), so neither the answer nor memory
  grows with the change; or the exchange answers at once with `more` and the change follows
  in `next` calls.
- **Alternatives:** a longer read timeout for `exchange` and `next` alone.

<a id="cr3-262"></a>

### CR3-262: a peer's directory is kept unchecked: an avatar that isn't an object breaks Settings › Linked servers and the Linked list

*Work pad: U08-9 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S11.3 (the directory: "display name, username, email, sex and
  stable id", "household name and avatar (S16.1)"), `link-protocol.md` §4.2 (the receiver's
  limits on what it keeps); DC-10.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/links/manage.py:327-331, 349-351, 363`:
  ```text
      h = payload.get("household") or {}
      link.peerName = h.get("serverName") or link.peerName
      link.householdName = h.get("name") or link.householdName
      if h.get("avatar") is not None:
          link.householdAvatar = json.dumps(h["avatar"])
  ...
                  username=str(r.get("username") or rid)[:64],
                  displayName=name,
                  email=r.get("email"),
  ...
          u.email = r.get("email")
  ```
  and `ui/links.py:214` / `ui/linked.py:83` give it back as `json.loads(link.householdAvatar)`
  into fields typed `dict | None`; `RemoteUser.email` is `str | None` (`ui/links.py:30`).
- **What goes wrong:** a peer (a newer release, a bug there, or a hostile peer) sends `"avatar":
  ["x"]` or an email that isn't a string: every admin's Settings › Linked servers answers 500
  (pydantic refuses the list), as does `/ui/api/linked` for everyone that household shares
  with, until the peer sends something else. Nothing bounds the server name, household name,
  remote id or avatar either: a 15 MB avatar is stored in the main database at each exchange
  and sent to every browser that lists links or linked households.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u08/t/test_u08.py::test_peer_avatar_not_a_dict_breaks_settings`
  (`householdAvatar = '["x"]'`: `GET /ui/api/settings/links` fails with `ValidationError:
  householdAvatar Input should be a valid dictionary`, `run3.txt`).
- **Remedy:** `apply_directory` keeps only what the protocol names, each checked: the avatar
  as S16.1 shapes it (an object of known keys, an image as a data URL of an image type under a
  size), names cut to §4.2's lengths, the email a string under 254 characters or none, the id
  a string under 64; anything else is dropped, never stored. A test with a malformed directory.

<a id="cr3-263"></a>

### CR3-263: a paused link holds back a share's removal, which S11.4 says still crosses

*Work pad: U08-10 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** privacy. **Req:** S11.4 ("A Pause (S11.7.2) stops data, never a removal or an end,
  which still cross"), R3.4 ("Revoking it ends access … on a linked server at its next
  exchange"), `link-protocol.md` §4.3.5; review 2's C18.
- **Code:** a paused side neither calls nor answers an exchange: `manage.py:445-448`
  (`if link.state not in ("active", "pending"): return`), `exchange_all` (`:655-662`, active
  and pending only), and `routes.py:188` `verified(request, ("active", "pending"))`, which
  refuses `link-paused`. Removals travel only inside a change (`serve.py:371, 390`), which only
  an exchange carries. An end has its own call (`/link/v1/end`, allowed while paused), so ends
  do cross; removals don't.
- **What goes wrong:** Alice stops sharing her brokerage account with her parents. Their admin
  had paused the link for two weeks (or pauses it again each time it resumes): Alice's server
  is refused `link-paused` at every exchange, and the parents' copy keeps the account, shown to
  whoever it named whenever the copy is unlocked, until the pause ends. S11.4 promises the
  removal crosses anyway.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** a paused side still answers an exchange, and still makes one, carrying only the
  registry's `removed` (and taking the peer's), no data and no directory; or a `removed` call
  of its own, allowed while paused, as `end` is.
- **Alternatives:** S11.4 says a removal waits for the pause to end (and R3.4 with it).

<a id="cr3-264"></a>

### CR3-264: a certificate change that a peer missed is never announced to it again

*Work pad: U08-11 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S11.1.3 ("a server whose certificate's key changes announces the new
  one to its peers … before its next exchange"), `link-protocol.md` §4.5.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/links/manage.py:180-189`:
  ```text
      if (key or None) == (told or None):
          return 0
      n = 0
      for link_id in ids_:
          with contextlib.suppress(LinkError):  # it learns at the next exchange instead
              call(srv, link_id, "/link/v1/certificate", {"fingerprint": key})
              n += 1
      with srv.db.tx() as s:
          settings.put(s, "linkCertificate", key, None)
  ```
  The new key is recorded as told whether or not each peer was reached, and nothing at the
  next exchange tells it (the comment says it learns then; no code does that).
- **What goes wrong:** B's own certificate key changes while A is offline for an hour (or A is
  only reached by B's calls, which come later). B marks the key told; A's next connection to B
  presents an unannounced key, is refused, and A's link waits for an admin to compare and
  accept it: the case §4.5's announcement exists to spare. In proxy mode the key is found by
  connecting to the server's own address (`certs.py:61-91`); when that fails (no hairpin route
  through the router), `own_key` answers None, which is announced as `{"fingerprint": None}`
  (ignored by the peer) and recorded, so the next successful probe announces again: harmless,
  but the same loop.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** keep, per link, the certificate key last told it, and announce to each link that
  hasn't been told, at every run of the task, until it has.

<a id="cr3-265"></a>

### CR3-265: one link's malformed change stops the hourly exchange for every link after it, unrecorded

*Work pad: U08-12 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S11.4 (each link pulled hourly), S11.9.5.3 (a change that fails "is set
  aside and reported").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/links/manage.py:663-667`:
  ```text
      for link_id in wanted:
          ...
          with contextlib.suppress(LinkError):  # recorded on the link; alerts follow it
              exchange(srv, link_id)
  ```
  `exchange` can raise more than `LinkError` after the call: `receive.take` raises
  `ChangeError` for a change whose signature fails (`receive.py:26`, `changes.py:88-94`),
  `KeyError` for an item without `id` (`receive.py:49`), `ValueError` for a `seq` or `more`
  that isn't a number (`receive.py:27`, `manage.py:500`), and `serve.sealed_receipt` raises
  `ChangeError` (`serve.py:434-435`). None is recorded on the link (the success was recorded
  just before, `manage.py:486`).
- **What goes wrong:** a peer on a release with a bug (or a hostile one) answers with a change
  whose signature doesn't check: that exchange raises `ChangeError` out of `exchange_all`, the
  task stops, and every link after it in the list isn't exchanged that hour, or any hour while
  the peer keeps answering so. The failing link shows a fresh *last exchange* and no error.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** `exchange_all` catches every exception per link, records it on that link
  (`lastError`, `failingSince`) and goes on to the next; `exchange` records failures after its
  first call too (U08-1's remedy).

<a id="cr3-266"></a>

### CR3-266: a linked server's address is exempt from the loopback refusal when its host is named like the SMTP server or the node

*Work pad: U08-13 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S11.2 ("never a loopback, link-local or cloud metadata address,
  whoever names it"), S13.5; review 2's K18.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/net.py:103-118`: the exemption is by host name:
  `allowed, named = host in self._hosts, host in self._named` … `for a in addrs if not named
  else ():`; `ui/prices.py:154-159` puts the SMTP host and an admin-named node in `named`, and
  the peers' hosts in `hosts`. A peer's host that is the same string as one of those is
  exempt.
- **What goes wrong:** the SMTP server is `127.0.0.1` (a relay on the host, a common setup). A
  code holder joins with address `https://127.0.0.1:2019`; A's admin approves the request (the
  address is shown, but small); from then on A sends its signed `exchange`, `deliver` and
  `end` POSTs to a service on its own loopback. Only `https` is used, so a plain service fails
  the handshake; one that speaks TLS gets the calls. Low: it needs an approval of a visible
  address, and the calls are fixed JSON.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** the guard keeps the exemption per use: `check` takes who asks (a link, the SMTP
  client, the node), and only an admin-named host asked for by its own client is exempt; or
  `join`/`answer_join` refuse a link address whose host resolves to a forbidden range,
  whatever is named.

<a id="cr3-267"></a>

### CR3-267: what crosses of documents differs from S11.5.1: a gift's or a donation's receipt files never cross, and a tax book's document list crosses without Include receipts (D2-2)

*Work pad: U08-14 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S11.5.1 ("Receipts cross only when the owner ticks Include
  receipts … Without the tick, … a gift's documents (their file names, notes and links) stay
  home too. A tax book's document files come later (L6)"), S11.4; D2-2.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/links/serve.py:256-262`:
  ```text
                  if it.kind == "gift-plan" and not it.includeReceipts:
                      doc = _without_documents(doc)
  ...
      for it in items.values():
          if it.kind == "account" and it.includeReceipts:
              for f in receipt_files(srv, it.id):
  ```
  and `allowed_file` (`:329-338`) allows only an account's receipts. A tax book's documents
  are its `/tax-books/{id}` document (`serve.py:170-174`, `ui/taxes.py:396-406`: each document's
  `receiptId` and `fileName`), sent as it is, tick or not.
- **What goes wrong:** with **Include receipts** on a gift plan, the gift's documents' names and
  notes cross but their files never do, so the linked Gifts screen lists receipts that can't be
  opened; the same for a giving plan's donation receipts. A tax book shared with the CPA's
  server *without* the tick sends each tax document's file name and receipt id. On D2-2: the
  code follows S11.5.1 and L6 (no tax book document file crosses); under the other reading
  (S11.4, the Share dialog's "a tax book's documents too", milestone 5) they are not built.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** with the tick, every receipt the shared item's screens name (gifts, donations,
  accounts) is a `file:` document and allowed by `allowed_file`; without it, the tax book's
  document list crosses without `fileName` and `receiptId`, as a gift's documents are
  stripped; D2-2's decision settles the tax book's files.
- **Needs the user:** D2-2 (whether a tax book's document files cross now or with L6).

<a id="cr3-268"></a>

### CR3-268: S11.8's records: a restart's release isn't recorded, and the copy's and the link's events don't say which link

*Work pad: U08-15 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S11.8 ("every unlock (who), failed unlock, release (unused, Lock
  now or restart), access key made, and reset of a linked copy"; "each pause and resume on the
  side that made it").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/links/copies.py:48, 64, 67, 83-89, 100, 147-153` and
  `manage.py:148, 162, 230, 285, 319, 398, 425, 629` record `link.copy-*`, `link.paused`,
  `link.ended` and the rest with no `subject_kind`/`subject_id`, and `ui/activity.py:123-140`
  words them as "a linked copy", "a link" ("{actor} unlocked a linked copy"); only
  `link.pulled`, `link.served` and `link.viewed` name their link. Nothing records the release
  at a restart (the copies live in memory, `copy.py:94-103`; no `link.copy-locked` with
  `restart` is written at startup).
- **What goes wrong:** a server with links to the parents and to the CPA shows "Carol gave
  wrong words for a linked copy" and "Margaret unlocked a linked copy" with nothing to say
  which household's copy; an admin can't tell from Activity whose data was opened. A restart
  that locks both copies leaves no record.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** every `link.*` entry carries `subject_kind="link", subject_id=link_id`, and its
  wording names the server; the startup records one `link.copy-locked` (`restart`) for each
  link whose copy has a key.

<a id="cr3-269"></a>

### CR3-269: the guides and a dialog say other than S11.1 and the code in three places

*Work pad: U08-16 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S11.1.1, S11.1.2, S11.9.8.
- **Where:**
  1. `web/ui/guides/dialog-link-join.md:6-7`: "sends this server's address, key, household
     name and users"; S11.1.2 and the code (`manage.py:266-269`: `{code, address, t}`) send
     no household or users before approval.
  2. `web/ui/components/links.js:348-349`, the invitation dialog: "It holds this server's
     link address and key fingerprint." (the code also holds a one-time secret, S11.1.1; its
     own guide, `dialog-link-invite.md`, says so; the mockup's version is D2-8).
  3. `web/ui/guides/settings-links.md` (Linked copies) and `links.js:80` ("Copy reset: make
     its new access key"): Reset copy deletes the copy and its key, and the admin makes new
     words next; S11.9.8 says Reset copy "makes a new copy key with new words", and the code
     (`copies.py:92-100`) does what the guide says.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** the join guide says "sends this server's address and key, and its certificate's
  fingerprint when its certificate is its own; nothing else until they approve"; the dialog
  names the secret; S11.9.8 says what Reset copy does (or the code shows the words at once).

<a id="cr3-273"></a>

### CR3-273: no link test goes through `net.Client`, so the ceiling, the timeouts and a long stream's checks are never run on a link

*Work pad: U08-20 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** test gap. **Req:** S13.5, `link-protocol.md` §1.4, §4.3.4; DC-1.
- **Code:** `web/server/tests/linkpair.py:47-76`: the `Wire` client hands each call to the peer's
  `TestClient`, so `test_links.py` and `test_copies.py` never pass through `net.Client`'s
  20 MB `_Capped` ceiling, its 10 s/20 s timeouts, or a stream long enough for U08-6's checks.
  `test_core.py:313-321` tests the client alone.
- **What goes wrong:** U08-1 (a receipt past the ceiling stops the link) and U08-6 (a
  minute-long stream refused) pass every test.
- **Remedy:** the `Wire` applies the same ceiling as `net.Client` (or wraps its answers in
  `net._Capped`), and tests send a receipt over it and one that takes over a minute (a clock
  moved between headers and end).

<a id="cr3-274"></a>

### CR3-274: the name a delivered receipt goes by is outside the signature

*Work pad: U08-21 in [`unit-08-linked-servers.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-08-linked-servers.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S11.1.3 (every call signed over method, path, body hash, time
  and nonce), S11.9.6.3 (a file renamed onto another receipt fails to open),
  `link-protocol.md` §1.2 ("not even a proxy in front of the peer reads it"), §4.3.4.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/links/client.py:195-200` signs the `deliver` call over
  the path and the sealed bytes' digest, and sends the receipt's id in `MK-Link-File`, which
  the signed text doesn't name; `routes.py:258, 299` takes the id from that header.
- **What goes wrong:** a reverse proxy in front of the receiving server (which terminates TLS,
  and which §1.2 treats as untrusted) swaps the `MK-Link-File` headers of two delivered
  receipts: both are kept under each other's ids, and since the associated data names the
  receipt, neither opens (*can't be opened*, and their type is never found). It reads nothing
  and forges nothing; it can only spoil receipts, which the next full change sends again.
  Low.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** the signed text of a `deliver` carrying a file names it (the path's query,
  `/link/v1/deliver?file=<id>`, which the signature already covers).

<a id="cr3-275"></a>

### CR3-275: with an upload limit of 19 MB, uploads are never counted against the 100 MB in flight

*Work pad: U09-2 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S13.7.6 ("at most 100 MB of uploads in flight per user"), S16.8,
  S16.10.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/middleware.py:242-258`:
  ```text
          limit = (
              self.upload_limit()
              ...
              else LINK_LIMIT
              if to_link
              else JSON_LIMIT
          )
          ...
          upload = limit != JSON_LIMIT and limit != LINK_LIMIT
  ```
  and `app.py:117-119` (`upload_cap`): `mb = max(settings.get(s, "importMaxMb"),
  settings.get(s, "receiptMaxMb"))` … `return (mb + 1) * 1024 * 1024`. `LINK_LIMIT` is
  `20 * 1024 * 1024` (`middleware.py:38`).
- **What goes wrong:** whether a request is an upload is told from the size of its limit, not
  from its kind. An admin who sets both size limits so the larger is 19 MB (Settings › Imports
  and › Receipts accept 1–200 and 1–100) makes `upload_cap()` exactly `LINK_LIMIT`, so every
  multipart upload, and every sealed receipt a linked server delivers, is treated as a JSON body:
  nothing is added to `in_flight`, and one user's uploads in flight are unbounded (each still
  at most 20 MB). Any other value works.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading (the arithmetic is exact).
- **Remedy:** keep the kind apart from the limit (`upload = ctype.startswith("multipart/") or
  (to_link and ctype.startswith("application/octet-stream"))`).
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

<a id="cr3-276"></a>

### CR3-276: a refusal for an address outside Settings › Network carries none of S13.7.7's headers and isn't logged

*Work pad: U09-3 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S13.7.7 ("Response headers on every response, refusals
  included"), S13.7.9 (failures logged), S16.14.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/app.py:283-296`, the order of the middleware (the last
  added runs first):
  ```text
      app.add_middleware(middleware.SecurityHeaders, tls=server.config.mode == "tls")
      app.add_middleware(middleware.AccessLog)
      app.add_middleware(middleware.AddressRanges, ranges=server.network_ranges)
      app.add_middleware(
          middleware.ClientAddress, mode=server.config.mode, trusted=server.config.trusted_proxies
      )
  ```
  `AddressRanges` (`middleware.py:166-175`) answers `_send_problem(404, …)(send)` with the
  `send` it was given, which is outside `AccessLog` and `SecurityHeaders`; `_send_problem`
  (`:56-68`) writes only `content-type` and `content-length`. The comment above the list
  (`app.py:283-284`: "the client's address, then the log, the headers, the Origin check …")
  names an order the code doesn't have: the ranges come before the log and the headers.
- **What goes wrong:** a browser outside the allowed ranges (a phone on mobile data, say, with
  `/ui` limited to the home network) gets a 404 with no CSP, `nosniff`, `frame-ancestors`,
  COOP, CORP, `Referrer-Policy` or, in `tls` mode, HSTS; and no access-log line is written for
  it, so an admin looking for why someone is refused finds nothing. The body is fixed JSON, so
  the missing headers expose nothing today; the missing log line is the practical loss.
- **Run:** yes, `/home/dev/.claude/jobs/b8ac83ff/tmp/u09/mw.py` (output `mw.txt`): with `/ui`
  limited to `10.0.0.0/8`, `GET /ui/api/session` answered 404 with no `content-security-policy`
  and no `x-content-type-options`, and no `moolakata.access` line was written for it.
- **Remedy:** add `AddressRanges` before `AccessLog` and `SecurityHeaders` (so it runs inside
  them), keeping `ClientAddress` outermost; a test that a refused address's 404 carries the
  headers.
- **Alternatives:** `_send_problem` adds the common headers itself, as every early refusal would
  then need.

<a id="cr3-277"></a>

### CR3-277: `trusted_proxies` takes whole networks, which S13.3 rules out

*Work pad: U09-5 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S13.3 ("forwarded headers are trusted only from the configured
  proxy addresses, each the proxy's own address and never a whole private range, since anything
  else in it could pose as the proxy"); review 2's K1 remedy ("the example must trust only the
  proxy's exact address").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/config.py:149-156`:
  ```text
      for entry in server.get("trusted_proxies", []):
          try:
              proxies.append(ipaddress.ip_network(entry, strict=False))
  ```
  and `middleware.py:82-87` (`_is_trusted`: `any(addr in net for net in self.trusted)`).
- **What goes wrong:** an operator who writes `trusted_proxies = ["172.16.0.0/12"]` (the
  pattern review 2's K1 found in the example, and what many proxy guides suggest for Docker)
  starts without a word; every container on any Docker bridge can then send
  `X-Forwarded-For: <any address>` and be believed: it escapes the address lockout of
  S13.7.5.1 (a new "address" per attempt) and passes Settings › Network's ranges by naming an
  allowed address. The example file and operations.md say the exact address (`moolakata.toml:16`,
  `operations.md:83`), so only a misconfiguration is exposed, which is what S13.3's "never"
  is there to refuse. Host commands no longer depend on it (they use the socket), so the
  damage stops at the lockout and the ranges. Low.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** accept only single addresses (`ip_address`, or a network whose prefix is the full
  length) and refuse the start otherwise, naming S13.3; a test.
- **Alternatives:** accept a range but log a warning at every start; weaker than S13.3 says.

<a id="cr3-278"></a>

### CR3-278: the self-signed certificate is never made again when it expires, and a pair half written is kept for good

*Work pad: U09-6 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S13.3 ("given no certificate, the server makes a self-signed one on
  first start … a change to the list makes it again"), S13.6 (a certificate it can't load
  refuses the start), S11.1.3 (peers pin its key).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/listener.py:33-35, 53, 58-67`:
  ```text
      if cert_path.exists() and key_path.exists() and set(names) <= _covers(cert_path):
          return cert_path, key_path
      ...
          .not_valid_after(now + dt.timedelta(days=825))
      ...
      fd = os.open(key_path, os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_TRUNC, 0o600)
      ...
      cert_path.write_bytes(cert.public_bytes(serialization.Encoding.PEM))
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** (1) the pair is kept as long as it covers the names, whatever its dates:
  825 days after the first start of a `tls`-mode server (every TrueNAS install, DC-2), it
  serves an expired certificate. Browsers that had accepted it refuse it again; the connector
  and linked servers pin the key and may go on, but anything that checks dates fails, and
  nothing alerts an admin beforehand. (2) The key is written over first and the certificate
  after, neither through a temporary file: a stop between the two (a full disk, a kill) leaves
  a new key beside the old certificate, which still "covers" the names, so every later start
  keeps the mismatched pair and refuses with "the TLS certificate or key cannot be loaded" until
  an operator deletes `tls/` by hand.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** remake the pair when it is within, say, 30 days of `not_valid_after` (keeping the
  key, so pins hold, and only re-signing the certificate), and write each file to a temporary
  name and rename both; S13.3 says the certificate is renewed before it expires.
- **Alternatives:** a much longer validity (browsers ignore it for a self-signed one, and the
  pins are on the key), saying so in S13.3.

<a id="cr3-279"></a>

### CR3-279: this server's own certificate is read over a raw socket, outside the egress guard

*Work pad: U09-7 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S13.5 ("Outbound calls go through one httpx client with an egress
  guard … Each call connects to the address the guard checked").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/certs.py:61-91` (`_probe`):
  ```text
          with (
              socket.create_connection((host, port), timeout=5) as raw,
              ssl.create_default_context().wrap_socket(raw, server_hostname=host),
          ):
  ```
  and again without verification (`:81-88`), to the host of `own_address(s)`, the link
  address an admin typed in Settings › Linked servers; called by `own_key` from link and
  connector routes (`ui/links.py:253`, `links/manage.py:172, 209, 267`,
  `connector/routes.py:144`, `connector/manage.py:81`), cached ten minutes.
- **What goes wrong:** the probe resolves the name itself and connects to whatever it resolves
  to, loopback, link-local and metadata addresses included, with up to two 5-second connects
  in the request that asked. The address is an admin's, so nothing is reached that an admin
  couldn't name; but it is a second outbound path that S13.5 says doesn't exist, and a link
  address on a loopback or metadata address, which the guard refuses for every other call
  (S13.5: "a link's address … never is" allowed by name), is reached here.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** make the probe through `net.Guard.check` (its address) or a `net.Client` with no
  pins whose TLS failure says "not public", then a second, pinned-to-anything handshake through
  the same backend; or S13.5 names this one exception.
- **Alternatives:** in `proxy` mode, take the certificate from the admin (paste the
  fingerprint), as it is the proxy's, not a call to make.

### CR3-280: the middleware's refusals carry an error id that is never logged

*Work pad: U09-8 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S13.7.8 ("Each error carries an id that is also in the log, with
  its status and code, refusals included"); review 2's K19 ("every problem logged with its id,
  status and code"), marked done in milestone 9 part 1.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/middleware.py:41-53`:
  ```text
  def _send_problem(status: int, code: str, detail: str, headers=()):
      body = json.dumps(
          {
              ...
              "errorId": new_error_id(),
  ```
  It writes no log line; `errors.problem_response` (`errors.py:75-83`) is the one that logs
  `problem <id> <status> <code>`, and the middleware doesn't use it. `_send_problem` answers the
  Origin refusal (403 `origin-refused`), the body limits (413 `too-large`, 429
  `uploads-in-flight`), Settings › Network's 404, and the locked server's 503 `locked`.
- **What goes wrong:** a user behind a proxy that drops `X-Forwarded-Proto` has every change
  refused with *This request did not come from MoolaKata's own page* and error id
  `err_3f2a…`; the admin searches the log for that id and finds nothing (and, for the 404 of
  U09-3, not even an access line). K19's half about refusals holds for route refusals only.
- **Run:** yes, `tmp/u09/mw.py` (output `mw.txt`): a `POST /ui/api/session` with a foreign

  404's). `test_middleware.py:118` checks a route's 404 only.

<a id="cr3-281"></a>

### CR3-281: between the unlock and readiness the API is served, not answered 503

*Work pad: U09-9 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S13.8.4 ("503 while the server is starting"), S13.6 (`starting`
  between the unlock and readiness).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/app.py:287`:
  ```text
      app.add_middleware(middleware.Locked, locked=lambda: server.store is None and not server.ready)
  ```
  `runtime.build` sets `server.store = store` (`runtime.py:186`) before it ensures the
  categories and benchmarks, registers the tasks, applies the egress guard, takes the host
  events, records the unlock, starts the tasks and the reader, and only then
  `server.ready = True` (`:393`). Nothing else checks `ready` for `/ui/api`, `/link`,
  `/connector` or `/mcp` (`grep -rn "\.ready" src`: `app.py`, `runtime.py` only).
- **What goes wrong:** for the seconds of that window (longer in dev with `--seed`), `/healthz`
  says `starting` while the API, `/link` and `/connector` answer as if ready: a linked
  server's exchange or the connector's report can arrive before `apply_egress` has set the
  guard's hosts or before the categories exist, and a page open on the locked screen that
  polls `/ui/api/unlock` may move on to sign-in and get answers from a half-built server. The
  window is short in production, so Low.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** the gate is `not server.ready` for everything but the open paths, with a 503
  `starting` and `Retry-After` while `server.store is not None`.
- **Alternatives:** set `server.store` last in `build`, after the rest is done on a local.

<a id="cr3-282"></a>

### CR3-282: each price refresh starts its provider's rate limits afresh, so two refreshes in an hour can make twice Tiingo's calls

*Work pad: U09-10 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S13.4 ("They run in a thread pool with per-provider rate limits"),
  S4.4.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/tasks.py:102-104`:
  ```text
      def set(self, provider: str, limits: Sequence[tuple[int, float]]) -> None:
          with self._lock:
              self._windows[provider] = [Window(n, s) for n, s in limits]
  ```
  called at every refresh (`prices/refresh.py:297-299`: `ctx.limits.set(src.name,
  cls.limits)` in `build`), with Tiingo's `((50, 3600), (1000, 86400))`
  (`prices/tiingo.py:20`). Each `Window` starts with an empty `seen`.
- **What goes wrong:** the daily refresh makes 50 calls to Tiingo; an admin presses **Refresh
  prices** ten minutes later (`run_now`), and the new run's windows are empty, so it makes 50
  more within the same hour: Tiingo answers 429 and the run records failures (and, past the
  threshold, an alert) for a limit MoolaKata was meant to keep. The same for the 1,000 a day,
  Massive's 5 a minute and Cboe's 30 a minute. And a refresh waiting for Tiingo's hourly
  window holds one of the pool's four workers (`tasks.py:130`, `workers: int = 4`) for as
  long as it waits, so with backups and link pulls running, mail and alerts queue behind it.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** `set` keeps the existing windows (and their `seen`) when the limits are
  unchanged; only a changed limit replaces them. A test that two runs within the hour share
  one window.
- **Alternatives:** register each source's limits once at start.

<a id="cr3-283"></a>

### CR3-283: the OpenAPI document lists only the codes written as literals; the link, copy, wallet and connector refusals, and any code a peer sends, are left out

*Work pad: U09-11 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S13.8.3 ("Codes are stable strings … listed in the OpenAPI
  document"); review 2's N16, marked done in milestone 9 part 7.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/contract.py:53-74` (`problem_codes`) collects the second
  argument of `Problem()`, `refuse()`, `FieldError()`, `_send_problem()`,
  `problem_response()` only when it is a string constant:
  ```text
              if (
                  name in PROBLEM_CALLS
                  and isinstance(arg, ast.Constant)
                  and isinstance(arg.value, str)
              ):
  ```
  Codes passed through a variable are skipped. Among them: `ui/links.py:243-245`
  (`refuse(…, e.code, e.message, status)` for every `LinkError`), `:371-373`
  (`Problem(status, e.code, …)` for copy errors), `ui/linked.py:150`, `ui/wallets.py:151`,
  `ui/connector.py:195`. `LinkError`'s codes (`links/*.py`: `already-linked`, `code-invalid`,
  `file-refused`, `no-certificate`, `not-moolakata`, `not-requested`, `state-invalid`,
  `unreachable`, and `certificate-changed` from `links/client.py:80-86`) and, through
  `links/client.py:143-147`, *any* code the peer answers with that matches `_CODE`, become
  this server's own API codes:
  ```text
          code = out.get("code")
          code = code if isinstance(code, str) and _CODE.match(code) else "refused"
          ...
          raise LinkError(code, refusal(code, r.status_code, out.get("protocols")))
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** `grep -c '"not-moolakata"\|"unreachable"\|"certificate-changed"\|"not-linked"'
  web/ui/api/openapi.json` finds none of them, though Settings › Linked servers answers each
  (the join answers `unreachable` with 502, a status S13.8.4 doesn't list). A client written
  against the document's enum meets codes it was told can't happen, and a linked server
  decides some of this server's codes. `/home/dev/.claude/jobs/b8ac83ff/tmp/u09/codes.txt`
  lists the 49 calls whose code isn't a literal; most pass a code that is a literal elsewhere,
  the link, copy, wallet and connector ones don't.
- **Run:** yes: `tmp/u09/codes.py` (an AST walk of `src/moolakata`, output `codes.txt`), and
  the grep above on the generated document.
- **Remedy:** each error class declares its codes (`LinkError.CODES`, …) and `problem_codes`
  adds them; a peer's code is mapped to one of this server's own (`refused` with the peer's
  in the log), never passed through; S13.8.4 lists 502 or the join answers 422.
- **Alternatives:** a test that walks the routes' refusals by calling them is heavier; the
  AST walk could follow a variable to its class's literal codes instead.

<a id="cr3-284"></a>

### CR3-284: the Dashboard has no upcoming recurring transactions and no giving against its target

*Work pad: U09-12 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** not built. **Req:** S12.2.1 ("upcoming recurring transactions, occasions inside
  their reminder time (S7.7.3), giving against its target, the **Plans** card …, and stale
  prices, stale valuations, or sources and imports that need attention").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/dashboard.py:55-65` (the `Dashboard` model: net worth,
  change, investments, cash, groups, attention, budget) and `web/ui/screens/dashboard.js`
  (KPI tiles, the net-worth chart, Accounts by group, `Plans`, Needs attention). Neither has a
  recurring-transactions card or a giving card; `_attention` (`dashboard.py:151-270`) adds
  occasions, filings, mismatches, overdue statements, lots without basis, uncategorised rows
  and stale prices, but no stale statement valuation (S4.3) and no failing source or import.
  Allocation is later work (L5), so it is not counted here.
- **What goes wrong:** a household with a pledge of $500 a year and a mortgage payment due on
  the 1st sees neither on the Dashboard; they are on Giving and on a budget's Recurring tab
  only. No milestone, gap or later item schedules them (`S17-implementation-09-milestones.md`
  names "Dashboard (net worth, accounts, needs attention)" and the Plans and budget cards).
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** a card of the next recurring transactions (from `budgets.recurring.detect` over
  the user's accounts, as `cash_short` does) and a giving card (the year's target and given,
  as the Giving screen's header); `_attention` adds a holding valued from a statement over 90
  days (as the alert does). Or S12.2.1 drops what the Dashboard won't show, with the mockup.
- **Alternatives:** schedule them as a later item.

<a id="cr3-285"></a>

### CR3-285: one failing check keeps every other check's *source failing* alerts from resolving

*Work pad: U09-13 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S12.11.2 ("a check that fails leaves its alerts as they were … it is
  resolved by itself when the cause clears").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/alerts.py:844-858` and `:908-912`:
  ```text
              except Exception:
                  ...
                  failed |= _KINDS[fn.__name__]
      ...
          if cause is None:
              if kind not in failed:
                  resolve.append(alert_id)
  ```
  ```text
  _KINDS = {
      "price_sources": {"source-failing"},
      "email": {"source-failing"},
      "bitcoin_source": {"source-failing"},
      "linked_servers": {"source-failing", "link-broken", "link-certificate", "link-key-waiting"},
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** what is held back is a *kind*, and four checks share `source-failing`.
  While `linked_servers` raises (an error reading any one link's row, say), Tiingo's alert, the email alert and the bitcoin alert stay open after their
  sources answer again, and their emails don't stop being true until the link check is fixed;
  the same the other way. The alert's `causeKey` (`prices:…`, `mail`, `bitcoin…`, `link:…`)
  already says which check raised it.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** hold back by the failed check's key prefixes (`prices:`, `mail`, `bitcoin`,
  `link…:`) rather than by kind, or give each check its own kind.
- **Alternatives:** none simpler.

### CR3-288: a long duration answers 500: `parse` has no ceiling and the overflow isn't a refusal

*Work pad: U09-16 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S12.13 ("read by one helper on each side …, which refuses anything
  else and says why"), R3.7 (refusals at the field).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/duration.py:44`: `total += int(m.group(1)) *
  UNITS[unit]`, unbounded; `ui/links.py:337-341`:
  ```text
      try:
          span = duration.parse(body.for_ if body else "1d")
      except duration.DurationError as e:
          raise refuse("for", "duration-invalid", str(e)) from None
      until = clock.now() + span
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** an admin pausing a link "for good" types `1000000w`: the browser's
  `parseDuration` accepts it, `parse` returns 7 million days, and `clock.now() + span` raises
  `OverflowError` (past year 9999): 500 *Something went wrong*. `999999999w` overflows inside
  `parse` itself (`OverflowError`, not a `DurationError`). Run: `duration.parse("999999999w")`
  and `duration.parse("99999999999999999999m")` both raise `OverflowError` (`isinstance(e,
  ValueError)` is False).
- **Run:** yes, the two calls above in `uv run python -I -c …`.
- **Remedy:** a ceiling in both helpers (a year, say, or what each field allows), refused with a
  sentence ("A duration is at most 52w."); `parse` turns any arithmetic error into a
  `DurationError`.
- **Alternatives:** the pause route clamps `until`.

### CR3-291: tooltips never reach screen readers, so the collapsed sidebar's links and several marks have no name

*Work pad: U09-19 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S12.1.3 ("styled tooltips that also show on keyboard focus and
  reach screen readers"), S12.8 ("every control is labelled").
- **Code:** `web/ui/components/tooltip.js:1-2` says "the text is also its aria-label when it
  has no text of its own", but nothing sets one: `show` (`:8-13`) only fills the `tip` signal,
  and `TooltipHost` (`:26-34`) draws `<div class="tooltip" role="tooltip">` that no element
  points to (`grep -rn "aria-describedby" web/ui/components web/ui/app web/ui/screens`: only
  `fields.js:24` and `term.js:81`). In `frame.js:180-187` a collapsed sidebar's link is an
  `aria-hidden` icon and a `.nav-label` set to `display: none` (`components.css:57-62`), named
  only by `data-tip`. Likewise `screens/accounts.js:40, 43` (the "Shared by me" / "Shared with
  me by …" marks are an icon and a number).
- **What goes wrong:** with the sidebar collapsed, a screen reader announces thirteen links as
  "link" with no name; the sharing marks on Accounts read as a bare number.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.

### CR3-294: the image's health check asks 127.0.0.1 whatever `[server] host` says

*Work pad: U09-22 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S13.3 ("`[server] host` sets the listening address, all interfaces by
  default"), S13.6 ("The image's own health check asks only whether the server answers").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/main.py:107-111`:
  ```text
      config = configured(args.config)
      scheme = "https" if config.mode == "tls" else "http"
      try:
          # Loopback to this container's own listener; its certificate may be self-signed.
          r = httpx.get(f"{scheme}://127.0.0.1:{config.port}/healthz", verify=False, timeout=4)
  ```
  while the listener binds `config.host` (`listener.py:82-84`).
- **What goes wrong:** an operator who sets `host = "192.168.1.20"` (one interface of a host-
  networked container, or a bare install with several) gets a server that answers on that
  address and a health check that finds nothing on loopback: `unhealthy`, so an orchestrator
  that waits for healthy containers (TrueNAS's, S13.6) never lets it come up. Also with
  `host = "::1"`-style IPv6-only binds.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** ask `config.host` (loopback when it is `0.0.0.0` or `::`), bracketing an IPv6
  address.
- **Alternatives:** the health check reads the host commands' socket (`/run/moolakata/sock`),
  which is always there and needs no address.

<a id="cr3-295"></a>

### CR3-295: a statement over 100 MB, which Settings › Imports allows, is refused as "uploads arriving at once: wait"

*Work pad: U09-23 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S13.7.6 (100 MB of uploads in flight), S16.8 (statements up to
  200 MB); D2-16 (Phase 1: the two limits disagree), judged here on the code.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/middleware.py:262-270`:
  ```text
          declared = int(length) if upload and length and length.isdigit() else 0
          if upload and self.in_flight.get(who, 0) + declared > IN_FLIGHT:
              await _send_problem(
                  429,
                  "uploads-in-flight",
                  "More than 100 MB of uploads are arriving at once. Wait for them to finish.",
                  [(b"retry-after", b"10")],
  ```
  with `upload_cap` up to 201 MiB (`app.py:117-119`, `importMaxMb` 1–200, `settings.py:255`).
- **What goes wrong:** an admin sets the statement limit to 150 MB; a member uploads a 120 MB
  PDF statement with nothing else in flight. It passes the size check and is refused 429 with
  *Wait for them to finish* and `Retry-After: 10`; the browser (or the connector, which
  counts its files the same way) waits and tries again, and is refused again for ever. The
  route's own 413 (*Statements are up to 150 MB*) is never reached. Whichever way D2-16 is
  decided, the refusal names the wrong cause.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** with D2-16's decision: either S16.8's range stops at 100 (and the setting's
  validator with it), or a single upload larger than `IN_FLIGHT` is refused 413 naming the
  100 MB ceiling rather than 429.
- **Alternatives:** `IN_FLIGHT` is the larger of 100 MB and the largest size limit.

<a id="cr3-296"></a>

### CR3-296: a rotation writes the new keyring before its re-encryption is in the journal, so a stop between them leaves a rotation that only re-wrapped, unrecorded

*Work pad: U10-5 in [`unit-10-encryption-settings.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-10-encryption-settings.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S15.7.1 ("so a rotation always re-encrypts"; "The
  outcome of each is recorded in Activity with the rotation"), S15.7.2 ("**A journal** … records
  each step before it is taken, the copies' choices among them"); review 2's K5.3–4.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/store/maintenance.py:516-531` (through the running
  server):
  ```text
      ring = master.rotated(store.keys.ring, store.keys.data_key, new_master, at)
      master.write_keyring(config.keyring_path, ring)
      new = master.Unlocked(ring.master_id, ring, store.keys.keys, store.keys.data_key)
      outcomes, later = {}, {}
      for c in copies.copies(config):
          choice = choices.get(c.name, "reencrypt")
          if choice == "delete":
              copies.remove(c)
      ...
      reencrypt.schedule(config, later, None, "rotation")
  ```
  and `keys/cli.py:306-313` (offline: `master.write_keyring(...)`, then
  `reencrypt.schedule(config, choices, None, "rotation")`, then `reencrypt.run(config,
  new_key)`). `host.py:88-93` catches only `RefuseStart`; the Activity record (`:95-103`) and
  `srv.store`'s new keyring (`:89`) come only after a clean return.
- **What goes wrong:** the server is stopped (a `docker compose restart`, an OOM kill, a power
  cut) after the keyring is written but before the journal is, or `copies.remove` raises on a
  copy chosen for deletion. The keyring is wrapped under the new phrase with the old data key,
  and no re-encryption is pending: the next unlock opens with the new phrase and re-encrypts
  nothing, so the old phrase plus any copy made before still opens everything made after.
  Nothing records the rotation (the route's Activity line and the command's host event come
  after), and `keys rotate` reports a transport error or "the server refused the rotation",
  though the keyring changed (K5.3's report, in a smaller window). The offline path is the
  same between `write_keyring` and `schedule`; there, if `run` then fails (U10-2), the command
  ends in an exception with the keyring rotated and nothing queued for Activity.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** write the journal first (`why: rotation`, the choices, and the new keyring as
  `keyring.next` wrapped under the new phrase), then delete the copies chosen for deletion,
  then put the keyring in place as a journaled step; record the rotation in Activity (or the
  host queue) as soon as the keyring changes, and report the outcome exactly when a later step
  fails.
- **Alternatives:** none simpler that keeps the old phrase usable until the journal exists.

<a id="cr3-297"></a>

### CR3-297: setting SMTP security to *None* needs no password again and keeps the stored password, which then goes to the server in the clear

*Work pad: U10-6 in [`unit-10-encryption-settings.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-10-encryption-settings.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S16.2 ("password (a secret, set with the
  password again) … Changing the host asks for the password again and clears the stored SMTP
  password"), S13.7.2, S14.4; review 2's K13.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/settings.py:46`: `REAUTH = {"smtpHost", "publicUrl"}`
  and `:57-60` (only `smtpHost` and `bitcoinNodeHost` clear their secret); `mail.py:131-139`:
  ```text
      else:
          conn = _vetted(smtplib.SMTP, address)(smtp.host, smtp.port, timeout=SMTP_TIMEOUT)
      with conn:
          conn.ehlo()
          if smtp.security == "starttls":
              conn.starttls(context=context)
              conn.ehlo()
          if smtp.username:
              conn.login(smtp.username, smtp.password or "")
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** K13's remedy protects the SMTP password against a change of *host*, but
  a change of `smtpSecurity` from STARTTLS to None (and the port to 25) is an ordinary save:
  no password again, the secret kept. The next email, or **Send a test email** at once, logs
  in with the stored password over plain SMTP, readable by anyone on the path to the
  provider. Whoever has an admin's session (the case S13.7.2's password-again guards against)
  gets the household's mail password out without ever seeing it.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** treat `smtpSecurity` (and `smtpPort`) like `smtpHost`: the password again, and
  the stored password cleared when security is lowered to None; or refuse AUTH over a plain
  connection unless the host is a private address.
- **Alternatives:** keep the password but refuse to send it without TLS (`login` only after
  `starttls` or on `SMTP_SSL`), so None works only for a relay that needs no login, as the
  field's hint ("None only for a relay on your own network") suggests.

<a id="cr3-298"></a>

### CR3-298: a failure after the database opens leaves the server neither locked nor serving, and a restore's follow-up is lost with it

*Work pad: U10-8 in [`unit-10-encryption-settings.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-10-encryption-settings.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S15.4.3, S15.4.5 (a refused unlock leaves the
  server locked), S15.6.8 ("At the unlock the server … reports anything it cannot open … and
  each link's copy starts again, pulled in full").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/runtime.py:184-206`: `server.store = store` (`:186`) is
  set before `ensure_categories`, `register_tasks`, `apply_egress`, `take_host_events`,
  `after_restore`/`check_restored` and `load_copy_folders` run; `_open` (`:369-393`) sets
  `ready` only after the Activity record, `Reader(server)` and the tasks start.
  `ui/unlock.py:87-88`:
  ```text
      if srv.store is not None:
          raise Problem(409, "unlocked-already", "MoolaKata is already unlocked.")
  ```
  and `store/maintenance.py:439-447` (`after_restore` deletes `restored.json` as it reads it,
  before `check_restored` has done its work).
- **What goes wrong:** any exception after `:186` (a secret row `open_sealed` fails on with
  something other than `SecretError`, a host-events file the server's account can't unlink, a
  worker that won't start) reaches the locked screen as a 500. The server now holds the store
  but is not ready: `GET /ui/api/unlock` says locked, and every further unlock is answered
  "already unlocked"; no task runs, and only a restart gets out. When the failure is in
  `check_restored`, `restored.json` is already gone, so after the restart nothing resets the
  links' copy state or writes the restore report: the links keep the backup's
  `copyPublicKey` and `gotSeq` (review 2's K9.4 again) and nothing says what didn't open.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** set `server.store` only once `build` has finished (or clear it, dispose the
  engine and stop any tasks on failure, so the server is locked again with the error shown);
  remove `restored.json` only after `check_restored` has written its report and reset the
  links.
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

<a id="cr3-299"></a>

### CR3-299: copies the host keeps that Key storage never lists: a backup cut off part-way, a set-aside `keyring.next`, `keyring.old`

*Work pad: U10-9 in [`unit-10-encryption-settings.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-10-encryption-settings.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S15.6.7 ("**Know where every copy is**: the
  copies on this host are listed in Settings › Key storage, with how each opens"), S16.13,
  S14.6 (backup pruning), S15.8.2.3.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/store/maintenance.py:192-195`:
  ```text
      part = config.backup_dir / (name + ".part")
      if part.exists():
          shutil.rmtree(part)
      part.mkdir(mode=0o700)
  ```
  `maintenance.backups` (`:221-228`) and `copies.copies` (`store/copies.py:62-64`) skip any
  name ending `.part`; `reencrypt.run` sets an unexplained `keyring.next` aside as
  `keyring.next.set-aside-<time>` (`store/reencrypt.py:224-228`) and keeps `keyring.old` while
  a run is unfinished (`:303-306`).
- **What goes wrong:** a backup stopped part-way (the server killed during the daily backup, a
  full disk) leaves `moolakata-<time>.part`, often a whole exported database, in the backup
  directory. Only a later backup with the very same second's name removes it, so it stays for
  good: never listed in Key storage or Backups, never pruned, never re-encrypted or offered for
  deletion at a rotation. After a leak, the admin deletes "every copy on this host" from the
  list and this one stays, under the old data key, in the directory the host snapshots and
  syncs. A set-aside `keyring.next` (a data key wrapped under a phrase) is likewise kept and
  never shown.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** remove any `.part` at the start of each backup (and at start-up); list a set-aside
  keyring in Key storage with **Remove**, as the copies are.
- **Alternatives:** list a `.part` as a copy ("cut off part-way") with **Remove**.

<a id="cr3-312"></a>

### CR3-312: the egress audit's no-network run can't see a native or worker connection: in a namespace with no interface, an attempt leaves nothing to find

*Work pad: U11-1 in [`unit-11-implementation.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-11-implementation.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S17.2.1 (dependencies.md, the egress audit rule 1).
- **Code:** `web/server/tests/test_no_network.py:15–30`, `:39–44`, `:78–79`:
  ```text
  for table in ("/proc/net/tcp", "/proc/net/tcp6", "/proc/net/udp", "/proc/net/udp6"):
      ...
          remote = line.split()[2]
  ...
  def connect(self, address, *a, **k):
      if isinstance(address, tuple):
          host = str(address[0])
          if not host.startswith(("127.", "::1", "localhost")):
              attempts.append(f"{host}:{address[1]}")
  ...
  assert watched == [], f"a socket was opened to {watched}"
  assert _peers() == set(), f"native code reached {_peers()}"
  ```
  run by `web/dev.sh:115`: `unshare -U -n uv run --frozen pytest -q -m audit tests/test_no_network.py`.
- **What goes wrong:** S17.2.1 says the run fails "on any socket opened to a non-loopback
  address", and the test's docstring says "by Python or by native code". Inside `unshare -n`
  there is no route, so a `connect()` or a UDP `sendto()` to any outside address fails at once
  with `ENETUNREACH`, and the socket never gets a remote address in `/proc/net/*`: `_peers()`
  is empty whatever native code tried. The only thing that can fail the test is the
  monkeypatched `socket.socket.connect`, which sees Python connects in the test's own process
  only: not `sendto` (a DNS lookup, a UDP beacon), not native code (a library's C or Rust
  client), and nothing in the receipt worker, which is a separate process (`Worker()` at
  `:63`). A dependency update that adds a native telemetry ping or a model download in the
  worker passes the run silently: it just fails to connect. (The worker's seccomp filter
  stops it in production, but the audit is meant to find it, so the register can say so.)
- **Run:** a scratch script in `unshare -U -n` (`tmp/u11/netns_probe.py`): a TCP connect to
  1.1.1.1:443 and a UDP `sendto` to 8.8.8.8:53 both fail with errno 101, and
  `/proc/net/tcp` is empty, `/proc/net/udp` shows the UDP socket with remote `00000000:0000`.
- **Remedy:** record attempts where they can be seen: run the server and the worker under a
  seccomp or `strace -f -e trace=connect,sendto,sendmsg` filter that logs every non-`AF_UNIX`
  destination (or an `LD_PRELOAD` shim, or a namespace whose only route goes to a local
  catcher that logs SYNs and datagrams), and fail on any non-loopback one.
- **Alternatives:** S17.2.1 and `dependencies.md` say what the run proves: that the server
  works with no network, not that nothing tried.

<a id="cr3-313"></a>

### CR3-313: a rerun of `release.yml` on a published tag rebuilds the images and moves the version's tag on both registries

*Work pad: U11-2 in [`unit-11-implementation.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-11-implementation.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S17.8.2 ("none that moves"), `RELEASE.md` §2
  ("A version is never reused"), §11 ("don't move its image").
- **Code:** `.github/workflows/release.yml:48–59` (push `"ghcr.io/$owner/$image:$version-${{ matrix.arch }}"`),
  `:84`:
  ```sh
  docker buildx imagetools create -t "$ref:$version" "$ref:$version-amd64" "$ref:$version-arm64"
  ```
  and `:132`: `docker buildx imagetools create -t "$to" "$from"`; nothing checks first whether
  `$ref:$version` exists.
- **What goes wrong:** *Re-run all jobs* on 0.2.0's run (a click anyone with write access can
  make, and §11 sends maintainers to that page for every failure) builds new images, whose
  digests differ (the build is not byte-reproducible: timestamps, bytecode), overwrites
  `0.2.0-amd64`/`-arm64`, re-joins `0.2.0` onto them, attests the new digests and copies them
  over `docker.io/mojokata/*:0.2.0`. Testers who pulled before hold one image, households after
  another, both "0.2.0", both attested; `release.sh check` passes for the new one. The git tag
  and the release are immutable, the image tag is not.
- **Remedy:** the build job (on `push`) and the joining job refuse when
  `ghcr.io/$owner/$image:$version` already resolves (`imagetools inspect` succeeds), unless its
  digest is the one being written; the Docker Hub job likewise refuses to replace an existing
  tag whose digest differs.
- **Alternatives:** RELEASE §11 warns never to re-run a release run after the joining job
  succeeded.
- **Run:** reading alone.

<a id="cr3-314"></a>

### CR3-314: `release.yml` builds and smoke-runs on pull requests only when the packaging changes, not the code that goes into the images

*Work pad: U11-3 in [`unit-11-implementation.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-11-implementation.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S17.10.2 ("On a pull request that touches what
  goes into the images, it builds and smoke-runs both"), `RELEASE.md` §3 step 7.
- **Code:** `.github/workflows/release.yml:13–19`:
  ```yaml
  pull_request:
    paths:
      - .github/workflows/release.yml
      - web/install/**
      - web/server/uv.lock
      - connector/Dockerfile
      - connector/requirements.txt
  ```
  while the images take `web/server/src`, `web/server/pyproject.toml`, `web/VERSION`, `web/ui`,
  `docs/mockups/MoolaKata-mock.html` (`web/install/Dockerfile:19–29`) and the connector's
  package.
- **What goes wrong:** a pull request that changes only server code (say, an import the
  slim image lacks, a start that no longer refuses with 78, or a change to `sandbox.py`'s
  aarch64 numbers) passes `check` (amd64, no image) and merges; the smoke run first meets it
  on the next release's pull request, which touches `web/install/` for the versions, long
  after the change and among everything else since.
- **Remedy:** add `web/server/src/**`, `web/server/pyproject.toml`, `web/ui/**`, `web/VERSION`,
  `docs/mockups/MoolaKata-mock.html` and `connector/**` to the paths.
- **Alternatives:** S17.10.2 says "that touches the images' packaging", and accepts that a
  code change is first smoke-run at the release's pull request.
- **Run:** reading alone.

<a id="cr3-315"></a>

### CR3-315: `release.sh sign` tags any commit it is given, one that was never on MojoKata's `main` included, and the push publishes it

*Work pad: U11-4 in [`unit-11-implementation.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-11-implementation.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S17.10.2.1; `RELEASE.md` intro ("A release is a
  `v<version>` tag on `main`"), §5 step 1.
- **Code:** `web/release.sh:120–123`, `:136`, `:140`:
  ```sh
  git -C "$ROOT" fetch -q "$up"
  commit=$(git -C "$ROOT" rev-parse --verify "${2:-$up/main}^{commit}")
  [[ "$(git -C "$ROOT" show "$commit:web/VERSION")" == "$version" ]] ||
  ...
  git -C "$ROOT" tag -s "v$version" -m "MoolaKata $version" "$commit"
  ...
  git -C "$ROOT" push "$up" "refs/tags/v$version"
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** `web/release.sh sign 0.2.0 HEAD` from a local branch with an unpushed
  fix signs that commit, and the tag push uploads the commit with it: `release.yml` builds and
  publishes code no pull request reviewed and `check` never ran on. The maintainer's ruleset
  bypass lets the push through, and the confirmation shows only the commit's subject.
- **Remedy:** refuse unless `git merge-base --is-ancestor "$commit" "$up/main"` holds (RELEASE
  §5's "that pull request's commit" is on `main`), and show whether `check` passed on it
  (`gh api repos/$REPO/commits/$commit/check-runs`).
- **Run:** reading alone.

<a id="cr3-316"></a>

### CR3-316: `release.sh publish` checks the draft's files by name only, so a changed file is published and locked for good

*Work pad: U11-5 in [`unit-11-implementation.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-11-implementation.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** `RELEASE.md` §8 ("checks that
  the draft holds exactly the tag's install files"), S17.10.2.
- **Code:** `web/release.sh:219–221`:
  ```sh
  wanted=$(install_files "$version" | xargs -n1 basename | sort)
  got=$(gh release view "v$version" -R "$REPO" --json assets --jq '.assets[].name' | sort)
  [[ $got == "$wanted" ]] || die "the draft's files aren't the tag's install files:
  ```
  then `:237–242` verify the published release and assets against GitHub's own attestation of
  what was published, not against the tag.
- **What goes wrong:** anyone with write access can replace a draft's `compose.yaml` (same
  name) between the draft job and `publish`, say with an image pinned to another digest. The
  name check passes, publishing makes the file immutable, and `verify-asset` then attests the
  substituted file as the release's.
- **Remedy:** compare each asset's SHA-256 (`gh release view --json assets` gives `digest`)
  with `git show "v$version:web/install/<name>" | sha256sum`, before asking for the typed tag.
- **Run:** reading alone.

### CR3-317: `release.sh copy` signs in to Docker Hub in the maintainer's own Docker config and leaves the `mojokata` credential there

*Work pad: U11-6 in [`unit-11-implementation.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-11-implementation.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** `RELEASE.md` §11 (the copy),
  §12 (the token's care: "without touching your own Docker logins").
- **Code:** `web/release.sh:300–301`:
  ```sh
  echo "Sign in to Docker Hub as $OWNER (its password, or a token with Read & Write):"
  docker login docker.io -u "$OWNER"
  ```
  where `cmd_token` (`:318–320`) and `cmd_check` (`:195–199`) use a throwaway `DOCKER_CONFIG`.
- **What goes wrong:** after a copy, `~/.docker/config.json` keeps the shared `mojokata`
  account's password or token (base64, unless a credential helper is set) for good, on the
  maintainer's machine, where any later `docker push` can write to the household images.
- **Remedy:** `TMP=$(mktemp -d)` and `DOCKER_CONFIG="$TMP"` for the login and the copy, as
  `token` does.
- **Run:** reading alone.

<a id="cr3-318"></a>

### CR3-318: `create-pr.sh` pins a digest it resolved apart from the one whose provenance it checked

*Work pad: U11-7 in [`unit-11-implementation.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-11-implementation.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S17.8.3.5 ("checks each
  image's provenance (S17.8.2) and pins it by its digest").
- **Code:** `publish/truenas/create-pr.sh:49–55`:
  ```sh
  pinned() {
    local image="ghcr.io/mojokata/$1:$2"
    gh attestation verify "oci://$image" -R "$REPO" --signer-workflow "$SIGNER" \
      --source-ref "refs/tags/v$2" --deny-self-hosted-runners >/dev/null ||
      die "$image: its provenance doesn't check out"
    docker buildx imagetools inspect "$image" --format '{{.Manifest.Digest}}'
  }
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** the tag is resolved twice, once by `gh` and once by `imagetools`. If
  `0.2.0` moves between the two (U11-2's rerun, or a push by anyone holding `packages: write`),
  the catalog request pins a digest no one verified; `check.sh`, run before, also tried the
  images by tag. TrueNAS's catalog then serves every catalog household that digest.
- **Remedy:** resolve the digest first, then verify `oci://ghcr.io/mojokata/$1@$digest` with the
  same flags, and pin that digest.
- **Run:** reading alone.

<a id="cr3-320"></a>

### CR3-320: the egress scan counts a URL on GitHub, Google, Cloudflare or PyPI as noise, so a new download or update check there passes unflagged

*Work pad: U11-10 in [`unit-11-implementation.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-11-implementation.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S17.2.1 ("scans the installed
  wheels and the vendored files for URLs … diffs the findings against the register").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/dev/audit.py:30–40`, `:55`:
  ```text
  NOISE = re.compile(
      r"(^|\.)(w3\.org|github\.com|githubusercontent\.com|readthedocs\.io|python\.org|pypi\.org|"
      ...
      r"cloudflare\.com|google\.com|googleapis\.com|googlesource\.com|chromium\.org|"
  ...
          if not NOISE.search(host):
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** the pattern drops every subdomain of these hosts, endpoints included:
  `raw.githubusercontent.com` and `objects.githubusercontent.com` (where model and binary
  downloads usually live), `api.github.com` (update checks), `pypi.org/pypi/<x>/json` (the
  common "a newer version exists" check), `storage.googleapis.com`, `jsdelivr.net`. A new
  release of rapidocr that fetches a model from GitHub, or a library that checks PyPI for
  updates, shows nothing new in `web/dev.sh audit`, and with U11-1 the no-network run doesn't
  catch it either.
- **Remedy:** treat as noise only documentation hosts by exact name (`docs.python.org`,
  `www.w3.org`, `github.com/<org>/<repo>` paths as links), and report API and download hosts
  (`api.github.com`, `raw.githubusercontent.com`, `objects.githubusercontent.com`,
  `*.googleapis.com`, `pypi.org/pypi`, CDNs) as findings, which the register then accepts
  once.
- **Run:** reading alone.

### CR3-324: the release gate's receipt read runs in the smoke container's own Python, not through the reader's worker and its sandbox

*Work pad: U11-15 in [`unit-11-implementation.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-11-implementation.md).*

- **Kind:** test gap. **Req:** S17.8.1 (the gate: "a receipt read"),
  S9.8.4.2 (the worker's sandbox, by architecture).
- **Code:** `web/install/smoke.sh:102–108`:
  ```sh
  docker run --rm "${PF[@]}" "${LOCKED[@]}" --entrypoint python "moolakata:$TAG" -c '
  ...
  from moolakata.receipts import ocr
  ...
  r = ocr.read(b.getvalue(), "image/png"); assert r.lines, "nothing read"; print("   read", r.method)'
  ```
  where the server reads through `receipts/worker.py` after `_lock_down` (seccomp from
  `sandbox.ARCHES`, `sandbox.py:27–28`, Landlock, no core).
- **What goes wrong:** the gate proves the OCR stack loads on each architecture, not that a
  receipt can be read the way a running server reads it: in the worker, under the seccomp
  filter whose numbers differ per machine, with `-I`, no environment and the read-only root.
  `check.yml` runs on amd64 only, so the aarch64 filter (`0xC00000B7, 198, 425`) is executed
  by no automated run; a wrong number there would kill or wrongly free every arm64 worker and
  still pass the release gate.
- **Remedy:** the smoke run reads a receipt through the running server (upload over the API as
  the signed-in admin, wait for its fields), or runs `Worker().read(...)` and the worker's
  `selftest` (no network, the data directory unreadable) inside the locked-down container on
  each architecture.
- **Run:** reading alone.

*Work pad: U12-9 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

  §4.1.3. Review 2's K3 point 3, marked done.

  ```text
          return keys.short(out.get("connectorFingerprint", pairing.fingerprint))
  ```
  and MoolaKata's answer names one (`web/server/src/moolakata/connector/manage.py:204`,
  `"connectorFingerprint": keys.fingerprint(caller_public)`). Both the page
  (`page.py:659-668`) and `pair` on the host (`main.py:194-195`) show what comes back.
- **What goes wrong:** the comparison is meant to be two independent workings; here the

### CR3-327: unpairing while the vault is locked forgets the pairing but keeps every credential and the sealing key

*Work pad: U12-11 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S18.4.4 ("at which the connector deletes the
  credentials it held for that server and its sealing key"), S18.11.4 (**Unpair** "forgets
  the pairing and the credentials"), `connector-protocol.md` §4.1.4.
- **Code:** `connector/moolakata_connector/core.py:150-160`:
  ```text
          keys.forget(self.data / "pairing.json")
          ...
          if not self.vault.locked:
              for cid in self.vault.ids():
                  self.vault.delete(cid)
              self.vault.keep_sealing_key(b"")
  ```
  reached from a signed `not-paired` (`:307-310`), which comes at any report, locked or not,
  and from `moolakata-connector unpair` (`main.py:227`, `sock.py:66-68`), which needs no
  words.
- **What goes wrong:** an admin unpairs a connector in MoolaKata after a restart that left it
  locked (or runs `unpair` on its host): the connector forgets the pairing at once and, having
  no pairing, never reports again, so nothing later deletes the credentials or the sealing
  key. They stay in the vault until the next `pair` (which deletes them) or `reset`. The page's
  Unpair unlocks first (`page.py:675`), so it is only these two ways.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** keep a "forget on unlock" mark beside the vault when it is locked, and delete at
  the next unlock; `unpair` on the host asks for the words, or says what it kept.
- **Alternatives:** S18.4.4 says the credentials go at the next unlock.

  and the error names the word (`words.py:46-47`, `f"word {n} ({token!r}) is not in the

- **What goes wrong:** an admin types the 12 words with one slip (*abandn*): the container's
  log keeps `word 7 ('abandn') is not in the word list`, a word of the real phrase; each later
