<a id="cr3-329"></a>

### CR3-329: `status`, the health check and its page wait for a whole round of reports, runs and posts

*Work pad: U12-15 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S18.3.2 ("The image's health check runs
  `status`, which answers whenever the connector runs … healthy means alive"), S18.11.3.
- **Code:** `connector/moolakata_connector/core.py:180-181` (`status` takes `self._lock`);
  `tick` holds the same lock through the report, every due driver run, every post and the
  second report (`:303-322`); the page's home and Unpair forms call `core.status()`
  (`page.py:425`, `:474`); `connector/Dockerfile:36-37` gives the check 5 seconds.
- **What goes wrong:** a Schwab Connection with four accounts, or an OFX server that is slow,
  makes a run of several calls of up to 60 seconds each (U12-16); a large post takes as long
  as the upload. For that whole time `moolakata-connector status` and the page hang. Three
  checks in a row past 5 seconds mark the container unhealthy, which an orchestrator that
  restarts unhealthy containers turns into a restart, and a restart locks the connector
  (S18.3.2). Someone opening its page to see what it does gets no answer.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** `status` reads a snapshot without the round's lock (the fields are set
  atomically, or copied under a short lock the round takes only to write them); the round
  holds the lock only around what it changes, not around network calls.
- **Alternatives:** the health check reads a heartbeat file the loop and the socket update,
  rather than asking the core.

<a id="cr3-330"></a>

### CR3-330: "at most 60 seconds" is per socket operation, so an answer that trickles holds a call without end

*Work pad: U12-16 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S18.2.2 ("A call it makes … waits at most 60
  seconds, and reads at most 25 MB of an answer").
- **Code:** `connector/moolakata_connector/client.py:119-131`: `timeout=60` on the connection
  and the socket, then `r.read(MAX_ANSWER + 1)`. The timeout bounds each `recv`, not the call.
- **What goes wrong:** an institution's server (or anything an OFX Connection's `url` names)
  answers one byte every 50 seconds: the call never times out, reads up to 25 MB that way
  (weeks), and with U12-15 holds the whole loop: no report (MoolaKata alerts *not heard from*
  after 15 minutes, which is right), no other Connection runs.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** a deadline for the whole call (checked between reads, closing the socket past
  it), as the server's `net.Client` does for its 20 MB ceiling.
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

<a id="cr3-331"></a>

### CR3-331: a box filed in the vault whose `credential/taken` failed is never acknowledged again, and marks the Connection failing at every report

*Work pad: U12-17 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S18.7.1 (the connector files the box and
  "confirms which box it took (by its time); MoolaKata then deletes that box"),
  `connector-protocol.md` §4.3.2.
- **Code:** `connector/moolakata_connector/core.py:385-391`:
  ```text
          if not isinstance(at, int | float) or at <= self.vault.sealed_at(c.id):
              c.state, c.message = "failing", "a box older than the credentials held was refused"
              return
          self.vault.put(c.id, opened.get("credentials") or {}, host, at)
          self.server.call(
              f"/connector/v1/connections/{c.id}/credential/taken", {"sealedAt": server_at}
          )
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** the `taken` call fails after the `put` (MoolaKata restarting and
  locked, a dropped connection): the exception also leaves `_apply` half done (the rest of the
  answer's Connections and its `removed` aren't applied that round). At the next report
  MoolaKata hands the same box again; its time equals the one filed, so it is refused as
  *older*, `taken` is never called, the box stays in MoolaKata for good, offered at every
  report, and every report says the Connection is *failing* until a run overwrites the state
  (once a day), then *failing* again at the next report. The owner sees "credentials sealed,
  waiting for the connector" (`connections.js:84-85`) for ever.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** a box whose time equals the credential's own is the one already filed: call
  `taken` for it again and change nothing else; catch a failed `taken` so the rest of the
  answer is applied.
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

<a id="cr3-332"></a>

### CR3-332: OFX Direct Connect sends the username, password and settings unescaped, in Latin-1 with "?" for the rest

*Work pad: U12-18 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S18.9.1 (OFX 1.0.2 client, signing in with the
  username and password), R1.9 is not at stake (no amounts).
- **Code:** `connector/moolakata_connector/drivers/ofx_direct.py:106-110`:
  ```text
      user, password = credentials.get("username", ""), credentials.get("password", "")
      ...
          f"<SIGNONMSGSRQV1><SONRQ><DTCLIENT>{_stamp(now)}<USERID>{user}<USERPASS>{password}"
  ```
  and `:132`, `.encode("latin-1", "replace")`.
- **What goes wrong:** OFX 1.0.2 is SGML: `&`, `<` and `>` in an element's value must be
  written `&amp;`, `&lt;`, `&gt;`. A password `p<ss&word!` is cut at `<` by the server's
  parser; a password with a character outside Latin-1 (`€`) is sent with `?`. The server
  answers 15500, which the driver reports as *needs a sign-in: the institution refused the
  sign-in*, so the owner re-enters the same correct password and gets the same answer.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** escape every value written into the request (`&`, `<`, `>`); refuse a
  credential the request can't carry with a message that says so, rather than altering it.
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

<a id="cr3-333"></a>

### CR3-333: a file over Settings › Imports' size is refused `file-refused`, not `too-large`

*Work pad: U12-19 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** `connector-protocol.md` §7 (`too-large`,
  413, "a file over the upload size"), S16.8. DC-13.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/connector/files.py:66-69`:
  ```text
      with srv.db.read() as s:
          limit = settings.get(s, "importMaxMb") * 1024 * 1024
      if len(data) > limit:
          raise Refused("file-refused", f"Files are up to {limit // (1024 * 1024)} MB.")
  ```
  after the owner's upload rate was spent on it (`:63-65`).
- **What goes wrong:** the connector drops the file either way (S18.8), but under the code
  the protocol reserves for content MoolaKata doesn't import; and a file above the body
  limit's cap gets the middleware's unsigned 413 instead (U12-6), so one case has two codes,
  signed and not. DC-13's reading holds: the limit is S16.8's (`importMaxMb`), not the
  receipts'.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** `too-large` (413), signed, for a file over `importMaxMb`, checked before the
  rate is spent; the protocol's §7 names S16.8 (DC-13's remedy).
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

<a id="cr3-334"></a>

### CR3-334: Reset makes new words and forgets the pairing without telling MoolaKata

*Work pad: U12-20 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S18.3.4 ("deletes the vault, makes new
  words … and unpairs"), `connector-protocol.md` §4 (`unpaired`: "the connector was told to
  forget this server").
- **Code:** `connector/moolakata_connector/core.py:162-176`, `reset`, forgets `pairing.json`
  and replaces the vault, and makes no call; `unpair` (`:140-148`) calls
  `/connector/v1/unpaired` first.
- **What goes wrong:** after **Reset** on the page or `reset` on the host, MoolaKata still
  holds the pairing as live: Settings › Connector shows it paired and confirmed, owners can
  still seal credentials to its old sealing key (boxes the new vault can never open), and 15
  minutes later admins get *the connector hasn't been heard from*, which reads as an outage.
  The admin must Unpair in MoolaKata by hand before pairing again.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** `reset` calls `unpaired` (signed with the identity key it still has) before it
  forgets the pairing, as `unpair` does, and goes on if MoolaKata can't be reached.
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

<a id="cr3-335"></a>

### CR3-335: the page's own certificate is written key first, neither through a temporary file

*Work pad: U12-21 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S18.11.1 (a certificate "made at its first start,
  kept in `tls/`"). The same pattern in MoolaKata's own certificate is U09-6 (2).
- **Code:** `connector/moolakata_connector/page.py:118-127`: the key is opened `O_TRUNC` and
  written, then `cert.write_bytes(...)`; `:95-101` keeps a pair whose certificate's names and
  dates still fit, without checking that the key matches it.
- **What goes wrong:** the connector is stopped (a `docker stop` past its grace, a full disk)
  between the two writes while remaking its certificate (names changed, or 30 days before the
  end): `key.pem` is new, `cert.pem` old. At every later start the old certificate still
  "fits", `load_cert_chain` fails, and `serve` refuses to start ("its page can't listen",
  exit 78), restarting for ever until someone deletes `tls/` by hand.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** write both to temporary files and rename the certificate last; at start, check
  that the key matches the certificate and make a new pair when it doesn't.
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

  Connection whose `url` names a host resolving to `fd00:ec2::254` (settings MoolaKata, or a

  MoolaKata as a file if it looks like OFX (it won't). Little to gain; the rule is stated
  as MoolaKata's and differs from it.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** the same `METADATA` set in `vetted`, and a test with each address.
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

<a id="cr3-341"></a>

### CR3-341: a locked copy's inbox is capped at 500 MB, and the protocol's own example of a large first change is 2 GB

*Work pad: D3-F1 in [`docs-3-protocols.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-3-protocols.md); found while checking, confirmed by the sweep.*

- **Kind:** bug candidate (behaviour specified in a way that can't hold).
  **Req:** S11.4, S11.9.5.4, S11.7.1.
- **Where:** `S11-linked-servers.md:202–203`:

  ```text
  4. If the inbox grows past 1,000 changes or 500 MB, the server discards it and asks the
     source for a **full snapshot**, sealed the same way.
  ```

  and `link-protocol.md:199`: "(*Up to July 2022, 47 MB of 2 GB*)", the parts of a large first
  change each "a change of its own" (§4.4.3).
- **What goes wrong:** while a copy is locked, each part of a large first snapshot is written
  to `inbox/` and applied only at the unlock. A source whose snapshot is over 500 MB (the
  protocol's example is 2 GB) overflows the inbox part-way, the server discards it, and asks
  for a full snapshot again, so a copy that stays locked through the transfer never fills.
  Nothing says whether parts count against the cap, or whether a first snapshot is exempt.
- **Remedy:** S11.9.5.4 says what counts toward the cap and either exempts a snapshot in
  progress (its parts are kept until the cap's own larger limit) or states that a copy locked
  during a large first snapshot restarts it.

### CR3-347: two picks of one lot can take more than the lot holds

*Work pad: U03-F1 in [`unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md); found while checking, confirmed by the sweep.*

- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ledger/lots.py:636-643` (`_consume`), the picks loop plans
  each pick against the lot's whole quantity, where the method's loop after it subtracts what the
  plan already holds (`room = lot.quantity - sum(...)`):
  ```text
  for p in picks:
      lot = by_id.get(p.lotId)
      take = min(p.quantity, lot.quantity, left) if lot else ZERO
      if take > 0:
          plan.append((lot, take))
  ```
  and `ui/transactions.py:645-657` accepts any list of `PickIn`: each lot is checked to be in
  the account and of the instrument, never that a lot appears once or that the picks add up to
  the sale.
- **What goes wrong:** a request (not the dialog) that sends the same lot twice, 6 and 6 of a
  lot of 10, plans 12 shares from it; `_take` then runs twice and leaves the lot at -2 shares
  (a short lot out of nothing), with its basis and a gain worked out on shares that were never
  held. Reading alone.
- **Remedy:** subtract the plan's share of the lot inside the picks loop, as the method's loop
  does, and refuse duplicate lot ids in `picks` at the route.

<a id="cr3-348"></a>

### CR3-348: an adjusted contract's deliverable is weighted by any user's last trade

*Work pad: U03-F2 in [`unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-03-lots-positions-prices-portfolios.md); found while checking, confirmed by the sweep.*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S4.10 ("a holding is valued from the viewer's
  own trades in it, never from another user's"), S3.10.5.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/options/__init__.py:189-206` (`deliverable`, the call at `:202`), no owner is
  passed, so `valuation.prices` falls back to every account's statements and every user's trades:
  ```text
  found = valuation.prices(s, [i.id for i, _ in rows], day)
  if all(i.id in found for i, _ in rows):
      values = [found[i.id].price * q for i, q in rows]
  ```
  `settle` (`:124`) takes its parts' shares of the basis and the cash
  from these weights. U03-2 raises the same call in `expire_due`; this one is the P7 remainder
  that "Earlier findings re-checked" names, and no claim holds it.
- **What goes wrong:** where an adjusted contract's parts (a spin-off's two stocks) have no
  stored close for the day, another user's trade in either stock sets the weights that split the
  settlement's basis between the parts in this user's account. The figures differ by the gap
  between that trade's price and the owner's own.
- **Remedy:** pass the account's owner (`valuation.prices(..., day, owner)`), as `per_share`
  can, and fall back to the shares when the owner has no price.

<a id="cr3-349"></a>

### CR3-349: a typed amount of `NaN` or `inf` passes the check and answers 500

*Work pad: U04-F1 in [`unit-04-projections-taxes.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-04-projections-taxes.md); found while checking, confirmed by the sweep.*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S13.8.3 (problems as problem+json, a field's
  error named).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/projections.py:150-154`, the typed amount is tested
  only as `float(...) < 0`, which is false for NaN and `inf`:
  ```text
  try:
      if float(built.get("amount", -1)) < 0:
          raise ValueError
  except TypeError, ValueError:
      errors.append(FieldError("builtOn.amount", "amount-invalid", "An amount."))
  ```
  and `projections/__init__.py:85-96` then reads it as `Decimal(str(spec.get("amount", 0)))`.
- **What goes wrong:** Case (run, scratch test `tmp/check/unit-04/test_f.py`):
  `POST /ui/api/projections` with `builtOn: {kind: "typed", amount: "NaN"}` (or `"inf"`)
  raises `decimal.InvalidOperation` out of the engine, an unhandled 500 with an error id in
  place of a 422 naming `builtOn.amount`. The transaction rolls back. Another unchecked input
  beside U04-7's.
- **Remedy:** `_check` accepts the amount only when `math.isfinite` and within a bound (as
  `_num` bounds `target`); the same for any other amount read as a Decimal.

### CR3-350: a changed review names at most one of the totals, and none when a row changed

*Work pad: U05-F1 in [`unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-05-budgets-gifts-giving.md); found while checking, confirmed by the sweep.*

  `not out` tests the list that the loop itself grows: the first total that differs is named, the
  other totals are not, and no total is named when any category row changed.
- **What goes wrong:** October is reviewed. A late $3,000 paycheck lands (income and To Budget
  both change) and a planned category's spending changes as well: the flag lists only the
  category (*Food: spent …*). With the row unchanged, income and To Budget both changing names
  only *Income*. The month is flagged either way, but the list of *what changed* is partial.
- **Remedy:** compare each total on its own (`then.get(k) != now.get(k)`, without `not out`) and
  list each that differs.
- **Alternatives:** keep the whole month's figures with the review and list every difference.

<a id="cr3-351"></a>

### CR3-351: pATCH `/persons/{id}` sets `private` to false whenever the body leaves it out

*Work pad: U07-F1 in [`unit-07-users-sharing.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-07-users-sharing.md); found while checking, confirmed by the sweep.*

- **Kind:** privacy. **Req:** S10.10.2 ("Its creator may make it
  **private**"; "A person is changed by ... an admin").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/people.py:30, 141`:
  ```text
      private: bool = False
  ...
          values["private"] = body.private
  ```
  `PersonIn.private` defaults to `False`, and `change_person` always writes it. The `/people`
  route's body has `private: bool | None = None  # left as it is when not given`
  (`ui/gifts.py:56`) and refuses a change by anyone but the creator (`:415-420`).
- **What goes wrong:** an admin (or the person's own user) PATCHes a private person *Ruth*, made
  by Alice, to correct a birth year, without a `private` field: Ruth becomes visible to every
  user in the household, her relationships and gift plans with her. The creator's own PATCH
  that omits the field does the same. Only the API reaches it (no screen calls this route).
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** make `PersonIn.private` `bool | None = None` and write it only when given, and
  only from the creator of a person who isn't a user (U07-15's remedy).

<a id="cr3-352"></a>

### CR3-352: sign-in and re-authentication hash the password while holding the database's one write lock

*Work pad: U07-F2 in [`unit-07-users-sharing.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-07-users-sharing.md); found while checking, confirmed by the sweep.*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S13.7.5.1, S13.7.3 (argon2id at 64 MiB, 3 passes).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/session.py:187-189` (and `reauth`, `:418-420`):
  ```text
      with srv.db.tx() as s:
          user = accounts.by_login(s, login)
          ok = passwords.verify(user.passwordHash if user else None, body.password)
  ```
  `Database.tx()` is `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` (`store/db.py:105-114`), so every other write in the
  server waits for the hash, about 0.1 s on the development machine, longer on a small host.
- **What goes wrong:** each sign-in attempt, right or wrong, from any address holds the
  server's only writer for a whole argon2 verification; twelve at once answered one hash apart
  (0.21 s to 1.36 s in a scratch run with the real parameters). A client sending sign-in
  attempts from several addresses keeps the writer busy, and a signed-in user's saves wait
  behind them (the busy timeout is 10 s, then an error).
- **Run:** partly: the serialisation was measured with 12 concurrent sign-ins; a competing
  write was not timed.
- **Remedy:** verify the password in a read (`srv.db.read()`), then open the write
  transaction only to record the failure, a rehash or the session.

<a id="cr3-353"></a>

### CR3-353: the middleware's problems carry their own title table, which names 404 and 429 "Error" and 503 differently from the routes

*Work pad: U09-F1 in [`unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-09-web-ui-architecture.md); found while checking, confirmed by the sweep.*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S13.8.3 (every error is a problem document with a
  `title`).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/middleware.py:41-47`:
  ```text
              "title": {403: "Not allowed", 413: "Too large", 503: "Not available"}.get(
                  status, "Error"
              ),
  ```
  against `errors.py:17-31` (`TITLES`: 404 "Not found", 429 "Too many requests", 503 "Not
  available yet"), which `problem_response` uses for every route's problem.
- **What goes wrong:** the middleware's 404 (Settings › Network's refusal), its 429
  (`uploads-in-flight`) and its 503 `locked` carry titles that differ from the same status
  answered by a route: "Error" for 404 and 429, "Not available" for 503. A client that shows
  `title` shows two wordings for one status.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** `_send_problem` takes its title from `errors.TITLES`.
- **Alternatives:** none.

<a id="cr3-354"></a>

### CR3-354: `keys init` run with `docker compose run` shows the recovery sheet on `/dev/tty`, which Docker's log keeps for a container with a tty

*Work pad: U10-F1 in [`unit-10-encryption-settings.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-10-encryption-settings.md); found while checking, confirmed by the sweep.*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S15.4.4 ("shows the phrase on the terminal, never on
  standard output, so that no log keeps it"), S15.1.7 (the logs hold no secrets), review 2's K10;
  `docs/operations.md:101-103` ("never in Docker's logs").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/keys/cli.py:41-62`:
  ```text
  class Terminal:
      """The controlling terminal, for a recovery sheet and the words typed back: never
      standard output, which Docker's logs keep (S15.4.4). A command that shows a sheet
      refuses to run without one."""
  ```
  with `say` and `ask` writing to the file `open_tty()` opened.
- **What goes wrong:** a container started with a tty (`docker compose run --rm moolakata keys
  init`, the documented way) has one pseudo-terminal, and the daemon's log driver reads
  everything written to it, `/dev/tty` included. A throwaway container run with `docker run -t`
  and `echo X > /dev/tty` showed `X` in `docker logs`. So the sheet and the three words typed
  back go to the container's log, kept past the command where the log driver is journald,
  syslog or a shipper (with the default driver and `--rm` the log goes with the container).
  `Terminal` keeps the sheet off standard output, which does not keep it out of the log.
  `keys rotate` is run with `compose exec` (`operations.md:280`), whose output the daemon does
  not log (not tested).
- **Run:** a throwaway container only (`docker run -t`, `/dev/tty` write, `docker logs`); the
  command itself was not run.
- **Remedy:** say in S15.4.4 and `operations.md` that `keys init` is run with `docker compose
  exec` against a started server, or with a log driver that keeps nothing; or write the sheet
  where no log reads it (the host's terminal, a file only the admin reads).
- **Alternatives:** none simpler that keeps `run --rm`.

<a id="cr3-355"></a>

### CR3-355: the host events queue is removed before its events are recorded, so a failed record loses them

*Work pad: U10-F2 in [`unit-10-encryption-settings.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-10-encryption-settings.md); found while checking, confirmed by the sweep.*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S15.4.7 ("What a one-off command did is queued … and
  reaches Activity within a quarter of an hour").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/store/maintenance.py:151-158` unlinks the file as it
  reads it; `web/server/src/moolakata/runtime.py:307-322` records the events afterwards in
  `with server.db.tx() as s:`.
- **What goes wrong:** an error in the record (a locked or full database, a failed write) after
  the unlink loses every queued event: a `keys verify` never reaches Activity, so Key storage
  and the yearly alert do not count it, and nothing says it was lost.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** rename the file to a private name, record, then delete the renamed file (this
  also closes U10-16's window).
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

<a id="cr3-356"></a>

### CR3-356: the no-network test's loopback tests are string prefixes and a suffix, so a name or an address that only looks like loopback is not counted

*Work pad: U11-F1 in [`unit-11-implementation.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-11-implementation.md); found while checking, confirmed by the sweep.*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S17.2.1 ("failing on any socket opened to a
  non-loopback address").
- **Code:** `web/server/tests/test_no_network.py:24-27` and `:39-41`:
  ```text
  if int(port, 16) == 0 or host.endswith("0100007F") or host.endswith("00000000"):
      continue
  ...
  if not host.startswith(("127.", "::1", "localhost")):
      attempts.append(f"{host}:{address[1]}")
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** `connect()` also takes a host name, so a connect to `localhost.example.net`
  or `127.0.0.1.nip.io` starts with a loopback prefix and is not recorded. In `_peers()`, the
  hex form is little-endian, so `endswith("00000000")` skips every IPv6 peer whose last 32 bits
  are zero, and `endswith("0100007F")` every one whose last 32 bits are `127.0.0.1`, whatever
  the first 96 bits are. Both are contrived next to U11-1 (in an empty namespace `_peers()` is
  always empty, and the patched `connect` sees only Python's own), but the checks do not test
  what their comments say.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** parse the address (`ipaddress.ip_address(host).is_loopback`, resolving a name
  first), and in `_peers()` compare the whole address, not a suffix.
- **Alternatives:** none needed once U11-1's remedy records attempts another way.

<a id="cr3-357"></a>

### CR3-357: a failed `sign-in` call abandons the rest of that round

*Work pad: U12-F1 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md); found while checking, confirmed by the sweep.*

- **Kind:** bug. **Req:** S18.5 (a report after each run), S18.7.1.
- **Code:** `connector/moolakata_connector/core.py:408-412` and `:445`, `_run`, call
  `/connector/v1/connections/<id>/sign-in` outside any `try`:
  ```text
                  if address and c.sign_in != address:
                      c.sign_in, c.sign_in_at = address, time.time()
                      self.server.call(
                          f"/connector/v1/connections/{c.id}/sign-in", {"address": address}
                      )
  ```
  and, after a run, `if r.sign_in:` the same call. `tick` (`:303-322`) runs `_run` for each due
  Connection inside `with self._lock`, and `run_forever` catches whatever comes out.
- **What goes wrong:** a `ServerError` (the owner's `limit`, a refusal) or an unsigned answer
  from this call leaves `tick` at once: the Connections after it aren't run, `_post_backlogs`
  and the after-run report are skipped for that round. The next round goes on, as `c.sign_in`
  already equals the address, so it is one lost round rather than a stall; the address still
  reaches MoolaKata in each report's `signInAddress`.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** catch the call's failure in `_run` and go on, as the file posts should (U12-6).
- **Alternatives:** none needed.

### CR3-358: schema.md's §3.2 "Reserved" records what is "not declared yet", a build status

*Work pad: D3-21 in [`docs-3-protocols.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-3-protocols.md).*

- **Kind:** CLAUDE.md (schema.md follows the requirements' no-status rule). **Needs the user:** whether a reserved prefix is a standing
  rule or a status line.
- **Req:** CLAUDE.md "Requirements are a statement, not a changelog" (`docs/schema.md` follows
  the same rule); `schema.md` §3.
- **Where:**
  - `schema.md:70`: "The prefixes of the tables that exist."
  - `schema.md:143–144`: "The prefixes of the entities the requirements name whose tables are not
    declared yet. A row moves to §3.1 when its table is." (one row: `Posn`, `Position`).
- **What goes wrong:** the two lists describe the build's progress (which tables exist), and the
  §3.2 row changes when the code does, not when the design does; CLAUDE.md keeps that out of
  schema.md. It also leaves R2.7's Position, a defined term, with a prefix and no table, which
  is Phase 2's to judge.
- **Remedy:** one table of prefixes, each row the entity's; whether a table is declared is the
  code's to say (the §5 check can still fail on a declared table with no row).
- **Alternatives:** keep §3.2, worded as a rule ("a prefix may be reserved for an entity the
  requirements name before its table is declared").

<a id="cr3-359"></a>

### CR3-359: S1.6.2.3 names each Bitcoin Core wallet by the account's id; `schema.md` §2 says nothing visible on disk carries an id

*Work pad: DC-7 in [`docs-compare.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/docs-compare.md).*

- **Kind:** contradiction across blocks (privacy). Not withheld: what it exposes is an account's creation time, on the household's own
  node, and the requirement is public; it tells no one how to attack a running server.
- **Ids:** S1.6.2.3 (block 1 digest: "a watch-only descriptor wallet per account named
  `moolakata-<account id>`") and `schema.md` §2 (block 3 digest: "file names and anything
  visible on disk … use separate random names, never an id").
- **Where:**
  - `S1-accounts.md:207–208`: "Bitcoin Core over JSON-RPC: a watch-only descriptor wallet for
    each wallet account (`moolakata-<account id>`)".
  - `schema.md:53–55`: "**IDs reveal when a row was created.** File names and anything else
    visible on disk, such as receipt files and a linked copy's files (requirements S11.9), use
    separate random names and never an ID."
  - `test-plan.md:44` (T3) expects the same name.
- **What goes wrong:** Bitcoin Core keeps each wallet as a directory named after it, on the
  node's disk, and lists it to anyone with RPC access; the UUIDv7 in the id gives the
  account's creation time to the millisecond, which the schema's rule exists to keep off disk.
- **Remedy:** the wallet is named `moolakata-<random>` with the name kept on the account, in
  S1.6.2.3 and T3.
- **Alternatives:** keep the id and add the node's wallets to `schema.md` §2 as a stated
  exception, with why.
- **Units:** 02 items-loans-education-bitcoin (S1.6).

### CR3-361: what could still roll over to a Roth IRA leaves out the earnings on old contributions

*Work pad: U02-10 in [`unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S1.8.4 ("how much could still roll over … under the lifetime
  limit …, excluding contributions of the last five years").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/education/__init__.py:237-246`:
  ```text
  cutoff = _years(today, -ROTH_RECENT_YEARS)
  old = sum((r.amount for r in rows if r.date <= cutoff), ZERO)
  eligible = max(old - used, ZERO)
  ...
      available=min(max(limit - used, ZERO), eligible),
  ```
  while the screen says (`web/ui/components/edu.js:313-314`): "Contributions of the last five
  years, and their earnings, can't roll over".
- **What goes wrong:** the cap is the old contributions alone, so the earnings on them never
  count. Case: a 529 opened in 2008 with $20,000 contributed by 2012, worth $60,000 now: the
  screen shows **Could still roll over $20,000**, where the account could roll the lifetime
  $35,000 (the balance less the last five years' contributions and their earnings is far
  above it). The figure contradicts the note printed under it.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** eligible = the account's value less the last five years' contributions (and
  their earnings, estimated by the account's return since each, or simply the contributions
  where earnings can't be told), less what was rolled over; `available` its minimum with the
  lifetime limit left.
- **Alternatives:** S1.8.4 and the note say the cap is the older contributions alone
  (conservative), and the code stays.

### CR3-363: a bitcoin transaction is dated in the server's zone, not its owner's

*Work pad: U02-17 in [`unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-02-items-loans-education-bitcoin.md).*

- **Kind:** wrong figure. **Req:** S1.6.2.6 (each transaction recorded), S14.9.2, S14.9.5
  ("Today" and dates for a user follow their zone), S8.1 (the fee's sale on Form 8949).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/bitcoin/sync.py:204` and `:222`:
  ```text
  zone = ZoneInfo(timezones.host_zone())
  ...
          date=t.confirmedAt.astimezone(zone).date(),
  ```
  where `timezones.zone_for(user.timeZone)` (`timezones.py:136-138`) gives the owner's.
- **What goes wrong:** a block time has no calendar date until a zone gives it one, and the
  owner's is the one S14.9 sets for them. Case: a server in America/Chicago, an owner whose
  Profile says America/Los_Angeles; coins sent at 22:30 Pacific on Dec 31 2026 (06:30 UTC,
  00:30 Chicago on Jan 1) are dated Jan 1 2027: the network fee's sale lands in the 2027 tax
  year and the transfer pairs (S1.7.1, 3 days) and lots are dated a day late for that owner.
  With *Auto* (the default) both agree, so it shows only for an owner who chose a zone.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** date each transaction in `timezones.zone_for(owner.timeZone)` of the account's
  owner.
- **Alternatives:** date bitcoin by UTC everywhere and say so in S1.6.2.6 (simpler, but a day
  off for every US owner in the evening).

### CR3-365: a PDF with a text layer on one page is never OCR'd on its scanned pages

*Work pad: U06-15 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S9.8.2.1 ("A page counts as having a text layer when it gives at
  least 20 characters"), S9.8.2.2 (scanned PDFs are rendered and OCR'd).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/receipts/ocr.py:159-164`:
  ```text
          texts = [pdf[i].get_textpage().get_text_range() for i in range(len(pdf))]
      finally:
          pdf.close()
      if not any(len(t.strip()) >= TEXT_LAYER_CHARS for t in texts):
          return None
      return [ln.strip() for t in texts for ln in t.splitlines() if ln.strip()]
  ```
  One page with 20 characters makes the whole file a text-layer read.
- **What goes wrong:** an emailed invoice whose first page is a typed cover letter and whose
  second is the scanned receipt: the reader keeps the letter's text only, finds no total, and
  the scan is never read or searchable. A requirement that judges per page reads page 2 by OCR.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** per page: take a page's text layer when it has 20 characters, else render and
  OCR that page (within the page cap), and keep the method as `text`, `ocr` or both.
- **Alternatives:** S9.8.2.1 says the file is read from its text layer when any page has one.

### CR3-366: a statement imported by a write sharer stays his receipt, seen in full after the share ends

*Work pad: U06-21 in [`unit-06-receipts-storage.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-06-receipts-storage.md).*

- **Kind:** privacy. **Req:** S9.6 ("Sharing follows what the receipt is attached to"; silent on
  statements, Phase 1's D1-25), S1.3.3 (the statement "kept as a receipt on the account"),
  S10 (removing a share ends what it showed). **Needs the user**, with D1-25.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/ui/imports.py:925` (unit 01's, noted there for this unit):
  ```text
      stored = receipts.store(s, srv, c.id, held.fileName or "statement.pdf", held.data)
  ```
  makes the importer the receipt's owner, and `receipts/__init__.py:226-227` gives an owner
  everything, whatever the account:
  ```text
      if r.ownerId == user_id:
          return "owner"
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** Alice gives Bob write on her checking account to help with the
  bookkeeping; he imports twelve monthly statements. In March she removes his share: her
  transactions disappear from his screens, but the twelve statement PDFs and their full text
  stay in his Receipts (*All*, his own), searchable, and he can still open each. Alice, the
  account's owner, can't delete them (only their owner can), and Activity names Bob as their
  uploader. Under D1-25's proposed reading (a statement is seen by whoever can read the
  account) the account decides; under the code's, the importer keeps a copy for good.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** a kept statement is owned by the account's owner (`account.ownerId`), with
  `linkedById` naming the importer, as the connector's path does with the connection's owner
  (`connector/files.py:142`).
- **Alternatives:** keep the importer as owner but have `level` give an `account`-linked
  receipt only the account's level to anyone but the account's owner.

### CR3-367: sign-up tells anyone whether a username exists

*Work pad: U07-10 in [`unit-07-users-sharing.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-07-users-sharing.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S10.1.4.1 (sign-up's answer "is the same … whether or not the
  address already has an account"), S13.7.5.1 (sign-in and reset hide whether a user exists).
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/auth/signup.py:75-77`:
  ```text
      errors = _name_problems(display_name) + _username_problems(s, username)
      if not username_errors(errors) and _waiting_for(s, SignupRequest.username, username):
          errors.append(FieldError("username", "username-taken", "That username is taken."))
  ```
  with `_username_problems` answering `username-taken` for any existing user
  (`auth/users.py:263-264`).
- **What goes wrong:** with sign-up on, anyone posts `/ui/api/signup` with `username: "dad"` and
  a bad email: a 422 `username-taken` says the account exists, `email-invalid` alone says it
  doesn't. Usernames are what sign-in and reset take, and the sign-in screen and the reset
  answer are built to keep this unsaid. Low: usernames are not secrets in S10.1.1, and
  `admin` is fixed; sign-up is off by default.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** check the username's uniqueness at approval only (it already does,
  `signup.py:177-185`) and answer a taken one like a taken email; or require the email to be
  confirmed before the username is checked.
- **Alternatives:** S10.1.4.1 says usernames are not hidden.

<a id="cr3-368"></a>

### CR3-368: a marriage an admin confirmed for someone who wasn't a user stays confirmed when that person becomes one

*Work pad: U07-16 in [`unit-07-users-sharing.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-07-users-sharing.md).*

- **Kind:** privacy. **Req:** S10.10.4 ("A spouse needs both people's confirmation, the other's
  from their own Profile (an admin confirms for a person who isn't a user)"), S10.10.1 (a new
  user "becomes that person"), S3.8, S8.14.1.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/people/__init__.py:42-50`:
  ```text
      taken = set(s.scalars(select(User.personId).where(User.deleted == false())))
      free = [p for p in same_name(s, name) if p.id not in taken]
      if len(free) == 1:
          p = free[0]
          p.sex, p.private = sex, False
          return p
  ```
  The person's relationships come with it, their `confirmed1At`/`confirmed2At` as they were;
  `may_confirm` (`:274-281`) let an admin confirm that side only because the person wasn't a
  user.
- **What goes wrong:** Mallory adds *Bob Smith* (not a user) as her spouse; an admin, asked to
  confirm it for gifts, confirms Bob's side. Months later Bob is invited and becomes that
  person: from his first sign-in the marriage counts, confirmed, for wash sales between his
  trades and Mallory's (`washes.recompute`, `spouse_user_on`) and for a joint tax book, though
  Bob never confirmed it. He sees it in Profile and can remove it, but nothing asks him.
- **Run:** no, reading alone.
- **Remedy:** when a person becomes a user (`for_new_user`, and a remote `join`), clear the
  confirmation of that person's side in each of their marriages, so it waits for them; tell
  them in Profile.

<a id="cr3-369"></a>

### CR3-369: `db shell` writes what a query returns to standard output, which Docker's log keeps, as the recovery sheet once did

*Work pad: U10-15 in [`unit-10-encryption-settings.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-10-encryption-settings.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S15.1.7 ("the logs, which must never hold
  amounts, names or secrets"), S15.4.4 (the sheet "on the terminal, never on standard output,
  so that no log keeps it"), S14.7; review 2's K10.2.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/store/maintenance.py:486-494`:
  ```text
          try:
              cur = conn.execute(buf)
              rows = cur.fetchall()
              if cur.description:
                  print(" | ".join(d[0] for d in cur.description))
              for r in rows:
                  print(" | ".join("" if v is None else str(v) for v in r))
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** `docker compose run --rm moolakata db shell` keeps the container's
  standard output in Docker's log while it runs, and for good where the daemon's log driver is
  journald, syslog or a shipper: `SELECT * FROM "Transaction"` puts payees and amounts there,
  outside the encrypted database. `keys init` and `keys rotate` moved to `/dev/tty` for this
  reason; the shell, which shows the data itself, did not.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** read and write the shell on the controlling terminal (`keys.cli.Terminal`), and
  refuse without one, as `keys init` does.
- **Alternatives:** say in S14.7 and `operations.md` that the shell's output is in Docker's log
  for as long as the daemon keeps it.

<a id="cr3-370"></a>

### CR3-370: `web/pins` holds no hash for Playwright's Chromium, only a revision checked after the download

*Work pad: U11-9 in [`unit-11-implementation.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-11-implementation.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S17.6 ("`web/pins` holds the versions and
  hashes of the dev-only downloads (Deno, Playwright's Chromium)").
- **Code:** `web/pins:5–7`: `PLAYWRIGHT_CHROMIUM_REVISION=1243`; `web/dev.sh:65–72`:
  ```sh
  (cd "$SERVER" && uv run --frozen playwright install chromium-headless-shell >/dev/null)
  local dir="${PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH:-$HOME/.cache/ms-playwright}"
  if [[ ! -d "$dir/chromium_headless_shell-$PLAYWRIGHT_CHROMIUM_REVISION" ]]; then
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** Deno's zip is checked against a SHA-256 before it runs; Chromium,
  which runs every browser test and the mockup crawl (and on CI), is whatever Playwright's CDN
  served, checked only by the name of the folder it unpacked to. A substituted build passes.
- **Remedy:** a SHA-256 of the headless-shell archive per architecture in `web/pins`, checked
  on the files Playwright unpacked (or download the archive in `dev.sh`, check it, and point
  `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH` at it).
- **Alternatives:** S17.6 says the Chromium is pinned by Playwright's version in `uv.lock` and
  its revision, without a hash.
- **Run:** reading alone.

<a id="cr3-371"></a>

### CR3-371: the seeded linked server's address is a real host name, which a dev server seeded without `--stubs` calls every hour

*Work pad: U11-13 in [`unit-11-implementation.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-11-implementation.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S17.7 ("never calls a real provider unless one
  is configured").
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/dev/seed_links.py:58`: `address="https://money.parents.ts.net",`
  (its docstring, `:4–5`: "Its address is never reached: dev mode with stubs makes no outside
  calls"); `runtime.py:133–137` skips the exchange only `if server.config.stubs`; `dev.sh run`
  allows `--seed` alone.
- **What goes wrong:** `web/dev.sh run --seed` (no `--stubs`, to try a real price source) makes
  the hourly exchange with `money.parents.ts.net`: a DNS query and, if it resolves, a signed
  link call to a name under Tailscale's real domain, from a developer's machine, unasked. The
  connector's seed uses reserved names (`ofx.umb.example`), the link's does not.
- **Remedy:** a reserved name (`https://money.parents.example`), or skip the seeded link's
  exchange in dev mode whatever `--stubs` says.
- **Run:** reading alone.

<a id="cr3-372"></a>

### CR3-372: the pairing code carries the link address before the public URL

*Work pad: U12-10 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** differs. **Req:** S18.4.1 ("MoolaKata's address"),
  `connector-protocol.md` §1.1, §4.1; `operations.md` §8, `DEPLOY.md` §8.3. D3-14.
- **Code:** `web/server/src/moolakata/connector/manage.py:72-73`:
  ```text
  def own_address(s) -> str | None:
      return settings.get(s, "linkAddress") or settings.get(s, "publicUrl")
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** D3-14's case, in the code: a server with a link address set for a
  proxy that passes only `/link/*` (operations §1.1) puts that address in the pairing code;
  the connector's `hello` to `/connector/v1/hello` there is answered 404 and the pairing fails
  `not-moolakata`, though `DEPLOY.md` §8.3 told the admin the code carries the public URL.
  The code follows `operations.md` §8's "the public URL, or the link address", in the other
  order, and neither D3-14 reading (public URL alone; or an address of its own).
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** with D3-14 decided: the public URL first (`publicUrl or linkAddress`), as DEPLOY
  says, and the screen's text says which address the code carries.
- **Alternatives:** an address of the connector's own in Settings › Connector, defaulting to
  the public URL (D3-14's alternative).

<a id="cr3-373"></a>

### CR3-373: anyone on the network can keep the connector from being unlocked, the host's `unlock` included

*Work pad: U12-12 in [`unit-12-connector.md`](code-review-0003-2026-10-24/unit-12-connector.md).*

- **Kind:** security. **Req:** S18.3.3 ("Five wrong attempts in a row, on
  its page and at the command line alike, make the next wait 15 minutes"), S18.2.4.5 (someone
  on the network "can't unlock, lock, pair, unpair or reset it"), S18.3.2.
- **Code:** `connector/moolakata_connector/vault.py:87-89, 102-106`: one counter and one wait
  for the page and the socket, checked before the words:
  ```text
          if self.waiting():
              raise VaultError(f"wait {int(self.waiting() // 60) + 1} minutes: too many wrong words")
  ```
- **What goes wrong:** after a restart, a device on the LAN posts five wrong phrases to
  `/unlock` every 15 minutes (the form needs no session; `_form`'s origin check is a browser's
  rule, `curl` sets `Origin` itself). The household's correct words, on the page or with
  `docker compose exec connector moolakata-connector unlock`, are refused *wait 15 minutes*
  for as long as it goes on: the connector stays locked and fetches nothing. The wait protects
  nothing either: the key is 128 random bits (S18.3.1), which no guessing rate reaches. New
  reason against S18.3.3 as written: it hands the network the one thing S18.2.4.5 says it
  can't have, keeping the connector locked.
- **Run:** no; rests on reading.
- **Remedy:** count waits per address on the page; the host's socket (whose access is the
  authority, S18.3.4) is never made to wait. **Needs the user**: S18.3.3 states the shared
  wait.
- **Alternatives:** drop the wait (the key can't be guessed) and keep the logging of wrong
  attempts.

## The documents and units read

each naming its work-pad id and file.

### The documents (Phase 1)

Phase 1 read the documents in three blocks, a session each, then compared their claims digests
in a fourth. `docs-1-money` read the requirements' README (R1–R3) and S1–S9 whole, with
`later.md`, `gaps.md` and `questions.md`; `docs-2-platform` the README and S10–S18 (S17's ten
item files included), with `out-of-scope.md` and `rejected-ideas.md`; both searched the mockup's
text for their screens. `docs-3-protocols` read `schema.md`, the link and connector protocols,
`operations.md`, `api.md`, `DEPLOY.md`, `RELEASE.md`, `test-plan.md` and `dependencies.md`, and
also `GENESIS.md` and `docs/external/README.md`, which §5 doesn't list. The comparison read only
the digests; five of its claims join eight block claims that are one problem seen from two
blocks (CR3-11, CR3-12, CR3-150, CR3-151, CR3-152), each under one id in place of theirs.

## Checked and sound

What each session looked at closely and found right, so the next review need not repeat it
unless the code changes. Earlier findings that still hold are named by review 2's ids; the
units' files give the lines.

**The documents.**

- `check_docs.py` found 0 problems at `6916954`; its words to read and uncited first-level
  items went to the blocks and units.
- Every digest line a comparison claim rests on matched its source. Dropped on reading: the
  order of account groups (S1.1's table is no display order), Q30 against S18.3.1, GENESIS's
  `DOCKERHUB_USERNAME` (a variable), operations §8's ranges, `If-Match`'s quotes in `api.md`.

**Unit 01, accounts and imports.**

- `importers/parsed.py`: every field from the reader's worker checked for type, length and a
  finite decimal; rows and securities capped.
- OCC parsing in three spellings; OFX option trades and ends; the stock legs of exercise and
  assignment.
- Statements: a card's CR/DR turned once, December lines on a January statement, the opening
  and closing anchors, a combined statement's parts checked apart and its PDF kept once.
- Statements expected: defaults, learning with the 20-day floor, grace, overdue (S1.11.7).
- Coverage gaps and overlaps, balance checks, the value check's ½% or $1.
- Access on every import route, held uploads bound to the uploader, jobs read by their owner.
- Migration 0029's rebuild (foreign keys off, checked after); re-import compared in cents.
- S1.4 and S1.5 built under other words (but vesting, and an exchange's API, which S1.5 leaves
  out).
- Still hold: B3, B5, B9, B18, B20, B43, C31, A22, A23.

**Unit 02, items, loans, 529 plans and bitcoin.**

- Addresses and keys: base58check, bech32/bech32m, mainnet only, BIP32 public derivation, the
  three script types, Core descriptors from ypub and zpub.
- Descriptors: the BIP 380 checksum, the taproot tweak, sorted multisig; unsupported shapes
  refused with a reason.
- The secret check first in `put_wallet`, never echoing the text.
- Esplora paging, Electrum's TOFU, Core's ranged descriptors; 6 confirmations, one row per
  txid, the fee as a sale, `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` against recording twice; bitcoin transfer pairs.
- Loans: level payments, daily and monthly interest, HELOC and ARM schedules, the last
  payment's rounding, typed splits, linking with write and an equal amount.
- The payoff planner's extra payments, recast and the invested comparison.
- 529 contributions, the 15-year date, the rollover limit; items' purity, fine ounces,
  valuations, depreciation, improvements and sale gains.
- Access on every route.
- Still hold: B6, B13, B23 (this unit's part), B36, B37, C36, K13, P3, A24, A25.

**Unit 03, lots, positions, prices and portfolios.**

- `ledger/lots.py`: the four lot orders, cent rounding with the remainder last, short options,
  expiry, assignment and exercise premium, return of capital, spin-offs, mergers with boot,
  the IRA exception, the anniversary rule with 29 February.
- `washes.py`: 30 days either side, nearest first, each replacement once, spouse on the sale
  date, taxable accounts only, proportional disallowance, the tacked holding period.
- Options: OCC tickers, settlement legs, `per_share` for adjusted contracts; strategies'
  payoffs, shapes, capital at risk and proposals.
- `prices/`: keys before enabling, the egress list from enabled sources, CBOE's chain once per
  underlying, Decimal JSON, per-instrument fallback, a typed price never overwritten.
- Valuation's order of prices; TWR, IRR and the benchmark; portfolios show viewers only what is
  shared with them.
- Rules, reimbursements, and `taxes/closed.py`'s closing of a filed year.
- Still hold: B1, B2, B8, B10, B11, B12, B22, B23, B37, C46, C47, A26, A27.

**Unit 04, projections and taxes.**

- `taxes/dates.py`: legal holidays, observed days, Emancipation Day.
- `taxtables.py`'s figures for 2016–2026 (from memory against the IRS's).
- TXF V042's codes, record order and counts; the 8949 CSV and golden files; `box_of` and the
  covered dates.
- `closed.closing_books` and its guard on the routes that use it.
- Dependents: ages, the qualifying child, two books flagged, Form 8615.
- `books.counted` and who may do what on a tax book.
- The engine's log-normal draws, fees, the 4% rule, withdrawals, education costs, retirement
  dates, seeding; `tracking()`'s band and alert; the projection routes' access.
- Still hold: P8, B3, B4, B14, B15, B16, B17, B22, C34, C41, C50.

**Unit 05, budgets, gifts and giving.**

- What a budget counts (splits, transfers, reimbursements, refunds, leave-outs), checked
  against the golden file by hand.
- The plan from a month on, yearly lines, rollover, the amber and red states.
- Reserves' balance worked out, never stored; landed money; only the active budget says what
  to move.
- Envelope To Budget and card payment lines; moves need write and are recorded.
- The active budget and a receiver's figures by their reach; recurring detection.
- Gifts: hidden from their receiver, upcoming and lead times, a person's deletion, the gift-tax
  flag, linking a purchase.
- Giving: the owner's accounts only, the DAF counted once, deductibility, the $250
  acknowledgement, Form 8283, donated shares.
- Money is `Cents` in every S7 table.
- Still hold: P2, P3, P7, B16, B22, B23, C48 (in code), N13.

**Unit 06, receipts and storage.**

- The receipt file format: a key per file, HKDF with a salt, chunk nonces with counter and last
  flag, file id and chunk as associated data; written to a temporary name, synced, renamed.
- `read_file`'s hash check; the file route's type, CSP sandbox, `no-store`, `nosniff`; 410 for
  a pruned file; type sniffing with a CHECK.
- Deduplication per owner; attach, detach and delete rights; search intersected with what the
  caller may see.
- The field parser (totals, dates, card digits); `save_reading` never overwriting a person's
  edit.
- The worker's process: `-I`, an empty environment, no pickles, bounded answers, restarts, the
  memory watch, ONNX threads and checked models.
- Retention periods and pruning (the password again, admin only, counts in Activity).
- The store: `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, read-only reads, WAL, foreign keys, soft delete, id CHECKs, the
  prefixes in code and `schema.md` agreeing.
- Migrations: a transaction per revision, a verified copy before, half-even conversions.
- `Cents`, `Dec`, `UtcTimestamp`, `Day`, `exactjson`; UUIDv7 ids; time zones from `TZ` and
  `/etc/localtime`; the secrets repository; Settings › Storage's figures and bounds.
- S14.2 built under other words (no test, CR3-243), S14.5 in unit 10, S14.8 later (L4).
- Still hold: K19, P3, N13, B39/C1, B23, B28 (this unit's part), K25.

**Unit 07, users and sharing.**

- Passwords: argon2id, the 1,024 maximum, the common list, a missing user hashed too.
- Sessions: hashed 256-bit tokens, lifetimes, rotation at a change of privilege, admin rights
  read live, every way of ending them.
- One-time tokens, two-step (RFC 6238, recovery codes), re-authentication on every S13.7.2
  route of this unit.
- Sharing: who saves, remote users read only on a live link, inactive users and groups refused,
  every read through `access` answering 404.
- Access tokens and MCP: constant-time hash, revocation, chosen items filtered, never an admin,
  60 calls a minute, Activity, foreign `Origin` refused.
- Sign-up, the welcome's phrases, the email queue, people and marriages.
- S10.5 built under other words.
- Still hold: K7, K8, K15/A12, K19, P5/C21, P8, C9, C35, C52, A1–A6, F5.

**Unit 08, linked servers.**

- Request and answer signing as `link-protocol.md` §3 says, the skew and nonce window, the
  receiver's fingerprint in the signed text.
- Joining: the key checked against the code, the secret's size, use and lifetime; nothing
  crosses before approval.
- The sealed format and the direction in its associated data; change signatures checked when
  taken and applied.
- The copy key: 12 words, never stored, wrong words limited, released and overwritten.
- The linked window's access; TLS verified or pinned, the vetted address used; only `read`
  shares to a link's active remote users cross.
- Still hold: K2, K25, P5/C20, B24, B26, B41, N9, C10/C14, C23, C33.

**Unit 09, the web app and its architecture.**

- Forwarded headers only from a trusted peer; the Origin check; the session cookie's flags.
- The locked gate's short list of routes; the egress guard's address rules.
- Errors without tracebacks or SQL; exact numbers in both directions; floats refused.
- The live stream's delivery and limits; alerts' causes, timing, recipients and emails; jobs
  seen by their starter only.
- The help pane's Markdown built as elements; dialogs' focus and `inert`.
- Configuration refusing unknown keys; durations parsed alike on both sides.
- S12.14 and S13.1 built; the five files citing no section serve requirements.
- Still hold: K2, K25, B38, B40, N15, C11, C45 (as an alert), C52, A7, A9–A11, A14, A16.

**Unit 10, encryption and settings.**

- The key hierarchy: master key, HKDF labels, the wrap, key ids and fingerprints.
- The BIP39 phrase; secrets each with a fresh data key; the streaming file format.
- The recovery sheet and typed-back words on the terminal only; `getpass` for unlock.
- The data directory's lock, file modes, keyrings written atomically; the host socket's
  directory and modes.
- Backups: verified, HMAC-signed manifests, a restore under an earlier phrase named, a newer
  revision refused.
- The unlock's counting and refusals; the welcome's phrases; re-authentication on Settings'
  sensitive changes; Settings' bounds against S16.
- S15.1, S15.2 and S15.5 built under other words.
- Still hold: K6, K25, C5, C11, A13–A15.

**Unit 11, implementation.**

- `web/ui/vendor/vendor.py`: the 14-day rule, SRI, npm provenance checked offline, the diff
  for review; `extract` verifies before unpacking.
- The Dockerfile: base by digest, uv and the lock by hashes, no `apt-get`, dev code never in the
  final image, read-only files, uid 10001.
- The compose files' hardening and the TrueNAS catalog apps' accounts, mounts and permissions
  step.
- `release.yml` and `check.yml`: tag and version, smoke before push, attestations, pinned
  actions, secrets only in `env:`; `release.sh`'s checks before signing and publishing.
- Dev mode refused in an image or a real data directory; stub sources only in dev.
- `dev/wheels.py`, `dev/audit.py check`, `static.py`'s path rules and caching, `/demo`
  sandboxed, the mockup builder's escaping; `smoke.sh` and `check.sh` against S17.8.
- S17.9's own items and S17.10 built; `ui/deno.json` serves S17.3.2 and S17.5.
- Still hold: K25 (dev mode out of the image), K1, K10 point 2, B28, C29, C51; the arm64 image
  now smoke-run on arm64.

**Unit 12, the connector.**

- The vault (AES-256-GCM under HKDF, written through a temporary file at 0600) and its 12 words
  shown on the terminal only.
- Signed calls and answers both ways (protocol §3); TLS with public roots or the pinned key.
- The sealed box bound to its Connection; the bound host and per-run allowlist; no redirects or
  decompression.
- Pairing on the server; file posts only to accounts whose sync names the Connection; reports
  keeping a name and last four digits at most.
- The connector's page: CSP, same-origin forms, small bodies, the words on every action.
- Its four test files: 36 passed.
- S18.2 built under other words.
- Still hold: review 1's F2, F3, F7; C23, B30, B31, N17, 11b.

## Decisions

1. **CR3-11** (Oct 28): option (a). A receipt's sealed bytes cross a link as they arrive,
   bounded by the largest receipt Settings allows (100 MB) sealed, on both sides; JSON bodies
   stay at 20 MB. Written into S13.7.6 and `link-protocol.md` §1.4.
2. **CR3-4** (Oct 28): as its remedy says. A receipt that can't be fetched or delivered is left
   out of its change and shown *not arrived* in the linked window, asked for again at each
   exchange; a failure after the exchange call is recorded on the link. S11.4, S12.10 and the
   mockup changed with it.

3. **The branch's history** (Oct 28): never rewritten. Branches are never rebased, only merged
   into, and a pull request is squashed, so the hints at withheld claims in the fork branch's
   history stay there and never reach `main`.
4. **CR3-3** (Oct 28): published with its fix (`05f523a`, merged in `4c7d7cc`), as no household
   uses a server yet.
5. **The other withheld claims** (Oct 28): published as they stand, for the same reason; their
   fixes come with the rest.

The rest are walked through with the user in a later session (§11.2).

## Where things stand

**Left unread or read lightly.** Each unit's file lists its own; together:

1. Migration 0001 by search only, and most later migrations for their pattern or tables, not
   their data steps.
2. Tests read by name and the parts the claims lean on, almost everywhere; unit 11's not run.
3. Browser code in part: the linked window's per-kind screens, the profile and users dialogs,
   the activity screen, the gifts and giving screens beyond the plan dialog, the stylesheets,
   most charts, the 529, items, loan and payoff components.
4. Help guides: many listed or skimmed by title only (units 03, 08, 09); the screenshots' gap
   is CR3-292.
5. Against the outside world, from memory or not at all: the brokerage statement layouts, CSV
   guesses for real banks and exchanges, the tax tables' figures, Schwab's API shapes, Bitcoin
   Core and Electrum (no node), Esplora's request load, the hashes in `build-requirements.txt`
   and `connector/requirements.txt`.
6. Not run here: Landlock (the host's kernel is 4.18), PDF viewers with `CSP: sandbox`.
7. Performance not measured: the projections list's Monte Carlo on every `GET /projections`,
   and the budgets' month walk.

**Process notes.**

1. Quoted code is fenced as `text`, never `python`: ruff formats Python fences under
   `docs/reviews/`, which would rewrite the quotes. Never run `ruff format` there without
   `--check`.
2. A unit session briefly put a scratch test in `web/server/tests/` and removed it; scratch
   files belong in the job's scratch directory.
3. The map left `web/ui/vendor/vendor.py` on no unit's list; unit 11 read it.
4. `GENESIS.md` and `docs/external/README.md` are in no §5 block; `docs-3-protocols` read them,
   and §5's row could name them.
5. The coordinator wrote two progress stamps without `date`; neither is in any file.
6. The checking pass ran after assembly (§11.4), on Sonnet 5.5 at high effort: sixteen
   checkers, 4 to 14 minutes each, then the sweep. Three things the checkers or the sweep
   judged security-relevant were moved to `withheld.md` after they had been pushed (a part of
   CR3-225, now CR3-5; CR3-72; CR3-354), and earlier pushes carried hints at withheld claims
   in the unit files; the words remain in the branch's history (`5f795a7`…`ed43a4c`), which only
   the user can decide to rewrite.

**Where to start.**

1. The walk-through (§11.2), one at a time. **The questions the checks raised** (status
   *question* in the tables): CR3-73, CR3-74, CR3-75, CR3-358, CR3-359, CR3-361 to CR3-374. **The decisions left open from
   Phase 1**, not walked before the units: CR3-6, CR3-99, CR3-7, CR3-9, CR3-11, CR3-10,
   CR3-12, CR3-151, CR3-358. From the units: CR3-74 (with CR3-9), CR3-75 (with CR3-7),
   CR3-366 (with CR3-9), CR3-267 (with CR3-10), CR3-373. CR3-4's remedy waits on CR3-11's
   choice, and unit 05 left CR3-90 to the walk-through.
2. Then the fixes, each commit naming its claims; a withheld claim's details go in with its fix.
   CR3-360 is rejected: a test already holds it.

**Time.** Phase 0: 4 minutes (06:29–06:33 PM). Phase 1: four sessions, 48 minutes
(06:43–07:38 PM). Phase 2: twelve sessions of 12–19 minutes, 3 hours 1 minute in all
(07:43–11:05 PM), no checking. Assembly: 11:10 PM to 11:16 PM. Against §12's serial estimates
(about 2 hours for Phases 0 and 1, about 13 for Phase 2 checked, a third less quick), this
quick, serial review of 101,892 lines took about 5 hours. The model experiment took 44 minutes
(11:23 PM to 12:07 AM), and the checking pass and sweep 1 hour 48 minutes (12:10 to 1:58 AM).
